# SIMF Decisions Log

Implementation decisions and assumptions that are not (or not yet) captured in a
controlled document. Each entry is dated and explains the reasoning, per the
project working rules. Newest first.

When a decision is later absorbed into a controlled document, the entry keeps a
note pointing to that document.

| ID | Date | Decision | Reasoning |
|----|------|----------|-----------|
| D-247 | 2026-06-03 | **App/CP API path-prefix split — Phase 1 (owner-directed: "FIX App\Cp Path-prefix, not only prefix, also directory … separation (project) in the future").** The single SIMF API is now segmented by audience. Every App + public route moved to `/api/v1/app/*` (**98 routes** — auth, account, public reads, and the sessions / programme / gates-operator / ai / feedback / networking / recommendations app surface) while the Control-Panel/admin surface stays at `/api/v1/admin/*` (**251 routes**, already there — so CP paths did not move). Mechanism = explicit `/app` route strings (chosen over a FastEndpoints `Group` for zero ambiguity + greppability). Two OpenAPI documents are emitted — `SIMF App API` (filtered to `/app/*`) and `SIMF CP API` (filtered to `/admin/*`). Shared typed clients were **moved, not forked**: `SimfAuthClient`→`api/v1/app/auth/`, `SimfAccountClient` & `SimfPublicClient`→`api/v1/app/`, `SimfAdminClient` inline gates/sessions→`app/` (admin base unchanged); Flutter mockup `auth_api.dart`→`/app/*`; `EmailRateLimitKeyMiddleware` credential-path allowlist→`/app/auth/*` (the one functional path-coupling, caught by 2 failing rate-limit tests); ~**454** app-side test literals across **91** files re-prefixed (admin literals untouched). `SIMF-API-001` amended to **v1.3** (§3 principles 2 & 5, §4, §13). **Verify:** Release 0/0; full suite **836/836** green (Api 720, ApiClient 13, Web 27, CP 55, Application 16, Domain 5). **Phase 2 (physical directory reorg `Endpoints/App` + `Endpoints/Cp`, split the 13 mixed app+admin files into per-surface files, for a future `SIMF.Api.App` / `SIMF.Api.Cp` project extraction) is the next step and is reorganisation-only.** | Owner wants the App API handed to an independent Flutter developer cleanly separate from the CP API, and the two surfaces extractable into separate projects later. A route-prefix split + per-audience OpenAPI docs delivers the developer-facing separation now; moving (not forking) the shared auth/account clients keeps one implementation of the security-sensitive surface, preserving API-001 principle 5 within an audience. Because CP routes already carried `/admin`, only the ~98 app routes + their callers moved, bounding the blast radius; the integration suite caught the single hidden path-coupling (the per-email rate-limit middleware). |
| D-246 | 2026-06-02 | **Data ↔ Identity DB separation reaffirmed permanent + stale "one DB" references corrected; change Definition-of-Done recorded (owner directive).** Owner: "start for separate DB context of Data from Identity permanently — NO ANY DUPLICATE OR ANY RELATION" + "update decision to 2 physically separated db". This **reaffirms D-157** (the 2026-05-29 split into two physical databases `SIMF_Identity` + `SIMF_App`) as the permanent design and **formally retires C-1** (the old one-shared-physical-DB design). **No code/schema change** — the split already shipped (separate connection strings, per-context migration histories, no cross-DB FK, bare-`Guid` cross refs resolved on read). What changed in this entry is **documentation/comment sync**: corrected the stale "shares one physical database (C-1)" text in `SimfAppDbContext`/`SimfIdentityDbContext` class docs + the two `RowAudit` config comments, and in `SIMF-DAT-001` §3 + §A.1 (which wrongly claimed "same database → one transaction → FKs still hold"), `SIMF-OIR-001` C-1 (marked superseded), and the `Developer-Guide` §17.1 — all now state two physically separated databases with **no cross-database relation/FK and no duplicated data** (the only Identity-data copies in `SIMF_App` are the deliberate immutable **audit snapshots** in `OperationLog`/`RowAudit`/`GateScan`, which are historical records, not live mirrors). Added the rule as a HARD RULE in the project `CLAUDE.md`. **Audit (this session):** confirmed no EF relation/FK crosses the two contexts (only doc-comments reference the other side), `TransactionRunner` runs on a single context, base `appsettings.json` injects strings via env (two DBs in Development). Also recorded the **change Definition-of-Done** (owner: "at end of any new function/page/api must update those docs and add e2e and other testing") — every new function/page/API ships with its docs (PAGE-INDEX + per-page) + its E2E catalogue file + unit/integration tests, in the same changeset. Historical planning/proposal docs (Sprint-1 plan C-1 row, Architecture-Refactor-Plan, DAT-001 revision-history) keep their point-in-time wording. | The two-physical-DB split (D-157) is a persistence-layer security boundary + deployment-independence win; leaving code comments + the controlled data model saying the opposite ("one shared DB", "FKs still hold") actively misleads and risks a future agent re-introducing a cross-DB FK or duplicated data. Cementing it in `CLAUDE.md` + memory + correcting the source-of-truth docs makes the invariant enforceable, per §18 (keep governing docs in sync). |
| D-245 | 2026-06-02 | **E2E test-case catalogue — full rebuild (owner directive).** The per-page E2E catalogue under `docs/tests/e2e/` (authority D-133 slice 7) was badly out of sync: only 2 of ~31 originally-scoped files were authored (the rest "pending" stubs), and **none** of the event-module / P2–P5 pages were catalogued; `PAGE-INDEX.md` was also stale (shipped pages still marked 🚧 Stub). Owner clarified the **purpose** — the catalogue is **agent-executable**: after a batch of fixes an agent reads every case and drives each page (enters real data, performs each CRUD/action, asserts each expected outcome) as a full regression pass proving production-readiness — and directed a parallel rebuild ("run many agents in parallel to re-write"). Ran a 72-agent workflow (one per real page) that read each page's actual `.razor` + endpoints + reference doc and authored a concrete, data-bearing, tool-agnostic Gherkin catalogue file: **74 pages catalogued (62 CP + 11 Web + gold-standard), ~983 scenarios** (7–21/page), each covering the golden CRUD path + every distinct function + empty/auth-gate/validation/conflict/server-500/RTL, with per-page Implementation-notes cross-mapping the covering `tests/SIMF.Api.Tests/*` xUnit cases. Regenerated `docs/tests/e2e/README.md` (full per-page index + scenario id ranges). Added a **HARD RULE** to the project `CLAUDE.md` (§ "E2E test-case catalogue"): a new page/screen/API is not done until its authored catalogue file exists + is indexed — mirroring the per-page permission rule. Auth-setup lines use the `Get-Totp` helper (no literal secret). **Verify:** all 74 docs have matrix + Gherkin + footer (0 incomplete); spot-checked `cp-admin-hall-arrivals` (accurate to D-244), `cp-admin-halls` (geofence D-240), `cp-admin-session-summaries` (محضر). Docs-only — no code change. **Follow-up:** `PAGE-INDEX.md` row refresh (shipped pages stub→real + doc/test links) still pending. | The owner's standing instruction (build the catalogue one-by-one per page/function/API) had not been followed for the recent waves, leaving no executable production-readiness proof for most of the system. A parallel rebuild grounded in the real source closes the gap fast; the CLAUDE.md HARD RULE + the memory entry prevent recurrence. |
| D-244 | 2026-06-02 | **P5.1d — operator hall-door QR-scan arrival (FDS-003 §5.4, the second arrival means).** An operator scans an attendee's badge QR at the hall door → records a `Method=QrScan` `HallAttendance`. `IHallAttendanceService.RecordQrArrivalAsync(operatorUserId, sessionId, qrId)` **reuses** the existing `IQrResolver` (D-148) to resolve the badge → attendee; requires an **Approved, non-locked** account (else 403 `ATTENDEE_NOT_APPROVED`); unknown/blank QR → 400 `ATTENDEE_QR_UNKNOWN`; unknown session → 404. **No geofence required** (the operator is physically at the door). Both arrival paths now share a DRY `OpenOrCreateArrivalAsync` helper (open-row check + insert + the D-243 race guard), so a QR scan **merges** into any existing open row (e.g. a prior geofence arrival) — never a second row. New permissions `HallArrivals.View/Record` (AdminOnly, additive DATA — no migration) gate a new CP **operator console** `HallArrivalsConsole` (pick active session → scan/enter badge → record; gated nav + page + `<AuthorizedAction>` per the HARD RULE) + admin endpoint `POST /admin/sessions/{id}/arrivals` + typed client + BFF; resolution returns the attendee name so the operator can confirm the scan. `QrArrivalResult`/`RecordQrArrivalRequest` contracts; 2 error codes. **No migration, no schema change** (`HallAttendance` + the `QrScan` enum value shipped in D-241; permissions are seeded). Audit reuses `HallArrivalRecorded` (actor=operator, subject=attendee). Tests `HallArrivalScanTests` (5: happy path, merge-with-geofence-one-open-row, unknown QR 400, non-approved 403, non-operator 403). **Verify:** Release 0/0; scan + attendance + permission 16/16; CP nav 55/55. Completes the FDS-003 §5.4 two-means hall arrival. | FDS-003 §5.4 mandates two arrival means (QR door scan + GPS geofence) merging into one row; D-241 built the GPS path, this builds the operator QR path. Reusing `IQrResolver` (the gate engine's resolver) avoids new identity-resolution surface; a dedicated console + permission (vs overloading the venue-gate operator console) keeps the session-scoped hall-attendance domain separate from venue `GateScan`. The shared helper removes the duplicate insert/guard. Additive data only — freeze-safe. |
| D-243 | 2026-06-02 | **P5.1 — pre-push adversarial-review fixes (review of D-240..D-242).** Two confirmed findings fixed before push: **(1, medium)** `HallAttendanceService.RecordGeofenceArrivalAsync` raced the one-open-row filtered unique index on a concurrent double-arrival (attendee mobile has no client busy-guard) → surfaced as an opaque 500 instead of the idempotent OK the contract promises. Wrapped the insert in `try/catch(DbUpdateException)` → detach the losing row + re-read the committed open row + return it (idempotent), mirroring `SeatReservationService.PersistWithUniquenessGuardAsync`. **(2, low)** the FR-704 gate query (`AnyAsync(sessionId,userId)`, no `LeaveUtc` filter) counts a closed/departed row as arrived — confirmed this is the **intended** reading (FDS-007 FR-704 = "has a HallAttendance enter record for the session", i.e. arrived this session, robust to a brief departure, and consistent with the future QR-path closed rows) and **documented** the deliberate divergence from the present-tense `HallAttendanceStatus.Arrived`; pinned it with a test (arrive→depart→can still ask in-window). Third finding was the same as (2). **Verify:** Release 0/0; attendance + gating 12/12 (incl. new test); idempotent re-arrival still green under the guard. | An attendee-facing idempotent action must not 500 on a double-tap; the in-repo uniqueness-guard pattern already exists. Pinning the FR-704 "ever-arrived" intent while fresh prevents a future reader "fixing" it into current-presence and breaking the spec reading. |
| D-242 | 2026-06-02 | **P5.1c — question-gating-on-arrival (FR-704) + FR-506 disposition.** `SessionQuestionService.SubmitAsync` now gates on **hall arrival**: when the session's hall has a geofence (D-240) the authoritative gate is a `HallAttendance` arrival record (D-241) — the self-assert `IsAtVenue` flag is ignored; when the hall has **no** geofence (QR-only / coordinates not yet seeded) it **falls back to the D-171 self-assert toggle**, so nothing breaks pre-seed. The venue gate moved to **after** the session load (so the hall's geofence state is known); 403 `NOT_AT_VENUE` with a hall-arrival-specific message in the geofence case. The session-end close stays the existing time-window check. Stale "geofence deferred" doc comments on the service + the submit endpoint were corrected (§18). Tests `QuestionArrivalGatingTests` (3: geofenced-no-arrival→403, geofenced-after-arrival→200, non-geofenced self-assert fallback both ways); the existing question/committee/recorded suites stay green (the fallback preserves the shipped path). **FR-506 disposition:** the *structural* requirement — "session attendance is derived from the hall-arrival records" — is satisfied by D-241 (`HallAttendance` IS the attendance record + the attendee status read); the *admin/reporting* read (per-session attendee list + live counts) is inherently the **deferred D6 statistics dashboard** (FDS-011 §5.4 reads exactly `VenueEntry`/`HallAttendance`), so no UI-less admin endpoint was built. **Verify:** Release 0/0; arrival-gating + question/committee/recorded/attendance 33/33. | FR-704 is "questions gated by hall arrival, closed at session end" — arrival = a HallAttendance record, close = the time window. The self-assert fallback keeps every non-geofenced hall (the ship-empty default) working exactly as before (D-171), so the gate strengthens only where a geofence is actually configured. Building FR-506 reporting now would duplicate the deferred D6 statistics surface. |
| D-241 | 2026-06-02 | **P5.1b — hall attendance + attendee GPS arrival/departure (FDS-003 §5.4, FR-305/506).** New `HallAttendance` entity on `SimfAppDbContext` (migration `App/D241_AddHallAttendance`, additive): SessionId+HallId real FKs (Restrict), UserId bare `Guid` (no FK, D-157), `Method` (new `AttendanceMethod {QrScan, Geofence}` enum), EnterUtc + nullable LeaveUtc. A **filtered unique index** `(SessionId, UserId) WHERE LeaveUtc IS NULL` enforces FDS-003's "one open row per attendee per session" — a door scan and a geofence crossing for the same session can only update the one open row. Attendee-facing `IHallAttendanceService` + endpoints (`RequireApprovedAccount`, actor = `sub`, self-service — no admin permission): `POST /sessions/{id}/arrival` (body lat/lon → server haversine-checks the point against the hall geofence D-240 → opens a `Method=Geofence` row; idempotent), `POST /sessions/{id}/departure` (closes the open row), `GET /sessions/{id}/attendance` (status). 400 invalid coordinate / `HALL_GEOFENCE_NOT_CONFIGURED`; 403 `NOT_AT_VENUE` outside the geofence; 404 unknown session. **Only the derived enter/leave times are stored — never the raw coordinates or a continuous track** (FDS-003 §10 sensitive PII; movement/dwell FR-1103 stays deferred). New app wire contracts (append-only). 2 audit events. Tests `HallAttendanceTests` (8). **Verify:** Release 0/0; HallAttendance 8/8. **Operator QR-door-scan path (`Method=QrScan`) split to a focused follow-on (P5.1d)** — a different actor (operator) with its own endpoint + permission + console surface; the entity + the one-open-row merge logic already support it. | The programme's FR-704 gating + FR-506 attendance both need an arrival record; the attendee's own device + the per-hall geofence (D-240) is the primary, app-driven path and is fully usable on its own. Storing only enter/leave (not raw GPS) keeps P5.1's PII surface minimal per FDS-003 §10 / NCA. Filtered unique index = the FDS-003 §6 one-open-row constraint enforced in the schema, not just the service. Additive on `SimfAppDbContext` — freeze-safe. |
| D-240 | 2026-06-02 | **P5.1a — per-hall GPS geofence config (FDS-003 §5.4 + OI-2; Completion Programme Phase 5, owner-approved freeze-lift via G-OI-2 "configurable circle, ship empty").** Added three **nullable** columns to `Hall` — `GeofenceCenterLat`, `GeofenceCenterLon`, `GeofenceRadiusMeters` (WGS-84 degrees + metres; migration `App/D240_AddHallGeofence`, additive only, no alters/drops). Per FDS-003 the geofence is **per-hall** (not one venue boundary); owner chose a **circle** (centre+radius) over a polygon. Validated all-or-nothing in `AdminHallService` (set all three or leave all null) with ranges lat −90..90, lon −180..180, radius 0<r≤100 km → 400 `HALL_GEOFENCE_INVALID`; surfaced on the existing CP Hall add/edit form (3 inputs + client all-or-nothing parse) and appended to `AdminHallDetail`/create/update contracts (append-only wire, D-219). **Ships EMPTY** — the event team seeds the real coordinates; a hall with no geofence records arrivals by QR door-scan only (P5.1b). **No new permission** (the existing `Halls.Create`/`Edit` gate the change); no behaviour change. Tests `AdminHallGeofenceTests` (5: round-trip, ship-empty null default, partial→400, out-of-range radius→400, update-clears). **Verify:** Release 0/0; geofence 5/5; CP 55/55. | The arrival/attendance chain (P5.1b/c) needs a boundary to evaluate GPS against; FDS-003 OI-2 explicitly says add the geofence to `Hall`. Nullable + all-or-nothing + ship-empty matches the "ship the mechanism, seed the value later" pattern (VenueMap/Organisation) and avoids inventing coordinates (§13). Additive on `SimfAppDbContext` — freeze-safe. |
| D-239 | 2026-06-02 | **P4.3 — AI provider seam confirmation (Completion Programme §6 "AI provider note").** Confirmed (no code change) the engineering state of all three AI touchpoints: (1) audience comments → `ICommentAiFilter` / `StubCommentAiFilter` (decides the comment landing status via `CommentAiFilterOptions.AutoApprove`), wired in `SessionCommentService.SubmitAsync`; (2) audience questions → `IQuestionAiFilter` / `StubQuestionAiFilter` (advisory verdict, D-236), wired in `SessionQuestionService.SubmitAsync`; (3) session summary / محضر → routed through the central `IAiService` + the seeded `session-summary` prompt on the Echo provider (D-238). All three ship **behind a swappable seam with a working stub** and make NO outbound calls. Switching to a real model is a **config / DI change, not code**: the summary flips `AiPrompt.Provider` from Echo to OpenAi in the CP (the real `OpenAiProvider` already exists, D-176); the two filters swap their DI registration to a provider-backed impl. The open question the plan raised — *are real AI keys available now* — is an **owner / ops decision** (the D7-style external-procurement deferral), not a code gap; until keys are procured the platform runs on the stubs. | The plan explicitly asked to "confirm whether a real AI provider + keys are available now, or we ship the seam + stub". Engineering answer: the seam is complete and swappable for all three touchpoints; flipping to a real provider needs only configuration once keys exist. Key availability is not something to invent (§13) — it stays an owner decision. |
| D-238 | 2026-06-02 | **P4.1b — Scientific-Committee AI session-summary / محضر desk (Completion Programme §6.4.1, Mockup screen 34).** The committee workflow on top of the D-237 data model: `IAdminSessionSummaryService` (List every active session + its summary state; Get; **Generate** — AI-draft via the central `IAiService` seam; **Save** — upsert/hand-write; **Publish/Unpublish**). Drafting **reuses** the existing AI module (new additive `AiFeature.SessionSummary`, a seeded idempotent `session-summary` prompt on the Echo stub provider — **no migration**; the real provider plugs in by editing the prompt's provider in the CP, no code change) rather than a new seam. New permissions `SessionSummaries.View/Edit/Publish` (AdminOnly, additive DATA) gate 6 admin endpoints + the CP `SessionSummariesList` page (gated nav + page + `<AuthorizedAction>` per the HARD RULE) + 6 typed client methods + 6 BFF routes; 4 audit events; 2 error codes; ~33 resx ×2. One summary row per session (publish flips visibility; edits are live). **Review-driven fixes (adversarial workflow):** (1) Generate writes the AI draft to the **Arabic** full-text column only — the prompt produces Arabic, so writing one language into both columns would surface wrong-language content once a real provider replaces Echo + would defeat the app's locale fallback; (2) aligned the Generate/Save lookups with `&& s.IsActive` to match every other query. **Deferred (review LOW, noted):** a `DbUpdateException`→409 concurrency guard on the first-write race (admin-only, mitigated client-side by the `_busy` button-disable; precedent in `SeatReservationService`), and dropping the currently-unexercised `SessionSummary.IsActive` column (schema change on a just-shipped table — needs owner approval; kept for soft-delete consistency with every other entity). **Verify:** Release 0/0; committee + public-read + permission 15/15; CP nav 55/55; full Api suite green. | The محضر pipeline is human-gated (the Committee reviews/edits/publishes), so AI is advisory drafting, not an auto-publish — reusing the central AI provider seam means the real model is a config change, not code (D-176 design). One-row-per-session + a publish stamp is the minimal model; the EN/AR fix prevents a silent wrong-language defect on the documented provider-swap path. |
| D-237 | 2026-06-02 | **P4.1a — AI session summary / محضر data model + public read (Completion Programme §6.4.1, Mockup screen 34).** New `SessionSummary` entity (additive table on `SimfAppDbContext`, freeze-safe under D-211/D-219; migration `App/D237_AddSessionSummary` — one table + FK to Sessions + unique `SessionId` + `(IsActive, PublishedAt)` index, no alters/drops): one summary per session, four bilingual content sections (key points [newline-delimited bullets], recommendations, speakers, full text — `nvarchar(max)` matching the News body), an `AiModel` provenance marker, a `PublishedAt` publish gate (News D-199 timestamp convention) + `PublishedByUserId`, `IsActive` soft-delete + audit stamps. Public read: `PublicSessionSummary` contract (both languages + a `GeneratedByAi` flag), `IProgrammeSessionService.GetSessionSummaryAsync`, anonymous `GET /programme/sessions/{id}/summary` (404 when missing/soft-deleted or no published summary). Gated on the summary's own `PublishedAt` (the Committee's editorial publish) + session active — **decoupled** from the broadcast `Session.Status`; anonymous like the public session detail (published editorial content, no attendee PII). Tests `SessionSummaryTests` (5: published both-languages + AI flag, hand-written flag false, draft 404, no-summary 404, soft-deleted-session 404). **Verify:** Release 0/0; SessionSummary 5/5; adjacent programme/session 29/29. Commit `1340b5f`. | The محضر is a per-session editorial document the app reads on screen 34, so a 1:1 published entity with its own publish gate is the FR build. Decoupling the publish gate from the broadcast lifecycle gives the Committee independent control of the minutes (a session need not be broadcast-Published for its minutes to be public). Bilingual sections + a publish-timestamp gate mirror the Session + News conventions. Additive on `SimfAppDbContext` — freeze-safe. |
| D-236 | 2026-06-02 | **P4.2 — advisory `IQuestionAiFilter` wired into question submit (Completion Programme §6, stage 1 of the Q&A pipeline D-212).** New `IQuestionAiFilter` (Application) + `QuestionAiVerdict(Verdict, Flagged)` record, with a deterministic `StubQuestionAiFilter` (Infrastructure) shipped behind it — mirroring the existing `ICommentAiFilter`/`StubCommentAiFilter` seam exactly. `SessionQuestionService.SubmitAsync` calls `ScreenAsync` before persisting and stores the bucket in `SessionQuestion.AiFilterVerdict` (the column shipped in D-233). The filter is **advisory only** — it never changes `Status`, so every question still lands Pending for the Scientific Committee; the stub returns the `stub-clean` bucket so the audit trail shows no real model ran. Registered `AddSingleton` (stateless). **No migration** (column pre-exists), **no new permission** (advisory tag, not a gated action). Tests: `SessionQuestionsTests` extended to assert submit tags `stub-clean` without changing `Status`. **Verify:** Release 0/0; SessionQuestions + committee 18/18. Commit `6473c43`. | The Q&A pipeline (D-212) defines an AI screen as stage 1 feeding a human committee decision (stage 2). Keeping it advisory matches the spec (the Committee decides, not the model) and the comment-filter precedent, and ships value now without an external provider — the real `IAiService`-backed filter swaps the DI line later with no service or test change (§20 design-for-change). |
| D-235 | 2026-06-02 | **P3.4 — public recorded-Q&A archive (Completion Programme §6; app screen reads a finished session's approved questions).** New `PublicRecordedQuestion` contract + `IProgrammeSessionService.ListRecordedQuestionsAsync` returning the **Approved** questions of an **active + published** session, attributed to the asker (display name resolved via a second round-trip on `SimfIdentityDbContext` per D-157, no cross-DB join). Attribution via the identity round-trip follows the established cross-context rule (D-157). |
| D-234 | 2026-06-02 | **P3.3 (pipeline) — Scientific-Committee Q&A queue + moderator desk (Completion Programme §6, stage 2 of D-212).** New permissions `Questions.View/Moderate/Escalate` (AdminOnly); `ISessionQuestionCommitteeService` + impl (`ListQueueAsync` oldest-first `.Take(200)`, `ApproveAsync`, `HideAsync`, `EscalateAsync`); committee endpoints `/admin/questions/queue` + approve/hide/escalate; CP `QuestionQueueList` page (gated nav + page + `<AuthorizedAction>` per the HARD RULE); typed client + BFF; audit `SessionQuestionApproved`/`SessionQuestionEscalated`; queue contracts. **Correctness fix (review-caught):** the moderator desk now derives `IsHidden` from `Status == Hidden` (single source of truth) and `SessionModerationService.ListAsync` filters `Status == Approved` — the old design persisted `IsHidden` separately, so re-approving a hidden question left a stale `IsHidden = true`; regression test `Re_approving_a_hidden_question_clears_the_hidden_marker`. No migration (data-only permissions; columns from D-233). Tests `SessionQuestionCommitteeTests` (7) + 2 updated. **Verify:** Release 0/0; full Api 681/681; CP nav green. Commit `66900d0`. | Stage 2 of the pipeline is a human committee gate, so the queue + approve/hide/escalate actions are the FR build; roles-only AdminOnly permissions match the access-control model (D-207). Deriving `IsHidden` from `Status` removes the asymmetry that let a question be both Approved and Hidden — one status field is the source of truth (§19 root-cause). |
| D-233 | 2026-06-02 | **P3.3 (foundation) — Q&A pipeline data model (additive, zero behaviour change).** New enums `QuestionPhase {Pre, Live}` + `QuestionStatus {Pending, Approved, Hidden, Escalated}`; `SessionQuestion` gains `Phase`, `Status`, `AiFilterVerdict`, `AssignedToRole`, `EscalatedByUserId`, `EscalatedAt` with max-lengths + indexes; submit sets `Phase` (pre/live vs session start) and `Status = Pending`. Migration `App/D233_AddSessionQuestionPipeline` hand-edited: `Phase defaultValue:1`, `Status defaultValue:1`, plus `UPDATE [SessionQuestions] SET [Status] = 2 WHERE [IsHidden] = 1;` to backfill already-hidden rows to `Hidden` without an EF `HasDefaultValue` (which would force the explicit value on every insert — the D-227 lesson). **Verify:** Release 0/0; SessionQuestions green. Commit `fd537be`. | Splitting the data model out as its own additive, behaviour-preserving slice keeps the schema change reviewable in isolation before the pipeline behaviour lands in D-234. Migration-side default + conditional `UPDATE` (not `HasDefaultValue`) is the correct backfill when the CLR default differs from the intended seed for existing rows. Additive on `SimfAppDbContext` — freeze-safe under D-211/D-219. |
| D-232 | 2026-06-02 | **P3.2b — session recording: storage + range-streaming + scoped stream token (Completion Programme §6).** Out-of-row recording bytes behind `ISessionRecordingStorage` (filesystem adapter + `SessionRecordingStorageOptions`, root mirrored across both appsettings per §18); `Session` gains 6 recording columns; admin upload (video extension → canonical content-type) + delete endpoints; a separate short-lived **stream JWT** (`JwtOptions.StreamAudience`/`StreamTokenMinutes`, `CreateRecordingStreamToken`, a 2nd `AddJwtBearer` `StreamToken` scheme reading `?access_token=`) backing a HTTP-range streaming endpoint (206) that sets `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. Migration `App/D232_AddSessionRecording`. Tests `SessionRecordingTests` (11). **Security fix (review-caught):** recordings were storable as arbitrary content-type (e.g. `text/html`) → browser MIME-confusion; fixed with extension-allowlist → canonical video content-type + `nosniff`. **Verify:** Release 0/0; full Api 674/674. Commit `e328a61`. | Recordings are large and not session-scoped reads, so out-of-row storage + a dedicated scoped stream token (not the main API cookie/JWT) is the right seam — the token authorises exactly one recording for a bounded window, and range-streaming serves players without buffering. | D-220 | 2026-06-01 | **B3 — Organisation lookup (visitor الجهة) + government-Excel bulk import (as-built, slice 4a).** New `Organisation` entity on `SimfAppDbContext` (NameAr required ≤256; NameEn/CommercialRegistration/Sector/City/Phone/Email/Website optional; `IsActive` soft-delete; timestamps) via additive migration `App/D220_AddOrganisationLookup` — filtered-unique index on `CommercialRegistration WHERE [CommercialRegistration] IS NOT NULL` + index `(IsActive, NameAr)`; nothing else altered. **Owner decisions (AskUserQuestion):** (1) Organisation is a **separate** table from `Company` — `Company` stays the curated CP exhibitor/sponsor (booths + provisioned accounts); `Organisation` is the large Saudi-companies lookup the team bulk-loads and a visitor picks as their الجهة. Built under the D-219 freeze-lift. **Verify:** `dotnet build SIMF.slnx -c Release` 0/0; CP tests 55/55; `OrganisationTests` + the two contended `AdminUpdateUserTests` green in a clean focused run (the prior full 609-test run was anomalously throttled at 1h56m and surfaced 1 real test bug — the picker test called the authenticated endpoint without a token, now fixed — plus 2 auth-rate-limit contention flakes that pass in isolation). | Owner authorised the Organisation lookup + Excel import; this is the as-built record. Separate table (not merged with `Company`) keeps a ~tens-of-thousands gov import out of the small curated booth/account set and avoids tangling soft-delete/account semantics. | D-219 | 2026-06-01 | **Owner lifted the D-110 freeze for the audit-driven build wave (App schema only).** Owner directive "NO Freeze, remove any freeze now" → the D-110 schema/enum freeze is lifted as an active gate for new **additive** App-context (`SimfAppDbContext`) schema work in this wave (Organisation lookup, Booth↔Company + booth-officer contact, audience-comment likes, networking connections, session speaker/host role + type, and any further audit-driven additions), each landing as an additive migration. The **Identity** schema stays frozen and the existing enums stay frozen against rename/reorder (additive values still allowed): nothing in this wave needs Identity changes, and the Identity wire surface backs the shipped mobile app + the NCA posture, so lifting it is deferred to an explicit separate decision. The freeze must be **re-instated before the production publish / handover**, and even with the schema freeze lifted the **shipped mobile wire contract** (public JSON field names the app decodes) must be preserved. Recorded in `CLAUDE.md` FREEZE section. | The owner owns the freeze and explicitly lifted it, satisfying the §6/D-110 "explicit owner approval" gate. Scoping the lift to additive App tables keeps the Identity/wire contract stable (the D-186/199/211/217 precedent) while unblocking the data-model corrections the EF-relationship audit surfaced. Keeping Identity frozen is the conservative default since no wave item requires it. |
| D-218 | 2026-06-01 | **P2.1 — FAQ management (the `/m/faq` stub → real `/admin/faq`).** Built the two-level FAQ CRUD authorised by D-211: new `FaqGroup` (NameEn/Ar, DisplayOrder, IsActive) + `FaqEntry` (FK FaqGroupId cascade, QuestionEn/Ar ≤512, AnswerEn/Ar ≤4000, DisplayOrder, IsActive) tables on `SimfAppDbContext` via one additive migration (`App/D211_AddFaq`). **Pattern:** mirrors the D-199 News module — `IAdminFaqService` (Application) + `AdminFaqService` (Infrastructure) querying the DbContext directly (validation in-service, one audit row per mutation, soft-delete via `IsActive`) — **not** the Interests repository split (the simpler direct-DbContext service is the current convention for new event modules). New `Faq.View/Create/Edit/Delete` permission codes (AdminOnly, additive DATA — no seed migration) gate all 10 endpoints + the CP page + the action buttons. CP page is a single self-contained `FaqManager.razor` (groups table + per-group entries table + two modals) rather than two `SimfDataGrid`s — FAQ is small-scale (a handful of groups, dozens of entries) so it loads all rows with no server paging, mirroring the custom `SessionSeatPlan` page; scoped `FaqManager.razor.css` (tokens only). **Scope:** CP-management only (like News/Media shipped CP-first under D-199); the public app/website FAQ read surface is deferred to the content wave. 6 audit events + 3 error codes + 36 resx keys ×2 cultures + 5 integration tests (`FaqTests`). | D-211 already authorised the FAQ tables + `Faq.*` perms; this is the as-built record. Following the News pattern (direct-DbContext service) over the Interests repo split keeps new modules consistent with the most recent precedent and avoids an unnecessary repository layer. Permissions as DATA on the pre-existing `Permission`/`RolePermission` tables keep it freeze-safe. The table-based CP page (vs two grids) is the right altitude for FAQ's tiny scale and avoids ~30 grid-label resx keys per grid; the public read endpoint is deferred because, like News, the consuming surface (app/website) is a separate wave and building it now would be a renderer with nothing wired to it. |
| D-217 | 2026-06-01 | **P1.7 — notification triggers: booking-confirmed dispatch + automated session-reminder worker (a targeted D-110 freeze-lift).** Two additive `NotificationKind` values (persisted by name, so no wire/schema impact): **`BookingConfirmed=40`** and **`SessionReminder=41`**, each with its `[Display]` + EN/AR `ResNotificationKind` strings. **(a) BookingConfirmed (`fea57cb`, no schema):** `SeatReservationService` now injects `INotificationDispatcher` and, after persist+audit on both `ReserveAsync` and `ReserveRandomAsync`, dispatches an in-app booking confirmation naming the seat + session (the session `Title`/`TitleArabic` are threaded through `SessionSnapshot`→`SessionContext`, already loaded in `BuildContextAsync`). The dispatch is wrapped in try/catch-and-log: the dispatcher writes to the **Identity** DbContext, a separate transaction from the already-committed seat on the **App** DbContext, so a notification failure must never fail/rollback the booking. In-app only (`SendEmail=false`), like `InvitationReceived`. **(b) SessionReminder (this entry, freeze-lift):** owner pre-authorised the **automated scheduler** option (over a manual/no-persistence approach). New `SessionReminderWorker : BackgroundService` (mirrors `RegistrationGateAutoCloseWorker` — 1-min poll, 1-min startup delay, scope-per-tick), 30-minute lead window. Its core is an `internal static RunReminderScanAsync` (InternalsVisibleTo, directly unit-tested): finds active sessions with `StartUtc` in `(now, now+lead]` not yet reminded, notifies every attendee with an active seat, then stamps the dedup guard. **Freeze-lift:** one **additive nullable column `Session.ReminderSentUtc`** on `SimfAppDbContext` (migration `D217_AddSessionReminderSentUtc`) — the chosen minimal persistent dedup (over a new dedup table, and over an in-memory set which would resend on restart). Granularity is **per-session-once** (a visitor booking *after* the reminder fired gets no late reminder — accepted for a "starts soon" nudge). Identity schema + all enum names/values stay frozen (additive values only). **Verification:** `-c Release` 0/0; SeatReservations+Notification 22, SessionReminder 2 new, full API suite green. | The owner declared the notification triggers mandatory and pre-chose the automated scheduler "needs freeze-lift" path, so this executes an approved freeze-lift rather than introducing an unapproved schema change. A persistent dedup is the robust choice the scheduler implies — an in-memory set double-sends after any restart during a session's reminder window, which `RegistrationGateAutoCloseWorker` already avoids by persisting its flag; a single nullable column is the least-surface way to get that robustness (no new table/entity/config). Per-session-once is the right granularity because the worker sends one batch per session and the lead window is short. Extracting the scan as an internal static keeps the worker testable without driving the BackgroundService loop (mirrors how the codebase keeps worker logic verifiable) and the try/catch-per-attendee guarantees one bad dispatch can't strand the dedup stamp or the rest of the batch. Booking-confirmed is in-app-only because a seat self-pick is low-stakes and there is no email template to invent. The lead time is a documented constant (matching `RegistrationGateAutoCloseWorker`'s constants); promote to an Options binding if ops needs to tune it. |

| D-214 | 2026-06-01 | **P1.3 — admin-queue bulk approve/reject parity, the `Others.Approve` gate fix, and per-user Edit (Visitor/Other) including login-email change.** Part A (committed `94c21ea`): the Admin (staff) pending queue gained bulk approve/reject mirroring the visitor/other queues (D-164/D-209), routing through the existing `ApprovalScope.Admin` single path; and a **security fix** — `BulkApproveOthersEndpoint` was gating on `Visitors.Approve` (flagged in D-210 as a separate decision) and is corrected to `Others.Approve`. Email change is built as a **guarded** update — uniqueness re-check, security-stamp roll, and refresh-token revoke (same protections as a sensitive identity change) — not a plain editable field; DisplayName/ProfileType-only edits skip the stamp roll. Approval state stays on the approve/reject path (not editable here). Gated by `Visitors.Edit` / `Others.Edit` (both already in the catalogue). | Bulk admin parity closes the last FR-212 gap (the admin queue had single-row approve/reject but no Select-All). The `Others.Approve` fix is a real privilege-escalation defect (an admin holding only visitor-approve could bulk-approve partner accounts), promoted from "observation" to "fix" because the owner declared all gaps mandatory. For Edit, the owner wanted the complete surface; email is the sign-in identity, so it cannot be a naive field write — re-checking uniqueness + rolling the security stamp + revoking tokens prevents a stale-session / duplicate-login hazard, matching how Identity-level changes are handled elsewhere. Editing DisplayName/ProfileType alone is low-risk and skips the stamp roll so a cosmetic rename does not sign the user out. |
| D-213 | 2026-05-31 | **Session-recording storage + delivery decided (engineering call, delegated by owner, made from researched best practices).** Owner asked for the best-practice approach to saving + streaming session video rather than choosing from options. Researched current practice (ASP.NET range streaming, filesystem vs object storage/MinIO, HLS vs progressive MP4, signed/token URLs). **Decision for V1.0.0:** recordings are produced out-of-band by venue AV, uploaded/linked by the Scientific Committee, stored on the **on-prem filesystem** at a configured root (reusing the D-90 path-in-DB / bytes-on-disk policy + `StorageOptions`, e.g. new `Storage:SessionRecordingBase`); served as **progressive MP4 over HTTP range streaming** (`enableRangeProcessing: true`, async I/O, no whole-file `ReadAllBytes` — unlike the small-file image/avatar path) through a **short-lived JWT** endpoint behind the reverse proxy; **volume-level encryption** (BitLocker) + the recordings folder added to backup/rollback. All behind a new **`ISessionRecordingStorage`** seam mirroring `IMediaImageStorage` / `IUserIdDocumentStorage`. **Deferred as a documented upgrade path:** MinIO/object storage + HLS adaptive (ffmpeg) + per-segment signed URLs. Broadcast model confirmed by owner as **Option A (recorded → published, no realtime)**. | A single annual forum is a few dozen recordings (low-hundreds-of-GB), not streaming-platform scale, so MinIO's multi-node scaling and an ffmpeg HLS transcoding pipeline add deployment/security/backup burden + deadline risk for little near-term gain; the `ISessionRecordingStorage` seam keeps both upgrades open without a redesign. The chosen stack still honours every best practice that matters at this scale — range streaming (no OOM, seekable), token-scoped access (no permanent public URLs), encryption-at-rest, behind the proxy — and reuses the codebase's proven D-90 file-storage pattern. Sources recorded in the research note in chat. |

Three open confirms before P3/P4: broadcast option, Request-Interview scope (screen 27, vs the existing delegation `MeetingRequest`), and AI-provider availability (real keys vs seam+stub). | The owner asked for one phased plan covering the remaining ~20–25%. **created a custom role "Programme Editor"** → opened its permission editor via the row action → ticked View/Edit sessions + View themes → **"Permissions saved."**; **created `programme.editor@simf.test` with the Programme Editor role** via the create-form multi-select → "Account created…"; reopened that user's **Edit-roles editor → Programme Editor pre-checked** (the existing-user assignment now works). **Zero console errors** across the whole session. | D-207 | 2026-05-31 | **Per-page / per-action permission system — enforced in both the API and the Control Panel, configurable by role.** Built the full authorization model the gap report (D-aafe769) flagged as the largest functional gap: a holder of a role sees and reaches only the pages and actions granted to that role, and cannot bypass it in either surface. **Owner-approved design:** assignment is roles-only (a user gets permissions by holding a role; no per-user grants); permissions are resolved from roles once and **baked into the JWT** as multi-valued `perm` claims (no per-request DB lookup); **Administrator = wildcard `*`** (never expanded into individual codes — keeps the token small and the super-admin un-lockout-able). **No schema change and no migration** — the `Permission` + `RolePermission` tables already exist in the D-110 frozen schema, so the catalogue is seeded as data only (additive, freeze-safe). Delivered in 6 commits. **Phase 1 — catalogue.** `PermissionCatalog` (SIMF.Common): ~132 `(Page, Action) → Code` codes as `const string`s (so Razor `@attribute` can use them) + a flat `All` list of `PermissionDef` the seeder iterates; the six pre-catalogue codes (D-148 gate triad, D-168 PR/VIP triad) keep their exact strings + grants; `IdentitySeeder` seeds the whole catalogue idempotently (replacing the 6 hand-written `EnsurePermissionAsync` calls with a loop); doc `SIMF-Permission-Catalogue.md`. **Phase 2a — API infra.** `IPermissionResolver`/`PermissionResolver` (Administrator→`["*"]`, else `IPermissionRepository.GetCodesForRolesAsync` — a `RolePermission⋈Role⋈Permission` join); `JwtTokenService.CreateAccessToken` mints the `perm` claims; all three mint paths thread it (`SignInService`, `SessionService` refresh, `DeviceKeyService`); a `PermissionRequirement` + wildcard-aware `PermissionAuthorizationHandler` + dynamic `PermissionPolicyProvider` (materialises `perm:<code>` on demand, delegates other names to the default provider). **Phase 2b — endpoint sweep.** Replaced the role gate (`AdministratorOnly` / `GatesManage` / `GatesOperate` / `GatesViewOwnReports` / `PublicRelationsAccess`) with the matching `perm:<code>` policy on **~190 admin endpoints across 58 files**, keeping `RequireApprovedAccount` + all rate-limit/upload config; removed the now-redundant trailing `GatesManage` role-policy from the 9 gate endpoints; `DELETE /admin/device-keys/{id}` intentionally stays `AdministratorOnly` (niche admin-only action, no catalogue code). **Phase 3 — CP enforcement.** A CP mirror of the policy spine + a `RequirePermissionAttribute`; `/auth/complete` copies the JWT `perm` claims into the auth cookie; `CpNavigation` items carry a `RequiredPermission` and `CpShellLayout` filters the side menu (hides empty groups; the dashboard + not-built stubs stay ungated); an `AuthorizedAction` component hides action buttons; **49 admin pages** switched `[Authorize(Roles=…)]` → `[RequirePermission(…)]` (`SessionModerationDesk` stays `[Authorize]` — its access is data-scoped per-session at the API). **Phase 4 — config + assignment UI.** `IAdminRoleService.Get/SetPermissionsAsync` (Set diff-applies the role's `RolePermission` rows, validates codes against the catalogue, refuses baseline roles, audits `Role.PermissionsUpdated`) + `GET/PUT /admin/roles/{id}/permissions` (`Roles.View` / `Roles.AssignPermissions`) + client + BFF proxy + `RolePermissionsEditor.razor` (grouped checkboxes, reached from the Roles details modal); `CreateAdminForm`'s single "Grant Administrator" checkbox became a role multi-select (the user→role assignment). **Phase 5 — bypass tests.** `PermissionEnforcementTests` (integration): a role granted only `Sessions.View` gets 200 on `/admin/sessions/list` and **403** on `/admin/themes/list`; Administrator wildcard reaches both; a no-permission admin is 403'd everywhere. `PermissionResolverTests`, `PermissionCatalogTests`, `CpNavigationPermissionTests`. **Bug found + fixed by the new tests:** in `PermissionCatalog` the `AdminOnly`/`GateOperator`/`PublicRelations` baseline lists were declared *after* `All = BuildAll()`, so static-init order captured their null defaults — every `BaselineRoles` came out null, which would NRE the seeder's grant loop at real startup; fixed by ordering the fields before `All`. **Simplify pass:** removed 4 resx keys my own edits orphaned (`Admin.Roles.Details.PermissionEditorDeferred`, `Admin.CreateUser.GrantAdministrator`, EN+AR). **Verification:** full solution builds `-c Release` **0 warnings / 0 errors**; **full suite green in Release — 685 tests, 0 failures** (Api 569, ControlPanel 55, Web 27, ApiClient 13, Application 16, Domain 5; 566→569 = +3 new integration tests, no regression from the 190-endpoint sweep). **Reported, deliberately NOT changed (per §14/§17):** the now-dead `Permissions` constants class + the 4 unused role-based `AuthorizationPolicies` (orphaned by the sweep, in files I didn't author — flagged for an owner-approved cleanup, not deleted unilaterally); the per-row seeder loop (acceptable one-time seeding; sibling methods batch, this doesn't); the triple JWT-parse in `/auth/complete` (pre-existing pattern the new extractor follows). **Known limitation:** `SimfCookieRefreshHandler` rotates the access token every ~28 min but does not re-derive the cookie's `perm`/role/`account_state` claims (pre-existing D-121 behaviour; Issue-1 widens it to `perm`), so a mid-session grant/revoke isn't reflected in the CP *UI* gates until re-sign-in — **the API always re-checks the fresh JWT, so this is UX/visibility staleness, not an authorization bypass.** | The gap report named the permission system the biggest gap, and the owner approved the shape (roles-only, JWT-baked, Admin=wildcard, sequence gap-report→permissions→E2E) before the build. Baking permissions into the token is the standard ASP.NET resource-action pattern (resolved at mint, not per request); the dynamic policy provider is Microsoft's documented way to avoid registering ~130 named policies. The wildcard keeps tokens small and guarantees the super-admin can never lock itself out. Doing it as data on the existing `Permission`/`RolePermission` tables means **zero freeze impact** — the largest functional feature of the increment needed no migration. The ~190-endpoint + 49-page sweeps were run as audited parallel workflows (each agent transformed one file against an explicit page→code map; the structured report was reviewed against the catalogue and the build + 640 tests are the backstop) — far less error-prone than 240 hand edits, and the wildcard design meant every pre-existing admin test stayed green. The bypass test is the proof that matters: it exercises a real limited-role token end-to-end and confirms 200-on-granted / 403-on-not-granted, which is exactly "can't bypass in the API." The deferred items honour §17 (one issue per change) and §14 (don't delete code you didn't author without surfacing it) rather than scope-creeping the cleanup into a security build; the cookie-staleness limitation is recorded honestly because it's a real (if low-severity, API-still-enforces) behaviour the owner should know before relying on live permission changes. |
| D-206 | 2026-05-31 | **Forced password change at first sign-in — in-flow ticket + popup (Control Panel), replacing the dead-end 403 message.** Owner-reported: a seeded/admin-rotated Control Panel credential (flag `PasswordChangeRequired=true`) hit the sign-in 403 `AUTH_PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED` with a *"use the password-reset flow"* message and no way forward. Reworked into the standard first-login change flow (AD / Cognito `NEW_PASSWORD_REQUIRED` / Okta "Update Password" pattern), **reusing the codebase's own mid-auth ticket mechanism** rather than inventing an anonymous credential endpoint. **Backend.** `SignInService.SignInAsync` — after the password is verified *and the audience gate passes* (so a wrong-surface caller hits `AUTH_WRONG_SURFACE` first and the branch is not an account-existence oracle; D-080 state-block ordering preserved), a `PasswordChangeRequired` account on the **`Cp` audience** is issued a single-use `SecondFactorToken` of new kind `SecondFactorKind.PasswordChange` (additive enum value — freeze-permitted per D-110; **no schema change**, reuses the existing hashed/TTL/single-use/attempt-capped ticket table) and the response carries it in a new optional `SignInResponse.PasswordChangeToken` field; **every other audience keeps the 403 unchanged** (mobile/web untouched). The forced-change gate still fires at every later token-mint path via `RequirePasswordChangeNotRequired` (verify-totp / verify-recovery / verify-otp / refresh), so no session is minted until the password is actually changed. New `PasswordService.CompletePasswordChangeAsync` (modelled on `ResetPasswordAsync`, authorised by the ticket instead of an emailed code): validates the ticket (kind/consumed/expiry — generic `AUTH_MFA_TOKEN_INVALID/EXPIRED`, no oracle), re-checks the flag, then in one transaction sets the new password (`SetPasswordAsync` — current password NOT re-collected, the ticket already proved it), consumes the ticket, clears the flag, revokes all refresh tokens, audits `PasswordChanged` + dispatches the shared security notice. New anonymous, ticket-gated endpoint `POST /auth/complete-password-change` (mirrors `verify-totp`'s anonymous+ticket posture — **not** a new credential surface) + validator (`StrongPassword` + confirm-match). New audit event `SignIn.PasswordChangeTicketIssued`. **Frontend.** Extracted the Profile change-password form into a shared, endpoint-agnostic `ChangePasswordCard` component (`ShowCurrentField` param + `OnSubmit` callback); **Profile refactored to use it** (no behaviour change), and the `/login` page shows it in a `SimfModal` popup (current-password field hidden — the ticket proves it; only new + confirm) when sign-in returns a `PasswordChangeToken`. On success: no session is minted, the popup closes, the password field clears and a success banner tells the operator to sign in with the new password. New `SimfAuthClient.CompletePasswordChangeAsync` (called directly, like ForgotPassword/ResetPassword — auth pages don't use the cookie BFF); 6 `Auth.ChangePassword.*` resx keys per culture (EN+AR). **Simplify pass:** deduped the password-changed security-notice into a shared `PasswordService.SendPasswordChangedNoticeAsync` used by both the authenticated-change and forced-change paths. **Tests (7 new/updated, all green):** `SignInTests` — old "blocked-403-for-CP" replaced by CP-returns-a-ticket (+ asserts the issued-audit), non-CP-still-403, complete-change-clears-flag-and-lets-sign-in-proceed, unknown-ticket→400; `ChangePasswordCardTests` (bUnit, 3) — current-field shown/hidden by param + OnSubmit payload. **Verification:** full solution `-c Release` = 0/0; full suite **663 green** (Api 566, CP 50, Web 27, ApiClient 13, Domain 5, Application 2). **Does NOT change:** the email-OTP reset flow (kept for genuinely forgotten passwords), the authenticated `/auth/change-password` endpoint, the sign-in 403 contract for non-CP audiences, EF schema (Identity frozen; only an additive enum value). | The owner asked to "open the change-password page instead of showing the message," and the right shape is the industry-standard forced-change-at-first-login: prove possession of the temporary credential, then gate the change on a short-lived single-use challenge — **never mint a session first.** The deciding discovery was that the codebase *already* has this primitive: sign-in returns a short-lived `MfaToken` (a `SecondFactorToken`) and routes to a second step, and `verify-totp` is already `AllowAnonymous` + ticket-authenticated. So the ticket approach is consistent with the codebase (§19) and **avoids a new anonymous credential endpoint** (the alternative — re-posting email+current+new anonymously — would be a genuine §4 exception with a user-enumeration/oracle to defend, and would re-ask the password the user just typed). Authorising the change by the ticket (not a re-typed password) is exactly the owner's "we already have the current password" instinct, done securely — the ticket is the proof. Gating on the `Cp` audience keeps mobile/web on the existing 403, so the blast radius is the Control Panel only. Reusing `SetPasswordAsync` + `ClearChangeFlagAndEndSessionsAsync` (the reset path's mechanics) and extracting `ChangePasswordCard` (one form, two hosts) honours §0 "one clear way" — the new surface is just an entry point + a popup. The additive `SecondFactorKind.PasswordChange` is freeze-safe (D-110 permits appending enum values) and needs no migration because the ticket table already stores `Kind` as an int. The one judgement call (no current-password re-entry) is sound: the ticket is single-use, short-TTL, attempt-capped and issued only after the password verified at sign-in, so re-verifying adds friction without security. |

Freeze clean (both migrations additive-only, Identity untouched, no enum rename/reorder). **2 majors found + FIXED:** (1) **half-migrated central tables** — News/Media/Archive/MediaPartner/Rating had left their error-code + audit-event strings as module-local consts (Booth/Sponsor/Company/SessionComment were already promoted), violating the "defined once, never literals elsewhere" rule → promoted all of them to `ErrorCodes.cs` / `AuditEvents.cs` **keeping the exact string values verbatim** (the wire/audit contract — confirmed unchanged: `AdminMediaTests` still matches `"media_invalid"`), deleted the module-local consts + `MediaConstants.cs`; (2) **Booth optional fields had no length validation at any layer** (§7) → added Exhibitor(256)/Sector(128)/Description(2048) checks in `AdminBoothService.ValidateAndNormalise` (create+update) throwing `BoothInvalid` + matching `MaxLength` on `BoothsList`. **3 §7/§18 minors FIXED:** bound `MediaImageStorageOptions` in DI + added `MediaImageStorage:RootPath` to both appsettings (Options pattern, §18 — operator can now relocate media); aligned `ArchiveList` Year UI range (1900-2200 → 2000-2100) to the validator; added `MaxLength` to `MediaPartnersList` fields. **1 nit FIXED:** stateless `StubCommentAiFilter` Scoped→Singleton. **Accepted/deferred with reasoning (NOT changed):** `AdminCompanyService` re-reads via `GetAsync` after save (matches the established Delegation template — consistency over a micro-optimisation); `StatisticsService` 14 sequential COUNTs (acceptable for a low-frequency admin dashboard); `SessionCommentFeedRow.UserId` exposure + News `ImageRelativePath` free-string (low-impact / latent-only — the News path is not file-read anywhere today; revisit if a News image-streaming endpoint is added, with a traversal guard); hand-rolled-vs-FluentValidation style on Company/Booth. **Verification of the fixes:** `SIMF.Api` + `SIMF.ControlPanel` build `-c Release` = 0/0; `SIMF.Api.Tests` = 563/563 (re-run after the central-table edits — no regression); `SIMF.ControlPanel.Tests` = 47/47. **This closes the delivery push** (App + API + CP + Website, D-199..D-205). | A release gate's job is to catch what per-increment review misses — and it did: the half-migrated central tables and the Booth validation gap are both *cross-cutting* defects invisible when each module is reviewed alone, surfaced only by auditing the whole push together. Promoting the consts verbatim fixes the maintainability/consistency rule without touching the audit/wire contract (changing a value would break SOC consumers + the audit tests — so symbol names were normalised, values were not). The Booth gap was a real §7 break (over-long input would have hit SQL as a 500 instead of a clean 400). The accepted items are genuinely lower-value or consistent-with-codebase, and logging them with reasoning (rather than silently fixing or silently ignoring) keeps the decision auditable. One verifier claim — a backslash in the currently-inside client URL — was rejected after I read the line and found forward slashes (a false positive); verifying review claims against source is as necessary as verifying a green build (§13). |

One verifier *false positive* — it claimed `SimfAdminClient.ListCurrentlyInsideAsync` built the URL with backslashes (`\r`); I read the line and confirmed it is `"gates/reports/currently-inside"` with forward slashes (correct) — disregarded. Gates dashboard loads via `OnAfterRenderAsync(firstRender)` (not `OnInitializedAsync`) because the `simfAccount` JS-interop module is unavailable during SSR prerender — the same idiom `GateOperatorConsole` uses. **Verification:** `dotnet build SIMF.ControlPanel -c Release` = 0/0; `SIMF.ControlPanel.Tests` = 47/47 (nav group-count + route-uniqueness intact with the 2 new unique hrefs). | These were the owner's "Gates page + Programme page not in the mockup" items; the right read of that gap was overview/summary surfaces (the CRUD admin pages already existed), built entirely on endpoints already shipped — so the cost is two read-only pages + nav/resx, no backend. | D-203 | 2026-05-31 | **Public-facing Website pages — Programme + Visit/Entry (built via an ultracode workflow with adversarial verification).** The public Website (`SIMF.Web`, Blazor SSR) had no public content pages — added two over the anonymous backend reads: **Programme** (`/programme`) — the live agenda from `GET /programme/sessions` (+ `/speakers`), grouped by local day, bilingual; and **Visit & Entry** (`/visit`) — a static bilingual attendee-info page (getting here / entry & badges / hours / accessibility). Central integration: `Program.cs` client registration (BaseAddress-only, mirroring `SimfAuthClient` + its non-Dev HTTPS guard), `MainLayout` nav links (Programme + Visit), 23 resx keys/culture (EN+AR). **Verification:** `dotnet build SIMF.Web -c Release` = 0/0; `SIMF.Web.Tests` = 27/27. The Visit page is intentionally informational (no public gate-data endpoint exists — gates are operator-only); its section copy can later be CMS-driven without layout change. | The public site is the attendee front door; a live Programme + a Visit/entry page are the minimum public surface for an event this size, and both run off endpoints that already exist (zero backend work). The ultracode workflow + adversarial verify is the reason this is shippable rather than merely compiling: three of the six findings (missing CSS, dead color property, wrong render mode) are invisible to `dotnet build` — they would have produced an unstyled, circuit-wasting page that passed CI. The X-App-Key discovery is the load-bearing one: blindly "matching SimfAuthClient" per the brief would have added a header the codebase doesn't use and a config key that doesn't exist, crashing startup — the skeptical reviewer reading the actual registrations (not the brief's claim) is exactly the check that caught it. Reusing existing shared CSS classes (rather than adding a `simf-programme-*` block to the global stylesheet) is the minimal-diff, lowest-blast-radius fix and keeps §8's centralised-theme rule intact. | 
| D-202 | 2026-05-31 | **Track-2 backend — Statistics dashboard + Company/exhibitor-sponsor provisioning (owner-approved, additive schema).** Two independent backend verticals built by 2 parallel agents (Workflow `simf-track2-backend`); I integrated the shared files centrally + generated the migration + verified. **(1) Statistics dashboard — NO schema:** a read-only aggregate `GET /api/v1/admin/statistics` → `StatisticsDashboard` (counts over the two existing DbContexts: attendees by `AccountState`/`UserType.Visitor` from `SimfIdentityDbContext.Users`; active Sessions/Speakers/Booths/Sponsors/News/Media/Delegations, Comments split Approved/Pending, Ratings count + average — each its own `AsNoTracking` count; `AverageAsync` over `(double?)Stars ?? 0` to survive an empty table). `IStatisticsService`/`StatisticsService` + endpoint; one DI line; no ErrorCodes/AuditEvents (read-only). **(2) Company + provisioning — ADDITIVE schema (owner OK'd \"new additive even for freeze\"):** new `CompanyType` enum (Exhibitor=0/Sponsor=1), `Company` + `CompanyMembership` entities, 2 EF configs, full admin CRUD (`/admin/companies` + `/admin/companies/{id}/accounts`), `IAdminCompanyService`/`AdminCompanyService` mirroring `AdminDelegationService`. Migration `D202_CompaniesAndProvisioning` is **purely additive** — `CreateTable Companies` + `CreateTable CompanyMemberships` + 3 indexes, nothing altered on existing tables, **Identity schema untouched** (the account link is a logical `Guid UserId` with NO cross-DB FK/navigation, per the D-157 cross-DB rule). **Account-provisioning model (the one judgement call, OWNER-REVERSIBLE):** \"create accounts under a company\" provisions a **least-privilege app-facing Visitor account** via the existing `IAdminUserProvisioningService.CreateVisitorAsync` (no new RBAC role, no CP/admin surface, no AllowAnonymous — §4-safe) and tags it to the company with a `CompanyMembership` row; the account lands `PendingApproval` with the standard 7-day invite email (existing admin-create pipeline behaviour — an admin may need to approve before sign-in). If the owner wants a dedicated partner role / distinct UserType / contact-only-no-login, that is a localised change to `ProvisionAccountAsync`. `CompanyType` is stored `HasConversion<int>()` and exposed on the wire as an int (matching the `AdminSponsorSummary.Tier`-as-int convention) to keep the Domain enum out of the Contracts. Integration: +2 DbSets, +2 DI registrations, +4 ErrorCodes (`COMPANY_INVALID/NOT_FOUND/INACTIVE`, `COMPANY_ACCOUNT_INVALID`), +4 AuditEvents (`Company.Created/Updated/Deactivated/AccountProvisioned`); un-sealed `UpdateCompanyRequest` + `ProvisionCompanyAccountRequest` so the endpoints can bind `{id}`+body via a derived `*Route` (the same CS0509 fix as D-199). **Verification:** `dotnet build SIMF.Api -c Release` = 0/0; migration confirmed additive-only by reading its `Up()`; `SIMF.Api.Tests` = **563/563**. **Follow-up (next commit):** the two CP pages (Statistics dashboard + Companies management/provisioning) over these endpoints. | The owner approved both the feature and additive schema, so the only real risk surface is (a) the freeze and (b) the account/permission model. The account model defaults to **least privilege** — an exhibitor/sponsor login is just an app participant tagged to a company, adding zero new admin/permission surface — because there is no exhibitor/sponsor portal in scope (mockup screen 08 descoped), so a heavier role/permission model would be speculative; it is isolated in one method and flagged reversible so the owner can upgrade it without touching the schema. Statistics is correctly schema-free (live COUNT/AVG, not a snapshot table) — the D-199 \"Statistics snapshots\" table can be added later if historical trending is needed, but the dashboard the CP needs now is a live read. Reusing `CreateVisitorAsync` (not a hand-rolled `UserManager`) keeps provisioning on the one audited, validated identity pipeline. |

| D-199 | 2026-05-30 | **Full-scope delivery push — four owner decisions + broad D-110 freeze lift.** To deliver the entire App (41 mockup screens) + CP + API under a hard 4-day owner deadline, a 14-agent assessment of `Mockup.html` vs the current code mapped the build backlog across 3 layers (backend endpoint → Flutter data-layer → screen).
