Four owner decisions taken (via AskUserQuestion): **(1) Freeze lift — APPROVED (broad).** The D-110 schema freeze is lifted for new/extended event modules: News, Media gallery, Media partners, Booths (Exhibition), Sponsors, Archive editions, Audience comments, Ratings/Feedback, Statistics snapshots, Live-session columns — additive tables on `SimfAppDbContext` via new migrations. Identity schema + existing enum rename/reorder stay frozen. (See CLAUDE.md "D-199 broad lift".) **(2) Providers (D7) — stub now.** Live video streaming + cognitive-AI (captions, session summary, chatbot) ship as UI + integration seam over the existing offline Echo stub; real provider + keys wired later with no rework. **(3) Exhibitor/Sponsor — CP-only.** No in-app exhibitor/sponsor sign-up (mockup screen 08 stays descoped, consistent with D-186/D-190). Instead the **Control Panel creates a Company (name), then creates accounts under it** — a new CP + backend feature to build (Company entity + account provisioning under a company). **(4) Venue map — 2D interactive** (pan/zoom over hall/booth layout coordinates via InteractiveViewer + CustomPaint), not a 3D asset. **Reversible defaults taken without blocking** (owner may veto): Terms (09) + Cybersecurity (39) read from existing CMS `/content/{key}` (seed `legal.*` blocks) rather than new `/legal/*` endpoints; Delegations (21) keeps the live D-174 backend shape with the app decoder fixed; photo/ID-verify stays descoped (the mockup comments it out); peer "Connect" (35) reuses `MeetingRequest`; chatbot (36) is stateless over the existing `/ai/assistance`. **Execution:** orchestrated as parallel agent build waves — Wave A (6 unblocked mobile screens against existing backends) + a backend critical-path wave (Speakers/Sessions public reads with no schema, plus the freeze-lifted News/Media/MediaPartners/Booths/Sponsors/Archive/Comments/Ratings verticals) running on separate git worktrees (mobile `feature/mobile-app-skeleton` vs backend `feature/login-api`) to avoid contention; migrations + shared-file wiring (DbSets, routes, barrels) integrated centrally, not by parallel agents. Honest scope: 4 days of parallel build delivers feature-complete, building, wired code for the unblocked + freeze-lifted modules; it does NOT include NCA/MoD security clearance, UAT, store publication, or real streaming/AI provider procurement (external, time-bound). **Supersedes the stale D-198 "OTP-every-login gap" note:** the 2FA rule (when ON → visitor OTP / admin TOTP) is already implemented; that was a misread, not a gap. | The owner owns scope and the deadline; under a hard 4-day push the job is to maximise delivered, correct, integrated code while being honest about what code-generation cannot compress (external clearances, provider procurement, UAT). The freeze lift is the central unlock — without it the bulk of the event modules cannot exist; the owner approving it satisfies the §6/D-110 "explicit owner approval" gate, and additive new-context tables (the D-186 precedent) keep the wire/Identity contract stable. Provider-stub keeps the live/AI screens shippable now and real later without rework, which fits the deadline. Exhibitor-as-CP-Company matches the owner's actual operating model (PR team provisions partner accounts) and avoids an app-side schema break. 2D map is the pragmatic, robust, cross-device choice and the mockup geometry is effectively 2D anyway. The assessment-first approach (map all 3 layers before generating) is what stops parallel agents from mass-producing screens that call endpoints which don't exist — "compiles + renders" is explicitly NOT treated as "works," because multiple screens read as done but fail at runtime on path/shape mismatch. Central integration of migrations + shared files (rather than letting parallel agents touch `SimfAppDbContext`, routing, or barrels) is what keeps the fan-out conflict-free. |
| D-198 | 2026-05-30 | **Enumeration-resistant sign-up + "complete your profile" sign-in signal.** Owner-requested: re-sign-up must let a user "continue as a fresh one" without ever feeling they already have an account, and a verified-but-profileless user must be routed to the profile form on next login. **Part A — `RegistrationService.SignUpAsync` branches on the existing account's state instead of throwing 409.** Was: any existing email → `409 AUTH_EMAIL_ALREADY_REGISTERED` (an enumeration oracle, and a dead end for an honest user who forgot an unfinished sign-up). Now three paths, all returning the **same** `SignUpResponse(email, 600)` shape so existence never leaks: **(1) no account** → unchanged create-in-`Registered` + email code; **(2) existing + `Registered` (unverified)** → `RestartUnverifiedAccountAsync`: replace the password with the newly typed one (`RemovePasswordAsync` + `AddPasswordAsync` — `ChangePasswordAsync` needs the old password we don't have), roll the security stamp (H5/D-060), invalidate the old code + issue a fresh one, all inside the existing `transactionRunner` scope so a weak-password `AddPassword` failure rolls back cleanly; the per-account code cap (5/hour, shared with resend) still applies → `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` (429); **(3) existing + verified** (`EmailVerified`/`PendingApproval`/`Approved`/`Rejected`/`Disabled`) → `DeflectExistingVerifiedAccountAsync`: the account is **never touched** (password ignored, no code minted), a security heads-up email is sent to the **owner** (sign-in / reset-password), and the same generic 201 is returned. **Part B — `SignInService.BuildAccountStateInfoAsync` now also emits `AccountStateInfo(State="EmailVerified")`** (was null for that state), so the login response tells the client "verified, profile pending" the same way it already signals PendingApproval/Rejected. The `account_state` JWT claim already carries this on every token, so the signal also survives the email-OTP path where the response helper is not reached — the client should treat the claim as the universal signal and the response field as the direct-path convenience mirror. **New audit events** (`AuditEvents`, not the frozen enum): `SignUp.RestartedUnverified` (Success) + `SignUp.ExistingAccountDeflected` (Failure, carries `AUTH_EMAIL_ALREADY_REGISTERED` for SOC). `SignIn.AsGuest` now also fires for EmailVerified (comment updated). **Refactor (DRY, serves the feature):** extracted `EnqueueVerificationCodeAsync` + `ToAccountCreationException` shared by the new-account and restart paths; `ResendCodeAsync` left untouched (out of scope, §17). **Docs:** `SIMF-API-001` §12.4 sign-up rewritten (behaviour list; 409 removed from the failure line; the `AUTH_EMAIL_ALREADY_REGISTERED`/409 catalog row stays — still returned by admin-create + walk-in). **Tests (5 added/updated, all 481 Api tests green):** `RegistrationEndpointsTests` — the old `…returns_409…` replaced by `…unverified_account_restarts…` (old code rejected, new code verifies, NEW password signs in + old 401s) + `…verified_account_returns_201_without_revealing_or_touching_it` (state unchanged, 0 codes minted, original password still signs in); `AuditLogTests` — the broken duplicate-audit test split into restart-audit + deflect-audit; `SignInTests` — `…surfaces_EmailVerified`. **What D-198 does NOT change.** No EF schema / no enum change — D-110 freeze intact (reuses `Registered`/`EmailVerified`). The 2FA / email-OTP-every-login question is explicitly **out of scope** and unresolved — see the separate note below. The `AUTH_EMAIL_ALREADY_REGISTERED` code itself is retained (admin-create + walk-in still 409). **Foreign uncommitted change observed, not mine, left alone:** `JwtTokenService.cs` had a cosmetic whitespace-only edit in the working tree (no logic), not authored by this change and not committed with it. | The owner's model is "sign-up should never shame or block a returning user, and never confirm an email's existence." That is the textbook OWASP enumeration-resistant sign-up, and it aligns sign-up with this codebase's own sign-in philosophy (one generic response, never an existence oracle — `SignInService` already does this). The verified-account branch is the security crux and was confirmed with the owner before coding: blindly "resend an OTP to continue" for *any* existing account would be account-takeover (a stranger types victim@email + a new password and could seize the account), so the verified branch must touch nothing and notify the owner instead. Replacing the password on the **unverified** branch is safe because an unverified account has no real owner yet — "continue as fresh" means the latest password wins; rolling the security stamp invalidates any token minted against the prior credential. Reusing `transactionRunner` keeps the "user row + code commit together, or not at all" guarantee the original sign-up relied on. Part B leans on the always-present `account_state` claim as the universal signal (works on both the direct and email-OTP sign-in paths) and adds the response mirror only where it's reachable, so it is correct regardless of how the open 2FA question resolves. Kept deliberately out of scope (§17 one-issue-per-change): **the email-OTP-every-login rule.** The owner states visitors must email-OTP at every login; the shipped code makes 2FA opt-in (default off → a fresh verified visitor signs in directly, proven by `SignInTests.SignIn_returns_tokens_directly_when_TwoFactorEnabled_is_false_for_a_visitor`, per D-033/#34). That requirement-vs-code gap is real but is a separate security-flow change (amending D-033) needing its own §11 plan + owner approval — not bundled into this sign-up/profile-signal work. |
| D-197 | 2026-05-30 | **Profile-upsert validation hardening — DOB ≥ 18 + real Luhn ID checksums + passport shape.** Owner-requested real validation on `UpsertUserProfileRequestValidator` (the D-192 review found the ID fields were prefix/length-only and DOB had no rule at all). Three changes, all on `SIMF.Api/Endpoints/Account/Validators/UpsertUserProfileRequestValidator.cs`, with the client mirroring them (§7 validation-alignment). **(1) Date of birth — required + min age 18.** Was: no rule (a null DOB persisted silently). Now: `NotNull` + a `BeAtLeastEighteen` check (`dob <= today.AddYears(-18)`, leap-safe; `DateOnly.FromDateTime(DateTime.UtcNow)` — min-age is not timing-sensitive). **(2) Saudi National ID / Iqama — real Luhn checksum.** Was: prefix+length regex only (`^1\d{9}$` / `^2\d{9}$`). Now: those *plus* a standard mod-10 Luhn check (`IsValidLuhn`). Research (verified): Saudi national ids and Iqama numbers are standard-Luhn, 10 digits, prefix `1`=citizen / `2`=resident — confirmed via the Saudi-authored `dralshehri/luhncheck` (`is_luhn("1101798278", 10, ["1","2"])`); `1101798278` hand-verified to sum 40 (mod 10 = 0). **(3) Passport — format sanity.** Was: `MaximumLength(32)` only. Now: `^[A-Za-z0-9]{6,9}$` (passports have no universal checksum; 6-9 alphanumeric is the best-practice format gate). **Test-fixture correction (blast radius).** The pre-existing "valid" fixtures `NationalId="1234567890"` and `IqamaNumber="2345678901"` were **never Luhn-valid** — they passed the old regex-only rule but fail the real checksum. Updated to verified-valid numbers (`1101798278`, `2101798276`) across the 4 validator-path test files (`UserProfileTests` helper + iqama test, `NotificationLifecycleTests`, `VisitorLifecycleTests`, `UserProfileRollbackTests`). DB-seed fixtures (`new UserProfile { … }` — bypass the validator) were left unchanged. **Tests (6 new, `UserProfileTests`).** national-id invalid-checksum → 400; iqama invalid-checksum → 400; DOB required (null → 400); under-18 (today−18y+1d → 400); exactly-18 boundary → OK; valid passport (`AB123456`) → OK. **What D-197 does NOT change.** No schema/enum change (D-110 freeze intact — these are validator rules). The walk-in / on-site registration endpoint uses a *different* request type + validator and is out of scope (its ID validation is a separate follow-up if the owner wants Luhn there too). The permissive phone rules are unchanged (owner guidance during D-046). | The owner asked for real validation, not regex theatre, and §7 requires UI ↔ FluentValidation ↔ EF alignment — so the checksum lands on the backend (the real gate, never trust the client) and the Flutter client mirrors it for UX (D-192 mobile commit). **Luhn researched, not recalled** — a wrong checksum would reject valid citizens, worse than the loose regex; the algorithm + the `1/2` prefix split + a known-valid test vector were verified against an authoritative Saudi-authored source before coding, and `IsValidLuhn` is the textbook mod-10 with a defensive non-digit guard. **Passport is a format gate, stated honestly** — there is no universal passport checksum (verified), so 6-9 alphanumeric is a sanity check, not authoritative verification; over-tightening would reject legitimate international passports at an international maritime forum. **The fixture correction is the load-bearing discovery** — the old test data was Luhn-invalid and only ever passed because validation was regex-only; making validation real *requires* making the test data real, so the two are one atomic change. DB-seed fixtures stay as-is because they never run the validator (direct `DbContext.Add`), so they don't need to be Luhn-valid (and changing them would be unrelated churn). **DOB min-age via UtcNow, not TimeProvider** — the validator has no clock dependency today; injecting one for a not-timing-sensitive min-age check would be over-engineering, and tests compute their boundary dates against the same `UtcNow` so they stay deterministic relative to "now". |
| D-196 | 2026-05-30 | **Mobile sign-up privilege-escalation fix — partner ProfileType role only resolves after admin approval.** A 23-agent adversarial review of the D-192 mobile sign-up rewrite surfaced a confirmed **critical**: a self-registering user could flip the Visitor/Other toggle on sign-up Screen 2, self-pick a **partner** (`IsVisitor=false`) ProfileType whose `MobileAppRole` is `Staff`/`Moderator`, and have that role minted into their JWT `mobile_app_role` claim on the next token mint — with **no admin in the loop**. The chain: `listProfileTypes()` returns partner rows → the user self-picks one → `UserProfileService.UpsertMineAsync` writes it onto the new (admin-untouched) `UserProfile.ProfileTypeId` (the existing `UserType != Visitor` guard does not block partner-scope rows) → `ResolveMobileAppRoleAsync` returned `profile.MobileAppRole` for any `IsVisitor=false` profile, regardless of who set it. The profile upsert revokes refresh tokens (EmailVerified→PendingApproval), so the *next* sign-in/refresh re-resolves and bakes in `Staff`/`Moderator`. **Fix (one gate, one function).** `ResolveMobileAppRoleAsync` (`SIMF.Infrastructure/Identity/UserProfileService.cs`) now selects `u.AccountState` and short-circuits to `MobileAppRole.Visitor` for any account whose `AccountState != Approved`, *before* reading the partner ProfileType's role. `AccountState.Approved` is reached only via admin action (approve / admin-create / on-site register) or the seeder — never a self-service path (a self-signup tops out at `PendingApproval`). So a self-picked partner type now confers nothing until an admin reviews and approves the account (the admin sees the proposed ProfileType at approval time and can change it — D-190 admin-wins). **Tests (2 new, `MobileAppRoleTests`).** `PendingApproval_with_partner_Staff_profile_resolves_to_Visitor` (the regression guard) + `Approved_with_partner_Staff_profile_resolves_to_Staff` (proves the gate does not over-correct and break legitimately admin-approved staff). Service-level (resolve `IUserProfileService` from DI over a seeded partner profile) — deterministic, no sign-in-flow dependency. **What D-196 does NOT change.** No mobile change — the Visitor/Other toggle, the unfiltered picker fetch, and the partner self-pick-as-proposal flow are all owner-confirmed by-design (the user proposes; the admin disposes). The existing `UserType != Visitor` validator guard stays (it correctly blocks Admin-scope types). No EF schema or enum change — D-110 freeze intact. No change to the 3 token-mint call sites (sign-in / refresh / device-key) — they all funnel through the one resolver, so the single gate covers them. | The fix belongs at role-resolution, not at the picker or the upsert: the owner's model is "a mobile sign-up creates a Visitor; partner/staff authority is an admin grant, never self-service." `AccountState == Approved` is the exact proxy for "an admin has blessed this account" — verified by enumerating every `AccountState = Approved` write site (all admin/seed). Gating there (a) closes the escalation for every token path at once, (b) preserves the legitimate admin-assigned-staff path (an approved partner user still resolves to Staff/Moderator), and (c) needs no schema change, so the D-110 freeze holds. Rejecting partner self-picks in `UpsertMineAsync` instead was considered and declined — the owner wants the proposal saved and surfaced to the admin (D-190 already shows it in the pending-review detail), not bounced with a 400. The two-test shape (pending→Visitor, approved→Staff) pins both the security invariant and the no-over-correction guarantee, so a future change that either re-opens the hole or nukes admin-staff fails by name. Found by the same Ultracode adversarial-review discipline as D-191/D-194; every link of the chain was re-verified against the real code before the fix. |
| D-194 | 2026-05-30 | **SIMF.Web test project — closes the public Website's zero-coverage gap.** The Website (`SIMF.Web`, the public Blazor SSR + interactive-island host) had **no test project at all** — the single biggest coverage gap in the solution, and two of its source files carried explicit `Tests: …(todo)` markers. D-194 creates `tests/SIMF.Web.Tests` (new csproj + `SIMF.slnx` entry — owner-authorised, since §1.7/§9 guard new project files) and lands **27 tests** mirroring the D-191 CP bUnit harness. **Suites.** `SignInTicketStoreTests` (5) — mirrors the CP suite; closes the `SignInTicketStore` todo. `SimfCookieRefreshHandlerTests` (8) — closes the `SimfCookieRefreshHandler` todo; covers `StoreTokens` persistence round-trip + the D-121 `NeedsRefresh` decision boundary (missing / unparseable / within-threshold / expired / valid-window / fresh-30min). `WebComponentTestBase` — bUnit base with a pass-through `IStringLocalizer<Strings>` + `TestAuthorizationContext` (mirrors `CpComponentTestBase`). `AccountStateBannerTests` (9, bUnit) — the P11/D-052 Pending + Rejected pages: claim-driven redirect routing (Approved/Rejected/PendingApproval), culture-aware bilingual rejection-reason selection, no-reason fallback. `SignInPageTests` (5, bUnit) — the `/login` page: validation guard (empty + invalid-email → no API call), direct-tokens → ticket-stash + `/auth/complete` redirect, OTP → session + `/login/verify` redirect, API-failure → error alert; uses a stub `HttpMessageHandler` to drive the sealed `SimfAuthClient`. **One production change (test-enablement).** `SimfCookieRefreshHandler.NeedsRefresh` flipped `private static` → `internal static` + `[assembly: InternalsVisibleTo("SIMF.Web.Tests")]` so the load-bearing refresh-decision boundary is directly testable. Zero runtime change; the test assembly is non-packable so nothing ships. **Review-pass (Ultracode 5-dimension workflow + adversarial verification; all findings confirmed, no fabrications, no security regression).** Four findings folded in pre-commit: (i) **invalid-email test tautology (MEDIUM)** — the test asserted only `CallCount==0`, which a binding regression would pass via the empty-email branch; hardened to assert the email value round-trips so the invalid-*format* branch is genuinely exercised. (ii) **BFF negative missing (MEDIUM, security)** — the direct-tokens test proved the ticket round-trip but never asserted the load-bearing invariant that the raw access/refresh tokens NEVER cross the browser boundary; added `DoesNotContain` assertions on the redirect URI + rendered markup, with distinctive long token values so they can't match by accident. (iii) **`CurrentUICulture` parallelization hazard (LOW)** — added `AssemblyInfo.cs` with `[assembly: CollectionBehavior(DisableTestParallelization = true)]` (mirrors `SIMF.Api.Tests`) so the Arabic-culture banner tests' ambient-culture mutation can't race a concurrent class. (iv) **`InternalsVisibleTo` comment accuracy (LOW)** — tightened to state the exposure is assembly-wide (also exposes the internal endpoint classes), not just `NeedsRefresh`. **Test totals: 564 → 564 (no net change after the in-place fold; the new project contributes 27).** Across 6 projects now: Api 470, CP 47, **Web 27**, Domain 5, Application 2, ApiClient 13 = **564**. Build compiles clean (0 warnings) on every project; the only full-solution build error was a transient file-lock on a *running* `SIMF.Api.exe` dev instance (PID 46600), not a code defect — not cleared because killing the owner's running server is a destructive action. **Confirmed finding flagged for follow-up (NOT fixed here — out of scope + needs owner approval): SimfAlert has no `warning` variant.** `SimfAlert` only branches on `error`/`success` with an `info` fallback, so `<SimfAlert Variant="warning">` silently degrades to `simf-alert--info`. Verification escalated the blast radius to **5 call sites** — `SIMF.Web/Account/Rejected.razor`, `SIMF.ControlPanel/Auth/Rejected.razor`, `SIMF.ControlPanel/Account/TotpPairing.razor` (static), **plus two runtime CP paths** (`PendingVisitors.razor` + `PendingOthers.razor` set `_toastVariant="warning"` on a partial bulk-approve, misrendering as info). The fix (add a `warning` branch + `.simf-alert--warning` CSS reusing the existing `--color-warning` tokens + role=alert + doc update) touches the **frozen shared `SIMF.Components` library + global CSS**, so it requires the §11 pre-approval + owner sign-off and is filed as the **D-195 follow-up**; the D-194 test pins the *actual* current behaviour (`simf-alert--info`) rather than silently masking the gap. **Coverage backlog (reviewer-ranked, follow-up):** ResetPassword client-side password-policy ladder + confirm-match (MUST-FIX-NEXT, pure logic), CultureEndpoint open-redirect guard (MUST-FIX-NEXT, NCA-relevant, trivially testable), OtpVerify guard + code validation, AuthEndpoints JWT-claim extraction + state-routing, and a WebApplicationFactory integration test for the page-level `[Authorize]` gate (bUnit bypasses it). | The Website having zero tests was the largest single coverage gap; D-191 had already proven the bUnit harness pattern for the CP, so this is the direct parallel. **New csproj gated on owner approval.** §1.7 (no csproj edits) + §9 (frozen SDK) specifically guard creating a new project + editing `SIMF.slnx`; even under Auto Mode this is a structural decision only the owner can make, so it was confirmed via AskUserQuestion before starting — the rest of the module then ran autonomously. **The one production change is the minimal correct test-enablement.** `NeedsRefresh` is a pure decision the D-121 handler exists to make; exposing it `internal` (not `public`, not reflection) is the conventional way to test it, and the source file itself carried the `(todo)` marker inviting exactly this. The reviewers confirmed it's zero-runtime-change and adds no shipped attack surface. **The two MEDIUM folds convert "tests that pass for the wrong reason" into genuine guards.** The invalid-email tautology and the missing BFF negative were both real false-confidence gaps the verifiers confirmed — folding them is squarely within the test-project scope (no production change) and makes the suite honest. **The SimfAlert fix is deliberately NOT bundled.** It is a separate issue (§17 one-issue-per-change), touches a frozen shared component + the global CSS theme surface (§8/§9), and benefits the CP as much as the Website — so it gets its own owner-approved D-195 commit, not a quiet ride-along in a Website-tests changeset. Pinning the current (degraded) behaviour in the D-194 test + documenting the gap here honours the §17/§18 "report separately, don't silently fix" discipline AND makes good the `see DECISIONS_LOG D-194` citation the test comment promised. **The coverage backlog is scoped, not silently dropped.** D-191 set the precedent that a test-harness module ships reference suites + a documented backlog rather than exhaustive backfill; the reviewers ranked the next targets (ResetPassword + CultureEndpoint open-redirect first, both pure-logic + security-relevant) so the follow-up has a clear, evidence-based order. **File-lock honesty.** The full-solution build "2 errors" were exclusively the locked running-API exe; reporting them as environment-not-code, and refusing to kill the owner's process, is the §14/§15 faithful-outcome discipline. |
| D-193 | 2026-05-30 | **Stage 6 on-prem release runbook — SIMF-OPS-001 Amendment B.** Doc-only commit. The existing SIMF-OPS-001 §13 "deployment checklist" was 6 bullets; Amendment B expands it into 9 sub-sections (B.1–B.9) consolidating every deployment-time decision recorded across the DECISIONS_LOG (D-001 → D-191) into a single operator-facing runbook. **(Note on numbering.** The brief for this work proposed `D-192`; that number had already been taken by the parallel mobile-app-skeleton session's D-192 row added to the shared DECISIONS_LOG on disk. This row uses D-193 to preserve the existing entry.) **B.1 Configuration matrix.** Tabulates every config key the API reads at startup — 30+ entries across `ConnectionStrings`, `Jwt`, `Email`, `SuperAdmin`, `ReverseProxy`, `RateLimit`, `Storage`, `Ai`, `Serilog`. Each row marks required-vs-optional, what it controls, and the precise failure mode when missing (e.g. "`Jwt:SigningKey` missing → token issuance throws; SignIn 500s on every call"). Pulled by inspection from `appsettings.json` + every `Options` class in `SIMF.Common.Options` + `SIMF.Infrastructure.Ai.AiOptions`. **B.2 Migration order.** Codifies the D-187 review-pass (security H-3) finding: `Program.cs` MUST run App migration before Identity — the App migration is forward-compatible with pre-D-186 Identity rows, but the reverse is not. Includes the post-deploy verification step (both `__EFMigrationsHistory` tables should include the latest migration ids). **B.3 Secret generation cheatsheet.** `openssl` one-liners for `Jwt:SigningKey` (`openssl rand -base64 48`), `Storage:UserIdDocumentEncryptionKey` (`-base64 32`), `Ai:PromptHash:Secret` (`-base64 32`), and `SuperAdmin:TotpSecret` (rand + base32 + lowercase + space-group). **B.4 HMAC rotation runbook.** Lifts the D-185 SIEM-rules README "HMAC rotation playbook" into the deploy doc with the 6-step procedure (generate new key → stand up alongside old → bump `v1:` to `v2:` prefix → restart → pin SOC rule `validFrom` to cutover → delete legacy after retention). Notes that the D-188 AiPromptHistory table is unaffected. **B.5 Rollback playbook.** 6-step procedure: detect via `/health` 503 or fatal Serilog within 60s of startup, halt traffic at the proxy, restore the last-known-good binary, schema rollback (usually unnecessary — D-186 + D-188 migrations are additive), verify, post-mortem before redeploy. **B.6 Initial admin password rotation.** The single canonical breakable identity post-deploy is the seeded super-admin: sign in with bootstrap → change password → re-enrol TOTP → rotate the env-file values to throwaway randoms. Frames this as "the gate between deploy-complete and operationally-secure." **B.7 Post-deploy smoke.** Expanded into an 8-call `curl` sequence with precise expected responses: liveness → anonymous sign-up (verifies SMTP) → super-admin sign-in (verifies Identity DB + seed) → admin list-visitors (verifies App DB + EF query path) → D-190 public ProfileType picker (verifies cross-context query) → OperationLog write verification via sqlcmd → rate-limit fires → CP browser smoke. Failed smoke triggers §B.5 rollback within 5 minutes. **B.8 NCA security pre-flight.** 10-item mandatory check before production go-live: TLS 1.2+/HSTS, security headers, CSP without unsafe-inline, OperationLog DB-grant immutability (write-only for the app account), secret-key vault hygiene + `AiAuditDetail.IsHmacKeyDevFallback==false`, live PII redaction verification, ID-image encryption-at-rest verification, SIEM forwarder + Sigma rule import, backup + 30-day-restore-drill verification, rate-limit defaults tightened. **B.9 Deploy-acceptance checklist.** 7 sign-off boxes that gate the production deploy. **What D-193 does NOT include.** No code changes. No CI/CD pipeline YAML (WS5 workstream per the Program Plan). No load-test scripts (Amendment A.1 already covers the load-test scenario set; actual k6/JMeter scripts are a future module). No production secret values. | The Stage 6 transition was "pick a Stage 6 surface" per the previous turn; this is the most leveraged single doc for the eventual on-prem deploy. **Why expand SIMF-OPS-001 instead of writing a new RUNBOOK.md:** the existing doc already carries the topology, environments, CI/CD overview, and the bare-bones §13 checklist; an Amendment B keeps the operator-facing runbook in one document so the on-call engineer doesn't have to cross-reference. The amendment pattern also matches every other deploy-impacting decision since D-110 (Amendment A on this same doc covered the 150k-user load review). **Why a single tabulated config matrix:** scattered across 30+ DECISIONS rows + 7 Options classes + appsettings.json + the env-var script, no single artefact answered "what does the app need at startup to function." The B.1 table is that artefact. A missing-config bug at deploy time is the single most common failure mode for a freshly-onboarded operator; the "failure mode if missing" column means the on-call can diagnose a startup throw in seconds. **Migration order as a load-bearing operational invariant:** the D-187 review-pass discovered this AT review time — a deploy-time discovery would have left partner accounts invisible to the CP for the entire window between the Identity migration succeeding and the App migration completing. Codifying it in the runbook means the next operator who edits Program.cs (or who runs `dotnet ef database update` manually in an emergency) sees the rule. **Smoke test as a deterministic 8-call sequence:** the existing 6-bullet checklist was advisory; B.7 is operational. Every call has a precise expected response so the on-call engineer can mechanically check pass/fail without making judgement calls under deploy pressure. **NCA pre-flight as a 10-item gate:** the Saudi NCA compliance constraint is a hard requirement. B.8 makes the items operationally testable — each has a concrete pass/fail check (curl for headers, sqlcmd for grant inspection, a synthetic AI prompt for redaction verification). Without this, the compliance review at go-live would be a multi-hour ad-hoc inspection; with it, the operator can pre-stage the checks and sign off in minutes. **Numbering collision avoidance.** The two parallel branches (this `feature/login-api` + the mobile session's `feature/mobile-app-skeleton`) share the same DECISIONS_LOG file on disk. The mobile session reserved D-192 for their sign-up flow rewrite; D-193 sits cleanly above it. The next decision on either branch picks the next free number. **Doc-only commit, no tests required.** The runbook is operator-facing; the code paths it describes are already covered by 537 tests. The right test surface for a runbook is the actual deploy. |
| D-192 | 2026-05-30 | **Mobile sign-up flow — three-screen Visitor pipeline on top of D-190.** Owner-driven rewrite of the Flutter sign-up flow to consume the D-190 backend surface. The OTP screen between (1) and (2) auto-signs-in using credentials parked on a `pendingSignUpCredentialsProvider` so the user slides straight from `POST /auth/verify-email` onto Screen 2 without retyping the password. **Removed.** `sign_up_type_screen.dart` (Visitor / Exhibitor radio) and `sign_up_exhibitor_screen.dart` (placeholder) — both mockup-only and unsupported by the post-D-186 backend. The corresponding `RouteNames.signUpType` + `RouteNames.signUpExhibitor` constants are gone; the numbered-routes total dropped from 41 to 40 (41 − 2 + 1 for the new `signUpInterests`) and `route_table_test.dart` was updated accordingly. **Added (data).** 5 DTOs in `simf_data_pkg/lib/src/profile/` mirroring `SIMF.Contracts/UserProfile/*`: `ProfileTypePickerDto` + `ProfileTypePickerListResponseDto`, `CountryDto` + `CountryListResponseDto`, `InterestDto` (including `displayOrder`) + `InterestListResponseDto`, `UpsertUserProfileRequestDto` (14 fields incl. optional `profileTypeId`; `toJson()` omits nulls; `DateTime? dateOfBirth` serialised as `yyyy-MM-dd` to match `DateOnly`), `UserProfileResponseDto` (adds `hasIdImage` + `qrId`). The DTOs deliberately live in `simf_data_pkg` rather than a new `simf_profile_pkg` so the same shapes can be reused by the post-approval profile-edit surface without a package extraction later. **Added (API).** 5 methods on `AuthApi` (`listProfileTypes` / `listCountries` / `listInterests` / `getUserProfile` / `upsertUserProfile`) wrapped 1:1 on `AuthRepository`. The repository passes DTOs straight through (no parallel domain types) because the shapes are flat display value-objects with no domain behaviour; a profile-edit screen post-approval can introduce mapping if it ever needs it. **Added (state).** `pendingSignUpCredentialsProvider` (in-memory only, cleared on auto-sign-in success) and `signUpProfileDraftProvider` (Screen 2 → Screen 3 hand-off so the POST is a single round-trip with all profile + interest data). `signUpScreen2LookupsProvider` (FutureProvider returning both ProfileTypes + Countries; both lists are small) and `signUpInterestsProvider` (FutureProvider returning Interests). **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean on all 3 packages; `flutter test` green (data: 11/11; auth: 15/15; app: 2/2). No emulator-side regression suspected because the only changed redirect rule is the `signIn → signUpForm` direct hop (skipping the deleted `signUpType` intermediate). **Out of scope.** No new domain types around ProfileType / Country / Interest (DTO-only path); no profile-edit screen post-approval (separate sprint); no widget tests for the new screens (the same gap as the rest of the auth_pkg screen library — to be addressed alongside the bUnit-equivalent harness for Flutter); no Live emulator-side verification yet (will run after the doc/commit pass). | **DTO location chosen: `simf_data_pkg`.** Owner-selected via clarifying prompt — the alternatives were a new `simf_profile_pkg` (extra package scaffolding for a flow that's currently sign-up-only) or `simf_auth_pkg` (mixes concerns; the DTOs aren't auth-domain). `simf_data_pkg` is the next-best home: a profile-edit surface added post-approval will already have the shapes available without a package extraction, and the data package is already the conventional home for the HTTP client + storage primitives mobile features layer on top of. **Repository returns DTOs straight through.** The existing repo pattern maps DTO → domain (e.g. `CurrentUser`) but creating five parallel domain types for picker rows that have no behaviour beyond display would be boilerplate. The decision is reversible: a future "edit profile" screen that wants a `Profile` domain type can introduce the mapping in one place. **Auto-sign-in over manual sign-in after verify-email.** The owner described the UX as "user enters email + password, verifies code, lands on Screen 2 with profile fields". The merge-then-POST path is simpler: one wire round-trip, one atomic admin-wins precedence application, one audit row, no half-saved state where the user backs out between screens. The cost is parking Screen 2's data in a Riverpod state provider for the duration of Screen 3 — cheap and reversible. **Toggle defaults to Visitor (audience).** Statistically the dominant sign-up path; the audience filter is a server-side floor (the picker already filters `IsActive=true AND UserType=Visitor`) so defaulting to audience does not bypass any rule. Picking "Other" re-filters on the next API call (no client-side cache eviction needed — the lookup provider returns all rows in one shot, and the filter is applied locally on `IsVisitor`). **`SegmentedButton` over two `ChoiceChip`s** for the toggle: it is the Material 3 idiom and gives keyboard / screen-reader semantics for free. **`DropdownButtonFormField` over searchable `Autocomplete`** for both ProfileType and Country pickers: the active-row counts are small (single-digit to mid-double-digit at SIMF scale) so a search affordance would be UX noise. If usage at scale ever demands search, swap one widget. **Interests as `GridView` not `Wrap`** because the visual rhythm matches the "card per row" Mockup.html intent more closely; switching to `Wrap` would yield variable-width chips that fight the visual grid. **The pre-existing `/users/me` placeholder in `AuthApi.getCurrentUser` is unchanged** — that endpoint mismatch (`/users/me` vs the shipped `/account/me`) was logged previously and is out of scope for this commit; the new flow doesn't depend on it. **Route number 4 + 8 are retired but unused, not reassigned** — the numbered-routes list has two gaps (4, 8) for human readers to spot at a glance that two screens were removed; renumbering 5–7 down would break every Mockup.html cross-reference and future-document cross-link to "screen 5" / "screen 7". |
| D-191 | 2026-05-30 | **bUnit harness for CP Razor components — closes the project-wide test-infrastructure gap recorded since D-177.** Every CP-only module since D-177 (D-180, D-182, D-183, D-184, D-185, D-186, D-187, D-190) shipped with the explicit footnote that the CP page behavior was not bUnit-tested — the project had no harness for `IJSRuntime` + `IStringLocalizer<Strings>` + `AuthorizationContext` setup. New `CpComponentTestBase.cs` extends `Bunit.TestContext` and pre-registers: (a) `IStringLocalizer<Strings>` mock that returns each lookup key as-is (so tests assert against resx keys, not EN strings — keeps tests resilient to copy edits); (b) `TestAuthorizationContext` pre-set as authenticated Administrator (so pages with `[Authorize(Roles="Administrator")]` render). `IJSRuntime` and `NavigationManager` come from `Bunit.TestContext` automatically; tests stub specific JS calls per-fact via `JSInterop.Setup<T>(matcher).SetResult(...)`. **Reference tests (9 across 3 files).** `ProfileTypeFormTests.cs` (5) — the canonical "modal form" pattern: Audience form omits the MobileAppRole picker; Partner form includes it; Audience create posts UserType="Visitor" + IsVisitor=true via `simfAccount.postJson`; Partner create posts IsVisitor=false; Edit mode preserves Initial.IsVisitor on the PUT payload (the D-187 review-pass binding fix has a regression guard here). `MeetingRequestsListTests.cs` (3) — the "page + filter + modal" pattern: Page load posts the list query with Top=50 + empty Filters; Status filter change mutates `_query` in place (preserves any future Sort/Search state) AND resets Skip=0 AND fires a second list call; the Respond button on a row opens the modal AND fires the D-185 detail-fetch via `simfAccount.getJson("/account/api/admin/meeting-requests/{id}")` AND swaps the modal's email field to the fetched value. `OtherProfileTypesListTests.cs` (1) — the "filter-pinned list" pattern: initial load pins both `userType=Visitor` AND `isVisitor=false` filters (the D-186 queue-isolation invariant the CP renders). **Mid-implementation lessons folded in.** (1) First attempt used `JSInterop.Setup<T>("simfAccount.postJson")` (identifier-only Setup); the bUnit handler didn't match and the page rendered the "LoadFailed" toast instead of the data. Fixed by switching to the predicate matcher form: `JSInterop.Setup<T>(inv => inv.Identifier == "simfAccount.postJson")` — the predicate matcher matches any identifier regardless of args, which is what the test wants (the test inspects args via `handler.Invocations` after the fact). (2) Async `OnInitializedAsync` doesn't complete before `RenderComponent<T>` returns — tests MUST `cut.WaitForAssertion(() => Assert.Single(handler.Invocations))` before reading the call. Both lessons documented in `CpComponentTestBase` so future test authors don't re-discover them. **Test totals: 537/537 across 5 projects** (Api 470/470, CP 47/47 — was 38, +9 bUnit; Domain 5/5, Application 2/2, ApiClient 13/13). Build clean, 0 warnings. **What D-191 does NOT include.** No backfill of bUnit coverage across every existing CP page (there are ~40 CP pages; covering them all would be a much larger module). The 9 reference tests are the harness pattern — future CP-only modules can copy + adapt them. No tests against the `CpShellLayout` itself (it pulls `AuthenticationStateProvider` + custom services that would need separate mocks; not needed for page-level behavior tests because bUnit doesn't render `@layout` references by default). No tests for MudBlazor / SimfComponents internals. | The gap had compounded across 8+ CP-only modules — each one shipped with a documented "no bUnit harness" caveat in the DECISIONS_LOG row. The harness is the one-time investment that pays off for every future CP commit: the 5 ProfileTypeForm tests would have caught the D-187 review-pass `IsVisitor` binding bug at commit time instead of via the post-commit Update_flipping integration test. **One csproj edit (`bunit` package)** — pre-cleared by the "go" on the proposed scope (which included the package). No other csproj edits; no shared csproj modifications. **`CpComponentTestBase` over per-test setup.** Centralising the three required service registrations means every future bUnit test starts with the same base + adds only the JS stubs it actually needs. The PassThroughStringLocalizer keeps tests resilient to EN-string changes — a CP resx rename only breaks the resx-coverage tests, not the bUnit tests that assert against the key. **Predicate-matcher Setup shape** is the canonical bUnit pattern when args don't matter for the match — the alternative is enumerating every arg literal at Setup time, which couples the test to the call shape and breaks on any future arg-list refactor. The Invocations list is the assertion surface; Setup is just routing. **`WaitForAssertion` over arbitrary delays** for async init — bUnit guarantees the next assertion runs after any pending render completes, with a default 1s timeout that's plenty for the JS-interop mock to land. **Reference tests target real D-185/D-186/D-187 behavior** rather than synthetic cases — each test pins a specific decision from the DECISIONS_LOG, so a regression against that decision fails the bUnit test by name. The "list + filter mutate-in-place" test pins the D-183 review-pass discipline; the "Respond modal detail-fetch" test pins the D-185 PII split; the "Edit preserves IsVisitor" test pins the D-187 review-pass binding fix; the "userType + isVisitor both pinned" test pins the D-186 queue isolation. The next CP-only module's reviewer can read each test name and trace it back to a DECISIONS row. |

| D-189 | 2026-05-30 | **D-181 last carry-over — NoLimiter behavior test (without the TimeProvider refactor the backlog item assumed was needed).** The D-181 backlog described this as "needs the rate-limit middleware to be `TimeProvider`-aware first" — implying we needed an invasive custom rate-limiter to inject a fake clock and advance time forward inside the test. After investigation: .NET's built-in `FixedWindowRateLimiter` does NOT accept a `TimeProvider` and there's no clean extension point to inject one without re-implementing the limiter from scratch. D-189 picks the pragmatic alternative — test the boundary directly with six sequential calls in under a second. The `ai-test:no-sub` partition is 5 permits / 1 minute; six sequential calls in any sub-minute window means the 6th MUST get 429 regardless of the clock implementation. **What shipped.** New `tests/SIMF.Api.Tests/NoSubRateLimitTests.cs` — single integration test `Six_requests_without_sub_claim_trip_the_no_sub_partition`. The test forges a JWT inline that authenticates as `Administrator` role (so the `AdministratorOnly` policy passes) but deliberately omits the `JwtRegisteredClaimNames.Sub` claim — exactly the claim-mapper-regression shape the D-181 defense-in-depth was meant to bound. The test mints the token using the same `SimfApiFactory` `Jwt__SigningKey` env var so the host's signature validation accepts it. Then it posts six times to `/api/v1/admin/ai/prompts/{Guid.NewGuid()}/test` (a bogus id is fine — the rate-limit middleware runs before the prompt lookup, so the per-call response shape is "anything other than 429" until the partition exhausts). Asserts: the first 5 calls do NOT return 429 (any other 4xx is acceptable — likely 404 because the prompt id is bogus); the 6th call returns `HttpStatusCode.TooManyRequests`. **Test totals: 519/519 across 5 projects** (Api 461/461 — was 460, +1; CP 38/38, Domain 5/5, Application 2/2, ApiClient 13/13). Build clean, 0 warnings. | The D-181 backlog assumed a TimeProvider refactor was the only way to prove the no-sub partition actually bounds requests. That assumption was wrong: a behavior test that exercises the boundary directly (six calls within the natural test runtime) doesn't need a fake clock — the FixedWindowRateLimiter's 1-minute window comfortably contains a sub-second test loop, so the boundary trips on the 6th call without any clock manipulation. The "TimeProvider refactor" framing came from a more elaborate test scenario (testing window-rollover behavior, where a 60-second wait WOULD need a fake clock) but the defense-in-depth contract is just "is the partition bounded at all" — that's a single-window test, no rollover needed. **Inline JWT forging** is the right shape because: (a) the existing `JwtTokenService` always sets a real `Sub` claim — calling it via the production code path can't produce the regression-shape token under test; (b) the test factory's signing-key env var lets us mint a parallel token with the same key the host validates against; (c) the test is self-contained — no new test helper / fixture / factory needed. The forged-token shape is deliberately minimal: Administrator role + Email + Jti + security_stamp + account_state + user_type + mobile_app_role, no Sub. **Test endpoint choice — `/admin/ai/prompts/{bogusId}/test`.** The `ai-test` rate-limit policy is only attached to this endpoint (per `AiPromptAdminEndpoints.cs`); using any other endpoint would test a different partition. The bogus prompt id keeps the test independent of any seeded data — the rate-limit middleware fires before the prompt lookup, so the per-call response is governed by the limiter, not by the prompt resolution. **The contract assertion is loose-on-success / tight-on-fail.** The first 5 calls assert "anything other than 429" rather than a specific status — different unrelated failures (404 unknown prompt, 401 missing claim, 500 internal) are all acceptable for the "not yet exhausted" branch. The 6th call asserts the exact 429 — that's the bound being proven. This shape survives any non-rate-limit refactoring of the test endpoint (different status codes for unknown ids etc.) without breaking. **Closes the last D-181 carry-over** — D-188 shipped the AiPromptHistory + AWS-context redaction; D-189 closes the NoLimiter behavior test. The D-181 backlog (per the original D-181 row) is now fully drained except for the one operator-side item (HMAC rotation operator playbook) which is documentation in the SIEM rules README's "HMAC rotation playbook" section that landed in D-185. |
| D-188 | 2026-05-30 | **D-181 architectural carry-overs — AiPromptHistory table + AWS context-only secret redaction.** Two of the three D-181 architectural items shipped together (third — NoLimiter TimeProvider refactor — needs its own commit because it's an invasive rate-limit middleware change). **(1) AiPromptHistory append-only snapshot.** New `SIMF.Domain.Ai.AiPromptHistory` entity captures the PRE-mutation state on every `AdminAiPromptService.UpdateAsync`. One row per (AiPromptId, Version) with the full `SystemPrompt` + `UserPromptTemplate` + `Provider` + `Model` + `Temperature` + `MaxOutputTokens` + `IsActive` + `ContentHash` (HMAC, same `v1:` shape as the audit row) + `CapturedFromUpdatedAt` + `UpdatedByUserId` + `CapturedAt`. New `App/D188_AddAiPromptHistory` migration adds the table + a unique (AiPromptId, Version) composite index (rejects duplicate snapshots at the same version) + a CapturedAt index for time-range queries. New `GET /api/v1/admin/ai/prompts/{id}/history` endpoint returns the snapshot list newest-first, scoped admin + approved + rate-limited (`auth` partition — matches the D-185 review-pass shape for per-record drill-down). New `AdminAiPromptHistoryEntry` contract on `SIMF.Contracts.Ai.AiPrompts.cs`. New `SimfAdminClient.GetAiPromptHistoryAsync` method. Snapshot writes are atomic with the live-row update — both land in the same `SaveChangesAsync` call so a snapshot-insert failure (e.g. concurrent edit racing through the unique index) rolls back the live-row mutation too. **(2) AWS context-only secret-key redaction.** D-181 added AWS AKIA / ASIA value redaction but the secret access key has no prefix and is just a 40-char base64-ish string — a value-only match would have a runaway false-positive rate. D-188 adds the `AwsSecretAccessKeyContextRegex` pattern that matches `aws_secret_access_key=<value>` / `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY=<value>` / `aws_secret_access_key: <value>` / `aws-secret-access-key=<value>` (case-insensitive on the prefix; the underscore vs hyphen tolerance covers both config-file and env-var conventions). The whole `prefix=value` span is redacted to `[REDACTED_KEY]` so the SOC pipeline cannot reconstruct either half. Runs alongside the AKIA / ASIA value match (the two patterns are independent — one catches the public access-key id, the other catches the secret-key by surrounding context). **Tests (3 new).** `Update_writes_an_AiPromptHistory_snapshot_capturing_the_prior_state` — directly asserts the snapshot's Version equals the PRE-bump value AND SystemPrompt + UserPromptTemplate match the original state AND ContentHash starts with `v1:`. `Get_history_endpoint_returns_snapshots_newest_first` — drives the GET endpoint through two consecutive updates, asserts two snapshots returned, Version=2 first then Version=1. `RedactValue_masks_aws_secret_access_key_by_context` `[Theory]` over four config / env-var shapes; asserts both the redaction marker AND absence of the raw value AND absence of the prefix substring. **Test totals: 518/518 across 5 projects** (Api 460/460 — was 454, +6: 3 D-188 tests + 3 review-pass-discovered tests folded into D-188). Build clean, 0 warnings. | The owner chose the in-DB AiPromptHistory path over the off-host WORM archive — the in-DB shape is simpler (one migration, queryable from the CP, no external storage credentials to manage), and the freeze partial-lift was already paid for in D-186 so adding one more migration costs nothing. The WORM-archive path remains a valid future migration if compliance ever demands an external-storage / external-immutability story; the snapshots can be replayed from `AiPromptHistory` into a WORM blob at that point. **`AiPromptHistory` schema decisions.** The snapshot captures the PRE-mutation state (not the post-mutation state) because the live row already carries the post-mutation state — the snapshot is specifically for "what did this prompt look like BEFORE this edit". The `Version` field records the PRE-bump value (matching `AiPrompt.Version` at snapshot time) so when the live row reads `Version=5`, snapshots exist for `Version=1..4`. The unique `(AiPromptId, Version)` index makes append-only enforcement a DB-level invariant, not a code-level one — a buggy retry path cannot land a duplicate snapshot. **No navigation property** between `AiPromptHistory.AiPromptId` and `AiPrompt.Id` because the snapshot must survive the live row's deactivation / hard-delete (rare but possible in a freeze-lifted future). The FK relationship is implicit via the Guid; ad-hoc joins still work. **`ContentHash` matches the audit-row format** (`v1:` prefix, HMAC-SHA256 over SystemPrompt + 0x1F + UserPromptTemplate) so a SOC analyst correlating a `AiPrompt.Updated` audit row's `contentHashOld` with a `AiPromptHistory` snapshot does a literal string compare — no re-hash needed. **GET endpoint is rate-limited** (`auth` partition) under the same logic as the D-185 review-pass meeting-request detail endpoint: per-record drill-down on audit-content is a PII drill-down vector — a compromised admin shouldn't be able to burst-scrape historical prompt text past the natural triage cadence. **Snapshot atomic with live update** because the alternative (snapshot writes in a separate `SaveChangesAsync`) would let a snapshot land with no matching live-row update (or vice versa) on a partial-failure path. EF's per-call atomicity is sufficient — the unique index gives the only relevant collision invariant. **No paging on the history endpoint.** A single AiPrompt accumulating more than a few hundred edits over an event lifetime is an unlikely shape; even at 1000 edits the response is well under the typical JSON-serialisation budget. If usage proves otherwise, paging via `GridQuery` is one-line addition. **AWS secret-key context regex is case-insensitive on the prefix** (`(?i)\baws[_-]?secret[_-]?access[_-]?key`) so config-file `aws_secret_access_key=` AND env-var `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY=` AND CLI-flag `--aws-secret-access-key=` all match. The value-side requires 20+ base64 chars (`[A-Za-z0-9/+=]{20,}`) — short enough to catch test-shape secrets (the 40-char real-world secret is well above), long enough to skip legitimate text like `aws_secret_access_key=YES` (literal config-file boilerplate that has no actual secret). **Both halves of the prefix=value span are redacted** because the prefix itself is informational ("this place holds AWS credentials") and a SOC pipeline that retains the prefix is still leaking the existence of the credential storage location to the audit log — though that's a much weaker leak than the value itself. Redacting the whole span is the higher-fidelity default. |
| D-187 | 2026-05-30 | **D-186 review-pass — 5 cross-validated HIGH findings + 3 new SIEM rules.** The 5 review-agent pass (code-reviewer, security, threat-detection, reality-checker, test-analyzer) flagged five HIGH-severity defects in the D-186 scope-guard shape and audit coverage; all folded in pre-commit. **(1) H-1 audience-set bug** — `ResolveProfileScopedUserIdsAsync` excluded Visitor users whose linked `UserProfile.ProfileTypeId` was null (the self-signup case: `UserProfileService.UpsertMineAsync` creates `new UserProfile { UserId, CreatedAt }` with ProfileTypeId never set). The audience set was built as `idsViaProfileType ∪ (visitorIds NOT IN withAnyProfileSet)` — a user with a profile row but null ProfileTypeId is in `withAnyProfileSet` (rejected from the union) and NOT in `idsViaProfileType` (rejected from the seed). Such users were invisible to BOTH approval queues even though they had self-promoted to PendingApproval and fired the AdminPendingVisitor notification. Rewrote the helper as `audience = visitorIds MINUS partner-linked` so null-ProfileTypeId users land on the Visitors queue correctly. **(2) H-2 + H-3 cross-scope ProfileType acceptance** — `CreateAccountAsync` only checked `profileType.UserType == userType` (Visitor==Visitor), never asserted `profileType.IsVisitor` matched the URL family. POST /admin/visitors could accept a partner ProfileTypeId; POST /admin/others could accept an audience ProfileTypeId; the resulting account landed on the wrong CP queue with the wrong audit-event mapping. `RegisterOnSiteAsync` had the same hole — the desk URL was decorative. Threaded a new optional `bool? expectedIsVisitor` parameter through `CreateAccountAsync` + `RegisterOnSiteAsync`. `CreateVisitorAsync` passes `true`; `CreateOtherAsync` passes `false`; the walk-in endpoints pass per-desk. Mismatch returns 400 `AdminProfileTypeInvalid` with bilingual messages naming which endpoint to use instead. **(3) Migration order** — security flagged that `Program.cs` ran `SimfIdentityDbContext.MigrateAsync()` before `SimfAppDbContext.MigrateAsync()`. If the App migration failed (or the process was killed mid-deploy), `AspNetUsers.UserType` was already folded to 'Visitor' but `ProfileTypes.UserType` was still 'Other' and the new `IsVisitor` column did not exist yet — partner accounts went invisible to both CP queues until the App migration finished. Reversed the order in `Program.cs:261-263` so App runs first; the comment block on the call site documents the rationale. **(4) IsVisitor flip audit-silence** — `AdminProfileTypeCommandService.UpdateAsync` allowed IsVisitor to be flipped at runtime (correct — it re-routes the row between approval queues) but the audit Detail did not capture the change. A compromised admin could mass-launder partner accounts into the audience queue by flipping their linked ProfileType.IsVisitor with zero SOC visibility. Captured `oldIsVisitor` before mutation; Detail now emits `isVisitorChanged=true; isVisitorOld=...; isVisitorNew=...; linkedAccountCount=...` when the flag changes (the count lets SIEM prioritise — a flip on a populated ProfileType has a much larger blast radius than a flip on a freshly-created one). **(5) Approval-scope probe invisibility** — `LoadPendingSubjectAsync` returns 404 on three branches: not-found, wrong-UserType, AND the new D-186 wrong-profile-scope branch. The 404 shape is identical to the caller so a probe ("try every visitor id against /admin/others/{id}/approve to find which are partners") leaks the boundary silently. Added `AuditEvents.AdminApprovalScopeMismatch = "Admin.ApprovalScopeMismatch"`; emitted on the third branch with `Detail.expectedScope` + `Detail.expectedIsVisitor`. **Three new SIEM rules under `docs/soc/siem-rules/`.** `m-003-profiletype-isvisitor-flip.yml` (level high; promote to critical when `linkedAccountCount>=5`). `m-004-approval-scope-probe.yml` (informational base + correlation `m-004b` at ≥10 mismatches / 10m, level high). `m-005-walkin-partner-burst.yml` (base + correlation at ≥5 partner-side walk-ins / 15m, level medium). The walk-in audit Detail also gained `profileTypeIsVisitor` so the M-005 rule can distinguish audience vs partner desk (the legacy `kind` field collapsed to always-Visitor post-D-186). **`SiemRulesShapeTests.ExpectedRuleFiles` updated** to include the 3 new rule files. **New test coverage (5 tests).** `AdminProfileTypeTests.IsVisitor_round_trips_through_Create_Get_List` `[Theory]` over `(true, false)` — locks the audience/partner discriminator through every wire surface. `Update_flipping_IsVisitor_persists_and_audits_the_change` — flips a row from audience to partner, asserts the response carries IsVisitor=false AND the OperationLog row contains `isVisitorChanged=true; isVisitorOld=True; isVisitorNew=False; linkedAccountCount=`. `Create_others_rejects_an_audience_profile_type` + `Create_visitors_rejects_a_partner_profile_type` — both directions of the H-2 cross-scope guard. `Pending_visitor_with_profile_row_but_null_ProfileTypeId_appears_on_Visitors_queue` — directly exercises the H-1 fix (seeds a SimfUser + a UserProfile with null ProfileTypeId via the DB scope, asserts the pending-visitors list returns the id). `WalkInRegistrationTests.Walk_in_with_partner_profile_type_on_visitors_desk_returns_400` + twin `Walk_in_with_audience_profile_type_on_others_desk_returns_400` — both directions of the H-3 walk-in cross-scope guard. **Bug fix on AdminUpdateProfileTypeRequest binding** — `UpdateAdminProfileTypeRouteRequest` was missing `IsVisitor` so the endpoint constructed `AdminUpdateProfileTypeRequest` with the default true regardless of the client's value (the Update_flipping test caught this — assertion failed because the response showed IsVisitor=true after sending IsVisitor=false). Added `IsVisitor` to the route request shape + wired it through. **Test totals: 512/512 across 5 projects** (Api 454/454 — was 447, +7 review-pass tests; CP 38/38, Domain 5/5, Application 2/2, ApiClient 13/13). Build clean, 0 warnings. | Five agents in parallel against a 38-file structural change surfaced five HIGH defects that would have shipped to production. Every one of them was a "wire-shape contract drift" — the D-186 cut moved discriminators from `UserType` onto `ProfileType.IsVisitor` but the existing code paths that read `UserType` did not all get reframed. Specifically: the scope-guard in CreateAccountAsync (legacy check on UserType only) and the migration order (legacy assumption that contexts were independent) both worked under the pre-D-186 contract and silently broke under the new one. The agent pass is the right place to catch these — local dev iteration on a 38-file change cannot cover every cross-path interaction, and the alternative is finding them in production. **Cross-scope guard reinstated** despite the initial D-186 cut's "the chosen ProfileType determines routing" comment. The reasoning behind dropping it was thin (one fewer check) and the reasoning for restoring it is concrete: the desk URL is the actor's intent expression; silently routing the account to a different queue than the URL implies erodes operator confidence in the audit-event mapping (`AdminVisitorApproved` vs `AdminOtherApproved`) and complicates SOC investigation when an "Others queue burst" actually contains audience accounts. The new bilingual error message ("use the Others endpoint to assign it" / "use the Visitors endpoint to assign it") gives the desk operator a clear next step. **Migration order rationale** — the App migration is forward-compatible (it can run against a pre-D-186 Identity DB where users still have UserType='Other'; the App-side fold runs cleanly), but the Identity migration is NOT forward-compatible with a pre-D-186 App DB (a user folded to UserType='Visitor' linked to a ProfileType still labelled UserType='Other' falls through the scope guard's UserType filter). Running App first means at worst the partial state is "App is D-186, Identity is pre-D-186" — partner accounts still flow through the legacy 'Other' UserType code path until Identity catches up on the next deploy attempt. **`linkedAccountCount` in the IsVisitor-flip audit** — without it, SOC cannot distinguish "admin flipped a brand-new test ProfileType" from "admin flipped the canonical Sponsor ProfileType that 3000 partners are linked to". The count is computed only when the flag actually changes (the cheap `CountAsync` is skipped on every other update path) so steady-state updates pay no extra cost. **Scope-probe audit shape decision** — emitting on the third branch only (not on the not-found or wrong-UserType branches) keeps the SIEM signal high-fidelity. The not-found case is operationally noisy (admins routinely paste typo'd ids); the wrong-UserType case was already covered pre-D-186 by an existing audit event. The new audit is specifically for the D-186 boundary the freeze partial-lift introduced. **m-003 / m-004 / m-005 rule IDs follow the established `m-*` family** (D-185 introduced m-001 + m-002 + m-002b for meeting-request audit events). The numbering keeps the SOC-side rule catalogue grouped: m-001 / m-002 = meeting-request reads; m-003 = ProfileType.Updated; m-004 = approval-scope probe; m-005 = walk-in registration. SOC operators landing on a new rule can identify the audit-event family from the prefix without reading the body. **Walk-in restored guard preserves the `kind` argument** on `RegisterOnSiteAsync` for endpoint-shape backward-compat — the legacy callers passing UserType.Visitor still work; the new behaviour is opt-in via the optional `expectedIsVisitor` parameter. This avoids a contract break to any future caller that uses the service interface directly. |

Owner authorised both the enum-value removal and the two new EF migrations on top of the D-110 frozen baseline. **Schema changes.** New `ProfileType.IsVisitor` bool column (default true; new (IsVisitor, IsActive) composite index for the CP partner-queue filter). EF migrations: (a) `App/D186_AddProfileTypeIsVisitor.cs` adds the column + index + runs two SQL backfills — `UPDATE ProfileTypes SET IsVisitor = 0 WHERE UserType = 'Other'` followed by `UPDATE ProfileTypes SET UserType = 'Visitor' WHERE UserType = 'Other'` (UserType is stored as a stringly enum via `HasConversion<string>()`, so the literal `'Other'` is what's on disk). (b) `Identity/D186_FoldOtherUsersIntoVisitor.cs` runs `UPDATE AspNetUsers SET UserType = 'Visitor' WHERE UserType = 'Other'` so every Other-typed account folds into Visitor in lockstep. Partner scope = explicit partner profile only); `LoadPendingSubjectAsync`, `ApproveAsync`, `RejectAsync`, `BulkApproveAsync`, `ListAccountsAsync`, `ListPendingAsync`, `BulkDeleteUsersByKindAsync`, `DuplicateUserByKindAsync`, `ExportUsersByKindAsync` all updated to honor the scope guard. `RegisterOnSiteAsync` simplified — no more cross-scope rejection; `UpdateAsync` now persists IsVisitor changes (mutable — flipping it re-routes the row between the CP approval queues without touching the underlying user accounts). `ListAllAsync` accepts a new `isVisitor` filter so the CP Visitors / Others profile-type pages can filter server-side. **API endpoints.** The /admin/visitors/* and /admin/others/* URL families both survive — semantically: visitors = audience scope, others = partner scope. `VisitorBulkEndpoints` pass `(UserType.Visitor, requirePartnerScope: false)`; The Update path treats IsVisitor as mutable because the consequence of flipping it is only the CP approval-queue routing, not any change to the underlying user accounts; an admin who mis-categorised a profile type can fix it without a data migration. **Admin stays at UserType=2** instead of renumbering to 1 because the DB stores UserType as a string (`HasConversion<string>()`) — the integer ordering doesn't appear on disk but the enum's integer values are stable across code generations + the test surface, and renumbering would silently break any caller still passing an int (or anything that hardcoded `2` against the enum). The integer `1` slot is documented as reserved in the enum XML-doc so a future re-use can't conflict. **Cross-context profile-scope filter via in-memory set** (`ResolveProfileScopedUserIdsAsync`) is the right shape at SIMF scale (<2k partner accounts at the planned single-event scope). The helper fetches a HashSet of UserIds matching the requested IsVisitor flag (with audience-side specifically including the no-profile users), then narrows the `dbContext.Users` query with `.Where(u => set.Contains(u.Id))`. SQL Server's parameter cap (~2000) is the bound; a future multi-event variant would need a cross-context join (replicate the IsVisitor flag onto SimfUser, or move ProfileType to the Identity DB). Documented in the helper. **Walk-in registration cross-scope guard dropped.** The pre-D-186 endpoint pair enforced "the kind in the URL must match the chosen ProfileType's UserType". The CP can still surface a warning if a desk picks a partner-side ProfileType on the Visitors page UI, but the API doesn't enforce it. **Migration backfill uses string literal `'Other'`.** UserType is stored stringly so the SQL `UPDATE ... WHERE UserType = 'Other'` is the literal that matches. A future DB-introspection check should pass over both the pre-D-186 enum-int columns (none here — all enum columns are stringly converted) and the new string literals; the migration is idempotent (runs once via EF's history table). **The freeze is partially lifted by owner direction**; CLAUDE.md project file (`d:\SIMF\System\V1.0.0\CLAUDE.md`) D-110 baseline section retains its original text — operators read the DECISIONS_LOG entry above as the canonical record of the lift. |

Now expressed as an event_count correlation (>=200/h admin dry-runs) with the SOC-platform-native token-sum variant documented in the `notes:` block (Splunk + KQL syntax verbatim). **AI-005 reconciled.** Description and detection now both list the full canonical 7-marker set (KEY, JWT, PAN, PEM, NID, PHONE, IBAN — D-184 README had 4, detection had 4 different ones). Detection switched from `InputJson|contains|any:` over hard-coded marker strings to `Detail.redactionCount|gte: 1` so the set membership lives in the canon table, not in the rule. **AI-007 IBAN-with-intent.** Same `Detail.redactionKinds|contains: 'IBAN'` membership form; intent regex moved off raw input (which would leak PII to the SOC pipeline) onto `Detail.inputPreview` (the D-181 redactor's sanitised + length-capped snippet). **AI-008 `redactionTokens` field replaced.** The D-184 draft referenced an undefined field; rewritten as a `value_count` correlation over `Detail.redactionKinds` with `condition: gte: 3`. S-001b (`SeatReservation.Released` burst, >=3 events/10s, level: high) catches scripted abuse that escapes the main S-001 1-minute window. **HMAC rotation playbook documented in README** — closes the D-181 carry-over: when operators rotate `Ai:PromptHashHmacKey`, the `v1:`/`v2:` cutover MUST pin `validFrom` on the new rule version, keep the old rule alive through audit retention, then delete the legacy rule when retention rolls over. **Structural test in `tests/SIMF.Api.Tests/SiemRulesShapeTests.cs`.** Five guard checks: every expected rule id has a file; no file contains legacy v1 pipe-aggregate syntax (`\| count()`, `\ntimeframe:`); every file has `title:` + `id:` + (`logsource:` OR `correlation:`); every correlation block uses the v2 keys (`type:`, `group-by:`, `timespan:`) and not the invented `aggregate:`/`threshold:`; no rule uses the non-existent `Detail|json` modifier or `CallerUserId` actor field. Text-contains assertions (no YamlDotNet dependency added — §1.7 forbids csproj edits without explicit approval). **(2) Meeting-request contract split — `AdminMeetingRequestRow` vs `AdminMeetingRequestDetail`.** Closes the security-engineer residual leak the D-184 review-pass flagged: the list response had been carrying `RequesterEmail` on every row even after the grid stopped rendering it, so a curl scrape of the list endpoint still pulled bulk PII. `AdminMeetingRequestRow` (list shape) drops `RequesterEmail` entirely; the new `AdminMeetingRequestDetail` record carries it (used by the new GET-by-id endpoint + the Respond response). The respond modal in `MeetingRequestsList.razor` now fetches the detail on click via `simfAccount.getJson("/account/api/admin/meeting-requests/{id}")` — modal opens with the row data immediately + the email arrives a moment later under the requester name with a "Loading contact details…" placeholder. New endpoint at `GET /api/v1/admin/meeting-requests/{id}` (admin + approved-account policies, no rate-limit since it's per-record drill-down); new `SimfAdminClient.GetAdminMeetingRequestAsync`; new CP proxy route at `/account/api/admin/meeting-requests/{id}`. **List-call now audited.** New `AuditEvents.AdminMeetingRequestsListed` constant (`"Admin.MeetingRequestsListed"`); emitted by `MeetingRequestService.ListAllAsync` with Detail JSON carrying `{count, total, top, skip, statusFilter, sessionFilter}` so SOC can distinguish a routine top-25 status-filtered view from a top-200 unfiltered scrape. **Per-record fetch also audited.** New `AuditEvents.AdminMeetingRequestViewed` (`"MeetingRequest.Viewed"`); emitted by the new `GetAsync` so SOC sees per-record PII access. **Service interface signature changes.** `ListAllAsync(GridQuery)` → `ListAllAsync(Guid actorUserId, GridQuery)` (actor needed for the audit); `GetAsync(Guid actorUserId, Guid id)` added; `RespondAsync` return type changed from `AdminMeetingRequestRow` to `AdminMeetingRequestDetail` (admin already paid for the read, return the full record). **CP wiring updates.** `MeetingRequestsList.razor._respondTarget` field type changed from `AdminMeetingRequestRow` to `AdminMeetingRequestDetail`; `OnRespond` → `OnRespondAsync` so the modal can fetch the detail on click; new `_loadingDetail` flag drives a placeholder under the requester name until the detail arrives.
