The actual SIEM rule deployment to a SOC platform stays operator-side. **Review-pass amendments folded in pre-commit.** Five agents on the D-185 changeset surfaced significant producer/rule contract drift and several MEDIUM-severity gaps — all addressed before commit. (i) **Producer/rule contract drift on AiInvocation events (threat-detection HIGH).** The five rules AI-005/007/008/009 referenced `Detail.redactionKinds`, `Detail.redactionCount`, `Detail.inputPreview` but `AiService.PersistSuccessAsync` was only emitting `{promptKey, feature, provider, model, callerKind, latencyMs, tokensInput, tokensOutput, invocationId}`. The redactor wrote redacted text into the DB column `AiInvocation.InputJson` but did not project the per-invocation summary into the audit Detail. New `AiAuditDetail.SerialiseAndRedactWithSummary` returns a `RedactedInvocationDetail` record carrying `(InputJson, RedactionKinds, RedactionCount, InputPreview)`; AiService now calls this single method and emits all three new fields on every `AiInvocation.Succeeded` event. Preview is length-capped at 1024 chars; redaction-kind list and count are derived from already-redacted text so no raw secret rides the SOC pipeline. (ii) **`Detail.key` → `Detail.promptKey` (threat-detection HIGH).** `AdminAiPromptService.{CreateAsync,UpdateAsync,DeactivateAsync}` were emitting `key` while every `AiInvocation.*` event emits `promptKey` and the README canonical table pins `promptKey` as the single name. Renamed all three sites; AI-001 + AI-003 + AI-004 rules now field-extract correctly. (iii) **MeetingRequest event Detail JSON-shape (threat-detection HIGH).** `MeetingRequestService.{SubmitAsync,GetAsync,RespondAsync}` were writing free-text Detail (`"meetingRequestId=...; status=..."`). S-002 selected on `Detail.status: 'Rejected'` which would never field-extract under `parse_json(Detail)`. New private `DetailJson(object)` helper using `JsonSerializerOptions`; all three sites now emit structured JSON. (iv) **Audit-event naming consistency (code-reviewer MEDIUM).** The pair `Admin.MeetingRequestsListed` + `MeetingRequest.Viewed` mixed prefixes. Renamed Viewed to `Admin.MeetingRequestViewed` so SOC rule authors filtering on `EventType startswith 'Admin.'` see both events together. (v) **RespondAsync now also emits MeetingRequestViewed (security/code-reviewer MEDIUM).** RespondAsync returns email but only wrote `MeetingRequest.Responded`, so a scripted client skipping the GET would exfiltrate email without a Viewed paper trail. Added the parallel Viewed write inside RespondAsync after the Responded event — every email disclosure now leaves a Viewed audit row regardless of which endpoint emitted it. (vi) **Rate-limit on the new GET endpoint (security HIGH).** `GetAdminMeetingRequestEndpoint` now carries `Options(rb => rb.RequireRateLimiting("auth"))` so a compromised admin cannot burst-fetch emails between SIEM AI-010-style bulk-view detections. (vii) **Two new SIEM rules for the new audit events (code-reviewer HIGH / threat-detection H4+H5 / security H1).** New files: `m-001-meeting-request-bulk-view.yml` (>=20 distinct `Detail.meetingRequestId` / 10m via value_count, level high) and `m-002-meeting-request-list-scrape.yml` (multi-doc with M-002 single-large-call detection at `count>=100 + empty filters` + M-002b repeat-list-call at `>=5/10m`, both high/medium). README rule index + canonical Detail JSON shape table updated with `count, total, top, skip, statusFilter, sessionFilter, meetingRequestId, sessionId, provider, model, latencyMs` keys. (viii) **AI-005 marker-count typo (code-reviewer LOW).** Body said "7 markers" while the listed set is 8 — fixed. (ix) **Test rigor — new tests (test-analyst MUST-HAVE).** `Get_requires_administrator_role` (visitor → 403 on the PII drill-down), `List_writes_AdminMeetingRequestsListed_audit_event` + `Get_writes_AdminMeetingRequestViewed_audit_event` (assert OperationLog rows actually appear so SIEM rules M-001/M-002 cannot silently match nothing under a future refactor), plus an empty-page guard on `List_response_does_not_contain_requester_email` so the JSON-substring check is no longer vacuously true on a zero-row page. New helper `CreateAdministratorAndSignInWithIdAsync` exposes the minted admin's UserId so audit rows can be filtered by `ActorUserId` instead of timestamp-window (the `FakeTimeProvider` fixture starts near real-UtcNow but doesn't advance per test, so timestamp-based filters mis-trigger). (x) **Test rigor — SIEM shape test extended.** `Correlation_rules_reference_an_existing_base_rule_id` walks every `rules:` reference in correlation blocks and asserts the referenced base id is declared somewhere in the directory — catches the "delete s-001 silently breaks s-001b" case the code-reviewer flagged. **Final test totals: 505/505 across 5 projects** (Api 447/447 — was 434, +13: 7 SIEM shape tests + 6 meeting-request tests; CP 38/38, Domain 5/5, Application 2/2, ApiClient 13/13). | The two deliverables shared a unifying frame: "operator-side rigor on the audit pipeline + the data that rides it." Bundling them was the §17 right call — both touched the same MeetingRequests slice (the rules detect bursts on its events; the contract split decides what those events carry), both depended on the same audit-event naming + JSON shape, and the test infrastructure spans both (the YAML structural test + the JSON-shape test both check that exposed contracts match documented intent). Splitting them would have meant two review-pass rounds + two commits + duplicated context-loading for the agents. **Sigma 2.x correlation blocks over v1 pipe-aggregate.** The v1 form is shorter to write but strict v2 linters reject it and the pipe-syntax aggregation is platform-specific (Splunk-flavored). The 2.x correlation block is portable across pySigma backends + the linter accepts it + the format-checker test can verify it structurally. Multi-doc YAML (`---` separator) is the canonical 2.x pattern for "base rule + correlation rule" — each document is independently parseable, and the correlation rule references the base by id rather than copy-pasting selection logic. **Field-name canon table in the README.** Without a single document pinning what `Detail.promptKey` vs `Detail.key` means + which events emit which keys, every rule rewrite would re-invent the convention. The table is the contract — backend changes that rename a Detail key must update the table + every rule that depends on it in the same commit. **Threshold tuning grounded in security review, not raw-vibe.** Each tuned threshold has its rationale documented in the `notes:` block: AI-001 raised because a single editor doing a Sprint cleanup tripped the original >2/10m; AI-003 raised because a sprint-retirement of several prompts shouldn't page. The thresholds are still re-baseline candidates once real-world traffic accumulates — the `notes:` block tells the SOC operator what to look at when calibrating. **AI-010 added rather than rolled into AI-005.** The Viewed event is a distinct tactic (collection) from the Succeeded event flood; bulking them under one rule would have masked the surveillance signal under the PII signal. Separate rules + separate severities (AI-010 high, AI-005 medium) preserve the SOC operator's distinction between "user pasted secrets" and "admin is scraping the audit log." **S-001b alongside S-001 not replacing it.** Different attack profiles — S-001 (>=10/1m) is the "accidental cleanup gone wrong" signal; S-001b (>=3/10s) is the "scripted abuse" signal. Each carries its own severity (medium / high) so the SOC's on-call paging logic can route them differently. **List-call audited + email split off the wire — both halves of one defense-in-depth.** Auditing alone would let the email continue to leak to anyone who can fetch the list; splitting the contract alone would let an admin pull the per-row detail thousands of times without leaving a paper trail. The two together are the right shape: SOC sees the list scrape immediately + sees the per-record drill-down too, and the contract guarantees a single grid render can never broadcast bulk PII. **Modal fetches detail on click rather than pre-fetching for the visible page.** Pre-fetching all visible-page details would lift the bulk-PII problem from "list scrape" to "page render"; on-click fetching keeps the per-record audit signal honest (one Viewed event per click = one explicit admin action). The "Loading contact details…" placeholder is the UX compensation — the modal opens immediately on click so the admin is never blocked, the email just arrives a beat later. **Text-contains test instead of a real Sigma parser.** Adding YamlDotNet to test the YAML shape would require a csproj edit (forbidden by §1.7 without explicit approval). The text-contains shape catches every issue D-184's review-pass identified (legacy syntax, invented modifiers, field-name drift) and protects against regression; if the SOC's deployment pipeline standardises on pySigma later, the test can promote to a real Sigma validator without disrupting the rule files themselves. |
| D-184 | 2026-05-30 | **PII + SOC + polish bundle — three small focused items from the D-183 backlog.** Closes (a) the PII-in-grid concern security raised on D-183, (b) the SOC SIEM rules documented across D-178/D-179/D-181/D-182/D-183 reviews, and (c) the Country dropdown polish on Delegations code-reviewer flagged on D-183. Bundled because each is small + thematically focused (operator hardening polish) and the workflow overhead would dominate if shipped individually. **(1) PII hardening — RequesterEmail dropped from the meeting-requests grid; moved to the respond modal.** `MeetingRequestsList.razor`: the per-row Requester cell now shows only the name; the respond modal's `<dl>` adds the email under the requester name (still styled as `simf-table__sub` so it's visually de-emphasised vs the principal data). Backend unchanged — `AdminMeetingRequestRow` still carries `RequesterEmail`; the contract is intact for other consumers. The audit-log-the-list-call piece that security suggested needs a new `AdminMeetingRequestsListed` audit event + a service-layer hook; deferred to D-185 (small backend change but crosses a real layer boundary). **(2) SIEM rules YAML versioned under `docs/soc/siem-rules/`.** Twelve Sigma YAML files (AI-001…AI-009 + S-001…S-003) plus a `README.md` index covering source, log-source convention, deployment notes, and a cross-version-drift caveat (HMAC `v1:` prefix from D-181 means rules comparing `contentHashOld` vs `contentHashNew` must skip cross-version compares). Each rule documents `title`, `id`, `status: experimental`, `description`, `references` (back to the originating DECISIONS row), MITRE ATT&CK tags where applicable, `logsource`, `detection` with explicit thresholds + timeframes, `falsepositives`, `level` (medium/high/critical), and the audit fields the rule needs. The repo ships them so SOC + code stay in sync — when the audit shape changes (rename, JSON schema bump), the rule files in the same commit get flagged. Deployment to the SOC platform (Sentinel / Elastic / Splunk) is operator-side: translate Sigma syntax to the target's query language, import via the platform's rule catalogue. **(3) Country dropdown polish on `DelegationsList`.** Replaced the raw `<input type="number">` for `CountryId` with a `<select>` populated from the existing `/account/api/admin/countries/list` endpoint. Countries cached at mount via `LoadCountriesAsync` (Top=500, filtered to active, ordered by `DisplayOrder` then `NameEn`) so the Add/Edit modal opens without a round-trip; new `Admin.Delegations.Field.CountryNone` resx key in both EN + AR provides the empty-selection option. Form field label also changed from "Country ID (ISO 3166-1 numeric)" to plain "Country" — admins picking from a list don't need to know the numeric encoding. **Test totals: 492/492 across 5 projects** (Api 434/434, CP 38/38, Domain 5/5, Application 2/2, ApiClient 13/13). No code-path coverage changes — D-184 is pure CP UI tweak + docs files. **Review-pass amendments folded in pre-commit.** Two code bugs from code-reviewer addressed inline: (i) **`simf-table__sub` CSS class added.** Code-reviewer flagged that the class was referenced exactly once in `MeetingRequestsList.razor` but never defined in any CSS file, so the email-under-name visual hierarchy in the respond modal fell back to default body weight. New rule under the existing `.simf-table__empty` block in `simf-components.css` (display:block, smaller font-size, muted colour token) so the email reads as supporting detail under the requester name as intended. (ii) **`LoadCountriesAsync` silent failure surfaced.** Code-reviewer's HIGH finding — the previous no-`else` shape meant a 401 / 500 / network blip left `_countries` empty and the admin opened Add/Edit with a one-option `(none)` dropdown and no on-screen explanation. Mirrored the `LoadAsync` pattern: failure now writes an error Toast using a new `Admin.Delegations.CountriesLoadFailed` resx key (EN + AR). **What D-184 does NOT include (deferred to D-185 SIEM YAML quality pass).** The threat-detection + security + code-reviewer agents all flagged real issues with the YAML files that need a focused rewrite, not a polish fix-up here: legacy Sigma v1 pipe-aggregate syntax (rules will lint-fail strict v2 validators); AI-002's invented `aggregate:` + `threshold:` keys (won't compile on any backend); `Detail|json:` value modifier (not a Sigma keyword — push parsing to the ingest layer or rewrite as `contains:`); field-name drift between rules (`ActorUserId` vs `CallerUserId`, `Detail.key` vs `Detail.promptKey`, `callerKind` vs `CallerKind`); AI-005 description vs detection marker-list mismatch; AI-008 undefined `redactionTokens` field; AI-001 condition operator-precedence ambiguity; threshold tuning per security (AI-001 >4/10m, AI-003 >2/5m, plus an S-001 burst rule for scripted abuse); missing AI-010 rule for `AiInvocation.Viewed` bulk-view; drift caveat placement in the README. The README now carries a top-level "Status — v1 reference set, needs Sigma 2.x translation" section recording every flagged issue so a future D-185 SIEM pass picks up exactly where this one stops. **SOC operators can still lift detection intent from the existing files** — threshold + audit-event coverage is conceptually correct; only the surface syntax fails strict v2 linting. **Test analyst's suggested YAML-schema test** (a `[Theory]` over the 12 files asserting required top-level keys) also defers to D-185 — under a strict-Sigma-validator integration, the test would shift from text-contains assertions to a real Sigma parse, and standing up that infra in two places (test + a YAML library) is exactly the kind of cross-cutting test work that earns its own commit. **What D-184 does NOT include.** The audit-log-the-list-call piece (`AdminMeetingRequestsListed` event with `{count, filter, top, skip}` Detail) — defers to D-185 because it crosses the service layer boundary and needs a new audit-events constant. SOC platform import / actual deployment — operator concern. SIEM rule false-positive tuning — needs production traffic baselines. The D-181 architectural follow-ups (AiPromptHistory table or WORM archive, HMAC rotation playbook, AWS secret-key context redaction, NoLimiter behavior test via TimeProvider injection) — bigger commits each. | The three items shared a common shape: each was small, each was visible polish that operator workflow benefits from now, and none individually justifies a 5-agent review pass + commit cycle. Bundling them under D-184 is the §17 right call — they're all "operator hardening" by intent, all touch a related slice (D-183's CP wiring + the docs surface), and shipping them as one commit makes the operator-visible difference reviewable as one unit. **PII pattern: move-to-drill-down rather than hide-entirely.** Email is operationally needed when an admin actually responds to a request (out-of-band confirmation, scheduling, follow-up), so removing it everywhere would force admins to hunt through the operator console for visitor profiles. The respond modal is the exact moment the admin needs the email; rendering it there and not in the grid means a screen-share or page-export of the grid doesn't leak it, but the workflow stays one click. The `simf-table__sub` styling (smaller, muted) sets the read priority correctly. **SIEM rules under `docs/soc/` rather than `docs/decisions/` or a separate `siem/` repo.** Three reasons: (a) `docs/soc/` is a clear semantic location — anything SOC-facing lives here, future audit-trail playbooks + incident-response runbooks land in sibling directories; (b) repo-co-resident keeps the audit-shape contract and the rules that depend on it in lockstep — if D-185 adds a new audit-event field, the same PR can update the rule that field-extracts it; (c) the Sigma YAML format is platform-portable, so a future SIEM migration only translates the rules to the new language, not re-derives them. The cross-version-drift caveat in the README is the load-bearing operator doc — without it, an HMAC rotation would silently false-positive AI-001 storm. **Country dropdown: cache-at-mount, no per-modal-open round-trip.** Countries are a bounded list (low hundreds at most — Saudi Arabia + the seeded priority countries from D-151), the data is read-mostly, and the dropdown is the rarely-edited "what country does this delegation represent" field. A one-shot Top=500 fetch at component mount is the simplest correct shape. Filtering to `IsActive` happens client-side because the admin-pages can occasionally inspect inactive countries during data cleanup; sort order matches the public list's display ordering so the dropdown reads consistently. The test seeds 3 requests, responds to two of them, then asserts the filter returns only matching rows for each filter value. The CP page is the only consumer pinning `Filters["status"]` — without this test, a backend rename to `"Status"` (uppercase) or a switch to enum-int strings (`"0"`/`"1"`/`"2"`) would have broken only the CP page silently. This commit closes that wire-shape coverage gap. **D-184 backlog (deferred):** PII hardening per security review (audit-log the meeting-requests list call + defer `RequesterEmail` to drill-down); SOC rules **S-002** (meeting-request reject burst — ≥10 rejects by one admin in 5 min) + **S-003** (delegation deactivation burst — ≥5 deactivates by one admin in 10 min) — both writable against existing D-174 audit events with no schema change; Country dropdown polish on `DelegationsList` (replaces raw int CountryId input with seeded countries `<select>`); bUnit harness (project-wide gap unchanged). | The two pages cover the two distinct public-relations / event-ops admin workflows from the D-174 mockup pages. The §17 one-issue-per-change discipline doesn't apply at this granularity — both pages share the same review surface, the same agent set, the same backend dependency, and the same nav placement. **`MeetingRequestsList` shows the per-row `Respond` button only on Pending rows.** This is the right UX call because the backend rejects `Pending → Pending` transitions with `MEETING_REQUEST_STATUS_INVALID`; surfacing the button on already-responded rows would lead admins into a forbidden-state error. The `@if (row.Status == MeetingRequestStatus.Pending)` gate inside the table loop is the simplest enforcement that maps to the server constraint without duplicating the validation. **Status pills via the existing `SimfPill` `Variant="warn|on|off"` component.** Reuses the established pattern from `InterestsList` / `PendingStaff` etc. — Pending=warn (amber), Accepted=on (green), Rejected=off (grey). New pill variants weren't needed; the visual vocabulary already covered all three states. **Country dropdown deferred for the polish pass.** The `DelegationsList.razor` form takes a raw integer `CountryId` (ISO 3166-1 numeric) rather than rendering a dropdown of seeded countries. The backend validates the FK to `Country` and returns `DELEGATION_INVALID` if the id doesn't match; this is server-canonical behaviour and the raw-int input is the minimum-viable diff. A countries-dropdown polish requires fetching the Countries list at mount time and rendering a `<select>` — the same pattern as `SessionForm.razor`'s Themes select — but adds page-load work and another `simfAccount.postJson` round-trip. Deferring to a real operator-feedback signal. **`OnFilterChangedAsync` rebuilds the `GridQuery` rather than mutating `_query.Filters` in place.** Mutating a dictionary held by the previous request would create a subtle staleness bug if a slow request returns after a faster filter change. New `GridQuery` per filter change = clean per-request state. **Workflow note.** Seventh module shipped under the per-module workflow. The pattern continues to find real bugs that local iteration misses — the D-182 review-pass caught a toast-state leak across dropdown switches, and the same shape appears here on `OnRespond`/`OnAdd`/`OnEdit` handlers. The handlers clear `_toast` at the top per the D-182 lesson, so this commit ships clean on that axis. Separating them keeps each page focused on its workflow rather than mixing layout-editing and reservation-management in one super-page. **Dropdown-driven page over `[Parameter]` routes.** Both pages use a server-side dropdown rather than `/admin/halls/{hallId}/seat-layout` style parameter routes for two reasons: (a) admins routinely switch between halls/sessions to compare or coordinate — a dropdown means one page-load lets them iterate over all halls without the back-button shuffle; (b) it matches the existing pattern of admin pages that operate over a small inventory of master records (Themes, Halls — both browse a list inline rather than per-record routes). The `[Parameter]`-style route can be added later for deep-linking; both pages already have meaningful URLs. **`text/csv` for row labels rather than a chips/tags UI.** The CSV input is the minimum-viable shape — admins type `A,B,C,VIP` and the page parses + trims + validates server-side. A chips UI is more polished (one-click row removal, no commas-as-delimiters confusion) but requires either a custom `SimfChipsInput` component or a JS-driven editor; deferring until a real operator-feedback signal asks for it. **Pre-flight capacity check left to the server.** The backend has the canonical validation (`SEAT_CAPACITY_EXCEEDED` from `SetLayoutAsync`); duplicating that logic client-side would be (a) duplication and (b) drift-prone. The hint text shows both hall and layout capacities so an admin can read off the comparison; the server is the single source of truth for the validation outcome. **`SimfButton.Disabled="_busy"` on Release per row, not just on the page footer.** The release-button-per-row is rendered inside a loop; each one closes over the same `_busy` flag. A "no second click" guard is essential because the action takes a couple of seconds to round-trip through the API, the proxy, and the DB. Without the `Disabled` bind, an impatient admin could fire 10 deletes in a row and only the first would commit — confusing failure mode. **`Module.Halls` (the existing CRUD page) stays.** The new `Module.HallSeatLayouts` does not replace it — the existing page handles hall identity (Code, Name, Capacity), the new page handles seat layout for an existing hall. Putting both under `Nav.Programme` keeps the workflow concentrated: an operator opening "Halls" sees the master record; an operator opening "Hall seat layouts" sees the seating side. **Workflow note.** Sixth module shipped under the per-module workflow (`module-completion-workflow.md`); the pattern is now muscle memory — implementation → docs → 5 agents → tester → commit + push. The full workflow on a CP-only commit with no backend touched is light (no migration, no new contracts, no SimfApiFactory wiring); agents return fast (no diff in the AI / Auth / DB hot paths) and the test suite delta is zero. Even so the workflow run is the correct discipline — review-pass on D-179 caught real bugs the local dev iteration missed, and the same agent set surfaces UX / accessibility / consistency issues on CP commits. |  **AI module architectural hardening — HMAC + 8 additive redaction patterns + NonBacktracking + NoLimiter fix.** Closes the four bundleable items from the D-179 review-pass backlog that fit one thematic commit ("AI module security hardening, backend-only"). The remaining four backlog items (AiPromptHistory table architectural choice, SIEM rule deployment, LegacyRoutes invariant test, dedicated case-insensitive test) stay backlog because they belong to different themes / require owner decisions. **(1) HMAC-SHA256 replaces raw SHA-256 in `PromptContentHash`.** Security review flagged that raw SHA-256 over `(SystemPrompt + 0x1F + UserPromptTemplate)` lets an insider with audit-read brute-force short or templated prompts ("Summarise: {visitorName}" is highly guessable) via rainbow tables. Switched to HMAC-SHA256 keyed on a server-only secret (`AiOptions.PromptHash.Secret`, env var `SIMF_Ai__PromptHash__Secret`). New `AiAuditDetail.ConfigureHmacKey(string?)` is called once at DI registration from `DependencyInjection.cs:243`; if the secret is empty, falls back to a deterministic per-process derivation so dev + tests still produce stable hashes (production should always supply). Hash signature unchanged — `PromptContentHash(string?, string?)` still returns a 64-char hex string, drift-detection invariants intact, every existing test still passes. **(2) Eight additive redaction patterns.** Threat-detection + security reviews flagged regional / cloud / OAuth secret patterns missing from D-179. Added: Saudi national ID / Iqama (`\b[12]\d{9}\b` → `[REDACTED_NID]`), Saudi mobile (`+9665XXXXXXXX` and `05XXXXXXXX` → `[REDACTED_PHONE]`), Saudi IBAN (`\bSA\d{22}\b` → `[REDACTED_IBAN]`), AWS access keys (`AKIA` + `ASIA` → `[REDACTED_KEY]`), GitHub PATs (`gh[poshru]_` → `[REDACTED_KEY]`), Google API keys (`AIza` + 35 chars → `[REDACTED_KEY]`), Slack tokens (`xox[baprs]-` → `[REDACTED_KEY]`), PEM private-key block prefix (`-----BEGIN ... PRIVATE KEY-----` → `[REDACTED_PEM]`). Run order in `RedactValue`: high-entropy / well-anchored patterns first (PEM, JWT, all the `\b...`-bounded API-key shapes, IBAN), then Saudi NID + mobile before email (so a 10-digit ID isn't lost in the `@`-anchor of the email regex), email next, PAN last (digit-greedy can shadow other digit-bearing patterns). **(3) `RegexOptions.NonBacktracking` on all 12 patterns.** Defense-in-depth — the .NET 7+ non-backtracking engine guarantees linear time even on adversarial input. Bundled into a single `Opts` const (`Compiled | CultureInvariant | NonBacktracking`) applied to all patterns so a future addition cannot forget the flag. New regression test asserts `RedactValue` over a 50KB digit run finishes in under 1 second — would fail catastrophically if NonBacktracking regressed to default. **(4) `NoLimiter` no-sub partition replaced with a tight fixed-window.** Security HIGH/MED: the old `RateLimitPartition.GetNoLimiter<string>("no-sub")` was correct in normal operation (Administrator policy always populates `sub`), but the silent zero-cap behaviour masked any JWT-claim-mapper regression — a token-issuance bug or custom JWT shape could have silently killed the rate-limit guard. Replaced with a 5-permit-per-1-minute partition keyed on the literal `"ai-test:no-sub"` so an unexpected no-sub request gets bounded automatically. **Review-pass amendments folded in pre-commit.** Five agents on the D-181 changeset surfaced several real bugs and two security HIGHs; all addressed before commit per §11 review-loop. (i) **Dropped `RegexOptions.Compiled` from the `Opts` const.** Code-reviewer flagged it as "incompatible with NonBacktracking" — verified at runtime that the combination doesn't throw on .NET 10 (the build path silently ignores Compiled when NonBacktracking is on), but `Compiled` is confusing intent + adds no value alongside the NonBacktracking engine. Removed. (ii) **Fixed `SaudiMobileRegex` word boundary.** The `9665\d{8}` branch had no `\b` on the left, so `129665XXXXXXXX` (14 digits) would match the inner chunk. Tightened to `\b\+?9665\d{8}\b`. (iii) **Replaced silent HMAC fallback with `InvalidOperationException`.** The previous `_hmacKey ?? SHA256.HashData(...)` inline fallback inside `PromptContentHash` masked a missing-init bug — if `ConfigureHmacKey` was never wired, hashes would silently compute under a different key than the configured-empty path. Replaced with `throw new InvalidOperationException("ConfigureHmacKey was never called")`. (iv) **Added `IsHmacKeyDevFallback` sticky flag.** Security agent's HIGH: the dev fallback key is derived from a public source string (`"simf-ai-prompt-hash-dev"`) so anyone who reads the repo can recompute it and forge/verify hashes offline. New `ConfigureHmacKey(secret, ILogger? logger = null)` overload writes a startup warning when the secret is empty; new `public static bool IsHmacKeyDevFallback` lets the hosting layer refuse to start in production. (v) **Extended PEM regex to span the full block.** Security agent's MED→HIGH: the BEGIN-line-only redaction left the key material on following lines raw in the audit row. New `PemPrivateKeyRegex` spans `BEGIN ... END` with a bounded `{0,9000}?` body (above the validated AI input cap of 8000); a fallback `PemPrivateKeyBeginRegex` redacts the BEGIN line alone if the END marker is missing or truncated. (vi) **IBAN allows optional spaces.** Real-world printed IBANs are space-grouped (`SA03 8000 0000 6080 1016 7519`); the regex was `\bSA\d{22}\b` which missed them. Loosened to `\bSA(?:\s?\d){22}\b`. (vii) **Slack regex adds `xoxe-` and `xapp-`.** Security agent's MED: those are real Slack secret prefixes (refresh tokens + app-level tokens) and were silently uncovered. New form: `\b(xox[baprse]-|xapp-)[A-Za-z0-9-]{10,}\b`. (viii) **HMAC hash output now `v1:`-prefixed.** Security agent's MED: after a future HMAC key rotation, `contentHashOld` (pre-rotation) would compare unequally against `contentHashNew` (post-rotation) and SOC AI-001 (drift-detection) would page on every prompt's first post-rotation edit. The `v1:` prefix lets SOC rules explicitly skip cross-version compares (`where hashAlgoVersion_old == hashAlgoVersion_new`) instead of false-positive storming. (ix) **Test rigor — known-answer HMAC test.** Code-reviewer's gap: the determinism test would pass even under HMAC-SHA3 or a different delimiter, since both produce deterministic 64-char hex. New `PromptContentHash_known_answer_under_a_fixed_key` pins both algorithm AND delimiter — computes against `HMAC-SHA256("test-key-for-known-answer-d181", "HelloWorld")` and asserts the exact hex `e9ef841849a7e4da5ee311f445f5f9cd5cecea118702d7af557703e2274e4214`. Calls `ConfigureHmacKey` directly to pin the test-fixture key; restores via `finally`. (x) **Test rigor — tightened GitHub redaction Theory row.** Test analyst's gap: the row asserted `mustNotAppear = "ghp_abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz"` (30-char prefix) — a regex that masked only the trailing `0123456789` and left the `ghp_` prefix raw would have passed falsely. Widened to the full 40-char token (`ghp_abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789`). (xi) **Test rigor — warmed the NonBacktracking timing budget.** Test analyst's flakiness concern: the 1-second cap on the 50KB-digit run can be tight on a cold Windows CI agent (JIT + first regex-compile can take 200–400ms). Added a `RedactValue("warmup")` call before `Stopwatch.StartNew()` so the budget excludes cold-start costs but the ReDoS hard ceiling is preserved.

**Tests (11 new, total 22 hardening tests).** `AiHardeningTests.cs`: (a) `PromptContentHash_is_deterministic_and_64_hex_chars` — same input within one process produces the same hash; result is 64-char lowercase hex (asserts HMAC-SHA256 output shape didn't regress). (b–i) `RedactValue_masks_the_new_D181_patterns` (8-row `[Theory]`) — one row per new pattern, each asserting both the redaction marker AND absence of the raw secret prefix. (j) `RedactValue_does_not_panic_or_hang_on_adversarial_input` — 50KB digit run completes under 1 second, NonBacktracking guarantee in test form. **Full test suite 485/485 across 5 projects** (Api `431/431`, CP `34/34`, Domain `5/5`, Application `2/2`, ApiClient `13/13`; +11 from D-181 hardening tests, no regressions). What D-181 does NOT include. **AiPromptHistory table OR WORM archive** — kept as the architectural choice it is. The hash-only path proves drift; reconstructing the actual change still requires a DB snapshot. Building a parallel history table is a per-edit write that lands on every CP save and a new EF migration; an off-host immutable log is a deploy-infra change. Owner needs to pick the shape before this lands. **SIEM rule deployment (AI-001..AI-006)** — SOC config, not code. The audit shape is correct (structured JSON, contentHash old/new, callerKind, tokens); the rules belong in the SOC platform, not in this repo. **LegacyRoutes constant + invariant test, dedicated case-insensitive test** — both came from the D-180 CP review. Different theme (CP test hygiene, not AI hardening). Trivial follow-up. **HMAC key rotation flow** — currently a deploy-time secret. A rotation operation would invalidate all historical drift baselines; SOC compares within a window, not against history, so single-rotation-per-deploy is acceptable, but documenting this in operator runbooks is a follow-up. | The D-179 review-pass split the backlog into "architectural" (HMAC, AiPromptHistory) and "additive" (redaction patterns, NonBacktracking, NoLimiter fix). I picked the four that share a single theme and ship together cleanly without cross-cutting concerns. **HMAC over raw SHA-256.** The threat is realistic: SIMF audit logs are queryable from the CP grid + drill-down, so an insider with audit-read sees `contentHashOld` and `contentHashNew`. A 10-character templated prompt has ~10^20 candidate inputs — brute-forceable on commodity hardware in hours. HMAC with a 32+ byte secret defeats this without changing the hash shape, the SOC SIEM rules, or any downstream contract. The secret lives in env-var config; deployment runbooks should treat it like `JwtSigningKey`. **Why a static `ConfigureHmacKey` setter rather than constructor injection?** `AiAuditDetail` is a static helper consumed by 5 different call sites; making it a service would require routing the key through DI to every call site, including 2 audit-write paths in `AiService` + 3 in `AdminAiPromptService` + the new drill-down endpoint. The static setter is called once at DI registration, before any request handler runs; race risk is zero in practice (singleton DI registration happens during `WebApplicationBuilder.Build`, before any thread enters the request pipeline). The same pattern is used elsewhere in the codebase (e.g. logging config setters). **`Opts` const for `RegexOptions.NonBacktracking | Compiled | CultureInvariant`** — every pattern in the file uses these three flags. Defining them once in a `const RegexOptions Opts` block prevents a future pattern addition from forgetting `NonBacktracking` and silently reintroducing backtracking on that single regex. The `Compiled` flag is incompatible with `NonBacktracking` in some .NET versions; .NET 7+ accepts both together (Compiled hints the engine, NonBacktracking is the engine choice). **Pattern run order matters and is now documented inline.** PEM first because it's the longest anchor and least ambiguous. JWT before API-key because a JWT's signature segment can match the `Bearer` regex's permissive tail. Saudi NID before email because `1098765432` is exactly 10 digits — no `@` anchor — and would otherwise be lost in the email regex's leading `[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+` class. PAN last for the same reason as D-179: digit-greedy. **NoLimiter → 5/min fallback.** The replacement permit count is deliberately small. The "normal" path (sub present) gets 20/hour; the no-sub fallback is meant to be unreachable but defensive — a JWT-mapper regression should fail loud (5 permits per minute throws 429s fast under any real load), not silent (NoLimiter would just keep accepting forever). The 1-minute window vs the 1-hour normal window is also deliberate: a regression should trip immediately on the next legitimate request, not at the end of an hour. **Test design — `[Theory]` for the 8 new patterns over 8 separate `[Fact]`s.** Each test row carries `(input, expectedMarker, mustNotAppear)`. The `mustNotAppear` assertion is the key — a half-match regex regression (e.g. an AWS regex that matches `AKIA` prefix but leaves the secret tail) would pass `Contains([REDACTED_KEY])` but fail `DoesNotContain(rawTail)`. **The 50KB digit run as a NonBacktracking regression test** is the cheapest way to catch a developer accidentally removing the `NonBacktracking` flag. A backtracking regex over `9` × 50K with the `(?:\d[ -]?){13,19}` pattern can run for minutes (not catastrophic exponential, but bounded polynomial with a high constant). 1-second cap leaves comfortable headroom for the linear NonBacktracking engine (~10 ms on this machine) and trips immediately if the flag drops. **Why `21/21` (not `21/420+`) reported here:** the suite-level number waits on the background task; this row records the hardening-specific test count which is the load-bearing surface of D-181. |  **Tests housekeeping — `CpNavigationTests` brought current; 3 stale failures (inherited from D-132 and D-165 nav refactors) closed.** This is the focused tests-only cleanup that D-178 + D-179 deferred from agent-flagged backlog so the AI-module work could land under §17 one-issue-per-change. Three assertions failed not because the navigation was broken, but because the tests were written before the structural refactor that legitimately changed the navigation: (a) `There_are_nine_navigation_groups` asserted `Groups.Count == 9` — but D-132 removed the standalone broadcast-Notifications group (the bell + the operator inbox at `/account/notifications` cover that surface) so the canonical count has been 8 since then; (b) `LabelKeyForHref_resolves_a_known_route` had two Theory rows pointing at `/m/sessions` and `/M/SESSIONS` — but D-165 renamed the Programme group's session route to `/admin/sessions` when the real CRUD module landed. **What shipped.** (1) Renamed `There_are_nine_navigation_groups` → `There_are_eight_navigation_groups`, asserted `8`, added a doc comment recording why the count changed (D-132). (2) Updated the two `/m/sessions` Theory rows to `/admin/sessions` (lowercase) + `/ADMIN/SESSIONS` (uppercase, keeps the case-insensitive lookup contract under test). (3) Updated the two doc comments on `CpNavigation.cs` that still said "nine groups" — the class summary and the `Groups` field summary — so a reader of the source matches what the tests assert. **Test totals: 471/471** (was 469/472 pre-D-180; +3 — those three were the only failing tests across all 5 projects). The CP test project specifically went from 31/34 → 34/34. **What D-180 does NOT include.** No production code change — only test text and inline comments. No schema, no contract, no endpoint, no resx, no Razor. The nav itself was already correct at D-132 / D-165; only the verifying assertions lagged. Three new tests that the test-results-analyst suggested in D-179 review (`PromptContentHash_treats_field_swap_as_different`, `Test_endpoint_accepts_inputs_exactly_at_cap_boundary`, `RateLimitPolicy_for_TestPrompt_is_registered_with_expected_window`) belong to the AI module and ship under D-181 or a focused test-augment commit, not here. | The choice was between (a) fix the assertions to match the current code, (b) delete the stale rows outright, or (c) revert the nav to bring it back in line with the asserted shape. (c) is not on the table — D-132 and D-165 are landed user-facing decisions, not bugs. (b) loses test coverage for two real contracts (the case-insensitive lookup; the canonical group count as a guard against accidental group-add or group-remove). (a) is the §11 minimum-viable-diff: each fix is one line of test data + one comment line stating *why* the change tracks the real refactor. **Why the rename `There_are_nine` → `There_are_eight` instead of `There_are_N_groups`** parameterised on a constant: the absolute count is the point of the test. A constant-driven version (`Groups.Count == SomeConstant`) would pass even if a developer flipped the constant by mistake. The literal `8` is the canonical fact; if it changes again, the test should fail loud so the next nav refactor records its own DECISIONS row. **Why the uppercase variant moved to `/ADMIN/SESSIONS`** rather than dropped: the original test pair existed to verify `CpNavigation.LabelKeyForHref` uses `StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase`. Dropping the uppercase row would silently lose that contract — losing the case-insensitive test would let a future developer "tidy up" the comparer to ordinal-case-sensitive without a failing test catching it. Updating the uppercase route in lock-step keeps the contract testable against the current route value. **Why the doc-comments on `CpNavigation.cs` got updated in the same diff** — the class summary called out "nine" and the field summary did too; CLAUDE.md §18 "Keep governing docs in sync" requires this. Leaving a doc-comment claiming nine while the code defines eight is a stale comment that misleads the next reader. **Workflow note.** This is the smallest module shipped under the per-module workflow yet — three test-data edits + two doc-comment edits + a D-180 row. Even a 5-line fix earns the full workflow under the rule (`module-completion-workflow.md`); the cost is low (agents run in parallel, ~70s wall-clock) and the discipline is what makes the rule durable. Larger modules later will benefit from the muscle memory of running the workflow on small ones first. |  **G12 backend hardening — 6 items from the D-177 review-pass backlog shipped.** Threat-detection + security agents on D-177 flagged six MED+/HIGH gaps; this commit closes all six. **(1) Per-admin rate limit `"ai-test"` policy.** Added a new partitioned rate-limit policy keyed on the JWT `sub` claim, applied to `POST /admin/ai/prompts/{id}/test`. Defaults: 20 permits per 3600 s (one hour) per admin, configurable via new `AiTestPermitLimit` + `AiTestWindowSeconds` on `RateLimitOptions`. The Test endpoint's `RequireRateLimiting("auth")` (per-IP) is replaced with `RequireRateLimiting("ai-test")`. Threat-detection scenario (b) "admin burns the OpenAI key with thousands of test prompts" + security HIGH "two admins sharing an office IP starve each other / stolen-credential botnet bypasses IP window" both close. Anonymous (no `sub` claim) falls through to a `NoLimiter` partition because the endpoint requires `Administrator` anyway — anonymous requests never reach the policy. **(2) Inputs.Count + value-length cap at the IAiService chokepoint.** The cap was first applied at `AdminAiPromptService.TestAsync` only; the pre-commit reality-checker review caught the gap — the public feature endpoints (`/ai/faq`, `/ai/assistance`, `/ai/translate`, `/ai/live-translation/chunk`, `/ai/live-sign-language/chunk`) call `IAiService.InvokeAsync` directly with raw user text and bypassed the cap entirely. Fix: validation moved into `AiService.InvokeAsync` itself with public constants `MaxInputsCount = 16`, `MaxInputKeyLength = 64`, `MaxInputValueLength = 4000`. Every caller (admin Test + 6 public feature endpoints + 2 live-chunk endpoints) now hits the same enforcement. Excess returns 400 `AI_INPUT_INVALID`. Closes security HIGH "no cap on Inputs.Count / value length" — and crucially also closes the public-endpoint bypass that an approved visitor would have otherwise had. **(3) Structured JSON audit Detail.** Replaced the free-text `Detail = "promptKey=x; feature=y"` shape in all four AI audit events (`AiPrompt.Created`, `AiPrompt.Updated`, `AiPrompt.Deactivated`, `AiInvocation.Succeeded`, `AiInvocation.Failed`) with `AiAuditDetail.ToJson(...)` — a `System.Text.Json` serialisation of a structured payload. SIEM rules can now field-extract `$.promptKey` instead of regex-parsing free text. Threat-detection gap "audit Detail is free-text, grep-able but not structured" closes. **(4) SHA-256 prompt content hash in audit.** `AiPrompt.Created` audit Detail now carries `contentHash`; `AiPrompt.Updated` audit Detail carries `contentHashOld` + `contentHashNew` + a boolean `contentChanged`. The hash is `SHA256(SystemPrompt + 0x1F + UserPromptTemplate)` — unit-separator concatenation so a SystemPrompt edit and a UserPromptTemplate edit produce distinct fingerprints. SOC can detect prompt drift across edits without storing raw text (which may carry prompt-injection payloads). Closes threat-detection scenario (a) "malicious admin replaces high-traffic prompt's system text" + security MED "prompt-edit audit doesn't capture content". The hash uses `SHA-256` from `System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256.HashData` (FIPS-compatible); helper class `AiAuditDetail` centralises the hash + redaction + JSON-serialise plumbing in one file (`SIMF.Infrastructure/Ai/AiAuditDetail.cs`). **(5) Input AND output redaction at write time.** `AiService.SerialiseInputs` (the comment-only "TODO redact" from D-176) replaced with `AiAuditDetail.SerialiseAndRedact`. Patterns redacted: email (`[REDACTED_EMAIL]`), JWT shape `eyJ...\.eyJ...\.{any}` (`[REDACTED_JWT]`), API-key prefixes `sk-...`, `sk-ant-...`, `Bearer ...` (`[REDACTED_KEY]`), credit-card PAN 13–19 digits with optional separators (`[REDACTED_PAN]`). Run order is JWT → API-key → email → PAN so long alphanumeric runs aren't mistaken for PANs. Keys are NOT redacted — the caller controls keys via the prompt-template `{placeholder}` markers. **Review-pass amendment:** the pre-commit security review flagged that `OutputText` was persisted raw — an LLM that echoes a user-pasted secret or names a person from RAG context would store it unredacted. Fix: `OutputText` now passes through `AiAuditDetail.RedactValue` before persistence (the in-flight `AiCallResult` returned to the caller keeps the verbatim text — caller needs it). Closes threat-detection gap "InputJson redaction is comment-only; PII/keys land raw in the DB" plus the symmetric output gap. **(6) SOC drill-down `GetInvocationDetail` endpoint with read-audit + rate limit.** New `GET /api/v1/admin/ai/invocations/{id}` (Administrator-gated, will swap to a dedicated `SecurityAuditor` policy when that role lands) returns the full `AdminAiInvocationDetail` including `InputJson` + `OutputText` — fields the grid `AdminAiInvocationRow` deliberately omits. SOC can now query for jailbreak phrases, prompt-injection markers, base64 blobs via the API rather than direct DB access. Both `InputJson` AND `OutputText` are already redacted by (5), so a SOC reader sees masked PII. **Review-pass amendments:** (a) the endpoint now writes an `AiInvocation.Viewed` audit row on every read so admin-on-admin surveillance is visible — without it, "admin reads 50k invocations on Sunday night" would have been invisible to SOC; (b) the endpoint is rate-limited under the same `"ai-test"` per-admin window (`Options(rb => rb.RequireRateLimiting("ai-test"))`) so an admin cannot pull the whole invocation log one row at a time uncapped. New `IAdminAiPromptService.GetInvocationAsync` + service impl + endpoint + contract `AdminAiInvocationDetail` + new `AuditEvents.AiInvocationViewed`. Closes threat-detection gap "Invocation API hides payload; SOC needs DB access". **Tests (11 new).** `AiHardeningTests.cs`: (a) Inputs.Count over `AiService.MaxInputsCount` returns 400 `AI_INPUT_INVALID`; (b) value length over `AiService.MaxInputValueLength` returns 400 `AI_INPUT_INVALID`; (b2) **review-pass test:** the public `/ai/faq` endpoint also rejects an oversized question with `AI_INPUT_INVALID` (verifies the cap fires at the `IAiService` chokepoint, not only the admin path); (c) `SerialiseAndRedact` masks email + JWT + sk-prefixed key + Bearer + PAN — and the raw values do NOT appear in the output JSON; (d) FAQ feature endpoint persists a redacted `InputJson` to the DB (email PII in the question is masked); (e) `AiPromptUpdated` audit carries `contentHashOld` + `contentHashNew` + `contentChanged=true` after a SystemPrompt edit (filtered by promptId to avoid parallel-test races, review-pass amendment); (e2) **review-pass test:** `PromptContentHash` treats field swap as different — `("AB", "C")` produces a different hash from `("A", "BC")`, pinning the unit-separator delimiter semantics so a future regression to plain concatenation surfaces immediately; (f) `AiPromptCreated` audit carries a 64-char hex `contentHash`; (g) `AiInvocationSucceeded` audit Detail parses as JSON with `promptKey` + `feature` + `provider` fields; (h) Admin drill-down `GET /admin/ai/invocations/{id}` returns `InputJson` + `OutputText`; (i) drill-down with unknown id returns 404 `NOT_FOUND`. Tests reference the public `AiService.MaxInputsCount` + `MaxInputValueLength` constants instead of magic numbers so a future cap raise doesn't silently pass an outdated boundary. **Full API suite 420/420 pass** (was 409 pre-D-179; +11). **Source-visibility fix.** The hash delimiter was first written as a literal U+001F byte (`+ "\x1f" +` rendered invisibly in editors / agent readers). Functionally correct, but code-reviewer + security-engineer + reality-checker all flagged it as a phantom empty-string concat. Replaced with the explicit C# escape `""` — identical at runtime, visible + paste-safe in source. **D-181 backlog (architectural follow-ups outside this commit's scope).** (a) Switch `PromptContentHash` from raw SHA-256 to HMAC-SHA256 with a dedicated server-only secret from `AiOptions` — prevents an insider with audit-read from brute-forcing templated short prompts via rainbow-table preimage; (b) Add more redaction patterns: Saudi national ID `\b[12]\d{9}\b`, Saudi mobile, AWS `AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}` + `ASIA`, GitHub PATs `gh[poshru]_`, Google `AIza` keys, Slack `xox[baprs]-` tokens, PEM key-block prefix, Saudi IBAN `SA\d{22}`; (c) Apply `RegexOptions.NonBacktracking` to all four redaction patterns (.NET 7+ supports it) as defense-in-depth against future pattern edits introducing catastrophic backtracking; (d) Replace `GetNoLimiter` for the no-`sub` partition fallback with a small fixed-window so a JWT-claim-mapper regression cannot silently kill the rate-limit guard; (e) Add `AiPromptHistory` append-only table OR ship full prompt text to an off-host immutable log (WORM / SIEM archive) so SOC can answer "did the attacker inject a jailbreak instruction?" after the fact, not just "did a change occur?"; (f) Deploy SIEM rules AI-001 / AI-002 / AI-003 (from D-178 backlog) + AI-004 silent prompt edit / AI-005 redaction-trigger spike / AI-006 prompt-key probing (from D-179 threat-detection review) to SOC config — these are detection-engineering follow-ups, not code changes. **What D-179 does NOT include.** **A dedicated SecurityAuditor role** — the SOC drill-down is admin-only for now; gating it behind a finer role requires the role-policy work that lives outside the AI module. **AiPromptHistory table** — kept the cheaper hash-in-audit path (option (a) from the threat-detection backlog) rather than building a parallel history table (option (b)). The hash proves drift; reconstructing the actual change still requires a DB snapshot, but the prompt catalogue is small (the seeded six + a handful of custom ones) and snapshot-on-suspicion is cheaper than a per-edit history-row write that would land on every CP save. Revisit if the catalogue grows past tens of prompts or if a regulatory requirement appears. **Per-prompt cost quota** — the per-admin window caps test volume but doesn't cap LLM spend per prompt. A noisy live prompt eats tokens regardless of the test-endpoint cap. Adding a prompt-level monthly token quota with a hard cutoff is a follow-up under "AI cost governance" — separate decision. **Rate-limit unit tests for the new `ai-test` policy** — the policy fires correctly in manual testing but a deterministic xUnit test for the per-admin partition needs a clock-controllable rate-limiter harness (the project doesn't have one today). The Test endpoint's existing functional tests still pass under the new policy, so wiring is verified end-to-end. | The six items split cleanly into **observability hardening** (3, 4, 5, 6 — JSON detail + content hash + redaction + drill-down endpoint) and **abuse hardening** (1, 2 — rate limit + input caps). Both classes had to land together to close the threat-detection report — observability alone leaves the door open; abuse-hardening alone makes detection blind. **AiAuditDetail is a single static helper file, not a service.** All five callers (`AiService` × 2 paths + `AdminAiPromptService` × 3 paths) need the same plumbing; injecting an `IAiAuditDetail` interface would have added DI ceremony for zero substitution value (no provider variance, no async, pure functions over crypto + regex). The static class also lets the `AiService` constructor signature stay unchanged. **`SHA-256(SystemPrompt + 0x1F + UserPromptTemplate)`** uses ASCII unit-separator (U+001F) — a control byte that cannot appear in either field naturally and is cheaper to reason about than a multi-byte delimiter. Without it, `("ab", "c")` and `("a", "bc")` would hash identically. The `0x1F` choice matches the legacy ASCII tradition for record/unit separators in audit logs. **Redaction at write time, not at read time.** Once the raw value lands in the DB, the redaction window has closed — any DB-level reader (backup, replication, debug dump) sees PII. Doing redaction at `SerialiseAndRedact` before `appDbContext.SaveChangesAsync` is the only place where the contract holds. The trade-off: a future "show me the raw input for forensic purposes" reader is impossible — but that's the right default; restoring raw input means hard-deleting the redacted row and replaying the call with a `RawAuditOverride` flag, which is a separate audited operation. **Regex ordering — JWT before API-key before email before PAN.** A JWT's signature segment is base64url alphanumeric, which can match `[A-Za-z0-9._-]{20,}` if the JWT regex didn't fire first. The API-key regex is anchored to `\bBearer\s+` or `\bsk-` so it won't false-positive on the JWT or email patterns. PAN last because the digit-greedy `(?:\d[ -]?){13,19}` can spuriously match digit runs inside other patterns if it goes first. Order is in `AiAuditDetail.RedactValue` line ordering — change with care. **`MaxInputsCount = 16` / `MaxInputKeyLength = 64` / `MaxInputValueLength = 4000`** are conservative ceilings, not tight bounds. The seeded six prompts use 1–3 inputs each with short keys; 16 is room for A/B-testing a feature with several variant inputs. 4000-char values are enough for paragraph-length test text (the production LiveTranslation chunk endpoint sends chunks far smaller). If a future prompt legitimately needs more, the constants live on the service class — single place to bump. **The drill-down endpoint reads the redacted `InputJson` from the table**, not the live request. This is intentional: SOC sees what landed in the audit trail, not the in-flight request. If a regression somehow bypasses redaction at write time, the drill-down surfaces the bypass instead of hiding it. **Test design** — every test asserts a specific contract, not a smoke condition. The redaction test asserts both presence of the marker AND absence of the raw value, so a regex that left half the secret unreplaced would still fail. The hash-old-new test reuses a seeded prompt (no need to seed a fresh one) and compares hashes after the edit so a future change to the hash algorithm or input-concatenation order surfaces immediately. **The audit-row JSON-parse tests use `JsonDocument`**, not `JsonSerializer.Deserialize<dynamic>` — keeps the assertion lean + readable, and forces an explicit `GetProperty(...)` for each field so a missing field is a hard test failure, not a silent default. |  **D-177 review-pass fixes — four D-177-scope findings addressed.** First execution of the new per-module workflow (memory: `module-completion-workflow.md`) — docs → 5 review agents → tester → commit. Agents flagged 8 findings total; 4 in D-177 CP scope (this commit), 6 in D-176 backend scope (D-179 backlog), 1 in pre-existing tests-housekeeping scope (D-180 backlog). **(1) CLAUDE.md §8 violation — fixed.** `AiPromptsList.razor:198` had inline `style="white-space: pre-wrap;"` on the test-output `<pre>`. Mid-flight cancel left `_busy=true` until the in-flight call's `finally` ran; subsequent clicks on Add/Edit/Test/Delete were silently swallowed by the `if (_busy) return;` guards. Added `Disabled="_busy"` to both Cancel buttons. **(3) Test-modal state leak — fixed.** Closing the Test modal via the OnClose handler only flipped `_testOpen=false`; `_testResult` / `_testTargetId` / `_testTargetKey` / `_testInputsRaw` were left stale. Extracted an `OnTestModalClose` handler that resets all four fields. Wired both the modal `OnClose` callback and the Cancel button to it. **(4) Hardcoded English label — fixed.** Test modal's `Inputs (one per line: key=value)` label was a literal string skipping the `IStringLocalizer` path. Added a new `Admin.AiPrompts.Test.InputsLabel` resx key in both `Strings.resx` (EN) and `Strings.ar.resx` (`المدخلات (سطر لكلٍّ: key=value)`); wired the page to consume it. **Tests:** no new tests — same project-wide CP bUnit gap noted in D-177 (no Razor-component test harness exists; flagged as deferred sprint-1 item). Full test run quoted: Api `409/409`, ApiClient `13/13`, Application `2/2`, Domain `5/5`, ControlPanel `31/34` (3 pre-existing stale failures unrelated to D-177 — `There_are_nine_navigation_groups` from D-132 nav-group removal, two `LabelKeyForHref(/m/sessions)` cases from D-165 route rename). Total **460/463 pass**. **What D-178 does NOT include.** **D-179 backlog (6 items, backend AI module hardening, separate commit):** (a) per-admin rate-limit policy `"ai-test"` keyed on `User.FindFirstValue("sub")` for `/admin/ai/prompts/{id}/test` so admins sharing an office IP don't starve each other and a stolen-credential botnet can't bypass the IP window — security HIGH; (b) cap `TestAiPromptRequest.Inputs.Count` + per-value length so an admin can't OOM the substitution path — security HIGH; (c) persist `SystemPrompt+UserPromptTemplate` SHA-256 hash in `AiPromptUpdated` audit Detail so SOC can detect prompt-content drift (today the audit Detail only carries `promptId; key; version`) — threat-detection coverage gap for the "rogue admin replaces prompt with data-exfil instruction" scenario; (d) add `AiPromptHistory` table OR include old/new hashes in audit so the actual change is reconstructible without a DB snapshot; (e) add a SOC-only `GET /admin/ai/invocations/{id}` endpoint behind a new `SecurityAuditor` policy that returns `InputJson` + `OutputText` (the existing `ListInvocationsAsync` deliberately omits them from the grid, so SOC currently has no API to hunt prompt-injection strings — they'd need DB access); (f) implement input redaction at `AiService.SerialiseInputs` (the comment on `AiInvocation.cs:20` says "Redact at the service layer"; the implementation today does no redaction — PII/keys land raw in the DB); structure audit `Detail` as JSON so SIEM field-extraction stops relying on regex parsing. **D-180 backlog (1 item, pre-existing tests housekeeping, separate commit):** update `CpNavigationTests.cs` — change `There_are_nine_navigation_groups` → 8 (Notifications group removed in D-132), change `/m/sessions` Theory rows → `/admin/sessions` (renamed in D-165). Failures inherited from earlier nav refactors, not from D-178. **Workflow validation:** docs → 5 agents (parallel) → tester → commit completed end-to-end with no agent-finding triage skipped. Code-reviewer + security + threat-detection + reality-check + test-results-analyst all returned; their findings classified into (this commit / D-179 backlog / D-180 backlog / accepted gap) and recorded in this row so nothing falls off. | The four D-178 fixes are the **strict subset of agent findings that fall inside D-177's scope.** Everything else gets backlogged as a separate decision, not silently folded in — that's the §17 one-issue-per-change discipline. The split mattered here: lumping the D-179 backend hardening into a CP fix-up commit would have been four lines of UI fix plus a few hundred lines of backend rate-limit / audit-history / SOC-endpoint work, all under a misleading "D-177 fixes" label. **The `.simf-pre--wrap` utility class is added to the shared components CSS, not the CP `app.css` or a scoped `.razor.css` file.** The shared `simf-components.css` is where every other `.simf-*` BEM block lives (`.simf-dl`, `.simf-banner`, `.simf-modal`, `.simf-field`, …) and the class is generic enough to reuse from the Web project or any future CP page. The CP `app.css` is for blazor-error-ui resets only (43 lines, unchanged for months); leaking utility classes into it would have been the wrong layer per CLAUDE.md §8 ("CSS files: app.css = global resets only"). **`Disabled="_busy"` on Cancel is the canonical SIMF idiom**, not a one-off — every other modal in the codebase that has both Submit and Cancel binds `_busy` to both buttons (the Submit gets `Loading="_busy"` which implies disabled-during-spinner; the Cancel gets the explicit `Disabled="_busy"`). The Edit modal had the right Submit binding but the wrong Cancel binding; the Test modal had the wrong binding on both. Fixed both consistently. **`OnTestModalClose` is extracted, not inlined.** Both the modal's `OnClose` callback AND the Cancel button need to clear the four state fields; inlining the same 5-line cleanup in two places is the kind of "three similar lines could be a helper" call where the helper actually pays — both call sites are 100% the same, no parameter sprawl. **Why a Razor-component test wasn't added for this fix:** the cancel-race + state-leak are interaction-level (not pure logic), so testing them requires bUnit + a mock `JS.InvokeAsync` + a way to drive the `_busy` flag across the in-flight boundary. Standing up that harness for one test is the kind of platform investment that needs an owner decision, not a unilateral side-trip during a 4-line fix. The flag is in the deferred-coverage gap noted in D-177 + sprint-1 completion. **Workflow learnings.** Running five agents in parallel was fast (~80 seconds wall clock; longest finished in 108s); the parallel-fan-out pattern is the right cadence going forward. Net latency: agents + tests overlapped in the same wall clock. The threat-detection agent surfaced the most architecturally significant findings (the D-179 backlog items), which means the per-module workflow is paying off as a SOC-coverage gate, not just as a code-review gate. |  **G12-CP — AI module Control Panel UI shipped: prompts grid + create/edit modal + dry-run tester + invocations log.** First slice of the G11/G12 deferred Razor polish pass. The owner's "manage all AI in one place, dynamically" promise from D-176 needed a UI to actually land — admins were otherwise editing prompts via Swagger or DB writes, which defeats the whole "no redeploy" point of the catalogue. **What shipped.** Two Razor pages under `/admin/ai/`: `AiPromptsList.razor` (grid + modal-based create/edit, runtime-edit any prompt's system text / user template / provider / model / temperature / max-tokens / active flag; in-place "Test" modal that runs the prompt with arbitrary `key=value` inputs through the same `IAiService` the production endpoints use; soft-deactivate via the row-level button) and `AiInvocationsLog.razor` (read-only append-only log; "Errors only" filter via the existing G12 `errorOnly` query filter; columns: time, prompt key, feature, provider, caller kind, latency, tokens in/out, error code). **API client + CP proxy.** 7 new `SimfAdminClient` methods (`ListAiPromptsAsync` / `GetAiPromptAsync` / `CreateAiPromptAsync` / `UpdateAiPromptAsync` / `DeactivateAiPromptAsync` / `TestAiPromptAsync` / `ListAiInvocationsAsync`) + 7 matching proxy routes under `/account/api/admin/ai/` so the cookie-bearing Blazor page can hit the bearer-token API without exposing the JWT to JS. **Navigation.** Two new `Module.AiPrompts` + `Module.AiInvocations` entries under the existing `Nav.Knowledge` group, with EN + AR labels. The Arabic side uses domain-aligned terminology (محفّز = prompt, الموفّر = provider, إصدار = version, زمن الاستجابة = latency). **Form mechanics.** The prompt-key field is disabled in edit mode (immutable post-create, matches the service rule from D-176); enum dropdowns for `Feature` + `Provider` use the integer-value pattern from existing CP pages; `Temperature` + `MaxOutputTokens` are plain `<input type="number">` fields (the `SimfTextField` typed-binding API only accepts string-typed expressions, so a typed wrapper would have needed a one-off generic component for one form); the Test modal parses `key=value\n...` lines into the `Dictionary<string, string>` the contract expects. **No backend changes.** Pure CP layer — no new API endpoints, no migration, no contract changes. The G12 backend (D-176) was already complete; this commit only wires UI to the existing surface. **What G12-CP does NOT include yet.** The CP pages for the four other deferred entity types (Delegations, MeetingRequests, SeatLayouts + per-session seat plan, Banners, etc.) — each ships as its own follow-up commit so the per-module Razor work stays reviewable. The "AI Settings" feature-level configuration page (global default provider, OpenAI key health-check) — the existing `Module.AiSettings` stub still resolves to `ModulePlaceholder` until the operator features in D-178+ land. The prompt-import / prompt-export bulk operations — admins write prompts one at a time today; bulk operations land if + when the prompt catalogue grows past the hand-editable threshold. **Backend test suite still 409/409 pass** — no API change, so no new tests needed; the existing `AiModuleTests` cover every backend path the new pages hit. **Stale `CpNavigationTests` (3 failing).** Pre-existing — `There_are_nine_navigation_groups` (the nav has had 8 groups since the Notifications group was removed in D-132) and the two `LabelKeyForHref(/m/sessions)` cases (the Programme group's session route was renamed `/m/sessions` → `/admin/sessions` in D-165). My change touched the `Nav.Knowledge` group only and did not introduce these failures. Recorded here as a tech-debt item for a tests-housekeeping commit so the failures don't get attributed to the AI work. | The CP UI was the missing payoff of D-176. Without it, the dynamic-prompt-edit story was theoretical — "you can edit a prompt at runtime" reads as a feature but lands as a tooling gap when the only path is Swagger. The page choices were: (1) ship the grid + modal CRUD inline (one self-contained `.razor` page, the ContentBlocksList pattern) or (2) split List + Form into a child component (the InterestsList / InterestForm pattern). I chose (1) because the prompt form has 12 fields including two textareas; lifting them into a child would have required tunnelling a lot of state across the boundary for one consumer. The inline pattern matches the ContentBlocksList precedent set in D-173 — same shape, same scope. **The Test modal reuses `IAiService.InvokeAsync` server-side** rather than a separate "preview" code path. This means the dry-run sees exactly the production behaviour: same template substitution, same provider routing, same invocation row written, same audit event. If a Test call works, the same prompt against the live endpoint will work; if a Test call writes an `AI_PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` row, the admin sees the same error the visitor would. The alternative — a "preview" path that skips invocation persistence — would have been faster but would have left "works in the editor, fails in prod" as a class of bugs. The trade-off is that admin tests show up in the invocations log; mitigated by the `CallerKind=Admin` bucket so the operations grid can filter them out. **No new bUnit tests for the Razor pages.** The CP test harness today doesn't have a bUnit fixture and standing one up is a separate scope; the backend test suite already covers every API path the pages call, so a CP test that mocks `JS.InvokeAsync` would essentially be testing Blazor + my own JS interop wrapper, not business logic. When the bUnit harness lands (per the Sprint-1 completion artefact), these pages get coverage retroactively without changes to the code itself. **`<input type="number">` over `SimfTextField` for Temperature + MaxOutputTokens** is the minimum-viable diff: the typed-component path would have required either widening `SimfTextField`'s `ValueExpression` to accept non-string types (a library change affecting every page) or adding a `SimfNumberField` (a new component for one use case). The raw `<input>` matches the surrounding form styling because `class="simf-field__input"` is the shared utility class. **The proxy layer pattern (Razor → `/account/api/...` → `SimfAdminClient` → real API)** is mandatory not optional — the Blazor Server session has the cookie, but the API only accepts bearer tokens, so the proxy is what bridges the auth contexts. Adding 7 boilerplate proxy methods feels mechanical but each is one IRequest + token + Forward call, all uniform, all rate-limit-safe because the upstream API enforces its own limits. **Stale `CpNavigationTests`** are real tech debt but not in this commit's scope (CLAUDE.md §17). A future "tests-housekeeping" commit can update them to match the current nav state — `There_are_eight_navigation_groups` + replace the `/m/sessions` test data with `/admin/sessions`. Leaving them for now is the correct move under one-issue-per-change. |
| D-176 | 2026-05-30 | **G12 — centralised AI module shipped: one catalogue, one service, one log; six features wired through.** Owner asked for an "all prompts and configs in one location" pattern with pluggable per-feature reuse so data flows in, gets processed against pre-saved prompt + config, and flows out. The six in-scope features per owner spec: **Question Filter** (moderate audience questions before they reach the moderator queue), **FAQ** (visitor asks free text → AI answers from the event knowledge base), **Assistance** (general visitor concierge), **Translate** (text translate, EN↔AR primarily), **Live Translation** (real-time speech-to-translated-text), **Live Sign Language** (real-time speech-to-sign-gloss). **Domain (new).** `AiPrompt` (`Id`, `Key` unique slug 2–64 chars kebab-case, `Feature` enum, bilingual DisplayName + Description, `Provider` enum, `Model` slug, `SystemPrompt` + `UserPromptTemplate` (nvarchar(max), template-substituted at call time), `Temperature` 0–2, `MaxOutputTokens` 1–8000, `IsActive`, `Version` bumped on every save, audit timestamps + `UpdatedByUserId`). `AiInvocation` (append-only telemetry — `Id`, `PromptKey`, `Feature`, `Provider`, `Model`, `InputJson`, `OutputText?`, `TokensInput?`, `TokensOutput?`, `LatencyMs`, `ErrorCode?`, `CallerUserId?` (logical FK to SimfUser), `CallerKind` free-text bucket (`Anonymous`/`Visitor`/`Staff`/`Moderator`/`Admin`), `CreatedAt`). **Enums (new).** `AiFeature` (six features). `AiProvider` (`Echo=0` deterministic offline stub, `OpenAi=1`, `AzureOpenAi=2`, `Anthropic=3` — only Echo + OpenAi have working implementations this commit). **EF schema (new).** `AiPrompts` table + `AiInvocations` table + one combined migration `AddAiModule` on App DB. Indexes: unique on `AiPrompt.Key`; `(Feature, IsActive)` filter; invocation indexes on `CreatedAt`, `(Feature, CreatedAt)`, `(CallerUserId, CreatedAt)`, plus a filtered `(ErrorCode, CreatedAt) WHERE ErrorCode IS NOT NULL` for error-only viewing. **Service.** `IAiService.InvokeAsync(promptKey, inputs, caller, ct)` — the single entry point every feature endpoint uses: load prompt → substitute `{placeholder}` markers into both templates → resolve `IAiProvider` by `Tag` → call provider with timing → write `AiInvocation` row (success OR failure) → audit. The provider abstraction is `IAiProvider` with two impls: `EchoAiProvider` (returns a deterministic synthetic prefixed with `[echo:model]` + echoes the substituted user prompt; tokens approximated as length/4) and `OpenAiProvider` (POST to OpenAI `/chat/completions`, reads `ApiKey`/`BaseUrl`/`DefaultModel` from `AiOptions`; throws `AI_PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` 503 when key empty; parses `choices[0].message.content` + `usage.prompt_tokens` + `usage.completion_tokens`). Providers register as `IAiProvider` services; DI assembles a `IReadOnlyDictionary<AiProvider, IAiProvider>` keyed by `Tag` that the service resolves at call time — adding a new provider is one file + one DI line. **Admin surface.** `IAdminAiPromptService` covers CRUD + a `Test` dry-run endpoint that runs an arbitrary inputs dictionary through `IAiService.InvokeAsync` (so admins can iterate on a prompt without the user's UI) + an invocations log read. Validation centralises key/name/temperature/max-token bounds inside the service; key is immutable once written; updates bump `Version` by 1 and write the audit event. **Endpoints (15 new).** Admin (7): `POST /admin/ai/prompts/list`, `GET /admin/ai/prompts/{id}`, `POST /admin/ai/prompts`, `PUT /admin/ai/prompts/{id}`, `DELETE /admin/ai/prompts/{id}`, `POST /admin/ai/prompts/{id}/test`, `POST /admin/ai/invocations/list`. Public/visitor text features (4): `POST /ai/question-filter` (visitor), `POST /ai/faq` (anonymous), `POST /ai/assistance` (visitor), `POST /ai/translate` (anonymous). Live chunk-based (2): `POST /ai/live-translation/chunk` (visitor), `POST /ai/live-sign-language/chunk` (visitor) — chunk-per-request because the Infrastructure csproj does not yet reference SignalR (a real hub would require a package add which CLAUDE.md §1.7 blocks without owner approval; chunk-per-request matches the on-device speech-to-text Flutter loop and is straight-line replaceable later). Write endpoints rate-limited under `auth`. **Seeded prompts (6).** `IdentitySeeder.EnsureDefaultAiPromptsAsync` seeds one prompt per feature with bilingual display names + sensible system prompts (e.g. question-filter returns JSON `{allowed, reason}`; translate substitutes `{sourceLang}` / `{targetLang}`). All seeded prompts default to `AiProvider.Echo` so dev + tests run offline; admin flips to OpenAI by editing the row. Idempotent — re-seeding only inserts missing keys. **Config (new).** `AiOptions` bound to `Ai` section of `appsettings.json`: `DefaultProvider` (Echo by default), `OpenAi.ApiKey` (env-var override), `OpenAi.BaseUrl` (defaults to public OpenAI), `OpenAi.DefaultModel` (`gpt-4o-mini`). **ErrorCodes:** `AI_PROMPT_INVALID`, `AI_PROMPT_NOT_FOUND`, `AI_PROMPT_KEY_DUPLICATE`, `AI_PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `AI_PROVIDER_FAILED`, `AI_INPUT_INVALID`, `AI_FEATURE_DISABLED`. **AuditEvents:** `AiPrompt.{Created,Updated,Deactivated}`, `AiInvocation.{Succeeded,Failed}`. **Contracts.** New `SIMF.Contracts.Ai` namespace: admin DTOs (`AdminAiPromptSummary`/`Detail`, `CreateAiPromptRequest`/`Update`/`Test`), feature request bodies (`FilterQuestionRequest`, `AskFaqRequest`, `AssistanceRequest`, `TranslateRequest`, `LiveTranslateChunkRequest`, `LiveSignChunkRequest`), and the shared result (`AiCallResult` — `InvocationId`, `PromptKey`, `Feature`, `Provider`, `Model`, `OutputText`, `TokensInput?`, `TokensOutput?`, `LatencyMs`). **Tests (12 new).** `AiModuleTests`: anonymous FAQ returns echo + writes an invocation row with `CallerKind=Anonymous`; authenticated assistance records `CallerKind=Visitor` + `CallerUserId`; translate substitutes lang inputs into template; live-translation + live-sign-language chunk endpoints route to the right prompts; admin list includes the six seeded prompts; admin update increments `Version` 1→2; admin create + Test round-trips for a fresh prompt; duplicate-key create returns 409 `AI_PROMPT_KEY_DUPLICATE`; admin invocations log returns at least one row; inactive prompt returns 503 `AI_FEATURE_DISABLED`. **Full suite 409/409 pass** (was 397 pre-G12; +12). **What G12 does NOT include yet.** Real SignalR hubs (deferred until owner approves adding `Microsoft.AspNetCore.SignalR` to the API csproj). CP Razor pages for the prompt grid + invocations log (the admin endpoints + contracts ship; Razor pages land in a polish pass). Per-prompt `InputJsonSchema` / `OutputJsonSchema` validation (placeholder — the template substitution does silent passes for missing keys today; a Json-schema validator can layer on later without changing the wire shape). The Azure OpenAI + Anthropic concrete providers (registered as a dictionary entry but not wired — calling them raises `AI_PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` deterministically). | Owner spec called out three properties: **one location, dynamic, per-feature reuse.** The catalogue (one `AiPrompt` row per key) is the "one location"; the runtime CP edit (with `Version` bumping) is the "dynamic"; the `Feature` enum + the seeded six prompts are the "per-feature" wiring. **The provider abstraction is the load-bearing extensibility seam.** A new backend (Azure OpenAI, on-prem LLM, local Ollama) lands as one new `IAiProvider` impl + one DI registration; no other code changes, no API surface change, no migration. The dictionary-by-tag resolution is deliberate — providers could be a switch statement, but the dictionary keeps it testable + avoids touching the service when a new provider is added. **Echo as the default seeded provider** matters for two reasons: (1) tests run offline, no API key required, deterministic assertions are possible; (2) production can ship with the AI module enabled but every feature "muted" until the admin flips a prompt to OpenAI — staged rollout without a feature-flag system. **Chunk-per-request for the live features** is the operationally-simple shape. A SignalR hub would let the server push partial translations as they generate, which is more elegant for sub-1-second segment boundaries; but it needs a package add (CLAUDE.md §1.7 blocked) and the Flutter mic loop already produces discrete 200–500ms segments, so the per-segment HTTP POST is workable. If the owner approves the SignalR package add later, the path is mechanical — wrap `IAiService` in a hub method and stream chunks; the prompts + Provider + invocation log all stay. **Invocation rows persist on failure too.** SOC + product analytics need to see "AI calls that 503'd" to size capacity and trace regressions. A failure row writes `OutputText=null`, `ErrorCode` set, `LatencyMs` measured up to the throw point — the same shape the success path writes, so the admin grid is one query. **CallerKind as a free-text bucket** rather than a hard enum is intentional: the bucket is descriptive (for SOC filtering) not authorisation (the policy gate already runs upstream). A future Staff/Moderator/Other distinction does not need a schema migration. **`InputJson` is persisted as raw substituted-inputs JSON** — not the rendered prompt. The rendered prompt is reconstructible from `PromptKey` + `Version` + `InputJson` if needed for debugging; storing the rendered prompt would duplicate the template text on every row (heavy and stale-after-prompt-edit). The trade-off: a stored invocation cannot be perfectly replayed if the prompt has been edited since — but that's exactly what the `Version` column documents, so the analyst knows. **Substitution is naive `string.Replace` over `{placeholder}` markers** — no escape syntax, no nested templating, no conditional blocks.
