For a prompt catalogue this size (low dozens of prompts), Mustache / Razor / Liquid would all be over-engineering. If a prompt needs branching, that's a sign it should be two prompts. **Validation is centralised in `AdminAiPromptService.Validate{Create,Update}`** rather than scattered across the endpoints — the CP, the admin Test endpoint, and any future programmatic admin path all hit the same bounds. The admin Test endpoint reuses `IAiService.InvokeAsync` so the dry-run behaves identically to the production call path. **One combined migration `AddAiModule`** ships both tables — they're conceptually two but their lifecycle is one, and bundling keeps the migration history readable. **The 409/409 pass rate** (was 397/397 pre-G12; +12 from `AiModuleTests`) covers the anon + visitor + admin caller paths, the Version-bump invariant, the duplicate-key gate, the disabled-prompt gate, the invocation log, the substitution behaviour, and the live-chunk endpoints. Two notable test gaps: (1) the OpenAI provider isn't unit-tested against a real backend (it would need a recorded fixture + a stub HttpClient handler; deferred), (2) the live-translation hub semantics (concatenation, segment boundaries) aren't tested because the chunk endpoint is a single-call HTTP shape today. Both ship as follow-up tasks. |
| D-175 | 2026-05-30 | **G11-D — Page 7 (visitor seat reservation) shipped as a per-session `SeatReservation` entity with three creation paths.** Owner picked the per-session option from the three I offered (per-session vs per-hall vs registration flag). The rules baked into the service match the owner spec verbatim: (1) admin in CP can paint a whole row as off-limits for one session, (2) a visitor can pre-pick their own seat, (3) the random allocator picks the first free seat for a user who doesn't want to pick. In every path the active reservation count for one session must not exceed the smaller of `Hall.Capacity` and the layout total (`RowLabels.Count * SeatsPerRow`); a `SessionCapacityOverride` (D-165) further caps that. Each seat is reserved for one and only one session at a time. **Domain entities (new).** `HallSeatLayout` (`Id`, FK `HallId` unique, `RowLabels` CSV ≤256 chars, `SeatsPerRow` 1–80, audit timestamps) — optional 1:1 with `Hall`. `SeatReservation` (`Id`, FK `SessionId` cascade, `RowLabel` ≤8 chars, `SeatNumber`, `Kind` enum, `ReservedForUserId` nullable logical FK to SimfUser, `CreatedByUserId`, `CreatedAt`, `ReleasedAt?`). The `Hall` table itself stays **frozen** under D-110 — layout columns live on the sibling table, not on Hall. **Enum (new).** `SeatReservationKind` (UserBooking=0, AdminReservedRow=1, RandomAssignment=2). **EF schema (new).** `HallSeatLayouts` table + `SeatReservations` table, both on App DB, in one migration `AddSeatReservations`. Two filtered unique indexes enforce the invariants: `(SessionId, RowLabel, SeatNumber) WHERE ReleasedAt IS NULL` (one active reservation per seat per session) and `(SessionId, ReservedForUserId) WHERE ReleasedAt IS NULL AND ReservedForUserId IS NOT NULL` (one active seat per visitor per session). Released rows stay for audit; the filter lets the seat be re-booked. **Service.** One `ISeatReservationService` covers all four surfaces: `GetSessionSeatMapAsync` (visitor — full grid + my-cell), `ReserveAsync` (visitor self-pick), `ReserveRandomAsync` (random allocator — scans the grid, inserts the first free cell, retries on race), `ReleaseMineAsync` (visitor cancel), admin `GetLayoutAsync` / `SetLayoutAsync` (layout edit — rejects if `Rows × Seats > Hall.Capacity`), admin `AdminReserveRowAsync` (row block — N rows of `AdminReservedRow` with `ReservedForUserId = null`), admin `AdminReleaseAsync` (clear any reservation), admin `ListSessionReservationsAsync` (grid). DB-level uniqueness is a race-safe backstop: the service still pre-checks, but on a `DbUpdateException` it parses the index name and translates into either `SEAT_ALREADY_RESERVED` or `SEAT_ALREADY_OWNED_BY_SESSION`. **Endpoints (9 new).** Visitor: `GET /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}/seats`, `POST /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}/seats/reserve`, `POST /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}/seats/reserve-random`, `DELETE /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}/seats/mine`. Admin: `GET /api/v1/admin/halls/{hallId}/seat-layout`, `PUT /api/v1/admin/halls/{hallId}/seat-layout`, `POST /api/v1/admin/sessions/{sessionId}/seats/reserve-row`, `DELETE /api/v1/admin/sessions/{sessionId}/seats/{id}`, `POST /api/v1/admin/sessions/{sessionId}/seats/list`. Visitor surface requires `RequireApprovedAccount`; admin surface requires `AdministratorOnly + RequireApprovedAccount`. Write endpoints are rate-limited under the `auth` policy. **ErrorCodes:** `SEAT_LAYOUT_INVALID`, `SEAT_LAYOUT_MISSING`, `SEAT_OUT_OF_BOUNDS`, `SEAT_ALREADY_RESERVED`, `SEAT_ALREADY_OWNED_BY_SESSION`, `SEAT_RESERVATION_NOT_FOUND`, `SEAT_CAPACITY_EXCEEDED`, `SEAT_SESSION_FULL`. **AuditEvents:** `HallSeatLayout.Updated`, `SeatReservation.{Created,Released}`, `SeatReservation.{RowAdminReserved,RowAdminReleased}`. **Contracts (new file).** `SIMF.Contracts.Sessions.SeatReservations.cs` — `SessionSeatMap`, `SessionSeatCell`, `ReserveSeatRequest`, `AdminReserveRowRequest`, `SetHallSeatLayoutRequest`, `HallSeatLayoutSnapshot`, `MySeatReservation`. Request classes are non-sealed so the endpoints can carry route parameters via inheritance (matches the D-168 / D-174 pattern). **Tests (7 new).** `SeatReservationsTests`: (1) visitor self-pick then release lets the seat be re-booked, (2) a second visitor cannot take a seat already taken (`SEAT_ALREADY_RESERVED` / 409), (3) a user cannot hold two seats in the same session (`SEAT_ALREADY_OWNED_BY_SESSION` / 409), (4) the random allocator returns a free seat with `Kind = RandomAssignment`, (5) the capacity cap blocks overbooking (1×1 hall — second visitor sees `SEAT_SESSION_FULL` / 409), (6) admin reserve-row makes row A unbookable but row B stays free, (7) admin layout request whose `Rows × Seats` exceeds `Hall.Capacity` returns `SEAT_CAPACITY_EXCEEDED` / 400. **Full suite 397/397 pass** (was 390 pre-G11-D; +7). **What G11-D does NOT include.** The CP Razor pages for hall-layout editing + session-row blocking; the admin endpoints + contracts ship now, the Razor grids land in the polish pass. The Flutter screens themselves live on `feature/mobile-app-skeleton` and will consume these endpoints once that branch picks up the contracts. | The owner spec gave three input modes (admin, visitor, random) and one invariant (no overbooking against `Hall.Capacity`). **The schema decision was per-session, not per-hall.** A per-hall reservation would make a seat permanently owned across the whole forum (wrong — different sessions in the same hall need different audiences); a registration-time flag would scale-of-one (wrong — the user wants seat-grained reservations). Per-session matches the mockup, the owner spec, and the natural relational model. **Filtered unique indexes over a hard delete.** Releasing with `ReleasedAt` + a `WHERE ReleasedAt IS NULL` filter keeps the audit trail (who held seat A3 between 10:00 and 10:15?) while letting the same seat be re-taken. A hard delete would drop the audit data and force every cancel to go through a soft-delete pattern elsewhere — both are worse. **Hall stays frozen** because D-110 lifts the freeze only for additive enum values (not new columns); the layout lives on its own table. The downside is one extra JOIN on the visitor seat-map call — measured at sub-millisecond on a single-hall layout fetch, well below noise. **Admin row block does not kick out existing visitors.** When an admin reserves row A and seat A3 is already booked by a visitor, the visitor's reservation stays; the row block only fills the *free* seats. The mental model is "reserved" vs "blocked" — the visitor's seat is reserved-for-them, the rest of the row is blocked-by-admin. The DB rejects the duplicate insert (`SEAT_ALREADY_RESERVED`) and the service swallows it as a race-loss rather than failing the whole row block. The trade-off: an admin who wants to truly clear a row must explicitly release the individual visitor reservations first. Documenting this in the CP UI is a polish-pass concern. **Random allocator walks rows in declaration order.** A truly-random pick would need a shuffle on every request; a deterministic top-down walk is simpler and the visitor-facing semantic ("a seat was assigned to you") doesn't change. The first-free walk is bounded by `Rows × Seats` ≤ 26 × 80 ≤ 2080 — well within an in-memory scan. **Capacity cap = min(LayoutTotal, SessionCapacityOverride ?? HallCapacity).** Two layers — the layout caps the visible grid, the session-level override caps the bookable count. This matches D-165's per-session capacity-override field exactly and means a 100-seat hall can run a 60-seat sit-down talk without the picker showing 40 phantom rows. **Test design.** Each test sets up its own hall + session (no shared fixtures) so the assertions are independent — a flaky seat test cannot poison the next one. The race-condition tests rely on the filtered unique indexes; SQLite (test DB) honours them just like SQL Server (production). The capacity test deliberately uses a 1×1 hall so the second visitor's allocation runs into a real-shaped capacity cap, not an off-by-one. The 397/397 pass rate (was 390 pre-G11-D; +7 from `SeatReservationsTests`) covers the three creation paths, the two uniqueness invariants, the capacity cap, the admin row block, and the layout-capacity validation. **Centralised AI module (G12) shipped in the same session as a separate decision** so this row stays focused on seats. |
| D-174 | 2026-05-30 | **Phase G11 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan — Mockup-driven changes (pages 26, 39, 21, 27) shipped.** PDF §2.11 lists 5 pages (7, 21, 26, 27, 39) to be actioned against the Mockup file the external UI/UX designer delivers. The Mockup landed mid-session as `D:\SIMF\System\15-04-2024\Mockup.html` (41 mobile-app screens, RTL Arabic, navy + gold palette). **Discovery — the 15-04-2024 folder rule narrowed:** the prior memory note "the 15-04-2024 folder is a superseded draft" was true only of the three prompt files in it (`final-prompt.md`, `my-style (1).md`, `professional-coding-agent-prompt.md`); the `Mockup.html` + `Overall Time & Plan.pdf` + `SIMF_Screen_Guide_and_User_Journey.docx` files in the same folder are controlled deliverables. Memory updated. **Page-by-page shipped:** **Page 26 — إرسال سؤال** (live-stream Send question, addressee selector). Backend extension: new `SessionQuestionRecipient` enum (Speaker=0, Host=1), default Speaker for backward compatibility; new `Recipient` column on `SessionQuestion` + migration `AddSessionQuestionRecipient`; wired through `SubmitSessionQuestionRequest` + `SubmitSessionQuestionRoute` + service + moderator queue projection so the moderator can see who the audience addressed each question to. **Page 39 — سياسات وضوابط الأمن السيبراني** (Cybersecurity Policies content page). No new code path — the G8 ContentBlock entity is the right home. `IdentitySeeder.EnsureCybersecurityPolicyContentAsync` seeds 13 ContentBlock rows verbatim from the mockup (`cyber.title`, `cyber.intro`, `cyber.pillar.0[1-5].title/body`, `cyber.reference`) with EN + AR pairs. Idempotent on key. The Flutter app reads them via `GET /api/v1/content/{key}`. Test fixture's `EnsureDatabaseCreated` now invokes the seeder explicitly since `Program.cs` skips it under the "Testing" environment. **Page 21 — الوفود** (Delegations list). New `Delegation` domain entity (`Id`, bilingual `Name/NameArabic`, optional `CountryId` real FK with Restrict, `MemberCount`, `IsPriority`, `IsInternational`, `DisplayOrder`, `IsActive`, audit timestamps). Two services split admin/public (matches the G8 pattern): `IAdminDelegationService` (full CRUD) + `IPublicDelegationService` (anonymous list, sorted `IsPriority desc, DisplayOrder asc, NameArabic asc`). 5 admin endpoints + 1 public endpoint (`GET /api/v1/delegations`). EF indexes: `(IsActive, IsPriority, DisplayOrder)` for the public list, `CountryId` for the country filter. **Page 27 — طلب مقابلة** (Meeting / interview request during live session). New `MeetingRequest` domain entity (`Id`, real FK `SessionId` with cascade, logical FK `RequestedByUserId` to SimfUser on Identity DB, `RequesterName ≤128`, `Subject ≤1000`, `Status` enum (`Pending`/`Accepted`/`Rejected`), `ResponseNote ≤2000`, `CreatedAt`, `RespondedAt?`, `RespondedByUserId?`). One service `IMeetingRequestService` covers public submission + admin list + admin respond. The submit endpoint is per-session (`POST /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}/meeting-requests`); admin endpoints (`POST /admin/meeting-requests/list`, `PUT /admin/meeting-requests/{id}/respond`) gate by `AdministratorOnly`. Status transitions validated: response `Status` must be Accepted or Rejected (Pending → Pending rejected with `MEETING_REQUEST_STATUS_INVALID`). **One combined migration `AddDelegationsAndMeetingRequests`** on the App DB ships both entities — they share the gap-phase commit and bundling reduces migration history clutter. **ErrorCodes:** `DELEGATION_INVALID`, `DELEGATION_NOT_FOUND`, `MEETING_REQUEST_INVALID`, `MEETING_REQUEST_NOT_FOUND`, `MEETING_REQUEST_SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `MEETING_REQUEST_STATUS_INVALID`. **AuditEvents:** `Delegation.{Created,Updated,Deactivated}`, `MeetingRequest.{Submitted,Responded}`. **Tests:** 11 new across `SessionQuestionsTests` (1: Host recipient round-trips), `CmsTests` (1: cybersecurity content blocks are seeded), `DelegationsTests` (4: admin create + public list, priority ordering, deactivation drops from public list, non-admin → 403), `MeetingRequestsTests` (5: submit returns pending, admin lists then responds Accepted, response with Pending → 400, unknown session → 400, empty subject → 400). **Full suite 390/390 pass** (was 379 pre-G11; +11). **What G11 does NOT include yet:** **Page 7 — visitor seat-row preference**. The mockup shows a yes/no radio + a seat-picker grid for the sign-up flow. Shipping it needs an owner decision on schema (is it a seat preference per session, per hall, or a session-agnostic registration flag? The mockup is ambiguous — the radio asks about media-coverage seating but the grid has no hall context). Recorded as a follow-up question for the owner. CP admin pages for Delegations + MeetingRequests (the backend endpoints + admin contracts ship; the Razor grids land in a polish pass). The Flutter screens themselves (they live on the `feature/mobile-app-skeleton` branch, not the current `feature/login-api` branch). **What changed in shared rules:** the SIMF CLAUDE.md rule about the 15-04-2024 folder was updated this session to clarify that only the three prompt files are superseded — the Mockup, the Plan PDF, the Screen Guide, the Tech Methodology checklist, and the logo PDF in that same folder are all controlled deliverables. | The biggest decision is **mapping the 5 mockup pages to the correct backend slice**. Three of the five (26, 39, 27) had natural backend homes — extend G6 / seed G8 / new MeetingRequest entity. The fourth (21 — Delegations) was a clearly-new entity. The fifth (7 — seat preference) was ambiguous and got deferred for owner input; shipping it on guesswork would have produced wrong-shape data the Flutter team has to rework. **Defaulting `SessionQuestionRecipient` to Speaker** is the load-bearing backward-compatibility decision: every existing G6 caller (the 8 G6 tests + any mobile-app client on the pre-D-174 wire) submits without the `Recipient` field and lands as Speaker — the same behaviour the original G6 implementation had. The migration adds the column with `default 0` for the same reason — pre-D-174 rows read as Speaker, never null. **Splitting Delegation services into `IAdminDelegationService` + `IPublicDelegationService`** mirrors the G8 CMS pattern: the public list runs on every Flutter app render, the admin CRUD runs once per editing session. Keeping the public service narrow (AsNoTracking + projection + no audit log) means the hot path's constructor signature tells the next maintainer "this only does reads". **The MeetingRequest service stays single (not split)** because the submission and admin paths share the same dependencies (AppDb + IdentityDb + AuditLog) and the volume is bounded (one meeting request per live audience question — peak hundreds per session, not the page-render firehose). **The cybersecurity content seed lives in `IdentitySeeder`** rather than a separate `CmsSeeder` because the IdentitySeeder already runs at startup, already touches both DbContexts, and already follows the idempotent-ensure pattern — inventing a CmsSeeder for one method would have been ceremony for no gain. The 13 content-block rows match the mockup verbatim (Arabic side) with English translations added so the existing bilingual `ContentBlock` contract is respected. **The test fixture had to start running the seeder explicitly** because `Program.cs` skips it under `IsEnvironment("Testing")`; the cybersecurity-policy test depends on the seed running. The change is one line in `SimfApiFactory.EnsureDatabaseCreated`. **One combined migration** (rather than two separate `AddDelegations` + `AddMeetingRequests`) keeps the migration-history table tidy — the two entities are conceptually independent but ship in the same commit, and splitting the migration would only matter for downgrade orchestration that pre-publish SIMF doesn't have. The 390/390 test pass rate (was 381/381 pre-G11; was 379/379 pre-G11-A; +11 from the four new test classes/sections) covers the recipient column round-trip, the cybersecurity content seed presence, the delegation priority ordering, and the meeting-request status-transition gate. The one omission worth calling out is the **lack of a Page-7 follow-up question on the gap doc itself** — that's tracked here in the "does not include yet" section and on the in-session todo, but the gap doc still lists G11 as a single phase. A future doc-update can split G11 into "G11.1 (pages 26/39/21/27) done" and "G11.2 (page 7) pending owner". |
| D-173 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase G8 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan — Dynamic content CMS (ContentBlock + Banner) shipped.** PDF §1 + §2.1 mandate that an operator can edit every piece of public copy (headings, body text, welcome message, banners, announcements, labels) without a code change. The owner had previously deferred G8 to end-of-project; the deferral was reversed mid-session ("continue", "make big job"). **Domain (`SIMF.Domain.Cms`):** `ContentBlock(Id, Key unique slug ≤128, ContentEn ≤8000, ContentAr ≤8000, IsActive, LastUpdatedByUserId, CreatedAt, LastUpdatedAt)` + `Banner(Id, TitleEn/TitleAr ≤256, BodyEn/BodyAr ≤2000, ImageUrl? ≤1024, LinkUrl? ≤1024, StartUtc, EndUtc, DisplayOrder, IsActive, CreatedAt, UpdatedAt?)`. Both live on the App DB. **Why two entities, not one:** banners have a lifecycle (announcement → live → ended) + an explicit display order; folding them into a key/value `ContentBlock` would push those columns into a stringified JSON blob and lose the EF schema benefits (start-end-window index, server-side filter). Two thin tables is the right trade. **EF configs:** unique index on `ContentBlock.Key` (the client codes against the slug — renaming is a wire-breaking change); composite index `(IsActive, StartUtc, EndUtc, DisplayOrder)` on Banner covers the hot "active banners now" query. Migration `AddCms` on the App DB. **Services (split admin / public on purpose):** `IAdminCmsService` owns admin CRUD; `IPublicCmsService` is a separate read-only contract — the public path stays narrow (`AsNoTracking` + projection straight to the contract record + single round trip even on batch). Keeping them split means the public hot path's constructor signature shows it touches only the two query operations, no audit log, no validation. **Admin upsert model on ContentBlock:** the admin endpoint is `PUT /api/v1/admin/content-blocks` (no id in the route) — the body's `Key` identifies the row, the service creates it if missing or updates in place if present. The owner edits content by key, not by id; the row id is a server detail. **Public endpoints:** `GET /api/v1/content/{key}` (anonymous, returns `PublicContentBlock`), `POST /api/v1/content/batch` (anonymous, returns `Dictionary<key, block>` for missing-key tolerant client code), `GET /api/v1/banners` (anonymous, returns currently-active banners filtered by `IsActive AND StartUtc <= now AND EndUtc >= now`, ordered by `(DisplayOrder, StartUtc)`). **If-Modified-Since handshake** on `GET /content/{key}`: the server emits `Last-Modified` truncated to one-second precision (HTTP date precision is one second, not millisecond); when the client sends `If-Modified-Since >= truncated-LastUpdatedAt`, the server returns **304 No Body** via `Send.ResultAsync(Results.StatusCode(304))`. The truncation is load-bearing — the original draft compared full-precision `DateTimeOffset` values and a millisecond drift made the very next request a cache miss on every page load. **Admin endpoints:** list/get/upsert/delete content blocks + list/get/create/update/delete banners (9 total) under `AdministratorOnly` + `RequireApprovedAccount`, writes rate-limited under the `auth` policy. **Client + BFF:** 9 `SimfAdminClient` methods + 9 CP BFF passthroughs in `AccountEndpoints`. **CP UI:** `ContentBlocksList.razor` at `/admin/content-blocks` (table grid + upsert modal with EN + AR + IsActive fields) + `BannersList.razor` at `/admin/banners` (table grid + create/edit modal with EN + AR title/body + image/link URLs + datetime-local start/end + display-order + IsActive). Nav adds `Module.ContentBlocks` + `Module.Banners` to the existing `Nav.Content` group (alongside the existing Media/News/Previous-editions stubs). **Contracts (`SIMF.Contracts.Cms` + `SIMF.Contracts.Admin.Cms`):** `PublicContentBlock`, `PublicContentBlockBatch`, `PublicContentBlockBatchRequest`, `PublicBanner`, `PublicBanners`, `AdminContentBlockSummary`, `UpsertContentBlockRequest`, `AdminBannerSummary`, `AdminBannerDetail`, `CreateBannerRequest`, `UpdateBannerRequest`. `UpdateBannerRequest` opened for inheritance so the endpoint can bind `Id` from the route (matches the D-168 pattern). **ErrorCodes:** `CONTENT_BLOCK_INVALID`, `CONTENT_BLOCK_NOT_FOUND`, `CONTENT_BLOCK_KEY_DUPLICATE`, `BANNER_INVALID`, `BANNER_NOT_FOUND`, `BANNER_INVALID_TIME_WINDOW`. **AuditEvents:** `ContentBlock.Upserted`, `ContentBlock.Deactivated`, `Banner.Created`, `Banner.Updated`, `Banner.Deactivated`. **Resx:** EN + AR parity for `Module.ContentBlocks` + `Module.Banners` + the full `Admin.ContentBlocks.*` + `Admin.Banners.*` groups (~50 keys total). **Tests (`CmsTests`):** 9 new — admin upsert creates then updates in place; public read returns active block; public read of inactive block → 404; If-Modified-Since with matching timestamp → 304 No Body; public batch returns only existing active keys; banner create + public list returns active-window-only; banner end-before-start → `BANNER_INVALID_TIME_WINDOW`; non-admin caller → 403 on upsert; delete makes subsequent public read → 404. **Full suite 379/379 pass.** **What G8 does NOT include yet:** brand-colour tokens edit (gap doc step 5 — owner-deferred to the existing `theme.tokens.css` flow per CPD-001 §8.4); a markdown renderer on the public read endpoint (the wire ships raw markdown; the Flutter app + Website render it client-side, matching the pattern every other markdown-CMS uses); a per-page editor surface that groups blocks by page (gap doc step 2 — the admin grid is keyed by slug today; an "editor groups by page-prefix" UX can land in a polish pass without changing the wire). | The big decision is **upsert-by-key** rather than full RESTful create-id / update-id. The owner edits content by name (`home.welcome.title`, `about.body.paragraph1`); making the admin generate a Guid first, then PUT it, is ceremony the workflow doesn't reward. The cost is one indirection on the admin side (the service does `SingleOrDefaultAsync(b => b.Key == key)` + branch); the gain is that the admin endpoint maps 1:1 to the editing mental model. The trade against a fully RESTful `POST /content-blocks` + `PUT /content-blocks/{id}` is symmetry: when the editor pastes a corrected sentence into the same key, they don't need to remember "was this a new row or an existing one?" — the service decides. The **public batch endpoint** (rather than only single-key) is a deliberate hot-path optimisation: a Flutter screen with 8 labels would otherwise issue 8 GETs; the batch fans them into one round trip and silently drops missing keys so the client renders fallbacks without an error-handling branch per label. **The If-Modified-Since handshake** is the second hot-path optimisation: every Website page render currently calls `/content/*`, and a `304 No Body` response is a fraction of the bytes of a fresh fetch. The second-precision truncation is the tricky bit — getting it wrong (the original draft did) makes the cache always miss; the comment in the endpoint explicitly calls out the trap so the next maintainer doesn't "fix" it by removing the truncation. **The Banner entity carries `ImageUrl` + `LinkUrl` as free-text strings** rather than uploading the binary to SIMF storage. The decision is that announcement banners typically link to existing event collateral (a press release URL on the venue's site, an image on a CDN) — pulling those into SIMF storage would require the admin to first upload, then paste — extra friction for a workflow that's measured in minutes-before-the-keynote. If a future requirement asks for "upload an image right here", that's an additive endpoint on the same row, not a refactor. **The `Nav.Content` group already had Media + News + Previous-editions stubs**; adding ContentBlocks + Banners there (rather than to a new Nav.Cms group) matches the existing mental model the admin already learned. The 379/379 test pass rate (was 370/370 pre-G8; +9 from `CmsTests`) covers the upsert lifecycle, the public read + 304 handshake, the banner window filter, the validation paths, and the role gate. The If-Modified-Since test in particular is worth calling out — it's the only test in the suite that exercises a 304 response, and it catches the precision-mismatch regression class on every run. |
| D-172 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase G10 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan — Face ID / Touch ID biometric sign-in shipped via ECDSA P-256 device-key ceremony.** PDF §2.5 mandates biometric sign-in as a peer of email + password; the gap doc records the device-key ceremony shape as the unresolved owner decision. **Owner-default decision: ECDSA P-256 (ES256, NIST P-256 / secp256r1).** Chosen over Ed25519 because .NET 10 has first-class `System.Security.Cryptography.ECDsa` with `ImportSubjectPublicKeyInfo` + `VerifyData(... IeeeP1363FixedFieldConcatenation)` and matching APIs on iOS (CryptoKit, Secure Enclave) + Android (Keystore, StrongBox); shipping Ed25519 today would need either BouncyCastle (heavy NuGet) or NSec (extra dep) — strictly more cost for the same security goal. ES256 is also the JWS standard for biometric flows, so the same key format works if a future "sign a server challenge as a JWS" upgrade lands. **Domain (`SIMF.Domain.IdentityAccess.DeviceKey`):** `DeviceKey(Id, UserId, PublicKey (256-char base64 SubjectPublicKeyInfo DER), Algorithm ("ES256"), Label ≤64, CreatedAt, LastUsedAt?, RevokedAt?, CurrentChallenge?, ChallengeExpiresAt?)`. Lives on **Identity DB** (auth concern). Single-challenge-per-key stored inline rather than as a separate `DeviceKeyChallenge` table — at most one challenge is alive per key at a time, the row already exists to attach it to, and avoiding the extra table keeps the join shape simple. Composite query index `(UserId, RevokedAt)` covers the "list my non-revoked keys" hot path. Migration `AddDeviceKeys` on the Identity DB. **Service (`IDeviceKeyService` + `DeviceKeyService`):** four operations — `RegisterAsync` (caller signed in, server eagerly validates the public-key bytes parse as a SubjectPublicKeyInfo for the curve, rejects unsupported algorithm strings with `DEVICE_KEY_ALGORITHM_UNSUPPORTED`); `ListMineAsync` (the caller's keys); `IssueChallengeAsync` (anonymous, generates 32-byte cryptographic random nonce, stores + returns base64; rejects revoked keys with `DEVICE_KEY_REVOKED` 401); `SignInWithDeviceKeyAsync` (anonymous, verifies the supplied challenge matches stored + within expiry + signature verifies via `ECDsa.VerifyData(... IeeeP1363FixedFieldConcatenation)`, consumes the challenge on success, mints a full JWT pair + refresh token via the same path `SignInService.IssueTokensAsync` uses); `RevokeAsync` (self-service caller-bound or admin-bypass). **Token mint helper** is structurally identical to `SignInService.IssueTokensAsync` — same `IJwtTokenService` + `IUserProfileService` + `IRefreshTokenRepository` + `OpaqueToken.Generate/Hash` + `RefreshTokenLifetime = 30 days`. Documented duplication, deferred extraction of a shared `ITokenIssuer` to a follow-up since refactoring `SignInService` mid-G10 violates §17 one-issue-per-change. **Endpoints:** `POST /api/v1/auth/device-keys` (authenticated — register), `GET /api/v1/auth/device-keys` (authenticated — list mine), `POST /api/v1/auth/device-keys/{id}/challenge` (**AllowAnonymous** — the device-key id alone is useless without the private key), `POST /api/v1/auth/sign-in-with-device-key` (**AllowAnonymous** — verifies + mints tokens), `DELETE /api/v1/auth/device-keys/{id}` (authenticated self-service — service returns 404 for non-owner callers to avoid enumerating other users' key ids), `DELETE /api/v1/admin/device-keys/{id}` (AdministratorOnly). All writes rate-limited under the `auth` policy. **Contracts (`SIMF.Contracts.Authentication.DeviceKeys`):** `RegisterDeviceKeyRequest`, `DeviceKeyEntry` (excludes the public key from the wire on the list response — the bytes already live server-side and shipping them on every list call is unnecessary surface), `DeviceKeyChallenge`, `SignInWithDeviceKeyRequest`. **ErrorCodes:** `DEVICE_KEY_INVALID`, `DEVICE_KEY_NOT_FOUND`, `DEVICE_KEY_ALGORITHM_UNSUPPORTED`, `DEVICE_KEY_REVOKED`, `DEVICE_KEY_CHALLENGE_INVALID`, `DEVICE_KEY_SIGNATURE_INVALID`, `DEVICE_KEY_OWNER_UNAVAILABLE`. **AuditEvents:** `DeviceKey.Registered`, `DeviceKey.Revoked`, `SignIn.WithDeviceKey`, `SignIn.WithDeviceKeyFailed`. **Tests (`DeviceKeySignInTests`):** 7 new — full ceremony round-trip (generate keypair, register, request challenge, sign with private key, sign in, receive JWT pair); wrong-signature → 401; replay of consumed challenge → 401 (challenge consumed on first success); revoked key → 401 on challenge; user cannot revoke another user's key (returns 404); register with non-base64 public key → `DEVICE_KEY_INVALID`; register with unsupported algorithm → `DEVICE_KEY_ALGORITHM_UNSUPPORTED`. **Full suite 370/370 pass.** **What G10 does NOT include yet:** sign-in throttling on `SignInWithDeviceKey` beyond the standard `auth` rate-limit (the same policy that protects password sign-in already covers this); a "register new device key during current sign-in" flow that piggybacks on the existing OTP step (the current path requires the user to be already signed in via password to register a key — bootstrap is therefore "first sign-in is always password, second-and-onwards can be Face ID"); device-attestation (Apple App Attest / Play Integrity) that proves the keypair was generated inside the Secure Enclave / StrongBox rather than software (out of scope — Phase 1 of G10 stops at the cryptographic ceremony; attestation is a separate decision); admin CP page to surface device keys per user (the endpoint exists; a Razor admin grid can land in a polish pass). | Choosing **ES256 over Ed25519** is the load-bearing decision. The PDF doesn't pin the algorithm — both are appropriate. Ed25519 would have been the modern-default pick on a greenfield server, but adding a Bouncy/NSec NuGet to the dependency graph for this single use case is exactly the kind of weight the rest of the SIMF dependency policy avoids; ES256 ships native, is what every mobile crypto library produces by default, and matches the JWS standard so any future "device key signs a server JWS" enhancement is a one-line change. The **inline-challenge-on-DeviceKey** model (rather than a `DeviceKeyChallenge` join table) is correct because the challenge lifecycle is strictly per-key — there is no business case for a key to have several alive challenges at once. The cost saved is a join on every sign-in; the cost paid is two nullable columns on the `DeviceKeys` row, which compiles to a few null bytes per row at rest. The **5-minute challenge lifetime** is a deliberate value: long enough that a user with a flaky biometric reader gets a second attempt without a fresh round-trip, short enough that a stolen challenge bytes blob is useless after coffee. **The "404 on cross-user revoke" path** is the most security-load-bearing endpoint detail: returning 403 would have leaked the existence of the deviceKeyId to a caller who isn't its owner; returning 404 collapses the "doesn't exist" and "exists but not yours" cases into a single unobservable outcome, matching the existing SIMF pattern on other per-user resources. The **token-mint duplication** is the only acknowledged structural debt — extracting `ITokenIssuer` is the clear follow-up but doing it inside G10 would have meant editing `SignInService` (which has 25+ tests pinning its current shape) for an issue that didn't ship the SignInService change as its primary concern. The 370/370 test pass rate (was 363/363 pre-G10; +7 from `DeviceKeySignInTests`) covers the full happy path including a real keypair generated + a real signature verified on every test run — no fake/stub crypto, the same `System.Security.Cryptography.ECDsa` path production runs is exercised. |
| D-171 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase G7 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan — Geofenced "Ask a question" shipped via self-assert venue toggle.** PDF §2.10 mandates a geofence guard on audience-question submission; the gap doc lists G-OI-2 (input source: lat/lon polygon vs venue-WiFi SSID vs self-assert toggle) as the unresolved owner decision. **G-OI-2 resolved (owner-default): self-assert toggle.** The audience client sends an `IsAtVenue` boolean alongside the question text; the server rejects the submission with `403 NOT_AT_VENUE` when the flag is false. **Why self-assert over the alternatives:** lat/lon requires the Flutter app to request GPS permission and the server to maintain a venue polygon per hall (and a SimfHall doesn't carry a geometry column today); venue-WiFi SSID requires admins to maintain a list of approved SSIDs per hall + the client to surface the connected SSID (which iOS no longer exposes without entitlements). The self-assert toggle costs zero permissions, zero schema, and zero ops — and it's strictly weaker than the other two, so hardening to a real geofence later is a pure additive layer on top of this gate. **Implementation:** `SubmitSessionQuestionRequest.IsAtVenue` added to the contract; `SubmitSessionQuestionRoute.IsAtVenue` on the FastEndpoints route shape (defaults false); `SessionQuestionService.SubmitAsync` raises `403 NOT_AT_VENUE` + writes a `SessionQuestion.RejectedNotAtVenue` audit row (Failure outcome) when the flag is false. The guard runs **before** the question-text validation so a remote bot sending `IsAtVenue=false` with valid text still gets a 403 + audit row, not silently dropped. **ErrorCodes:** `NOT_AT_VENUE`. **AuditEvents:** `SessionQuestion.RejectedNotAtVenue`. **Tests:** 1 new in `SessionQuestionsTests` (submit with `IsAtVenue=false` → 403 NOT_AT_VENUE). Existing G6 tests updated with `IsAtVenue=true` on every submission. **Full suite 363/363 pass.** **What G7 does NOT include yet:** the `VenueBoundary` table or `Hall.PolygonGeoJson` column (deliberately deferred — the self-assert layer is enough until the owner asks for stricter); a CP affordance for "force-disable submissions globally" (covered by the existing G6 hide-via-moderator flow, which trumps a remote-troll submission anyway); a tamper-evident "device attestation" layer that proves the toggle wasn't injected by a debug client (out of scope — SIMF is a friendly-audience event, not a hostile one). | The toggle is the **minimum-viable geofence**, in the literal sense of the term: it costs nothing to ship, it gates the obvious abuse case (a remote attacker sending questions to a live session), and it leaves every stronger layer reachable as a pure additive change. Choosing it over the alternatives is **not** an opinion that lat/lon or WiFi SSID are wrong — it's that they each require a decision the owner hasn't been asked for yet (which polygon? which SSID list?), and shipping the toggle today moves the gate from "absent" to "present" without prejudging those decisions. The decision is recorded with the explicit upgrade path documented so a future owner enabling GPS or SSID hardening reads this row and knows what was deferred. Putting the gate **before** the text validation means a remote attacker can't probe the rejection shape by sending an empty body — every disallowed call costs them an audit row tied to their UserId. The audit row outcome is `Failure` (matching the existing `RateLimit.Rejected` precedent) so the SOC dashboard counts venue-rejected attempts in the existing "rejected submissions" view without bespoke wiring. |
| D-170 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase G9 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan — "Meet People Like You" recommendations engine shipped.** PDF §2.8 mandates a smart-recommendations surface over the visitor interest set. **Service (`SIMF.Application.Recommendations` + `SIMF.Infrastructure.Recommendations`):** read-only `IRecommendationService.MeetPeopleLikeYouAsync(callerUserId, take, ct)`. Algorithm: Jaccard similarity `|A ∩ B| / |A ∪ B|` over the two interest sets, plus a small `SameProfileTypeBonus = 0.05` so VIPs meet VIPs / Staff meet Staff when overlap ties. Pulls all Approved non-Admin profiles in a single App-DB query, scores in memory, returns top N sorted `(Score desc, SharedInterestCount desc, EnglishName asc)`. Empty result when the caller has no profile or no interests, or when no candidate shares at least one interest. **Endpoint:** `GET /api/v1/account/recommendations/meet-like-you?take=N` under `RequireApprovedAccount`. **Contracts (`SIMF.Contracts.Recommendations`):** `RecommendationsResponse(IReadOnlyList<RecommendationEntry>)`, `RecommendationEntry(UserProfileId, EnglishName, ArabicName, JobTitle?, ProfileTypeName?, ProfileTypeNameArabic?, SharedInterests[], SharedInterestCount, Score)`, `MatchedInterest(Id, Name, NameArabic)`. **Tests (`RecommendationServiceTests`):** 6 new — caller with no interests returns empty; one-overlap candidate ranks above zero-overlap (zero-overlap is excluded entirely); higher overlap ranks above lower; caller excluded from own list; non-Approved users excluded; `take` parameter clamps. **Full suite 362/362 pass.** **What G9 does NOT include yet:** the 24-hour per-user materialised cache the gap doc lists as step 4 (deferred — at SIMF scale of low-thousands attendees the in-memory pass is sub-100ms and a cache adds invalidation complexity; can be added as `IMemoryCache` later if perf bites); the Flutter screen (gap doc step 3 — wiring lands when the mobile app team picks it up). | Jaccard over an in-memory pass is the right shape for SIMF scale. The interest catalog is bounded (under 100 rows in practice), the attendee pool is in the low thousands, and the comparison is O(catalog) per candidate after the HashSet build — well under 100ms in the worst case. A materialised cache would have introduced an invalidation problem (when does a profile's interest set become stale?) that the spec doesn't ask for; deferring it keeps the first pass honest. The same-ProfileType bonus is set deliberately small (0.05 — about one interest-overlap of a 10-interest profile) so it breaks ties without ever flipping a real overlap difference; a SOC analyst reviewing the ranking can mentally subtract 0.05 to recover the pure Jaccard. Excluding **zero-overlap candidates entirely** (rather than including them with score 0) prevents the response from devolving into "here is everyone alphabetically" when interest data is sparse — a recommendations endpoint that returns "no matches" is more honest than one that returns "any 20 people". Excluding non-Approved users matches every other public surface in SIMF — pending / rejected accounts are not addressable. The 0.05 bonus + Jaccard rule + same-tier bias are documented as named constants + comments in `RecommendationService.cs` rather than buried in Options because the scoring is the **product semantic** of the recommendations endpoint, not a configurable knob; tuning it later means a new D-decision, not an admin form. |
| D-169 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase G6 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan — SessionQuestion + per-session moderator grant shipped.** PDF §2.7.2 mandates a "session-question moderator" role distinct from D-161's `MobileAppRole.Moderator`; gap doc §4.2 records the naming collision and requires the two never share a label. **Domain (`SIMF.Domain.SessionQuestions`):** two new entities. `SessionQuestion(Id, SessionId, SubmittedByUserId, QuestionText ≤1000, Order, IsHidden, IsPushed, PushedAt?, CreatedAt)` — audience-submitted from the mobile app; logical FK on `SubmittedByUserId` (cross-DB to Identity), real FK on `SessionId`. `SessionModerator(SessionId, UserId, AssignedByUserId, AssignedAt)` — composite PK; the per-resource permission grant. Both cascade-delete from Session. **Migration `AddSessionQuestions` on the App DB.** **Services (`SIMF.Application.SessionQuestions.Abstractions` + `SIMF.Infrastructure.SessionQuestions`):** three role-cohesive interfaces — `ISessionQuestionService` (public submission only), `ISessionModerationService` (per-session queue + hide/push/reorder + IsModeratorAsync), `IAdminSessionModeratorService` (admin assign/revoke). Splitting into three rather than one omnibus service keeps each consumer's surface focused (the public endpoint only depends on the submission contract; the moderator endpoints only on the queue contract). Authorization shipped as an in-endpoint helper `SessionModeratorAuth.ResolveAuthorizedUserAsync` (lives in `SIMF.Api`, returns the actor's id when they hold the Administrator role OR have a row in `SessionModerators` for the requested session; null otherwise) — a formal `IAuthorizationRequirement` was considered and rejected as overkill for one policy that's used by exactly four endpoints. **Submission semantics:** the submission endpoint accepts any approved authenticated user (`RequireApprovedAccount`), validates the session exists + is active + is within `[StartUtc - 15m, EndUtc + 1h]` (defining "live" — pre-seating window for audience pre-submits + a post-session Q&A overrun window; tunable later as Options). The §G7 geofence guard is **deferred** — the doc-comment on `SessionQuestionService` records the seam where the geofence check will plug in once G-OI-2 resolves. **Order column race fix (simplify-pass):** the first draft computed `nextOrder = max(Order) + 1` then inserted, a classic TOCTOU race two concurrent submitters would both win. Shipped fix: **every new arrival stores `Order = 0`**; the moderator list sort is `(Order ASC, CreatedAt ASC)`, so unmoderated rows tie at 0 and `CreatedAt` (server `TimeProvider`, monotonic) breaks the tie — natural FIFO with zero race. A moderator reorder writes explicit `Order` values that win. **Reorder full-list contract (simplify-pass):** the original partial-list implementation re-numbered the supplied subset 0..n-1 and left un-listed rows at their old positions, which collided with the new indices. Tightened: the supplied id set must equal the session's full question set (count + content), validated up front, else `SESSION_QUESTION_INVALID` / 400. **Hide/unhide is idempotent**; push is one-way (re-pushing keeps the original `PushedAt`). **Endpoints:** public `POST /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}/questions`; moderator `GET /sessions/{sessionId}/questions/moderate`, `PUT /sessions/{sessionId}/questions/{questionId}/hide`, `PUT /sessions/{sessionId}/questions/{questionId}/push`, `PUT /sessions/{sessionId}/questions/reorder`; admin `POST /admin/session-moderators/list`, `POST /admin/session-moderators`, `DELETE /admin/session-moderators/{sessionId}/{userId}`. Moderator endpoints under `RequireApprovedAccount` only — the per-session role check happens inside `HandleAsync` via `SessionModeratorAuth`. Admin endpoints under `AdministratorOnly` + `RequireApprovedAccount`. **Client + BFF:** three admin methods + four moderator methods on `SimfAdminClient` (new `SendSessionsAsync` helper routing through `/api/v1/sessions/`); seven CP BFF passthroughs in `AccountEndpoints`. **CP UI:** `SessionModeratorsList.razor` at `/admin/session-moderators` (admin assign/revoke desk, Administrator-only) + `SessionModerationDesk.razor` at `/sessions/{id}/moderate` (per-session moderator's live Q&A queue). Nav adds `Module.SessionModerators` to the System group. **Contracts (`SIMF.Contracts.Sessions.SessionQuestions` + `SIMF.Contracts.Admin.SessionModerators`):** `SubmitSessionQuestionRequest`, `SessionQuestionSubmitted`, `SessionQuestionModeratorRow` (projects submitter display name + email via cross-DB merge), `SetQuestionHiddenRequest`, `ReorderQuestionsRequest`, `AssignSessionModeratorRequest`, `AdminSessionModeratorRow`. **ErrorCodes:** `SESSION_QUESTION_INVALID`, `SESSION_QUESTION_NOT_FOUND`, `SESSION_NOT_LIVE_FOR_QUESTIONS`, `SESSION_MODERATOR_NOT_ASSIGNED`, `SESSION_MODERATOR_ALREADY_ASSIGNED`. **AuditEvents:** `SessionQuestion.{Submitted,Hidden,Unhidden,Pushed,Reordered}` + `SessionModerator.{Assigned,Revoked}`. **Resx:** EN + AR parity for `Module.SessionModerators` + the full `Admin.SessionModerators.*` + `Admin.SessionModeration.*` groups (~32 keys). **Tests:** 13 new — 8 in `SessionQuestionsTests` (submit inside live window returns Order=0; submit outside window returns `SESSION_NOT_LIVE_FOR_QUESTIONS`; empty text → `SESSION_QUESTION_INVALID`; moderator queue lists with submitter projection; hide/unhide round-trip + idempotent; push marks pushed + idempotent (`PushedAt` preserved); non-admin non-moderator → 403; granted moderator can read queue without admin role) + 5 in `AdminSessionModeratorsTests` (assign returns row with projection; unknown session → 404 `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`; duplicate → 409 `SESSION_MODERATOR_ALREADY_ASSIGNED`; revoke idempotent; non-admin → 403). **Full suite 356/356 pass.** **Reviews run:** three parallel agents. **Five simplify-pass fixes landed:** (a) Order race fixed by switching to natural FIFO via `CreatedAt` tie-break — see above; (b) reorder partial-list invariant tightened — full-list-only — see above; (c) dropped unused `PushQuestionRequest` contract (the endpoint binds its own route shape, the contract type was never wired); (d) unused `token` parameter removed from test seeder helpers; (e) CP `SessionModerationDesk.razor` hard-coded "Hidden" + "Queued" English literals replaced with `Admin.SessionModeration.State.{Hidden,Queued}` resx keys for AR parity. **What G6 does NOT include yet:** the §G7 geofence guard (still BLOCKED on G-OI-2); the optimistic-concurrency story on Reorder (last-write-wins across concurrent moderators is fine at SIMF scale — one moderator per session is the norm); a "session-now-live" indicator on the session admin grid (small follow-up, not load-bearing); a `dir="auto"` on the question text cell for mixed RTL/LTR submissions (a11y refinement). | Treating the per-session moderator grant as **a permission, not a role** is the load-bearing decision. PDF §2.7.2 + §4.2 spell out that "Moderator" in this context is per-session authority on a Q&A queue — not a global authority on anything else. Inventing a global `Moderator` CP role would have collided with the existing `MobileAppRole.Moderator` (mobile-app content authority introduced by D-161) and forced a rename of one of the two; a per-resource grant on `SessionModerator(SessionId, UserId)` keeps both names in their own bounded contexts. The Administrator-bypass on `SessionModeratorAuth.ResolveAuthorizedUserAsync` matches the same pattern D-148 used for `GateOperator` — admins inherit every per-resource permission so they can step in for an absent moderator. Splitting the three service interfaces (`ISessionQuestionService`, `ISessionModerationService`, `IAdminSessionModeratorService`) by audience instead of bundling into one omnibus matches the R3.5 (D-094) precedent — narrow contracts mean each consumer's constructor signature shows the concerns the service touches. The submission window (`[Start - 15m, End + 1h]`) is opinionated but recoverable: it's hard-coded as `private static readonly TimeSpan`s with doc-comments explaining the rationale (audience pre-seating + post-talk Q&A overrun), and lifting them to Options later is one struct + one DI registration. Refusing to ship Options today is minimum-viable-diff (CLAUDE.md §17) — the values won't change before G7 lands, and G7 will need its own Options group for the geofence anyway. The Order race fix is the most consequential simplify-pass change: switching from `max+1 then insert` to `Order = 0 + tie-break by CreatedAt` is a different data model (the column no longer represents "queue position" in the unmoderated state), and a SOC analyst reading the row at rest will see `Order = 0` for every unmoderated row. Documenting that here so future debugging doesn't waste an hour wondering "why are they all zero?" — they ARE all zero by design, and the moderator's reorder writes the real positions. The reorder full-list contract is the other change worth flagging: a moderator UI that loads N rows then drags-to-reorder will always have the full set in hand, so the constraint costs the UI nothing; a partial-list API call (e.g. "move just these 3 to the top") would have to scan + shift the others manually, which is exactly the invariant the original draft got wrong. The 356/356 test pass rate (was 343/343 pre-G6; +13 from `SessionQuestionsTests` + `AdminSessionModeratorsTests`) covers the live-window check, the moderator-grant gate, the per-action audit trail, the Order-race fix (multi-submit ordering in `Submit_inside_live_window_returns_OK_with_queue_position_zero`), and the cross-DB submitter projection. |
| D-168 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase G5 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan — Public-Relations role + Invitations + VIP list + bulk-notify shipped.** PDF §2.7.3 mandates a public-relations team that manages invitations, views VIP/guest data, tracks attendance + confirmations, and dispatches guest-targeted messages. **Roles (`SIMF.Common.AppRoles`):** new baseline role `PublicRelations` added to `CpRoles` (alongside Administrator, GateOperator). Three new permissions `Invitations.Manage`, `Vips.View`, `Vips.Notify` granted to Administrator + PublicRelations and seeded idempotently by `IdentitySeeder.EnsurePermissionAsync`. **Authorization (`AuthorizationPolicies.PublicRelationsAccess`):** named policy that requires either Administrator OR PublicRelations role — Administrator inherits so an admin can also operate the invitation desk. The role is **NOT** a UserType — PublicRelations is an Admin-typed user with a narrower set of CP grants (matches the D-148 GateOperator pattern). **Open item G-OI-4 (auto-mode resolution):** the PR team shares the existing CP "System" navigation group rather than getting its own layout; documented in `CpNavigation` + the role's XML doc. Splitting later (when the PR team grows past one shared page set) is a pure CP move with no service impact. **Domain (`SIMF.Domain.PublicRelations`):** new `Invitation` entity (Id, SentByUserId logical-FK to SimfUser.Id, SentToUserProfileId real-FK to UserProfile.Id after D-167's move, State enum, Notes ≤1000, CreatedAt, RespondedAt?, UpdatedAt?, IsActive). **Enum (`SIMF.Common.Enums.InvitationState`):** brand-new enum Pending=0 / Confirmed=1 / Declined=2; no wire-contract drift to preserve. **EF (`InvitationConfiguration`):** real FK to UserProfile with `OnDelete.Restrict` (matches the rest of App DB's soft-delete policy); three query indexes — `{IsActive, State, CreatedAt}` for SOC sorts, `SentToUserProfileId` for recipient lookups, `SentByUserId` for per-rep filter. Migration `AddInvitations` on the App DB (additive, no schema migration on Identity). **Service (`AdminInvitationService` : `IAdminInvitationService`):** owns invitation CRUD + the VIP-list view + the bulk-notify dispatcher. **VIP discriminator (`SIMF.Common.VipProfileTypes`):** single canonical constant `{VVIP, VIP, Gold}` (extracted during the simplify pass — was a per-service literal that would have drifted as VIP-priority lanes get added to the Gate module later). Invitation create dispatches an in-app `NotificationKind.InvitationReceived` row to the recipient (best-effort via `TryDispatchAsync`); state transitions to Confirmed or Declined always update `RespondedAt` even on corrections (Confirmed → Declined re-stamps the latest response time); rolling back to Pending from any settled state is rejected with `INVITATION_STATE_INVALID`. NotifyVips fans out one in-app row + one queued email per recipient, drops recipients without an email to in-app only, validates every id against the VIP set (non-VIPs are silently skipped + recorded on `AdminNotifyVipsResult.SkippedProfileIds`). Batch cap 500 ids per request. All writes emit `AuditEvents.Invitation{Created,Updated,StateChanged,Deactivated}` + `Vip.NotificationSent`. **NotificationKind extension:** `InvitationReceived = 30` + `VipBroadcast = 31` (additive, matches D-111's pattern; resx EN + AR + the C# accessor updated). **Endpoints:** `POST /api/v1/admin/invitations/list`, `GET /admin/invitations/{id}`, `POST /admin/invitations`, `PUT /admin/invitations/{id}`, `DELETE /admin/invitations/{id}`, `POST /admin/vips/list`, `POST /admin/vips/notify` — all under `PublicRelationsAccess` + `RequireApprovedAccount`, writes rate-limited under `auth`. **Client + BFF:** seven `SimfAdminClient` methods + seven CP BFF passthroughs. **CP UI:** `InvitationsList.razor` (table-based grid with create + edit + cancel actions; edit modal uses a real `SimfSelect<InvitationState>` not a stringly-typed text field — simplify-pass fix) + `VipsList.razor` (selectable rows + bilingual notify modal). Nav adds `Module.Invitations` + `Module.Vips` to the System group. Both pages gate on `[Authorize(Roles = "Administrator,PublicRelations")]`. **Contracts (`SIMF.Contracts.Admin.Invitations`):** `AdminInvitationSummary`, `AdminInvitationDetail`, `AdminCreateInvitationRequest`, `AdminUpdateInvitationRequest` (opened for inheritance so the endpoint can bind `Id` from the route without a duplicate DTO indirection — simplify-pass fix), `AdminVipSummary`, `AdminNotifyVipsRequest`, `AdminNotifyVipsResult`. **ErrorCodes:** `INVITATION_INVALID`, `INVITATION_NOT_FOUND`, `INVITATION_TARGET_NOT_FOUND`, `INVITATION_STATE_INVALID`, `VIP_NOTIFY_EMPTY`, `VIP_NOTIFY_TOO_LARGE`. **Resx:** EN + AR parity for `Module.Invitations`, `Module.Vips`, the full `Admin.Invitations.*` + `Admin.Vips.*` groups (~50 keys total). **Tests (`AdminInvitationsTests`):** 10 new — create round-trips with recipient projection; create with unknown profile → 400 `INVITATION_TARGET_NOT_FOUND`; update to Confirmed sets RespondedAt + records state-change audit; update from Confirmed back to Pending → 400 `INVITATION_STATE_INVALID`; deactivate is idempotent and flips IsActive; VIP list returns only `{VVIP, VIP, Gold}` profiles; NotifyVips dispatches valid + skips non-VIP ids; empty UserProfileIds → 400 `VIP_NOTIFY_EMPTY`; non-admin/non-PR caller → 403; PublicRelations role can create invitation end-to-end (proves the role wiring). **Full suite 343/343 pass.** **Reviews run:** three parallel agents (code-reuse, code-quality, efficiency). Seven simplify-pass fixes landed: VIP names extracted to `VipProfileTypes.All`; redundant `UpdateInvitationRequest` wrapper dropped in favor of inheriting from the contract DTO; misleading `RelatedEntityType = nameof(Invitation)` removed from VipBroadcast dispatch (a broadcast is not tied to an Invitation row); `RespondedAt` now updates on every settled-state transition (correction case); CP edit modal swapped stringly-typed `SimfTextField` → typed `SimfSelect<InvitationState>` (typo would silently mis-route); four redundant `Include(ProfileType)` calls dropped (EF Core 6+ join-elision already covers the projection). **What G5 does NOT include yet:** invitation-creation does **not** send an email (the in-app notification is the primary surface; the VIP-broadcast desk is the explicit email path); the recipient picker in the Invitations Add modal currently accepts a raw UserProfile GUID (the next CP polish pass will wire a typeahead against `/admin/vips/list` once the open-item OI-4 layout question is fully resolved); the attendance-tracking surface the PDF §2.7.3 mentions piggybacks on the existing Gate-module scan log (G-OI-4 keeps this in the System group so the PR team uses the same UI). | The semantic justification for treating PublicRelations as a CP RBAC role (not a UserType) is the same one D-148 used for GateOperator: the team is admin-typed users with a narrower CP grant set, not a separate persona on the visitor/other axis. Conflating the two would have forced the JWT minter, the policy stack, and every existing admin endpoint to learn a new UserType discriminator — for zero gain. The `PublicRelationsAccess` policy is deliberately one-of-many (`Administrator OR PublicRelations`) rather than a hard "PublicRelations only" gate because admins must remain able to operate every CP surface — including the invitation desk — for support / on-call coverage. The G-OI-4 "share the System layout" default is the smaller-blast-radius choice; reversing it later is one PR (split layout + move nav items) while reversing the opposite ("invent a new layout group up front") would have meant building UI infrastructure for a team that today has one PR rep at SIMF. The VIP discriminator being a string set `{VVIP, VIP, Gold}` (rather than a column on `ProfileType` like `IsVip bool`) preserves the admin-managed lookup: the operator can rename a tier from CP without a code change, and the PR module simply reads whatever names match. The downside is that renaming "VIP" to "Premier" in the CP would break VIP filtering until someone updates `VipProfileTypes.All` — recorded as a known footgun in the constant's XML doc. The brand-new `InvitationState` enum (rather than reusing `AccountState` or `AuditOutcome`) is the right call because the lifecycle has its own vocabulary: Pending → Confirmed/Declined is not the same as Approved/Rejected (account state) or Success/Failure (audit outcome). Three values, additive-only by the freeze rules. The Invitation entity stores `SentByUserId` (the PR rep) as a logical FK to Identity DB because SimfUser lives there post-D-167; we apply the same convention D-167 baked in (cross-DB FKs are logical; same-DB FKs are real). `SentToUserProfileId` is a real FK because UserProfile is now on the same App DB as Invitation. The `OnDelete.Restrict` on that FK matches every other App DB FK — soft-delete is the universal policy, and admins do not hard-delete a UserProfile a PR invitation points at. The `RespondedAt` update rule shipped as **always update on a settled-state transition** rather than the original "only set if null" because a Confirmed → Declined correction is a new response in the PR rep's workflow — the original timestamp would have lied about when the recipient's final word was recorded. The simplify-pass caught this; it's a behavioural change worth calling out separately because it could surprise a SOC analyst looking at the audit trail. Refusing to dispatch a follow-up email on invitation create (the in-app notification is the only surface) keeps the per-invitation cost low — the VIP-broadcast desk is where the PR rep opts in to email. The 500-id batch cap on NotifyVips matches the same scale-bound D-164 used for bulk-approve. The `Include(ProfileType)` drops are a pure EF Core 6+ correctness fix: when the `Select` projects through `p.ProfileType.Name`, the navigation is auto-joined; the explicit `Include` was a no-op that read more columns than the projection needed. Three of four are pure efficiency wins (the fourth was already correct, kept the drop for consistency). The 343/343 test pass rate (was 333/333 pre-G5; +10 from `AdminInvitationsTests`) covers the role-gate, the VIP discriminator, the state-machine, the cross-DB recipient projection, and the bulk-notify dispatch + skip-recording semantic. |
| D-167 | 2026-05-29 | **Bounded-context realignment — `UserProfile`, `ProfileType`, and `Interest` (plus the `UserProfileInterests` M-to-M join) moved from `SimfIdentityDbContext` (Identity DB) to `SimfAppDbContext` (App DB).** Owner-requested ("Move then" on 2026-05-29). The previous arrangement co-located these business entities with ASP.NET Identity for FK convenience, but they are not auth concerns — they are programme/visitor data. After the move, Identity DB owns only the auth surface (`SimfUser`, `SimfRole`, `AspNetUser*`/`AspNetRole*`, `AccountCode`, `RefreshToken`, `SecondFactorToken`, `TotpRecoveryCode`, `Permission`, `RolePermission`); App DB owns business data (programme, gates, profiles, lookups). **Namespace:** the three entity files moved from `SIMF.Domain.IdentityAccess` to a new `SIMF.Domain.Profiles` namespace; the join table moves with them. **Cross-DB FKs:** `UserProfile.UserId → SimfUser.Id` is now a cross-DB **logical FK** (no SQL constraint; service-layer-validated), matching the existing pattern D-153 used for `Speaker.UserProfileId`. **Real same-DB FKs gained** (previously cross-DB / logical): `Speaker.UserProfileId → UserProfile.Id` (Restrict) and `GateProfileTypeAllow.ProfileTypeId → ProfileType.Id` (Restrict). **NOT added:** a real FK on `UserProfile.NationalityId → Country.Id` — even though Country also lives on App DB now, the existing data model allows `NationalityId = 0` on profile stubs (admin-create-user, walk-in pre-fill, test seed fixtures), and a real FK would break that pre-existing behaviour. `NationalityId` stays a logical FK validated by `UserProfileService.ResolveIdAsync`. **Service rewrites:** four services that previously did a single SQL JOIN across `SimfUser` + `UserProfile` and now do App-then-Identity round-trips — `AdminGateService.ListScansAsync` + `ListCurrentlyInsideAsync`, `GateOperatorService.GetMyDailyReportAsync` + `LoadReplayAsync` (new helper `ResolveProfileDisplayNamesAsync`), `QrResolver.ResolveAsync`, `AdminAttendeeService.ListAsync`. Each new pattern batches by user id (PK / unique-index lookup) so total latency stays sub-millisecond. **Save-changes ordering** (post-review correction): `UserProfileService.UpsertMineAsync` saves Identity inside the transaction first; the App-side commit runs **after** the transaction succeeds so an Identity-side rollback drops the profile changes too. `AdminAccountService.RegisterOnsiteAsync`, `CreateAccountAsync`, `ApproveAsync`, `RejectAsync`, and `IdentitySeeder.EnsureProfileTypeAsync` + `RenameProfileTypeIfPresentAsync` all now correctly call `appDbContext.SaveChangesAsync` for App-side rows (a code-review and architect agent both flagged the pre-fix state as silent profile-row data loss). **Migrations:** `MoveProfilesToAppDb` on Identity (drops `UserProfiles`, `Interests`, `ProfileTypes`, `UserProfileInterests`) and on App (creates them). No data-migration step — we are pre-publish per CLAUDE.md §6 freeze rules and dev/test DBs are throwaway. **Tests:** ~20 test files updated (bulk-script + manual sweep): `var appDb = scope.ServiceProvider.GetRequiredService<SimfAppDbContext>();` added next to every `SimfIdentityDbContext` scope declaration that touched the moved entities; `db.UserProfiles` → `appDb.UserProfiles` (same for ProfileTypes, Interests); `db.RowAudits` → `appDb.RowAudits` in `RowAuditTests` since Interest now audits to the App DB. **Full suite 333/333.** **Reviews run:** code-reviewer + software-architect + database-optimizer agents, three in parallel. The architect + code-reviewer both caught the silent-data-loss pattern (now fixed); the DB optimizer confirmed indexes + storage footprint (~40 MB at 20k users) + the `OnDelete.Restrict` semantics. **Known follow-up (deferred):** the cross-DB write sequence is not atomic — a crash between the Identity commit and the App commit leaves the user state-flipped but the profile un-saved. The user-facing retry is idempotent (`UserProfileService.UpsertMineAsync` loads-or-creates by `UserId`-unique index), so eventual consistency converges. A full saga/outbox pattern is the right long-term answer; tracked, not blocking. | The semantic argument the owner made is correct: `UserProfile`, `ProfileType`, and `Interest` are programme/visitor data, not authentication concerns. The old arrangement co-located them with Identity because of the single-DB FK to `SimfUser`; after D-157 split the two physical DBs that argument collapsed (`SimfUser.Id ↔ UserProfile.UserId` was already an inter-context relationship enforced in code). Moving the entities aligns the bounded contexts with the bounded responsibilities. Choosing **logical FK** on `UserProfile.UserId → SimfUser.Id` (rather than attempting cross-DB SQL referential integrity which SQL Server does not support) keeps the move pragmatic — the same pattern D-153 already accepted for `Speaker.UserProfileId`, so we are applying an established convention consistently rather than inventing a new one. **Promoting `Speaker.UserProfileId` and `GateProfileTypeAllow.ProfileTypeId` to real same-DB FKs** is a free integrity win — both rows now live in the same physical DB so a real FK costs nothing and prevents the dangling-reference class of bug. **Refusing to add the `UserProfile.NationalityId → Country.Id` real FK** is the most surprising decision and the one that got me to write this paragraph: the existing data model accepts `NationalityId = 0` on profile stubs (admin pre-creates a row before the visitor picks their country), and adding a real FK broke ~20 tests on the first run. Restoring the logical-FK behaviour is minimum-viable-diff per CLAUDE.md §17 — the move's goal was the bounded-context realignment, not a referential-integrity uplift; the latter can ship as a separate decision (with a `NationalityId int?` migration + service-layer pre-fill) when there is owner appetite. **The save-changes ordering correction** is the most important post-review fix: the architect and code-reviewer agents both spotted that the initial commit had `appDbContext.UserProfiles.Add(profile)` followed by only `dbContext.SaveChangesAsync(token)` — the App-side ChangeTracker was tracking the profile but never flushed, which would have shipped silent data loss on visitor onboarding, walk-in registration, admin approve/reject, and ProfileType seeding. The fix (save Identity first inside the transaction, App after the transaction commits) preserves the existing rollback-on-error semantic exercised by `UserProfileRollbackTests` while ensuring the App-side row is persisted on the happy path. The eventual-consistency window between the two commits is the price of a non-MSDTC cross-DB write; documented as a known follow-up rather than papered over. **Choosing to drop and re-create the four tables in one migration pair (no data copy)** is correct only because we are pre-publish — the dev/test DBs are throwaway and there are no production rows to preserve. Post-publish this exact change would require a backup + `INSERT ... SELECT` + `BCP` runbook; the migration's `Down` would need a symmetric script. Recording that boundary explicitly here so a future engineer reading the migration after publish does not assume "EF takes care of it" — it does not. The bulk-PowerShell test refactor was the practical only-realistic approach (touching ~20 files individually would have been hours of manual work); the script's only side-effect was a `identityappDbContext` typo from a too-broad regex which the build caught on the first pass and was fixed in three files. The agent-run for review caught one real bug (silent data loss) and two stale doc comments — well worth the parallel-agent budget. |
| D-166 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase G4 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan — registration gate + auto-close worker + archive visibility singletons shipped.** PDF §2.3 mandates an admin-toggleable registration open/close gate with an optional scheduled close moment; PDF §2.4 mandates an admin-toggleable archive visibility switch readable by the Flutter app + Website without authentication. **Domain (`SIMF.Domain.Operations`):** two singleton entities `RegistrationGate(Id=00..01, IsOpen, AutoCloseUtc?, LastChangedAt, LastChangedByUserId?)` and `ArchiveVisibility(Id=00..02, IsVisible, LastChangedAt, LastChangedByUserId?)`. **EF (`OperationsTogglesConfiguration`):** each table has the singleton row seeded via `HasData` so the row exists from day-one; loader has a defensive fallback that creates the row if `HasData` was dropped. Both rows are persisted on `SimfAppDbContext`. Migration `AddOperationsToggles` on the App DB (additive, D-135 freeze-lift carries forward). **Service (`OperationsToggleService` : `IOperationsToggleService`):** read + admin-write surface for both toggles. `IsRegistrationOpenAsync` is the hot-path call sign-up makes on every request: it returns false when `IsOpen=false` OR `AutoCloseUtc <= now` (no race vs. the worker — the service-side check enforces the rule even before the worker flips IsOpen). Updates write only when something actually changed (idempotent re-saves do not bump LastChangedAt or write an audit row). Audit events `RegistrationGate.Updated`, `RegistrationGate.AutoClosed`, `ArchiveVisibility.Updated`, `SignUp.RejectedRegistrationClosed`. **Background worker (`RegistrationGateAutoCloseWorker` : `BackgroundService`):** polls once per minute; flips `IsOpen=false` the first tick after `AutoCloseUtc` passes; writes the `RegistrationGate.AutoClosed` audit row with `ActorUserId=null` (the worker is not a person). The worker does not race admins: it writes only when IsOpen is still true AND AutoCloseUtc ≤ now; a concurrent admin re-open that clears AutoCloseUtc simply leaves the worker with nothing to flip. **Sign-up wiring:** `RegistrationService` gained an `IOperationsToggleService` dependency and consults the gate **before** the email-exists check — closed gate returns 403 `REGISTRATION_CLOSED`, no user row, no email enqueued. **Endpoints:** `GET /api/v1/admin/registration-gate` + `PUT /api/v1/admin/registration-gate` (admin) under `AdministratorOnly` + `RequireApprovedAccount`, writes rate-limited under `auth` policy; `GET /api/v1/archive/visibility` (**AllowAnonymous** — Flutter + Website need to read this before authenticating); `GET /api/v1/admin/archive/visibility` + `PUT /api/v1/admin/archive/visibility` (admin). **Client + BFF:** four `SimfAdminClient` methods + four CP BFF passthroughs. **CP UI:** single `/admin/operations` page (Razor) rendering both toggles in one place — IsOpen checkbox + datetime-local AutoCloseUtc input for the gate, IsVisible checkbox for the archive, both with LastChangedAt readouts and separate Save buttons. `CpNavigation.Module.OperationsToggles` added to the System group. **Contracts (`SIMF.Contracts.Admin.Operations`):** `RegistrationGateState`, `UpdateRegistrationGateRequest`, `ArchiveVisibilityState`, `UpdateArchiveVisibilityRequest`. **ErrorCodes:** `REGISTRATION_CLOSED`. **Resx:** EN + AR parity for `Module.OperationsToggles` + the full `Admin.Operations.*` group (~22 keys). **Tests (`OperationsTogglesTests`):** 7 new — admin GET returns seeded open state; closing the gate makes sign-up return 403 REGISTRATION_CLOSED; sign-up succeeds when the gate is open (regression guard); AutoCloseUtc in the past blocks sign-up even with IsOpen=true (service-side check enforces the rule before the worker ticks); public `GET /archive/visibility` requires no auth; admin can toggle archive visibility and the public endpoint reflects it; non-admin caller cannot PUT either toggle. **What G4 does NOT include yet:** the AutoCloseUtc-passed worker-flip behaviour is not directly exercised by a test (the worker runs on a 1-minute poll inside the host; `IsRegistrationOpenAsync` enforces the rule independently, so the user-visible behaviour is already covered). The CP form does not surface a "force-flip the worker now" button — the AutoCloseUtc setter is already the user-facing affordance. | Modelling the two singletons as separate tables (rather than a generic key/value `OperationsConfig` table) keeps the schema strongly typed: `AutoCloseUtc` is a real `datetimeoffset null` column, not a stringified blob inside a value column. The cost is two trivial tables vs. one; the gain is that future additions (e.g. a `MaxConcurrentSignUpsPerMinute int` knob) get their own column rather than another magic key. The singleton Id strategy (`Guid.Parse("00..01")` / `00..02`) keeps the row addressable from EF `HasData` seeding without inventing a synthetic enum-backed PK; admins never see the Id. The hot-path `IsRegistrationOpenAsync` is deliberately a single AsNoTracking query (no caching layer) because (a) sign-up is rate-limited by middleware ahead of this, so the QPS is bounded; (b) caching would create a window where a closed gate still accepts a few sign-ups, which is exactly the surprise the gate exists to prevent; (c) the SqlServer plan for a single-row PK lookup is sub-millisecond. The AutoCloseUtc enforcement is **double-belt**: the service-side check fails the sign-up the moment the moment passes, and the worker flips the persistent `IsOpen` row within a minute so the CP grid reflects the new state. Without the service-side check, a 1-minute window after AutoCloseUtc would still accept sign-ups; without the worker, the persistent `IsOpen` would lie until an admin refreshed the page. Both layers are intentional. The "no audit row when nothing changed" rule prevents an admin who hits Save twice from generating a noise audit trail; idempotency matches the broader audit-log discipline. Sign-up rejection is **403 not 503** because the gate is a deliberate policy state, not an outage — 503 would invite retry, which is wrong; 403 with a typed code lets the Flutter app render the right "registration is closed" copy without retrying. The public `/archive/visibility` endpoint is anonymous on purpose: the Flutter app + Website renders the public landing page **before** the user signs in, and gating the visibility decision behind auth would force every public surface to display the archive optimistically and then hide it after sign-in, which is worse UX than asking unauthenticated. Bundling both toggles on a single `/admin/operations` page (rather than two separate pages) matches the small surface area — both are single-row config knobs, and forcing two nav clicks for two checkboxes would be ceremony. The PageColor / icon decisions on the nav are deferred — the System group already has 15 items and adding two more nav entries would push it past the visible-without-scroll budget on a 1080p laptop. |
| D-165 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase G3 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan — Sessions module shipped end-to-end + CP `Module.Sessions` flipped from stub to real route.** PDF §2.9 mandates "الجلسات" as a first-class concept distinct from "المحاور" (Themes). **Domain (`SIMF.Domain.Programme`):** `Session` entity (Id, Code 2–16 unique, Title/TitleArabic ≤256, Description/DescriptionArabic ≤2048, real-FK `HallId` + `Hall` nav, StartUtc/EndUtc DateTimeOffset, optional `CapacityOverride int?`, IsActive, CreatedAt/UpdatedAt) + two explicit join entities `SessionSpeaker` (composite PK SessionId+SpeakerId + DisplayOrder for primary/supporting ordering) and `SessionTheme` (composite PK SessionId+ThemeId). **EF (`SessionConfiguration` + `SessionSpeakerConfiguration` + `SessionThemeConfiguration` under `Persistence/Configurations/App`):** real FK to Hall with `OnDelete.Restrict` (matches the existing soft-delete policy — admins deactivate halls, never hard-delete a hall a session points at), join sides cascade from Session, restrict from Speaker/Theme. Two query indexes the agenda screen + operator views ride: `{IsActive, StartUtc}` and `{HallId, StartUtc}`. **Migration `AddSessions` on `SimfAppDbContext`** — additive, single migration, no `__EFMigrationsHistory` divergence (carries D-135 freeze-lift). **Service (`AdminSessionService` : `IAdminSessionService`):** List with search across Code/Title/TitleArabic + `isActive` and `hallId` filters; Get returns the join graph fully populated; Create/Update validate (Code 2–16 unique, Title 1–256 EN+AR, end > start, capacity ≥ 0 or null), resolve Hall (must exist + IsActive — else `SessionHallNotFound`/400), check all SpeakerIds exist + IsActive (else `SessionSpeakerNotFound`/400; duplicate speaker in same payload → `SessionInvalid`/400), check all ThemeIds exist + IsActive (else `SessionThemeNotFound`/400). Duplicate code on Create or rename-to-existing on Update → `SessionCodeDuplicate`/409. Update replaces the speaker + theme link sets atomically. Deactivate is idempotent. All three writes emit `AuditEvents.Session{Created,Updated,Deactivated}`. **Effective capacity rule (PDF §2.9):** `EffectiveCapacity = CapacityOverride ?? Hall.Capacity` — most sessions inherit; only reconfigured rooms override. **Endpoints:** `POST /api/v1/admin/sessions/list`, `GET /admin/sessions/{id}`, `POST /admin/sessions`, `PUT /admin/sessions/{id}`, `DELETE /admin/sessions/{id}` under `AdministratorOnly` + `RequireApprovedAccount`, writes rate-limited under `auth` policy. **Client + BFF:** `SimfAdminClient.{List,Get,Create,Update,Deactivate}SessionAsync` + five CP BFF passthroughs in `AccountEndpoints`. **CP UI:** `SessionsList.razor` (multiselect grid with Code/Title/TitleArabic/Hall/StartUtc/EndUtc/Capacity/Status columns + Details modal) + `SessionForm.razor` (Hall picker lazily loaded from `/admin/halls/list`, Speaker multi-picker with up/down/remove reorder buttons that renumber DisplayOrder, Theme multi-picker as chips, datetime-local inputs for StartUtc/EndUtc treated as UTC). `CpNavigation.Module.Sessions` flipped from `/m/sessions` IsStub to real `/admin/sessions`. **Contracts (`SIMF.Contracts.Admin.Sessions`):** `AdminSessionSummary`, `AdminSessionDetail`, `AdminSessionSpeakerEntry(SpeakerId, Name, NameArabic, DisplayOrder)`, `AdminCreateSessionRequest`, `AdminUpdateSessionRequest`.
