Hall's seat count is exposed as `HallCapacity` (matches the Hall entity's actual `Capacity` field name). **ErrorCodes:** `SessionInvalid`, `SessionNotFound`, `SessionCodeDuplicate`, `SessionInvalidTimeWindow`, `SessionHallNotFound`, `SessionSpeakerNotFound`, `SessionThemeNotFound`. **Resx:** EN + AR parity for the full `Admin.Sessions.*` group (≈70 keys). **Tests (`AdminSessionsTests`):** 8 new — create with hall + default capacity inherits; create with capacity override wins over hall capacity; unknown hall → 400 `SessionHallNotFound`; end ≤ start → 400 `SessionInvalidTimeWindow`; duplicate code → 409 `SessionCodeDuplicate`; speakers + themes persist and round-trip in detail (with DisplayOrder); deactivate is idempotent + flips IsActive; non-admin → 403. **Full suite 326/326 pass.** **What G3 does NOT include yet:** the public session page on the Website + Flutter agenda binding to the new Session table (separate work — the admin CRUD is the prerequisite); the session-question moderator workflow (lands as G6 once `SessionQuestion` ships). | Sessions are the highest-impact PDF §2.9 gap because the Mockup's entire agenda screen, the booking module, and the future SessionQuestion thread all bind to this entity — shipping the admin CRUD first means every later increment binds to a real table rather than a stub. Storing the join sets as **explicit** entities (SessionSpeaker, SessionTheme) instead of implicit M-to-M follows the D-148 GateProfileTypeAllow precedent: the join row carries metadata (DisplayOrder for primary/supporting speakers) and the explicit type makes future surface additions (e.g. "is this speaker the chair?" boolean) cheap. The capacity override defaults to NULL precisely because the PDF §2.9 case — "most sessions inherit the hall, a few reconfigured rooms override" — would otherwise pollute every row with a duplicate of the Hall capacity. The effective-capacity computation lives in the service projection rather than the entity to keep the entity dumb and the rule in one place. Choosing `OnDelete.Restrict` for the Hall FK matches the existing soft-delete policy across the rest of the programme module (Speaker→Country was also Restrict in D-153) — admins deactivate, they never hard-delete a referenced row. The `SessionSpeakerNotFound` / `SessionThemeNotFound` error codes are deliberately distinct from `SessionInvalid` because the front-end needs to know whether to re-load the speaker / theme picker (the row was deactivated mid-edit) or surface a generic validation error. The CP form's Up/Down DisplayOrder buttons (instead of drag-and-drop) match the SimfDataGrid + SimfSelect language already used across the CP — drag-and-drop would have required a new Component and JS interop hook just to reorder a list that typically holds 1–4 speakers. The Theme picker is a flat multi-select rather than a primary-vs-secondary split because the PDF §2.9 only mentions "themes belong to a session" — promoting one as primary is not a stated requirement, and we can add it as an additive column later without breaking the wire. Flipping `Module.Sessions` from the `/m/sessions` stub route to the real `/admin/sessions` route in this same commit closes the "navigation lies about what exists" debt that piles up when stub routes survive past the moment their destination is real. |
| D-164 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase G2 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan — bulk-approve "Select All" affordance shipped + Moderator-naming clarification documented.** PDF §2.7.1 mandates a "Select all" bulk-approve for the security team. **Service:** `IAdminUserApprovalService` gains `BulkApproveVisitorsAsync` and `BulkApproveOthersAsync`, both returning a typed `AdminBulkApprovalResponse(Approved, Skipped, Failures[])`. Per-subject failures are captured (with the typed `ReasonCode`, English + Arabic message) and do not block the rest of the batch. Server clamps the batch at 500 distinct ids per request to bound the transaction window. **Endpoints:** `POST /api/v1/admin/visitors/bulk-approve` and `POST /api/v1/admin/others/bulk-approve` under `AdministratorOnly` + `RequireApprovedAccount`, rate-limited under the existing `auth` policy. Empty `Ids[]` is `AdminBulkActionInvalid`/400. **Client + BFF:** new `SimfAdminClient.BulkApprove{Visitors,Others}Async` methods + two CP BFF passthroughs. **CP UI:** `SimfDataGrid` gains a new `OnApproveSelected` `EventCallback<IReadOnlyList<TItem>>` parameter + `ApproveSelectedLabel` string parameter. Toolbar renders an "Approve selected" button when the parent supplies the handler, enabled only when ≥ 1 row on the current page is selected. `PendingVisitors.razor` and `PendingOthers.razor` wire `OnApproveSelected="OnBulkApproveAsync"` with localised labels. Toast surfaces `Approved {N} / Skipped {M}`. **Resx:** EN + AR parity for `Admin.Pending.Action.BulkApprove` and `Admin.Pending.BulkApproved.Toast`. **Tests:** `AdminBulkApprovalTests` — 4 new (3-subject happy path → all flip to Approved with FK verified in DB; one good + one unknown id → 1 approved / 1 failure with the unknown id surfaced in `Failures[0].UserId`; empty array → 400 `AdminBulkActionInvalid`; non-admin → 403). **Doc:** MAA-001 §8.1.1 added — disambiguates `MobileAppRole.Moderator` (mobile-app content authority, global JWT claim) from the **session-question moderator** (per-session permission, لقب المحاور in the PDF, lands in gap-plan phase G3). Both authorities can coexist on one user; the Flutter app must not infer one from the other. **Full suite 318/318 pass.** **What G2 does NOT include yet:** the session-question moderator workflow itself — the `Session` entity does not exist yet, and the per-session permission grant requires it. That work is scheduled in phase G3 alongside the Sessions module. | The bulk-approve service deliberately processes each subject in its own atomic step (rather than one big transaction) so a single bad row does not roll back the rest of the batch — a 500-row "Select All" with three already-rejected subjects should still approve the 497 valid ones. Returning the per-subject failures by id + email + bilingual reason matches the audit-log pattern (one row per subject) and gives the CP enough information to render inline errors next to the rows that did not flip. Adding `OnApproveSelected` to `SimfDataGrid` as a new event (rather than reusing `CustomToolbar`) keeps the toolbar layout consistent across grids — the bulk-approve button sits next to the bulk-delete button by design. The change is additive: every existing grid that doesn't pass the handler renders identically. The MAA-001 §8.1.1 callout is the most important defensive doc-write of this commit: the PDF and D-161 use the same English label "Moderator" for two genuinely different authorities, and a future engineer reading either document alone would silently conflate them. The doc table forces the disambiguation up to the surface. Deliberately deferring the session-question moderator implementation to G3 honours `CLAUDE.md` §17 minimum-viable-diff — that work needs the `Session` entity which does not yet exist, and inventing it just to host one role grant would be the wrong order. The 500-id batch cap is sized for the SIMF event scale (a typical pending queue runs in the dozens to low hundreds, not thousands) and bounds the transaction window without artificially capping the UX. |
| D-163 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase G1 of the SIMF-Requirements-PDF-Gap plan shipped — `UserProfile.JobTitle` column + seeder defaults for `Media` and `Sponsor` Other-tier profile types + CPD-001 Theme-vs-Session clarification + FDS-002 OI-6 / OI-9 status updates.** **Schema:** `UserProfile.JobTitle string?` (nullable, ≤128) on `SimfIdentityDbContext`. Migration `AddJobTitleToUserProfile` applied to dev. **Contract surface:** field threaded through `UpsertUserProfileRequest`, `UserProfileResponse`, `AdminWalkInRegistrationRequest`, `AdminUserProfileView`, `PendingProfileResponse`. Service writes in `UserProfileService.UpsertMineAsync` and `AdminAccountService.RegisterOnsiteAsync`; service reads in `AdminApprovalReadService.GetFullProfileAsync` + `GetAsync`. Validators (`UpsertUserProfileRequestValidator` + `AdminWalkInRegistrationRequestValidator`) cap at 128 chars with a bilingual error message. **UI:** Website `/account/profile` shows the picker after the English-name field; CP `WalkInRegistrationForm` adds the picker in the same place; resx EN + AR for `Account.Profile.JobTitle{,Hint}` and `Admin.WalkIn.Field.JobTitle{,Hint}`. **Seeder:** `IdentitySeeder.SeedProfileTypesAsync` ships two new Other-tier rows — `Media (إعلامي, PageColor #F59E0B amber, MobileAppRole=None)` and `Sponsor (راعي, PageColor #8B5CF6 purple, MobileAppRole=None)`. Both are display categories with no operational authority — a sponsor representative is not automatically a gate operator. **Docs:** `SIMF-CPD-001` table clarifies that **Theme** = pillar (editorial grouping + colour token) and **Session** = scheduled run-of-show talk (HallId + time window + speakers); the two coexist and the PDF §2.9 "Agenda → Sessions" rename applies on public surfaces only (CP labels were already "Sessions"). `SIMF-FDS-002` OI-6 is updated to "partially closed" (Other-tier seeds added; Visitor-tier still owner-pick) and OI-9 is updated to "mandated by D-162, scheduled phase G2". **Tests:** all 314 pass under the new column. No new tests added — the column is read/write pass-through, the existing UpsertUserProfileRequest round-trip exercises the path. **Files touched:** 13 code, 4 resx (2 EN + 2 AR), 2 docs, 1 decisions-log. | The JobTitle column is the smallest schema delta that closes a PDF requirement (§2.6 "المسمى الوظيفي اختياري") end-to-end across every read/write seam — domain, EF, contract, service, validator, UI, resx. Treating it as a single G1 sub-phase rather than parking it as a deferred enhancement matches §17 minimum-viable-diff: the column is shipping anyway; threading it now is cheaper than reopening every layer later. Seeding `Media` and `Sponsor` as canonical Other-tier types matches the PDF §2.5 owner-quoted set (`إعلامي`, `راعي`) — these are the only two PDF-named Other subtypes beyond `Staff`, and they ship with `MobileAppRole=None` precisely because the PDF assigns them no operational authority. The CPD-001 Theme-vs-Session clarification preempts a real bug class — a future engineer reading "Theme" and "Session" as synonyms would corrupt the agenda-rename work in phase G3. Updating the two FDS-002 open items in this commit (rather than waiting until G2 actually ships the bulk-approve) keeps the canonical OI table in sync with the gap-doc plan; the alternative — leaving OI-9 as "decide whether" while the gap doc says "scheduled" — would leave the truth split across two documents. The G1 commit deliberately does not start G2 (bulk-approve) or G3 (Sessions module) work, even though both are next in the gap doc, because they are bigger ships with their own decision logs and should not be conflated with the G1 quick-wins commit. |

| D-161 | 2026-05-29 | **Other-ProfileType → mobile-role mapping shipped as data — `ProfileType.MobileAppRole` admin-curated column + JWT `mobile_app_role` claim. Closes the first of the two §8.5 / staff-app blocking decisions.** **Enum (`SIMF.Common.Enums.MobileAppRole`)** — None (0) / Visitor (1) / Staff (2) / Moderator (3). Numeric values are stable, persisted on `ProfileType.MobileAppRole`, and travel as the stringly-typed `mobile_app_role` JWT claim. **Domain column** — `ProfileType.MobileAppRole MobileAppRole` (non-nullable, defaults to `None`). EF persists as the enum-name string with `HasDefaultValue(MobileAppRole.None)` so the migration backfills any existing rows safely. **Migration** — `AddProfileTypeMobileAppRole` on `SimfIdentityDbContext` adds one nvarchar(16) column with default `None`. **Seeder default** — `Staff` (Other) is seeded with `MobileAppRole.Staff` out of the box; the other operational types (Volunteer / Programme Coordinator / Operations Lead) are admin-seeded at runtime per event, not hardcoded — keeps the "data, not code" promise. **JWT pipeline** — `IJwtTokenService.CreateAccessToken` gained a `MobileAppRole` parameter; both callers (`SignInService.IssueTokensAsync`, `SessionService.RefreshAsync`) resolve it via the new `IUserProfileService.ResolveMobileAppRoleAsync(userId, ct)`. Resolver logic: `Visitor → MobileAppRole.Visitor` (from UserType, not from a ProfileType row); `Admin → MobileAppRole.None`; `Other → profile.ProfileType.MobileAppRole` (or `None` when no profile type assigned). The Flutter app reads the claim once at sign-in; no extra round-trip to a lookup endpoint. **Admin CRUD** — `AdminCreateProfileTypeRequest` / `AdminUpdateProfileTypeRequest` gained an optional `MobileAppRole` string field (defaults to `None`). `AdminProfileTypeSummary` exposes it back. `AdminProfileTypeCommandService.ParseMobileAppRole` rejects unknown values and explicit `Visitor` (which is resolved from UserType, never from a ProfileType row) with a typed 400. The endpoint `UpdateAdminProfileTypeRouteRequest` forwards the new field to the service request DTO. **Tests** — `MobileAppRoleTests` (6 new): CRUD round-trip carries the value; default-None when omitted; explicit `Visitor` rejected 400; update mutates the value; Visitor sign-in JWT carries `mobile_app_role=Visitor`; Admin sign-in JWT carries `mobile_app_role=None`. **Deferred to a follow-up commit:** the CP form / picker for editing `MobileAppRole` (admin can already edit it via the API; the picker UI is a separate concern), the resx labels, and the SIMF-FDS-002 §8.5 doc closure note that references this entry. **Full suite 314/314.** | Choosing "data, not code" for the mapping is the single most important decision here: the alternative — hardcoding a per-ProfileType-name switch in the JWT issuer — would have shipped a list that goes stale the first time an admin renames a profile type or adds a new operational one. The cost (one nvarchar column + one admin field) is trivial; the saved future bug is real ("after I created the Lead Coordinator ProfileType last week, why don't they have Moderator authority in the app?"). Rejecting `Visitor` as a settable value on a ProfileType row is honest about the data model: Visitor authority is computed from `UserType`, not assigned per ProfileType. Letting it through the API would have created two sources of truth for the same value. The JWT claim plumbing went through `IUserProfileService` rather than a new `IMobileAppRoleResolver` because the resolver method is exactly one DB query and the interface already owns "things you ask about a user's profile" — adding a third abstraction for one method violates §17 minimum-viable-diff. The CP-UI / resx deferral is the same calibration: admins can curate via API right now (Postman, curl, or the eventual CP form), and shipping the form requires a meaningful UI design pass that's not on the staff-app critical path. The data layer is the bottleneck; the picker is convenience. Numeric stability of the enum values matters because we'll likely persist them in claim caches and downstream-mobile event-bus envelopes; ordering them None=0 → Moderator=3 lets `MobileAppRole > Staff` be a meaningful authority comparison if the Flutter app ever needs to express "this surface requires ≥ Moderator." Setting `Visitor = 1` between `None` and `Staff` is deliberate: an unauthenticated request is Guest (= 0 in the Flutter app's own enum) and a signed-in but un-elevated user is Visitor; both are below operational tiers. |
| D-160 | 2026-05-29 | **`POST /api/v1/gates/{gateId}/visitors/list` — cursor-paged staff-app view of scans at a single gate, backed by the D-158 snapshot columns. Approved wire contract.** Closes the second of the two open §8.5 / staff-app blocking decisions from the owner. **Request shape:** opaque base64-JSON `Cursor`, client-hint `PageSize` (server-clamped 1..200, default 50), optional `Direction` / `Outcome` / `SinceUtc` / `UntilUtc` filters. `Outcome` defaults to `Allowed` when null — the "who's at this gate right now" view is the staff app's primary use case, so the default matches it. **Response shape:** `Items` (snapshot-backed identity fields — `DisplayName`, `ProfileTypeName`, plus `QrIdAtScan`, `UserProfileId`, `Direction`, `Outcome`, `DenialReasonCode`), opaque `NextCursor` (null when no more), `AsOfUtc` server clock for clock-skew detection on the polling staff app. **Cursor strategy:** single `lastSeenScanId` encoded as base64 JSON `{"lastId":N}`; the JSON wrapper means the wire format can grow without breaking older clients. WHERE-clause is `Id > cursor` on the existing `IX_GateScan_UserProfile_ScannedAt` ordering — bigint identity PK is monotonic, no fragmentation under inserts. **Refresh behaviour:** same endpoint, same cursor — staff app polls every ~10s with the previous response's `NextCursor`; server returns only items with `Id > lastSeenScanId`. No separate "delta" endpoint, no WebSocket today. **Snapshot fields, not JOIN.** `DisplayName` and `ProfileTypeName` come straight from D-158's `GateScan.ScannedDisplayName` / `ScannedProfileTypeName` columns; the endpoint never reaches across to `SIMF_Identity`. Zero cross-DB latency, zero stale-data risk under cross-DB drift. **Authorization:** `GatesViewOwnReports` policy (same as `GET /gates/my-reports/today`); the service-layer assignment check returns `GateNotFound`/404 for an unknown gate and `NotAssigned`/403 for a known gate the caller doesn't operate — same shape as `POST /scans` already uses. **PII excluded by design:** no email, no national-id, no phone, no avatar URL in the list view — staff at a gate confirming "Ahmad walked in" doesn't need those fields, and dragging them through every poll would 10x the response size for no UX gain. A per-scan detail endpoint (out of scope here) can return the richer envelope when an operator taps a row. **Malformed-cursor handling:** treated as no cursor, first page returned (rather than 400). Friendlier under the operator-poll loop — a corrupted cursor recovers automatically instead of failing every subsequent poll until the app restarts. **Tests:** 7 new (`GateVisitorsListTests`) covering snapshot fields populated, cursor paging end-to-end (5 scans / pageSize 2 → 3 pages), default `Allowed` filter excludes denied, explicit `Denied` returns only denied, operator-not-assigned 403, unknown-gate 404, malformed cursor → first page. **Full suite 308/308.** | The endpoint design is a direct application of every prior decision in this arc: D-148 (the gate engine), D-149 (the operator surface), D-157 (the split DBs), D-158 (the snapshot columns). The snapshot pattern was specifically motivated by this exact endpoint — without it, the list view would have to JOIN across `SIMF_App.GateScans` and `SIMF_Identity.UserProfiles` per row, which under physical separation is two queries plus an in-process merge, scaling badly. The snapshot makes the read a single-table query against an already-indexed column. Cursor-over-offset is non-negotiable for an append-only log polled continuously: offset pagination breaks under concurrent inserts (rows shift between pages), but `lastSeenScanId` is stable under any number of new inserts. The JSON-wrapped cursor (vs a bare integer) buys forward compatibility for ~zero cost: a future increment that needs to also encode "scanned-by user" or "filter snapshot" can add fields without breaking older clients that only know `lastId`. The Allowed-as-default-outcome is a usage-shaped decision, not a generic one — "show me everyone inside" is the primary need; "show me everyone refused entry" is the secondary need (and gets its own `Outcome=Denied` parameter); "show me both" is the rare reconciliation need (and gets a client-side merge). Excluding PII from the list shape is the same calibration: forensic review wants the richer envelope, operational glance wants display-name + outcome + time. Different views, different shapes. The 403-vs-404 split for "wrong gate" vs "unknown gate" mirrors the `POST /scans` decision (D-148): leaking "gate X exists, you're not assigned" is safer than leaking "gate X does not exist" via timing differences, and the existing failure-rate circuit + audit log already track both cases at the engine layer. The malformed-cursor-is-not-fatal decision is operations ergonomics: a polling client that crashes on its first malformed response will keep crashing every 10s forever; one that quietly resets to first page recovers itself. |
| D-159 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase 3 — `Speaker.CountryId → Country.Id` promoted to a real DB-enforced foreign key.** Both rows live in the App database (`SIMF_App`), so unlike the cross-DB references discussed in D-157 this one *can* be a real `FOREIGN KEY` constraint. EF config gains `HasOne<Country>().WithMany().HasForeignKey(speaker => speaker.CountryId).OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Restrict)` in `SpeakerConfiguration`; the existing manual `HasIndex(speaker.CountryId)` is removed because `HasForeignKey` auto-creates the FK index. Migration `AddSpeakerCountryForeignKey` emits a clean single-statement DDL: `ALTER TABLE [Speakers] ADD CONSTRAINT [FK_Speakers_Countries_CountryId] FOREIGN KEY ([CountryId]) REFERENCES [Countries]([Id]) ON DELETE NO ACTION`. **`OnDelete=Restrict`** matches the soft-delete policy admins already use for the Countries CRUD (D-155) — deactivation via `IsActive=false`, never hard delete. **Application-side existence check** in `AdminSpeakerService.EnsureCountryIsValidAsync` stays for now (catches the bad id earlier in the request and translates to a typed `SpeakerInvalid`/400 instead of relying on EF surface a 500 from the FK violation); a future commit can drop the pre-check once the global error middleware learns to translate `SqlException 547` (FK violation) into a typed `SpeakerInvalid` envelope. Migration applied to dev DB; all 301 tests pass; 0 warnings / 0 errors solution-wide. | Promoting same-DB logical FKs to real constraints is non-negotiable engineering: the cross-DB cases lost FK enforcement to architecture (D-157), but the same-DB cases never had that excuse. The app-side existence check stays as defence-in-depth and as the *primary* error-translation site — a 400 with `SPEAKER_INVALID` and a bilingual message is a better caller experience than a 500 from an unwrapped SqlException, even though both block the bad write. Removing the redundant `HasIndex(speaker.CountryId)` matches EF's auto-index-on-FK behaviour; carrying the manual line would mean the migration tries to create an index that already exists (or worse, two indexes on the same column). The two-explicit-OnDelete-passes pattern (Restrict here, SetNull elsewhere) is the right default for SIMF: data-loss-on-delete is reversible-via-restore at best and silent-corruption at worst, so the DB blocks it and forces the admin to deactivate explicitly. Closing the freeze-removal arc here: D-110 frozen → D-151 freeze removed → D-152/153 cross-context FKs (logical) → D-157 split DBs → D-158 snapshot pattern → D-159 same-DB FK promoted to real. The persistence layer is now where it should have been before the freeze: every FK that can be a real DB constraint is one; every FK that crosses a physical-DB boundary is a logical reference backed by a snapshot. |
| D-158 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase 2 — dual-reference snapshot pattern for action logs across the split-DB boundary.** Three log entities in the App DB now carry frozen-at-write-time snapshots of the user identity their loose Guid references point at (across the physical DB boundary to Identity). **`GateScan`** — new `ScannedDisplayName` (≤128) + `ScannedProfileTypeName` (≤128) columns, both nullable (null when QR didn't resolve). Filled from the existing `QrResolution` / `DenialContext` data the engine already builds for the wire response; one shared `BuildScan` helper carries them through both the allowed and denied paths. **`OperationLogEntry`** — new `SubjectDisplayName` (≤128) + `ActorDisplayName` (≤128) columns. `AuditEntry` (the SIMF.Application DTO) gains matching optional fields so callers that have the names already (admin services that just looked up the user) can pass them; the audit log falls back to the JWT `display_name` claim for the actor when the caller didn't supply one. **`RowAudit`** — new `ActorDisplayName` (≤128) column on both the Identity-side and App-side row-audit tables. `RowAuditingSaveChangesInterceptor` reads it from `IRequestContext.ActorDisplayName` (new property), which sources the JWT `display_name` claim — zero per-write DB query, no caller changes needed. **Test sweep:** all 301 tests pass after both migrations applied across the two physical DBs. **What this buys us:** an audit reviewer can read "Ahmad Salem scanned at Gate G-MAIN-1 at 14:03 UTC" directly off the App-DB row, even if the linked `UserProfile` in `SIMF_Identity` has since been renamed (e.g. legal name change) or deleted (e.g. GDPR forget request). The audit row tells you who Ahmad *was* at the moment of the action, not who he *is* now — which is the correct property for an audit log. **What the snapshot does NOT do:** it doesn't replace the `UserProfileId` / `SubjectUserId` / `ActorUserId` loose pointers — those stay for "show me every scan by user X" queries that should follow the current identity. The two columns serve different questions and live together. | The snapshot pattern is the audit-design industry default for cross-domain references (cf. event sourcing, financial audit logs, healthcare HIPAA logs): **always store both the FK and a name snapshot**, because the FK answers "who is this person (today)" and the snapshot answers "who were they at the moment of the action." For SIMF the cross-DB physical split (D-157) makes the FK answer cross-DB and therefore loose — but that doesn't change the snapshot's purpose. Even under one shared DB the snapshot would still be the right call for audit-grade logs. Sourcing the actor's display name from the JWT `display_name` claim rather than a per-write DB query is a perf decision: `RowAuditingSaveChangesInterceptor` fires on every SaveChanges across the system, so a synchronous cross-DB lookup on the hot path would be measurable. The JWT claim is a frozen-at-token-issue snapshot too — which is fine for our retention window because tokens expire in 30 minutes per JwtOptions and a name change forces a re-login (security_stamp invalidation). The decision to make `SubjectDisplayName` on `AuditEntry` optional rather than required came from §17 minimum-viable-diff: requiring it would force ~30 audit-write call sites to look up the name before logging, and most of those call sites already have an email + user-id, so the column is rarely null-and-also-meaningfully-missing anyway. A future commit could backfill from those call sites; today the column is the right shape for the future fill. The `BuildScan` helper signature grew by two parameters rather than `DenialContext` being inlined into it — the existing 8-arg signature was already at the noise ceiling, but adding two strings is acceptable when each name is well-bounded and the alternative (passing the whole `DenialContext`) couples the helper to a private struct it doesn't need to know about. |
| D-157 | 2026-05-29 | **Identity and App data fully separated into two physical SQL Server databases (`SIMF_Identity` + `SIMF_App`); cross-DB references stay logical; snapshot pattern queued for action logs in Phase 2.** Owner direction after rejecting Option A (one DB / two contexts with cross-context FKs via lookup configuration) and explicitly choosing physical separation: "First totaly seprate Identify DB from DataDb." **Connection string split:** `SimfDb` → `SimfIdentityDb` + `SimfAppDb` in `appsettings.json` + `appsettings.Development.json`. DI rebinds (`AddInfrastructure`) read both strings, throw a typed `InvalidOperationException` on either missing. Each context still owns its own migration history table (`__EFMigrationsHistory_Identity` / `__EFMigrationsHistory_App`) — now in its own physical database. **Design-time factories** updated to fall back to `SIMF_Identity` / `SIMF_App` on `Server=.` and to read context-specific env vars (`SIMF_DESIGN_TIME_IDENTITY_CONNECTION`, `SIMF_DESIGN_TIME_APP_CONNECTION`); the legacy `SIMF_DESIGN_TIME_CONNECTION` is kept as a tier-2 fallback for compatibility with existing dev scripts. **Migrations regenerated as fresh `InitialModel` per context** — the prior 7-migration chain (Identity InitialCreate + App InitialCreate / AddThemes / AddHalls / AddGates / AddSpeakers / AddCountries / EnhanceSpeakers) collapsed into one `InitialModel` migration per context capturing the current model state. No migration content changed semantically; the prior incremental chain was a historical artefact of when each module landed during the freeze-removal arc, never deployed to production, so a clean baseline is the right move. **Test fixture (`SimfApiFactory`)** now provisions two throwaway LocalDB databases per test class (`SIMF_Test_Identity_xxx` + `SIMF_Test_App_xxx`) and tears both down on dispose. **All 301 tests pass under the new architecture**, 0 warnings / 0 errors at solution level. **Cross-DB references remain logical FKs**: `UserProfile.NationalityId → Country.Id`, `Speaker.UserProfileId → UserProfile.Id`, `GateProfileTypeAllow.ProfileTypeId → ProfileType.Id` — none of these can be DB-enforced under physical separation because SQL Server has no cross-database FK constraint syntax. **Phase 2 queued (next commit):** snapshot pattern for action logs in App DB (`GateScan`, `OperationLogEntry`, `RowAudit`) — each row gains `*DisplayName` / `*ProfileTypeName` columns filled at write time so the audit log is self-contained and survives even if the linked Identity row is deleted or renamed. **Phase 3 queued:** same-DB real FK `Speaker.CountryId → Country.Id` (both in App, no cross-DB issue) added with `OnDelete=Restrict`. **What stays logical only (intentionally):** every cross-DB reference, because the snapshot pattern (not the FK) is the correct mitigation for cross-DB integrity. | Two real reasons to physically separate the databases that the owner weighed and accepted: (a) **security boundary at the persistence layer** — the Identity DB can be backed up, encrypted, and access-controlled on a different policy than the App DB; a compromise of one doesn't immediately expose the other's contents; (b) **deployment independence** — auth-side schema and event-data-side schema can be migrated, restored, or rolled back on different cadences. The cost the owner accepted: every cross-DB reference becomes a logical FK that the application layer must defend. This is honest engineering — the snapshot pattern (Phase 2) replaces the lost FK enforcement with a stronger audit property anyway (historical truth: who was this person *at the time of the action*, not who are they *now*). The clean `InitialModel` regeneration also matches the spirit of the D-110 freeze being removed — no production data exists yet, so a single migration captures the schema baseline without the per-decision archaeology a deployed system would require. The earlier Option-A work (lookup configurations + cross-context `HasForeignKey` calls) was reverted because under Option C those configurations are no longer valid: `ExcludeFromMigrations` only works when both contexts share a physical DB; under physical separation, EF on the Identity side has no way to issue an FK referencing a table in a different physical SQL Server database. The dev environment is now bootstrapped via `dotnet ef database update` against each context independently, which the design-time factory fix unblocked (it was previously hardcoding the localdb / `SIMF` single-DB fallback and silently merging both contexts into the same physical database — a footgun that surfaced as "There is already an object named 'AspNetRoles' in the database" the moment Phase 1 tried to apply migrations). The two-tier env var fallback in the design-time factories (`SIMF_DESIGN_TIME_IDENTITY_CONNECTION` → `SIMF_DESIGN_TIME_CONNECTION` → hardcoded dev default) protects existing dev scripts that set the single legacy variable while letting new scripts use the explicit per-context names. |

Findings: (1) **`/admin/countries` list** rendered correctly — 57 seeded rows visible, columns ISO ID / Code / Name (EN+AR) / Dial code / Order / Status all populated; SA = 682 / +966, AE = 784 / +971, BH = 48 / +973, all matching the AddCountries seed (`d155-countries-list.png`). For the Id field on `CountryForm`, also collapsed the Add/Edit branches into a single `@bind-Value="_idInput"` controlled by `Disabled="@(_busy || Initial is not null)"` — same behaviour, no branch duplication, no separate read-only display. (3) **Re-smoke after fix:** both modals open cleanly, console clean, all controls present. `d155-countries-add-modal.png` shows the ISO id spinbutton with the "682 = SA" helper text. `d155-speakers-add-modal.png` shows the full bilingual form including the Country picker pre-populated with all 57 seeded countries ("Saudi Arabia (SA)", "United Arab Emirates (AE)", etc.) and the two consent checkboxes labelled with the user-quoted Arabic ("Allow meeting requests (السماح بطلب مقابلة)" / "Allow data sharing (السماح بمشاركة البيانات)"). (4) **CP rebuild step:** the running CP process held the bin DLLs; classifier blocked the proactive kill (correctly), owner stopped the process, rebuild and relaunch succeeded. **Two side-findings worth flagging (not blocking):** (a) the `SimfDataGrid` summary formatter shows "Showing 2–51 of 57" instead of "Showing 1–50 of 57" — an off-by-one that exists on every grid I copied from (ThemesList, HallsList) so it's a pre-existing `SimfDataGrid` bug, not new in D-155; (b) the seed for the `SimfAppDbContext.SuperAdmin` user landed with `PasswordChangeRequired=true` (H4 enforcement from a prior commit) and the dev DB needed a one-off `UPDATE AspNetUsers SET PasswordChangeRequired=0 WHERE Email=...` to unblock the smoke — this is fine for dev but the seeder should clear the flag on the super-admin (whose password is owner-managed) rather than treating them like a self-registered user. | The bug pattern is one I'd not seen before in this repo because every prior form (`ThemeForm`, `HallForm`, `GateForm`, `InterestForm`, `RoleForm`) used `@bind-Value` exclusively for `SimfTextField`. When the field needed manual change-handling logic (e.g. trimming), the convention was to put the trim in the model setter or in `HandleSubmitAsync`, not in a separate `ValueChanged` callback. My `CountryForm` and `SpeakerForm` mixed both styles inside one form because the read-only Id-on-edit case naturally wants `Value` without binding, and once that pattern was in the file I propagated it to the other inputs that didn't actually need manual change-handling — a copy-paste drift. The fix proves the pattern: every input that can change should use `@bind-Value`. The smoke caught a class of bug that the test suite would never have caught — `AdminCountriesTests` + `AdminSpeakersTests` only exercise the HTTP layer, never render the Blazor form — exactly the reason CLAUDE.md §17 makes the live-browser smoke a hard gate before declaring a UI feature done. Adding a `simfDataGrid` unit test for the summary formatter to catch the pre-existing off-by-one is a follow-up worth doing once a developer touches the component for a feature change. | D-155 | 2026-05-29 | **Phase D — Country admin CP CRUD UI shipped.** Closes the "add Country CRUD in CP" half of the original D-151 user instruction (Phase A delivered the backend, Phase B made it the canonical nationality lookup, Phase C consumed it from Speaker, Phase D surfaces the admin pages). **`CountriesList.razor` (`/admin/countries`)** — canonical `SimfDataGrid` master grid with ISO numeric Id / alpha-2 Code / Name (EN) / Name (AR) / Dial code / Order / Status columns; default page size 50 (the seed is 56 rows, so an admin sees the full table without paging on first load). Add / Edit / Details modals mirror the same triad every other admin module uses. **`CountryForm.razor`** — handles the unique-to-this-module wrinkle that **the PK is a manually-assigned int** (ISO 3166-1 numeric), not an auto-generated Guid: the Id field is a required `<input type="number">` on Create with helper copy that names the standard ("Manually assigned, e.g. 682 = SA, 784 = AE. Once set it cannot be changed."), and a disabled read-only display on Edit so the admin sees but cannot mutate the PK. Client-side guard: Id is parsed as int and constrained to 1–999 (ISO 3166-1 numeric range; the spec allots 001–894 today with room for future allocations). Code is exactly 2 letters, auto-upper-cased on submit; PhonePrefix is optional (≤8 chars); DisplayOrder is non-negative; both names are 1–128 chars. The `IsActive` checkbox is shown only on Edit (Create always lands `IsActive=true` — admin can deactivate the row later if they decide it shouldn't appear in pickers). **`CpNavigation`:** `Module.AdminCountries` added to `Nav.System` between `AdminInterests` and `AdminVisitorProfileTypes`, marking countries as a reference-data lookup peer to Interests. **`Strings.resx` + `Strings.ar.resx`:** EN + AR parity for `Admin.Countries.*` (~60 keys × 2 locales) plus the nav label `Module.AdminCountries`. **Build:** 0 warnings / 0 errors. No API change → 301 suite unchanged. | The manually-assigned int PK is the only place across the entire CP where an admin types the row's primary key. The form makes the convention explicit through copy (the helper names ISO 3166-1 numeric directly with worked examples) rather than relying on the admin to know — the rest of the CP uses Guid PKs that are never user-visible. The decision to validate the Id range client-side to 1–999 is a defensive guard against fat-fingered entries (a typo like `12345` would otherwise sail through to the API which would reject it for a different reason — uniqueness — with a less actionable error code). It also forces the conversation: a partner who wants to seed a row outside that range (the spec leaves room for future allocations) hits the validator and has to consciously update the cap rather than silently introducing a stranger Id. The page-size default of 50 (vs the 20 the other modules use) is calibrated to the 56-row seed — the full picker fits on one page so an admin scanning for a country doesn't have to paginate; if the table grows past the picker-ergonomic limit (~250 ISO rows), 25 becomes the sensible default again. Placing the nav entry under `Nav.System` rather than spinning up a new "Reference Data" subgroup matches D-152's framing of Countries as a system-managed lookup alongside Interests, Roles, and ProfileTypes — adding a third nav group for one item violates the §17 minimum-viable-diff. The Phase A → B → C → D arc is now closed: Country exists, UserProfile.NationalityId points at it, Speaker.CountryId points at it, and an admin can curate the master list end-to-end in the CP. The end-to-end live-browser smoke for both Countries and Speakers is deferred to the next session (alongside any tests an admin discovers gaps in). |

The `SIMF-Gate-Module-Plan` §13 lists 16 planned decisions-log entries numbered D-132…D-147, but those IDs are already shipped against unrelated work (D-132 = Interests canonical migration; D-133 = full documentation sweep; D-134 = Sprint A/B Programme modules; D-135 = D-110 freeze-lift). To keep the log scannable, the 16 promised sub-entries collapse into **this umbrella entry D-148** for the kickoff state + a single **D-149** entry written at the end of the build summarising what shipped — matching the D-134 pattern (one umbrella + per-shipment sub-entries) rather than the 16-row-per-increment shape the plan §13 envisaged. **Freeze:** the Gate Module's single additive migration (`AddGates` adding `Gate`, `GateProfileTypeAllow`, `GateAssignment`, `GateScan`, `ScanIdempotency` on `SimfAppDbContext`) falls under the existing **D-135 freeze-lift umbrella** — no separate L-29 freeze-lift bullet needed. All five D-135 carry-forward constraints apply: (a) entity + EF config + migration in the same commit; (b) reversible `Down`; (c) single `__EFMigrationsHistory_App` ledger; (d) additive enums only (`DirectionMode`, `ScanOutcome`, `ScanSource`, `DenialReasonCode` are new — no shadowing existing names/values); (e) all writes D-109-audited **except** `GateScan` (it is itself an append-only audit log; an INSTEAD-OF UPDATE/DELETE trigger refuses mutation, and it opts out of `RowAudit` for that reason — this opt-out is the per-decision rationale D-135 (e) requires). **Build order (per plan §12 + adapted for one-batch shipment):** (1) SIMF-API-GATES-001 spec doc — landed; (2) DAT-001 §5.3 amendment — landed; (3) FDS-003 amendment — landed; (4–6) domain entities + EF configs + migration; (7) IQrResolver extracted from existing `QrLookupEndpoint`; (8) `IAdminGateService`, `IGateOperatorService`, `IGateConfigCache` (IMemoryCache), `IScanIdempotencyStore`, `IGateRateLimiter`, `IGateFailureCircuit`, 13-step constraint engine; (9) contracts + FastEndpoints + FluentValidators; (10) Identity seeder — new `GateOperator` baseline role + `Gates.Manage` / `Gates.Operate` / `Gates.ViewOwnReports` permissions; (11) CP pages — `/admin/gates` master-detail + `/admin/gates/operator` role-adaptive console + admin reports + XLSX; (12) Strings.resx + Strings.ar.resx parity; (13) tests; (14) `simplify`; (15) final commit + E2E sweep. **Reserved seams (not in this increment):** device API-key auth, time-window constraint (engine step 9.5 + `OUTSIDE_TIME_WINDOW`), booking-required (engine step 11.5 + `BOOKING_REQUIRED_MISSING`), offline queue. Their wire contracts ship in this increment so the device side plugs in without server-side change. | The plan §13 IDs collided with already-shipped work; remapping linearly preserves the plan's narrative ordering without rewriting either log. Collapsing the 16 promised sub-entries into D-148 (kickoff) + D-149 (shipped) matches the D-134 pattern actually used in this log and keeps the ledger readable rather than padded. D-135 already lifted D-110 for `SimfAppDbContext`; the Gate Module's single additive migration is exactly the kind of work D-135 anticipated, so layering a second freeze-lift bullet would duplicate state without adding clarity. Making the `GateScan` row-audit opt-out **explicit** in this entry (not buried in code) is the per-decision rationale clause (e) requires — an audit log auditing itself is dead weight, and `GateScan` already carries `ScannedAtUtc` + `ScannedByUserId` + `CorrelationId` + an `IpAddress` + `UserAgent`, all of which the `RowAudit` interceptor would otherwise duplicate. The IMemoryCache for gate config is a deliberate single-process cache (5-minute TTL): the CP and the operator console both run in the same process for this increment, and the Flutter app increment ships its own on-device cache — there is no second process to invalidate, and the 5-minute drift is acceptable because gate config changes are operator-initiated and the operator sees the new state on their next refresh anyway. The failure-rate circuit (≥10 denials / 60 s → 5-min lockout) is per-gate not per-token because the panic loop the circuit defends against is "misconfigured allow-list at gate X" not "misbehaving operator Y". |

Choosing `admin-interests.md` + `cp-admin-interests.md` + `cp-auth-flow.md` as gold-standard reference files (rather than authoring all 31 page docs to identical depth) keeps the commit reviewable: each reference file is ~250 lines of fully-fleshed-out content; the remaining files use the template to a serviceable depth, leaving 80% complete coverage with the canonical examples to copy from for the last 20%. The 29 outstanding per-page E2E files were intentionally scoped (Coverage matrix in README) rather than auto-stubbed — stubs decay; the matrix is small enough to be authoritative without rotting. |
| D-133 | 2026-05-28 | **Full SIMF documentation pass — vertical-slice kickoff.** Owner's brief: "document all pages, user manual, admin manual, programmer manual, test case e2e, use case and any need docs per each page. full documentation." Scope is large (~15–20 working days for the full pass) so this commit lands a vertical slice the owner can review before the rest. **New folder structure:** `docs/pages/{cp,web,mobile}/` for per-page reference docs + `docs/tests/e2e/` for per-page Gherkin-style scenarios. **Authored in this slice:** (1) `docs/pages/PAGE-INDEX.md` — master cross-reference table covering all 36 CP routes + 9 Website routes + Mobile placeholder rows, with status (✅ Real / 🚧 Stub / 🔒 Auth-only), audience, doc path, test path; (2) `docs/pages/_TEMPLATE.md` — the 13-section template every per-page doc follows (Purpose → Audience → Screenshots → UI affordances → Data flow → Validation → Edge cases → i18n + RTL → Accessibility → Use cases → Tests → Related docs → Changelog); (3) `docs/pages/cp/admin-interests.md` — full reference doc against the D-132-migrated `/admin/interests` page, used as the gold-standard sample so the remaining 35+ docs can be written by copy-and-adapt; (4) `docs/manuals/Admin-Manual.md` — scaffold + intro + sign-in + shell + canonical-CRUD-pattern overview + full **Interests** module chapter (most-common-tasks, screenshots, troubleshooting, security boundaries, glossary); (5) `docs/pages/README.md` — folder entry point that explains how to navigate + how to add a new doc. Existing stubs not touched in this slice: `docs/manuals/User-Manual.md` (23 lines), `docs/manuals/Developer-Guide.md` (334 lines), `docs/manuals/Test-Guide.md` (87 lines), `docs/SIMF-UCS-001` (333 lines). **Coverage gate:** every ✅ Real row in `PAGE-INDEX.md` without a linked doc is a tracked gap. **Build:** no source code touched — docs only, 0/0. **Freeze (D-110):** zero schema / migration / enum impact. **Phasing the rest:** the user picked "vertical slices" — next slices ship one CP module fully (page doc + manual chapter + E2E catalogue entry + UCS entry) per commit so the shape is visible after each step. **Deferred:** the 22 stub modules (D-134) stay un-docced until they're built. | The brief was huge — "full documentation" — and shipping a vertical slice was the right call (CLAUDE.md §1.2 / §1.3) so the owner can correct the shape before 15+ days of writing land in the wrong format. Choosing the migrated `/admin/interests` as the sample page is deliberate: it's the freshest in the team's context (D-132 just shipped this hour), it's the simplest CRUD shape, and it exercises every section of the template (banner, modals, soft-delete, bilingual fields, edge cases). The 13-section template is biased toward CRUD pages because that's most of the CP — non-CRUD pages (Print badge desk, Logs viewer, Reset 2FA, Auth pages) will populate fewer sections (mark unused ones "N/A — {reason}") rather than warp the template to fit them. The Admin Manual chapter for Interests deliberately includes screenshots, troubleshooting, and "what you cannot do" — that last subsection is the most operator-valued in the field, because operators waste hours trying to do things that aren't supported. The folder split (`docs/pages/` for reference / `docs/manuals/` for narrative) keeps the daily-operator audience separate from the per-page schema; the test catalogue lives a third place (`docs/tests/e2e/`) because tests cross-reference both. The owner will see the cost: each subsequent per-page doc is ~250 lines, each manual chapter ~150 lines, each E2E entry ~100 lines, each UCS entry ~50 lines — so ~550 lines per CRUD page × ~30 pages = ~16 000 lines of documentation across 5+ commits. That estimate is honest before they accept the spend. |

left-joins `UserProfiles` so a target that hasn't filled the form yet still returns identity + UserType + a null body for every form field); `src/Backend/SIMF.Api/Endpoints/Admin/GetPendingVisitorProfileEndpoint.cs` + `GetPendingOtherProfileEndpoint.cs`; DI registration in `DependencyInjection.cs` (direct `AddScoped`, matching the D-100 pattern for new focused services). **New tests:** `tests/SIMF.Api.Tests/PendingProfileReadTests.cs` — 8 integration tests covering happy-visitor + happy-Other + approved-target-returns-404 + cross-type-on-visitor-route-returns-404 + cross-type-on-others-route-returns-404 + unknown-id-returns-404 + non-admin-returns-403 + read-does-not-mutate-state. **Decision-log ID drift vs. Prompt C:** the spec called for D-122 but D-122 was already taken by the Cropper DI fix that landed earlier today, so this work ships under D-124 (D-123 was the same Cropper line's follow-up). **Spec deviation, documented:** the Prompt C acceptance criterion (g) asked for a `RowAuditOperation` assertion on the successful read; D-109's SaveChanges interceptor only fires on writes, so a `.AsNoTracking()` read produces no audit row and the assertion was unachievable as written. The 8th test replaces (g) with a non-destructive-read assertion (post-read, the target's `AccountState` is still `PendingApproval`) so the test count + intent stay matched. The Prompt also called for returning the 404 via `Send.NotFoundAsync(ct)`; that emits an empty body and breaks the project's `ApiResult<T>` envelope convention every other endpoint follows, so the endpoints throw `ApiException(ErrorCodes.NotFound, 404, …)` instead — uses the existing `ErrorCodes.NotFound` constant per the spec's "no new ErrorCodes" rule. **Build:** 0 warnings / 0 errors at Release. **Tests:** 311 / 311 (2 Application + 5 Domain + 13 ApiClient + 34 CP + 257 Api — Api went from 249 → 257 with the 8 new tests). **Freeze (D-110):** zero EF schema / migration / enum changes — the DTO is a projection over existing columns. **Frontend consumer:** D-125 (this commit) wires the CP `PendingVisitors.razor` + `PendingOthers.razor` View modals against these endpoints; `SimfAdminClient.GetPending{Visitor\|Other}ProfileAsync` and matching CP BFF passthroughs in `Endpoints/AccountEndpoints.cs` ship alongside. | Closes the long-standing approval-flow gap the owner flagged on `/admin/visitors/pending` ("must have view btn to show all details of visitor before approve") and shipped the symmetric Other variant in the same diff because Prompt C explicitly covered both kinds. The 404-for-all-mismatch is the load-bearing security choice — the alternative (different codes for "missing id" vs "wrong state" vs "wrong type") leaks enumeration signal the owner explicitly rejected when answering Q-G. The scoped read is intentionally narrow rather than "general admin-read-any-profile" because the owner's Q-G answer (recorded in `docs/SIMF-CP-Grid-Banner-Modal-Plan.md`) said exactly that: read access stays pinned to PendingApproval + matching type. The new service is registered with direct DI (the D-100 pattern) rather than the older factory-delegate co-residency on `AdminAccountService` because the surface is independent — no shared private helpers, pure read, AsNoTracking — and the D-100 split was already the established direction for new admin services. The DTO carries `ProfileTypeId` AND `ProfileTypeName` (+ Arabic) so the CP modal can render the human label without a second lookup. The left-join on `UserProfiles` is correct because admin-created Visitor / Other accounts can land in PendingApproval before the user fills the profile form — without it the read would fail for those rows, defeating its purpose. |
| D-123 | 2026-05-28 | **Cropper.Blazor — load `cropper.min.js` BEFORE `cropperJsInterop.min.js` (finishes D-116 part 2).** Adds the missing first `<script>` tag in `Components/App.razor` so the underlying cropperjs JS sets up the `window.cropper` interop bridge that `cropperJsInterop.min.js` then extends. Mirrors V10 ERP `App.razor` lines 32-33 byte-for-byte. Without the first script, the bridge is incomplete — the component renders the dialog and the cropper canvas tries to come up, but on `CropperComponent.DisposeAsync()` the JS-interop call `cropper.destroy` resolves the path `window.cropper.destroy` and crashes with `Microsoft.JSInterop.JSException: Could not find 'cropper.destroy' ('cropper' was undefined).`, which tore down the SignalR circuit and forced a page reload. **End-to-end smoke proof:** with both scripts loaded, `window.cropper` is an object (verified via `evaluate_script`); injecting a PNG file via `DataTransfer + dispatchEvent('change')` opens the modal, now showing BOTH the "image to crop" canvas AND the "image to preview" pane (cropperjs is actually initialised now — before D-123 only the dialog frame appeared); clicking Cancel closes the modal cleanly with zero console errors and no `'cropper' undefined` crash. Screenshot reused — same modal shape as `docs/screenshots/d122-cropper-modal-opens.png`. **One-file diff:** `src/ControlPanel/SIMF.ControlPanel/Components/App.razor` — one new `<script>` line + a 4-line comment block. **Build:** 0 warnings / 0 errors at Release (CP only). **Tests:** unchanged 303 / 303. **Freeze (D-110):** zero schema / migration / enum impact. **No csproj edit, no DI edit, no Razor code edit** — only the script-load order in App.razor. | The cropper-on-dispose crash is the second half of "what V10 wires in App.razor that SIMF didn't". D-116 added `<link>` for `cropper.min.css` and `<script>` for `cropperJsInterop.min.js` but missed the first cropperjs script the interop bridge depends on. D-122 registered the cropper service in DI so the component would even render; this entry restores the cropperjs runtime so the rendered component doesn't crash when it's torn down. Both are pure "use as in erp exactly" fixes — the V10 file's two cropper script tags side-by-side were the canonical source of truth. The dispose path was missed in the D-122 smoke because the smoke didn't close the modal; this entry closes that hole. The 401-on-stale-session observation from D-122 is still out of scope and still owed a follow-up. |
| D-122 | 2026-05-28 | **Cropper.Blazor DI registration + SignalR message-size bump — finishes D-116 (cropper crashed on first render).** Adds the two pieces missing from D-116's CP `Program.cs`, mirrored byte-for-byte from V10 ERP's `Program.cs`: (a) `using Cropper.Blazor.Extensions;` + `builder.Services.AddCropper();` — without this DI registration, `CropperComponent` crashes at runtime the first time `<SimfImageCropperModal>` tries to render, with `System.InvalidOperationException: Cannot provide a value for property 'CropperJsIntertop' on type 'Cropper.Blazor.Components.CropperComponent'. There is no registered service of type 'Cropper.Blazor.Services.ICropperJsInterop'.` The package adds the type via an `IServiceCollection` extension method; the `AddCropper()` call is the only thing that registers the JS-interop service in DI. (b) `MaximumReceiveMessageSize` on `AddHubOptions` raised from 256 KB (originally sized for the QR SVG render diff) to **10 MB**, matching V10's `AddSignalR(... 10 * 1024 * 1024 ...)` (V10 Program.cs lines 43-46) — the cropper sends base64 data URLs of the source image (up to the 2 MB avatar policy) through JS interop over the same SignalR transport, so a >256 KB source PNG would fail mid-crop. **End-to-end smoke proof:** with the running CP rebuilt, a programmatic file injection into `<input id="avatar-input" type="file">` (via JS DataTransfer + `dispatchEvent('change')`) opened the `<dialog>Crop your avatar</dialog>` modal cleanly — Cancel + "Crop and save" buttons render, `CropperComponent` mounts without throwing, zero console errors. Screenshot at `docs/screenshots/d122-cropper-modal-opens.png`. **Build:** 0 warnings / 0 errors at Release on the CP project (the change is CP-only). **Tests:** unchanged, 303 / 303 (the new DI line has no test coverage today — `SimfImageCropperModalTests` would need a TestRenderer fixture, deferred). **Freeze (D-110):** zero schema / migration / enum impact. **No csproj edit** — the Cropper.Blazor NuGet reference was already added in D-116; this commit only fixes the runtime wiring. **Out of scope (logged, not fixed):** during this smoke I also reproduced the user-reported `api/profile → 401` on a stale session — API log shows D-121's refresh chain ran successfully 3 times yesterday (21:40, 22:09, 22:38) and then went silent for ~14 hours, after which a fresh page load got 401s with no refresh attempt at all. Suggests a separate gap (cookie `ExpireTimeSpan` of 8h crossed, or `OnValidatePrincipal` not running on requests where the cookie middleware concludes the cookie is already invalid). Per CLAUDE.md §17 "one issue per change", deferred to a separate investigation. **Also flagged:** V10 persists ASP.NET Core data-protection keys (V10 Program.cs lines 70-73, `AddDataProtection().SetApplicationName(...).PersistKeysToFileSystem(...)`) — SIMF does not. That's the canonical fix for "users get bounced to /login after `dotnet run` rotates the key", and is the natural companion to the 401 investigation. Out of scope for D-122. | The cropper was committed in D-116 with all the visual + component plumbing in place but missing the single line that registers `ICropperJsInterop` in DI — a runtime-only failure that the build, the test suite, and the bUnit-less smoke all let pass because none of them actually mount `<CropperComponent>`. The first time the modal was opened by hand (the user) the property-injector ran, found no service, and crashed the circuit. The fix is one line straight out of V10 ERP, exactly per the owner's "use as in erp exactly to save my time" direction. The SignalR bump is in the same diff because it is the second half of "what V10 does for cropping" and a bigger source image would silently fail without it; carrying it now avoids a follow-up bug ticket for a 1 MB JPEG. The 401 + data-protection observations are recorded so they are not lost, but kept out of this commit so the cropper fix lands clean and reviewable on its own. |
| D-121 | 2026-05-27 | **Cookie-auth refresh hook — closes the stale-JWT 401 that blocked every JS-driven BFF call past the 30-minute mark.** Adds `SimfCookieRefreshHandler` (duplicated into both `SIMF.ControlPanel` and `SIMF.Web` — matching the established `SignInTicketStore` / `AuthEndpoints` split between the two host projects), wired into each project's cookie scheme via `options.Events.OnValidatePrincipal = SimfCookieRefreshHandler.OnValidatePrincipalAsync` in `Program.cs`. The hook reads `access_token` / `refresh_token` / `expires_at` from the cookie's stored tokens; when the access token has ≤2 minutes left (comfortably outside `JwtBearerSetup.cs`'s 30-second clock-skew tolerance), it calls `SimfAuthClient.RefreshAsync` and persists the rotated pair + a fresh `expires_at` back into the cookie, setting `ShouldRenew = true`. On a definitive refresh failure (refresh token revoked / expired / rejected) the handler `RejectPrincipal()`s + `SignOutAsync`'s — the user goes through `/login` again, the same end state the cookie's 8-hour expiry would have delivered, just reached sooner. A transient cancellation leaves the principal alone so a brief API blip does not log the user out. The sign-in endpoints in both projects now persist `expires_at` from `AuthTokens.AccessTokenExpiresInSeconds` alongside the existing access/refresh tokens (via the new `SimfCookieRefreshHandler.StoreTokens` helper) so the first cookie write is already correct — without that, the very next request would needlessly refresh. **Root cause (verified):** access-token lifetime = 30 minutes (`JwtOptions.AccessTokenMinutes = 30`); cookie lifetime = 8 hours with sliding renewal. Refresh-token rotation was already implemented end-to-end on the API (`POST /api/v1/auth/refresh` + `SessionService.RefreshAsync`) and exposed on `SimfAuthClient.RefreshAsync` — but **nothing in either web project ever called it**. So past 30 min, every `/account/api/*` BFF forwarder read an expired JWT from the cookie via `GetTokenAsync("access_token")` and forwarded it; the API's `JwtBearerSetup.OnTokenValidated` failed lifetime validation and returned the standard 401 envelope — even though the user's cookie was still valid for hours. The user saw "Authentication is required" on every JS-driven action with no clear remediation. **Why centralise in the cookie middleware (vs. per-endpoint retry):** the cookie-validate hook runs ahead of every authenticated request, so every existing BFF forwarder picks up the fresh token transparently — zero per-endpoint plumbing, zero changes to `AccountEndpoints.cs`. **Front-end consumer:** every `/account/api/*` route in both `SIMF.ControlPanel.Endpoints.AccountEndpoints` and `SIMF.Web.Endpoints.AccountEndpoints` immediately benefits — no per-route changes were needed. **Build:** 0 warnings / 0 errors at Release. **Tests:** full suite still 303 / 303 (2 Application + 5 Domain + 13 ApiClient + 34 ControlPanel + 249 Api). **Freeze (D-110):** zero schema / migration / enum impact — API contract unchanged, only consumer wiring added. **No csproj edit:** the helper lives inside each host project (where the ASP.NET Core auth namespaces are already in scope via the Razor SDK), not in the shared `SIMF.ApiClient` class library — initial attempt at the latter failed compile with CS0234 (`Microsoft.AspNetCore` missing) and was reverted rather than dragging an ASP.NET dependency into a pure HttpClient lib (CLAUDE.md §1.7). **Future test:** `SimfCookieRefreshHandlerTests` placeholder noted in `// Tests:` headers — the handler runs in cookie middleware and is awkward to unit-test in isolation; a focused integration test (in-memory API + cookie scheme) is the right shape but is deferred to a separate task. **Hand-off delivered alongside this fix:** `docs/handoff/PROMPT-C-pending-profile-read.md` — standalone extract of plan §11C — Prompt C, ready to paste into a fresh backend session for the §11.3 scoped pending-profile read endpoints. | Stopped the "Authentication is required" wall on every JS-driven Control Panel action past 30 minutes since sign-in (smoke-test blocker for D-117 / D-118 / D-119 / D-120 verification). The same bug existed in the public Website auth path; fixed in the same change since the cause + fix shape are identical and `SignInTicketStore` already establishes the duplicated-by-design pattern between the two host projects. Centralising the rotation in the cookie scheme means every present and future BFF endpoint is correct by default, with no per-endpoint retry-on-401 plumbing to maintain. Failure mode of the rotation (revoked refresh token / API down) is identical to today's "cookie expired" UX — re-login at `/login` — so no new failure surface was introduced. |
| D-120 | 2026-05-26 | **ProfileTypes PageColor field gains a native colour-swatch picker (closes the D-118 polish note).** Replaces the plain `SimfTextField` for `PageColor` in `ProfileTypeForm.razor` with a paired text + `<input type="color">` swatch. Both bind to the same `_model.PageColor`; the text input stays the **source of truth** and accepts the existing free-text contract (CSS variables, 3-digit hex shortcuts, anything ≤ 32 chars). The swatch is a visual write-only shortcut that always writes `#rrggbb` back into the text field on change. When `_model.PageColor` is NOT a canonical `^#[0-9A-Fa-f]{6}$` hex (so the picker can't display it), the picker falls back to the brand navy `#244A77` for *display only* — no silent write happens until the user actually picks. New CSS in `simf-components.css`: `.simf-field__control--with-swatch` (flex row, gap, swatch hugs its content) + `.simf-field__color-swatch` (2.5-rem wide, token-driven border/radius/background, focus ring + disabled state — zero raw hex, zero inline styles). 1 new resx key per locale (en + ar): `Admin.ProfileTypes.Field.PageColorPicker` (aria-label for the swatch — required because the swatch has no visible text). No SimfTextField change — the new markup hand-rolls the `simf-field*` class structure that SimfTextField uses internally, keeping the shared primitive untouched per CLAUDE.md §1.6 (no scope creep). **Build:** 0 warnings / 0 errors at Release. **Tests:** full suite still 303 / 303. **Freeze (D-110):** zero schema / migration / enum impact. **No API change** — the text shape sent to `POST /admin/profile-types` / `PUT /admin/profile-types/{id}` is unchanged. | Closes the explicit follow-up note from D-118 ("PageColor was rendered as a plain text input… a real picker is a follow-up polish"). The picker-+-text pairing keeps the existing free-text capability that the D-115 contract allows (`PageColor` accepts `"#FFD700"`, `"var(--brand-blue)"`, etc.) — a picker-only field would have silently stripped CSS variables and 3-digit hex shortcuts on every save, which would surprise an admin who picked from a brand stylesheet. The text-as-source-of-truth direction was chosen because `<input type="color">` only renders `#rrggbb` values; making it the source of truth would force every model value into that narrow space. Falling back to `#244A77` for the swatch when the text isn't a 6-hex value gives the field a sensible appearance without surprising the user — the picker doesn't appear "broken" or "wrong" because the matching text is visible next to it. The new CSS classes are scoped to `.simf-field__control--with-swatch` so other surfaces can opt in by adding the modifier; the swatch class itself doesn't change the layout unless paired with the modifier. |
| D-119 | 2026-05-26 | **Account avatar adopts the D-116 SimfImageCropperModal (first cropper consumer).** The Profile page's avatar upload flow now goes through the cropper: picking a file opens `<SimfImageCropperModal>` with the picked image as a base64 data URL; on confirm the cropped 400×400 PNG is POSTed to the existing `/account/api/avatar` multipart endpoint (no backend / API change). On cancel the pick is discarded and the file input is cleared so the same file fires the change event again. **New JS helpers** in `wwwroot/js/simf-account.js`: `readFileAsDataUrl(inputId)` (FileReader → `{ ok, dataUrl, fileName, mimeType }`), `uploadDataUrl(url, dataUrl, fileName)` (fetch → blob → multipart upload via existing endpoint), `clearFileInput(inputId)` (resets `input.value` without `.click()`, since `triggerFileInput` would re-open the OS picker). **`Profile.razor` markup change:** the explicit "Upload new image" `<SimfButton>` is removed — picking a file now opens the cropper directly, and the cropper's own "Crop and save" button replaces the previous Upload action. The "Remove avatar" secondary button stays. The hidden `<input type="file">` gains an `@onchange="OnAvatarFilePickedAsync"` handler. **3 new resx keys per locale** (en + ar): `Profile.Avatar.Crop.Title`, `Profile.Avatar.PickFailed`, `Profile.Avatar.UploadFailed`. The legacy `Profile.Avatar.Upload` key stays orphaned per CLAUDE.md §1.6 (no hidden cleanup). **Build:** 0 warnings / 0 errors at Release. **Tests:** 303 / 303 still passing. **Freeze (D-110):** zero schema / migration / enum impact. **No API change** — the cropper outputs a data URL, the JS helper decodes it to bytes, and the existing multipart endpoint accepts it transparently. | Closes UI plan §11.5's "first consumer" follow-up. The avatar flow is the cleanest first consumer because it's already a single-file upload with the right shape (square output ≈ avatar) and the existing endpoint accepts arbitrary image bytes. Removing the explicit Upload button is a UX simplification — picking immediately means "I want to use this image", so an extra confirm step before the cropper would be friction; the cropper's own Save action is the confirm. Going through the existing multipart endpoint (rather than adding a new base64-accepting endpoint) keeps the backend untouched per §1.7 and avoids two parallel write paths for the same file. The data-URL → blob conversion happens in the browser via `fetch(dataUrl)` which is the modern idiomatic shortcut over manually base64-decoding into a typed array. The `clearFileInput` helper is a thin sibling of `triggerFileInput` rather than a `triggerFileInput(silent: true)` variant because the call sites read more clearly with two named methods than one boolean flag. |

Both follow the canonical D-117 pattern: SimfBanner page-top, SimfDataGrid with Multiselect + full numbered pager, modal-based Add / Edit / Details / Delete. **One reusable form** `ProfileTypeForm.razor` (Name + NameArabic + PageColor + IsActive) hosted in both Add and Edit modals; the host page passes either a fresh `UserType` (Create) or an existing `AdminProfileTypeSummary` via the `Initial` parameter (Edit). The 8 bulk methods were inlined (not refactored to a shared helper) to honour CLAUDE.md §1.6 "no hidden refactors" — the duplication is accepted, cleanup deferred to a deliberate sweep. **13 new BFF forwarders** in `src/ControlPanel/.../Endpoints/AccountEndpoints.cs`: 8 type-scoped visitors/others bulk forwarders **(closes the D-114 runtime gap — those toolbar buttons would have 404'd at runtime without the forwarders even though the build passed)**, plus 5 ProfileType CRUD forwarders. Excel export forwarders use the same `Results.File(...)` pattern as admins export; import forwarders use `DisableAntiforgery()` per the SameSite=Lax stance (D-029). **Nav additions** in `CpNavigation.cs`: 2 new entries under the System group — `Module.AdminVisitorProfileTypes` → `/admin/profile-types/visitor`, `Module.AdminOtherProfileTypes` → `/admin/profile-types/other`. **Resx** (en + ar): 44 new keys per locale — 2 nav labels + 2 page titles + None empty-state + 5 column headers + Active.{Yes,No} pills + Add/Edit/Details/Delete modal copy + 4 field labels + 4 field hints + 3 field-invalid messages + Submitting + Submit.{Create,Update} + Cancel + Created/Updated/Saved toasts + Fallback + Delete.{Success,InUse,Confirm,Submit,Cancel}. **Build:** `dotnet build -c Release` — 0 warnings / 0 errors. **Tests:** full suite still 303 / 303. **Freeze (D-110):** zero schema / migration / enum impact. **Backend not touched** — entirely consumes D-115 (ProfileTypes CRUD) and D-113 (per-kind bulk endpoints) that already shipped. | Closes UI plan §11.4. Layout choice (Q-H.1): option (b) — two routes — matches the existing left-nav rhythm where `/admin/{admins,others,visitors}` already split per UserType, so reading order is predictable and the page title can be unambiguously localised. The single-page-with-filter-pill alternative would have meant another UI surface (the filter chip) and an extra round-trip on tab switch with no clarity gain. UserType scope (Q-H.2): Visitor + Other only — Admins don't carry a profile type (the API rejects `UserType=Admin` with 400 via the D-115 service guard) so a third route would have been a dead button. **The D-114 BFF gap deserves a separate note:** when D-114 shipped, the new VisitorsList/OthersList toolbar wired callbacks to `/account/api/admin/{visitors,others}/{bulk-delete,duplicate,export,import}` but the CP's `AccountEndpoints.cs` had no matching forwarders — meaning every toolbar button beyond Add/Delete-via-grid-default would have 404'd at runtime. Build + tests passed because nothing exercised the new buttons end-to-end. Combining the gap fix with D-118 (rather than a separate hotfix commit) keeps SimfAdminClient + AccountEndpoints changes coherent in one diff. The D-114 entry above remains correct as-shipped; this entry documents the runtime gap and its closure. PageColor was rendered as a plain text input (not a colour picker) to match the existing field surface and ship the CRUD now; a real picker is a follow-up polish that doesn't block the lookup-table management. Edit reuses the row data already in `_page.Items` instead of issuing a separate GET — the row record carries every editable field, so the extra round-trip would add latency without information. The `ConfirmDeleteAsync` surfaces the API's bilingual error message verbatim when it comes back (e.g., `ProfileTypeInUse` with both en/ar via `MessageForCurrentCulture()`); falls back to the localized `Delete.InUse` string when the envelope is empty. The `simf-dl` description-list primitive from D-117 is reused for the Details modal body — Q-G's "no general admin profile-read endpoint" doesn't apply here (ProfileType isn't user data) so the modal can render the row directly. |

Public surface is parameterised: `ImageUrl` (required), `AspectRatio` (default 1), `OutputWidth`/`OutputHeight` (default 400×400), `OutputMimeType` (default `image/png`), `FillColor` (default `#ffffff` — only place this hex appears, supplied as a Cropper-API value not a styling color), `Open`, `Title`, `OnCropped`, `OnCancel`. Static assets `_content/Cropper.Blazor/cropper.min.css` + `cropperJsInterop.min.js` added to `Components/App.razor`. CSS additions in `simf-components.css` use existing tokens (`--color-surface-sunken` for the canvas backdrop, `--radius-card` for corner) — **zero raw hex, zero inline styles, zero hardcoded fonts** (CLAUDE.md §8). 4 new resx keys in both `Strings.resx` and `Strings.ar.resx` (`Crop.Title`, `Crop.Cancel`, `Crop.Submit`, `Crop.Processing`). **No consumer wired yet** — first consumer is the Account avatar-upload flow, scheduled as a follow-up. **Freeze (D-110):** zero schema / migration / enum impact. **CLAUDE.md §1.7 override:** the csproj edit was explicitly authorised by the owner on 2026-05-26 ("install package but use cropper code idea as in ERP"). | Honors the "same package, same code" ask while preserving SIMF's no-MudBlazor / no-inline-style / no-hex rules. | D-099 | 2026-05-26 | **Credential-flow emails also dispatch an in-app notification.** Closes backlog item §3 L33. New `NotificationDispatcherExtensions.TryDispatchAsync(dispatcher, request, logger, ct)` mirrors the H23 / D-083 `IEmailQueue.TryEnqueueAsync` shape — wraps `INotificationDispatcher.DispatchAsync` with a log-and-swallow guard so an in-app dispatch failure does not break the credential flow that has already returned to the caller. `PasswordService.ForgotPasswordAsync`, `RegistrationService.SignUpAsync`, `RegistrationService.ResendCodeAsync`, and `SignInService.SignInAsync` (email-OTP branch only — TOTP returns earlier with no email) each get a `notifications.TryDispatchAsync(...)` call right after their existing `emailQueue.TryEnqueueAsync(...)` site. Each dispatch carries `SendEmail = false` (the email was already queued; this is the in-app trail only) and bilingual `Title` / `TitleArabic` / `Body` / `BodyArabic`. Four new Kinds: `Credential.PasswordResetRequested`, `Credential.EmailVerificationSent`, `Credential.EmailVerificationResent`, `Credential.SignInOtpSent`. `NotificationTests.CreateUserAndSignInAsync` test helper now calls a new `ClearNotificationsAsync(userId)` step (`ExecuteDeleteAsync` against the `Notifications` table) so the three existing count-based assertions (List, UnreadCount, Dispatcher) start from a clean slate — without the clear, sign-up's new in-app notification would be the 1st row each test sees. Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Closes the last small follow-up from §3 of the backlog. The notification gives the user a visible "yes, this email was sent" trail inside the app after sign-in (pre-auth flows like forgot-password and sign-up create the notification before the user has a session; they see it later). The TryDispatch extension matches the H23 TryEnqueue shape so the credential services have one consistent pattern for "fire-and-forget side-effect after the persisted commit". Notification kinds use a stable `Credential.*` prefix so a future filter (e.g. "hide credential trail by default") can target them. `SendEmail = false` is the load-bearing flag — without it the dispatcher would queue a duplicate email. Test-helper clear pattern is cleaner than per-test +1 counting because the +1 might shift again as new credential kinds are added; tests stay readable. |
