| D-097 | 2026-05-26 | **IT / Support email distribution list for send failures.** New `EmailOptions.FailureAlertRecipients` field — a comma-, semicolon- or whitespace-separated string of operations addresses. When configured, `EmailBackgroundService` sends a short alert email to each address whenever an outbound message fails to send (SMTP rejected, network outage, etc.). The recipient list is parsed once per failure (`Split(RecipientDelimiters, RemoveEmptyEntries \| TrimEntries)` + distinct, case-insensitive). The alert email's body carries the failed recipient, the original subject, and the exception type + message (HTML-encoded). The alert path is fire-and-forget — if the alert ITSELF fails to send, the exception is logged but no second alert is fired to avoid amplifying a sustained SMTP outage into a per-recipient storm. Default value is empty, so existing deployments without a configured DL stay quiet (the audit row from `Email.EnqueueFailed`, H10/D-065, is still written either way). Tests: 222 / 222 unchanged (the failure-alert dispatch is a side-effect on a different background worker; the existing `EmailEnqueueFailureTests` continue to exercise the audit-row path). Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Closes backlog item §3 L32 ("Email distribution list — Support / IT recipients for failure-alert emails"). The integration point is the background sender (not `TryEnqueueAsync` in Application) because (a) the most common failure mode is SMTP send failure, not enqueue failure — the queue is in-memory and rarely fails; (b) putting the alert path in the background service keeps Application code free of an operational concern; (c) the existing `OperationLog` audit row covers the enqueue-failure case, and the alert addresses are written into `EmailOptions` which is an Infrastructure concern. No-recurse-on-alert-failure is the load-bearing safety: a sustained SMTP outage would otherwise issue one alert per failed message per configured recipient, multiplying the storm. The empty default preserves the no-op posture for any deployment that hasn't opted in yet — the alert is purely additive. |
| D-096 | 2026-05-26 | **CP TOTP re-pairing surface: `GET /api/v1/auth/totp/pairing` + `/account/totp-pairing` page.** Closes the backlog `myComment.txt L12` item (CP exposure of the seeded super-admin's TOTP QR). New `ITotpEnrollmentService.GetCurrentPairingAsync(userId, ct)` returns a `TotpSetupResponse` for the user's CURRENT active secret without rotation; the `TotpEnrollmentService` impl reads `accounts.GetAuthenticationTokenAsync(user, "[AspNetUserStore]", "AuthenticatorKey")` and re-builds the otpauth URI + QR via the existing helpers — no DB writes, no audit row, no token store mutation. New `GET /api/v1/auth/totp/pairing` endpoint (`TotpPairingEndpoint.cs`) is bearer-protected and rate-limited under the `"auth"` policy; returns 404 when the account has no active secret. New `SimfAccountClient.TotpPairingAsync` API-client method. CP proxy: new `MapGet("/totp/pairing", ...)` in `AccountEndpoints.cs`. New `/account/totp-pairing` Blazor page (`Components/Pages/Account/TotpPairing.razor`) loads via JS interop, renders the QR SVG + secret text, and routes the user to the Profile page when no active secret exists. New EN + AR resx keys under the `TotpPairing.*` namespace (Title, Instruction, SecretHint, NoSecret, GoToProfile, Loading, LoadError). Two new integration tests: `Pairing_returns_404_when_the_account_has_no_active_secret`, `Pairing_returns_the_same_QR_for_the_enrolled_users_active_secret` (proves idempotency vs the rotating Setup endpoint, with the post-Confirm refresh-token rotation noted because the security stamp rolls on enrolment). Tests: **222 API** (220 + 2 new) + 34 CP + 13 client + 6 domain + 2 application = **277 / 277**. Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Owner reported the seeded super-admin's TOTP secret (`dbji csx7 c3mj s2qa sjcl rbcl kiqk ovr3`) was "not working" — most likely cause is a paired-then-unpaired authenticator that needs to re-scan the SAME secret, not a rotation. The standard `POST /auth/totp/setup` rotates the secret on every call, which would break sign-in for an admin who had already paired once. The new pairing endpoint is the read-only counterpart: same secret, same QR, every call. The endpoint is bearer-protected (the admin has to sign in first — which they can do via the bypass `super-admin sign-in is allowed without TOTP for the seeded admin` only if such bypass exists; for the typical case the admin still needs TOTP, which is the chicken-and-egg this fix solves PROVIDED the seeded secret matches what they have in their authenticator). If the authenticator is truly lost (no recovery codes, no other paired device, the seeded secret was rotated), the admin needs the next-tier fix: a SuperAdmin-only "reset my own TOTP" path. That's a follow-up. The test that proves the same QR is returned across calls is the load-bearing contract — without it a future change to the Setup endpoint that accidentally rotates the active secret would silently break the pairing page. |

| D-094 | 2026-05-26 | **R3.5 — Split `IUserAccountRepository` into 5 role-cohesive sub-interfaces.** Five new interfaces in `src/Backend/SIMF.Application/IdentityAccess/Abstractions/`: `IUserAccountStore` (5 methods — Find by email/id, Create ×2, Update), `IUserCredentialStore` (5 — Check/Add/Remove/ChangePassword + UpdateSecurityStamp), `IUserLockoutTracker` (3 — IsLockedOut, AccessFailed, ResetAccessFailedCount), `IUserRoleStore` (4 — GetRoles, IsInRole, AddToRole, RemoveFromRoles), `IUserTwoFactorStore` (5 — GetAuthenticatorKey, Set/Get/RemoveAuthToken, SetTwoFactorEnabled). `IUserAccountRepository` is now a marker that inherits all five — existing consumers (six Application services + IdentitySeeder) compile unchanged. DI updates: one scoped `UserAccountRepository` instance backs all six registrations (aggregate + five sub-interfaces) via factory delegates so the change-tracker scope and per-request state stay shared (same pattern R2 — D-075 used for `AdminAccountService`). New code is encouraged to inject only the narrower contract it actually uses, so constructor signatures advertise the concerns the service touches. Tests: **275 / 275** unchanged. The 22-method aggregate was a method-for-method port of `UserManager<TUser>` — fine as the transitional surface during R3, but it forced every Application service to depend on every concern. With the seam split, the next time someone reads `SignInService`'s constructor they can see immediately that it touches users, credentials, lockout and roles, but not 2FA tokens — exactly the kind of constructor-as-documentation `§17` ("design for change") recommends. The marker-interface inheritance keeps the migration cost zero for existing services; new services can pick the narrow seam by default. Sub-interface migration of the existing services is a low-priority follow-up — no functional benefit, only signature clarity. The DI shape (one scoped impl, six registrations pointing at it) is structurally identical to R2's `AdminAccountService` split (D-075), so the pattern is consistent across both interface decompositions. |

New `DomainPurityTests` in `tests/SIMF.Domain.Tests/`: three Facts assert (a) Domain assembly has no `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity*` reference, (b) Domain has no `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore*` reference, (c) `SimfUser.BaseType == typeof(object)` (so a future commit cannot re-introduce `IdentityUser<TKey>` inheritance without flipping the test red). Tests: **220 API + 34 CP + 13 client + 6 domain (3 baseline + 3 R5g purity) + 2 application = 275 / 275** (was 272 — three new). Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. **Arch SEV-1.1 fully closed.** | The architectural unit tests are the load-bearing complement to dropping the package reference: removing the reference proves the dependency was unnecessary today, but the test pins it for the future — any commit that adds a project ref or `using` that re-pulls Identity into Domain will fail the test on CI, not when someone reads the csproj later. The `BaseType == typeof(object)` assertion is a backstop against a different failure mode (someone reads the package back into Domain via a transitive dep without noticing). Splitting the role rather than POCOing it in Domain is justified by zero Application usage — no consumer above Infrastructure references `SimfRole`, so a Domain `SimfRole` POCO would be a type with no purpose. Pre-R5g `Application/IdentityAccess/Abstractions/UserOperationResult.cs` already provided the SIMF-owned alternative to `IdentityResult`/`IdentityError`; H21 (D-082) migrated every Application callsite. The four stale Application `using` lines remained because the compiler doesn't warn about unused namespaces by default and the file-level lint pass didn't catch them. The `<see cref="SimfRole"/>` reference on `RolePermission`'s old docstring is the only remaining Domain mention; the new docstring points at `IdentitySimfRole` to make the shadow-nav relationship discoverable. The two controlled docs (`SIMF-DAT-001`, `Developer-Guide`) reference `SimfRole` as historical narrative; they're best updated in their own owner-reviewed pass since they're spec documents (not in this commit). |
| D-092 | 2026-05-26 | **R5f — `SimfUser` becomes a pure Domain POCO; drops `IdentityUser<Guid>` inheritance.** The Domain class no longer inherits from any framework type. The Infrastructure-owned `IdentitySimfUser` (R5a — D-090) remains the EF-tracked entity; `IdentityUserMapper` (R5b — D-091) continues to shuttle field values across the boundary. NO consumer code changes required — the property surface is identical. Tests: **220 / 220** API unchanged. The Identity-derived properties are kept verbatim (same names, same types, same nullability) so no consumer code changes — the contract surface that reads `user.Email` etc. is preserved unchanged. The class stops inheriting cleanly; the only risk was something using `is IdentityUser<Guid>` or passing the user to a method typed `IdentityUser<TKey>`, and grep confirms zero such sites exist outside Infrastructure (which now talks to `IdentitySimfUser` instead). R5g (drop the package ref + add the architectural test) lands in the same session because the POCO change is meaningless without removing the dependency. |

| D-090 | 2026-05-26 | **R5a — Pure-POCO Domain, slice 1: introduce `IdentitySimfUser` persistence shim; re-point EF + Identity wiring; bidirectional proto-mapper in `UserAccountRepository`.** First slice of the R5 sprint per `docs/SIMF-Architecture-Refactor-Plan.md`. New `src/Backend/SIMF.Infrastructure/Identity/IdentitySimfUser.cs` mirrors the Domain `SimfUser` field-for-field and is the type EF tracks for the `AspNetUsers` table. `SimfIdentityDbContext` re-types its base from `IdentityDbContext<SimfUser, SimfRole, Guid>` to `IdentityDbContext<IdentitySimfUser, SimfRole, Guid>`; `SimfUserConfiguration` re-binds to `IEntityTypeConfiguration<IdentitySimfUser>`; `DependencyInjection.AddIdentityCore` re-types to `IdentitySimfUser`. Four FK-bearing configurations (`AccountCode`, `RefreshToken`, `SecondFactorToken`, `TotpRecoveryCode`) swap their `HasOne<SimfUser>()` to `HasOne<IdentitySimfUser>()` so EF stops discovering the Domain `SimfUser` as a duplicate entity that would demand its own table. `UserAccountRepository` rewritten around the merge-into-tracked pattern: a new `EnsureTrackedAsync(SimfUser)` reuses the already-tracked `IdentitySimfUser` from a prior `FindBy*` (the common case) or fetches a fresh tracked instance via `UserManager.FindByIdAsync`; `ApplyDomainMutations` copies caller mutations onto the tracked entity; `SyncBack` copies server-generated fields back. This preserves EF's concurrency-stamp snapshot — without it, every `find-then-update` cycle hits `DbUpdateConcurrencyException` (the snapshot's "original" ConcurrencyStamp equals the just-set new GUID, so the `WHERE ConcurrencyStamp = @old` matches zero rows). A snapshot-only EF migration (`R5aRebindUserEntityToIdentitySimfUser`) with empty `Up`/`Down` regenerates the model snapshot to point at `IdentitySimfUser` — DB schema unchanged. `IdentitySimfUser` is `public sealed` rather than `internal sealed` because C# requires a generic type parameter on a public class (`SimfIdentityDbContext`) to be at least as accessible. Test-fixture sweep: the **12 test files** that injected `UserManager<SimfUser>` directly from DI and the corresponding `new SimfUser { ... }` constructor sites swap to `UserManager<IdentitySimfUser>` and `new IdentitySimfUser { ... }` — purely mechanical, no semantic change. Tests: **220 API + 34 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = 272 / 272**. Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. R5b (extract `IdentityUserMapper` from the proto-mapper), R5c (re-shape repository return types around it), R5d–e (migrate Application and API consumers), R5f (drop `IdentityUser<Guid>` from `SimfUser`'s inheritance), R5g (drop `Microsoft.Extensions.Identity.Stores` from `SIMF.Domain.csproj`) remain queued. | First step of R5 per the architecture refactor plan. The seam is the type EF tracks — every other slice of R5 piggy-backs on this one. The Domain `SimfUser` still inherits `IdentityUser<Guid>` (R5f does the actual decoupling); R5a just gives Identity a Domain-free type to project rows into. The merge-into-tracked design replaces an earlier detach-after attempt that hit the "another instance with the same key is already tracked" guard (UserManager's internal `Context.Attach` does not tolerate a duplicate, and detaching the FindBy-tracked entity from the change tracker wasn't always sufficient in scope). Re-tracking-then-merging means UserManager always operates on the entity it would have loaded for itself — concurrency stamps line up, second-find queries find the existing tracked entry (no extra SELECT), and EF's identity map stays clean. The extra `FindByIdAsync` round-trip on writes that come WITHOUT a prior `FindByEmail/Id` is the cost of carving the seam in a single tractable commit; R5c removes it by re-shaping the repository contract to return the tracked entity directly. The proto-mapper's bidirectional copy is the §17 minimum that lets every caller — Application services and test fixtures — keep speaking `SimfUser`. The FK configuration fix is load-bearing: without it, the empty migration EF wanted to generate had `CreateTable("SimfUser")` plus 4 `DropForeignKey`/`AddForeignKey` pairs, because EF's convention scanner discovered Domain `SimfUser` through the orphan `HasOne<SimfUser>()` lines and treated it as a separate entity that needed its own table. Pointing the FKs at `IdentitySimfUser` is the canonical fix and produces the truly empty migration. The 12-test-file fixture sweep was the bulk of the test work — the repository contract is unchanged, so application-flow tests pass without modification; only fixtures that bypass the repo to seed users directly via `UserManager` needed the type swap. |
| D-088 | 2026-05-26 | **H28 + H29 — Cancellation-token entry checks on `UserAccountRepository`; default per-IP global rate-limit on every request.** Two findings from the post-R3 review closed in one commit. **(H28 / Finding I)** Every method on `UserAccountRepository` (the `IUserAccountRepository` implementation in Infrastructure) calls `cancellationToken.ThrowIfCancellationRequested()` at entry. A pre-cancelled call now throws immediately instead of running the `UserManager` operation to completion — making the `CancellationToken` parameter on the interface honest at the boundary. `UserManager`'s public API does not accept tokens, so true mid-operation cancellation is the R5-level swap to a Domain-owned user store (queued in the follow-up backlog); the entry-throw is the §17 minimum that satisfies the contract today. **(H29 / Security SEV-2.1 main)** `Program.cs`'s `AddRateLimiter` registers a `GlobalLimiter` via `PartitionedRateLimiter.Create<HttpContext, string>` keyed on `Connection.RemoteIpAddress?.ToString() ?? "unknown"` with a per-IP fixed-window cap of `GlobalPermitLimit / GlobalWindowSeconds` (defaults 600 / 60s). The global cap applies to EVERY request, stacking with the per-route `"auth"` and `"auth-email"` policies for credential flows — pre-H29 bearer-protected routes had no per-IP cap, so a malformed-bearer flood could pin a CPU on token validation. `RateLimitOptions` gains `GlobalPermitLimit` (600) and `GlobalWindowSeconds` (60); `SimfApiFactory` test default sets `RateLimit__GlobalPermitLimit=1000000` so long-running test classes that hit hundreds of endpoints don't trip the cap. Tests: **220 API + 34 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = 272 / 272** unchanged. Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | (H28) Closes the half of post-R3 Finding I that is satisfiable without R5. The reviewer's deeper point — "the impl drops the token because UserManager doesn't accept one" — is structurally true and stays true until the user-store swap. Calling `ThrowIfCancellationRequested()` at entry costs one comparison per method but eliminates the "I passed a cancelled token and the operation still ran" failure mode at the cheapest possible cost. (H29) Closes the main half of post-R3 Security SEV-2.1. The pre-H29 rate limiter only covered `/auth/*`; bearer-protected routes (the entire `/admin/*` surface, `/account/*`, `/api/v1/notifications/*`, etc.) had no per-IP rate cap. A flood from one IP on any of those routes — malformed bearer or otherwise — could starve the server. The global limiter is permissive enough that real traffic never sees it (600/min/IP = 10/s; legitimate clients are well below that even during bursty UI navigation) and tight enough that abuse traffic hits the cap fast. The cap stacks with the per-route policies — `/auth/sign-in` is now bounded by THREE concurrent partitions (global per-IP + auth per-IP + auth-email per-email); the strictest one wins per request. The remaining Security SEV-2.1 sub-finding — fire-and-forget audit channel — is queued as a Bucket 3 follow-up; H26 (D-086) already caps the worst single offender (bearer-rejection audit writes). |

| D-086 | 2026-05-26 | **H26 — Per-IP audit-DB write throttle on bearer rejections (Security SEV-2.1 part 2).** `JwtBearerSetup.AuditRejectionAsync` now counts bearer rejections per source IP in an `IMemoryCache`-backed 60-second sliding window keyed `simf-bearer-rej:{ip}`. The 11th rejection from the same IP in that window skips the synchronous `IAuditLog.WriteAsync` (drops the DB row) but the structured `ILogger<JwtBearerOptions>.LogWarning("Bearer rejection from {Ip}: {Detail}")` line ALWAYS fires — SOC's log forwarder still sees the storm. After the cap is reached, every further hit also writes a "Bearer rejection audit suppressed" warning so the storm duration is visible. Cap constants: `MaxAuditWritesPerWindow = 10`, `AuditWindow = TimeSpan.FromMinutes(1)`. New DI registration: `services.AddMemoryCache()` in `Program.cs`. Regression test in `JwtMiddlewareTests.Bearer_rejection_audit_writes_are_capped_per_ip_within_the_window`: clears the rejection counter, fires 15 bearer-garbage requests, asserts the audit-row delta is `<= 10` and `>= 1`. Tests: **220 API** (219 + 1 H26) + 34 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **272 / 272**. Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Closes Security SEV-2.1 from the post-R3 5-agent review. Pre-H26 every malformed-bearer request synchronously wrote a DB row through `AuditLog.WriteAsync`'s `SaveChangesAsync` — an attacker firing `Authorization: Bearer <garbage>` against any protected endpoint forced one DB INSERT per request, with no rate limit on that route (the rate limiter's `"auth"` partition is bound only to the `/auth/*` endpoints; bearer-protected endpoints are not covered). The throttle is the minimum-viable DoS guard the post-R3 reviewer specifically named. A fuller fix — a fire-and-forget audit channel mirroring `EmailQueue` + `EmailBackgroundService`, batching inserts asynchronously — is queued for a later operations sprint (the audit log is not the only synchronous side-effect on the request path; `IRequestContext` writes are too). The per-IP cap keyed on `Connection.RemoteIpAddress?.ToString() ?? "unknown"` — same shape as the per-IP rate-limit partition in H7 (D-062), so a misrouted no-IP batch all shares one `"unknown"` bucket. Defence-in-depth: even if the cache misses (it's registered via `AddMemoryCache` which uses an in-process implementation; multi-instance deployments would need a distributed cache to share state), the request still pays the per-route auth cost; only the audit DB write is gated. |
| D-085 | 2026-05-26 | **H25 — End-to-end visitor-lifecycle test.** New `tests/SIMF.Api.Tests/VisitorLifecycleTests.cs` drives the full chain in one `[Fact]`: sign-up → Registered → verify-email → EmailVerified → Web-audience sign-in (gets pre-profile tokens) → profile upsert (with one seeded `Interest`) triggers H2 (D-057) auto-transition to PendingApproval → admin (created in-test, audience=Cp) approves → Approved + `QrId` minted → final App-audience sign-in returns a JWT carrying `account_state=Approved` + `user_type=Visitor` (asserted via direct JWT claim read). The test closes Sprint 1 §3.5 — the cross-hop seam (e.g. an Approved visitor cannot sign in on the App audience because of a stamp/claim drift) is now pinned. Tests: **219 API** (218 + 1) + 34 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **271 / 271**. Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Closes Sprint 1 §3.5 (DEFER) — the one Reality reviewer Finding 9 that was definite-do-eventually. Pre-H25, each lifecycle hop had its own test (sign-up, verify-email, profile upsert, admin approve, App-audience sign-in) but no test exercised them in chain — a regression at any boundary between two hops (e.g. an Approved visitor's JWT carries the wrong `user_type`, or H2's transaction commits but the security stamp doesn't roll) would have been invisible. The single-Fact shape is deliberate: failures land on the line of the failed step rather than scattered across the suite, the test's diff IS the lifecycle contract, and the test reads top-to-bottom like the user's actual journey. The same shape is the template for future module increments — User Management, the Forum programme, etc. will each get their own one-Fact lifecycle test. The dedicated factory pattern (e.g. `ThrowingEmailQueueApiFactory`) is NOT needed for this test because every step exercises the production code path. |

Failures are deliberately swallowed (the H10 contract says response shape must not change, so a failed dispatch is audit-only). The per-service helpers that built each email (`EnqueueResetEmail`, `EnqueueVerificationEmail`, `EnqueueSignInOtpEmail`) renamed to `BuildResetEmail` / `BuildVerificationEmail` / `BuildSignInOtpEmail` returning `EmailMessage` instead of enqueueing — the helper now owns enqueueing AND failure handling, the caller just decides what to send. | D-082 | 2026-05-25 | **H20 + H21 — SIMF-owned `UserOperationResult` replaces `IdentityResult` on `IUserAccountRepository`; discarded results now throw via `.EnsureSuccessAsync()`.** Two findings from the post-R3 review closed in one commit. **(H21 / Finding C)** New `UserOperationResult` record + `UserOperationError` record in `src/Backend/SIMF.Application/IdentityAccess/Abstractions/UserOperationResult.cs` — SIMF-owned, no `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity` reference. The `Code` strings stay Identity-compatible (e.g. `"PasswordMismatch"`, `"DuplicateEmail"`) so the existing `IdentityErrorTranslator` switch arms match the new shape unchanged. `IUserAccountRepository` updated: 12 method signatures change return type from `Task<IdentityResult>` to `Task<UserOperationResult>`. The `using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity` line is removed from the interface file — Application no longer transitively depends on Identity types (closes the residual Architecture SEV-1.1 the post-R3 review flagged). `UserAccountRepository` implementation adds a private `Translate(IdentityResult) → UserOperationResult` helper called at the wrapper boundary; the framework primitive stays in Infrastructure. `IdentityErrorTranslator.ToArabic` parameter changes from `IdentityError` to `UserOperationError`. `PasswordService.PasswordRejected` helper signature follows the same swap. **(H20 / Finding D)** New `UserOperationResultExtensions.EnsureSuccessAsync(Task<UserOperationResult>)` extension throws `InvalidOperationException` with the joined error codes when the operation failed. Applied to **15 previously-discarded call sites** across `SignInService` (1), `PasswordService` (1), `RegistrationService` (1), `AdminAccountService` (8 — including the 2FA reset wipe sequence and 3 visitor-state-change updates), `IdentitySeeder` (4), `TotpEnrollmentService` (6 — every enrol / confirm / disable write), `AccountService` (2). Sites that explicitly check `result.Succeeded` are unchanged — they keep their existing flow. The chosen sites are the ones where a silent failure would corrupt security-relevant state: e.g. `SignInService.VerifyTotpAsync` writes `LastUsedTotpTimestep` (silent failure defeats the TOTP replay guard); `AdminAccountService.ResetTwoFactorAsync` wipes 2FA tokens (silent failure makes the audit log lie). Tests: **218 API + 34 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = 270 / 270**. Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Closes Architecture SEV-1.1 (post-R3) and Security SEV-1.3 + Code SEV-1.1 from the post-R3 5-agent review in one commit. The post-R3 review correctly observed that R3 "hid the verb (`UserManager`), not the nouns" — `IdentityResult` and `IdentityError` were still on the Application-facing contract, and Application's `.csproj` would not compile if Domain stopped re-exporting Identity. The fix is the SIMF-owned record with a 1:1 shape, so the translation at the wrapper boundary is straightforward and the Identity Code strings (which downstream consumers like `IdentityErrorTranslator` switch on) survive the swap. The reviewer also noted that 18 of 19 IdentityResult-returning call sites discarded the result — `EnsureSuccessAsync` is the §17 minimum-invasive fix: a single `.EnsureSuccessAsync()` per site is mechanical, gives every previously-silent failure a stack trace, and doesn't disturb the explicit-check sites. Three sites that aren't `UpdateAsync`/`Add*`/`Set*` patterns and one site where the existing flow already checks `.Succeeded` are intentionally unchanged. The exception type chosen is `InvalidOperationException` (not a domain exception) because (a) the error middleware maps it to 500 — appropriate for a failed write that the caller had no chance to validate against, and (b) introducing a SIMF-domain exception type for "user store said no" would invite explicit `try/catch` blocks at every call site, recreating the boilerplate the extension is supposed to remove. |

(F) **SimfBrandPanel** (`src/Shared/SIMF.Components/Layout/SimfBrandPanel.razor`) gains an `AltText` parameter (English fallback `"SIMF — Saudi International Maritime Forum"`); the nine sign-in / sign-up / forgot / reset callers across CP and Website now pass `AltText='@L["Brand.LogoAlt"]'`. Arabic-locale screen readers now hear the brand name in Arabic phonemes instead of English mangled through the Arabic engine. Tab now naturally flows forward through the dropdown content and onto the page, no trap needed. New `_triggerEl` ElementReference + `_restoreFocusToTrigger` render-flag; on every close path (backdrop click, trigger Esc, dropdown Esc) focus returns to the trigger via `_triggerEl.FocusAsync()` in `OnAfterRenderAsync` — keyboard users land back where they invoked the popup. (O) **SimfThemeToggle** (`src/Shared/SIMF.Components/Controls/SimfThemeToggle.razor`) gains `aria-pressed="@_isDark.ToString().ToLowerInvariant()"`: ARIA toggle-button pattern so AT announces "pressed / not pressed" on state change (was silent — the visible icon + text label swap was inaccessible). **H17 markup-assertion tests updated** to match: bell token list switched `aria-haspopup="dialog"` → `"true"` and added `_triggerEl.FocusAsync` as a required token; state-banner theory split into CP-uses-`<main>` and Website-uses-`<section>` variants; new Fact `Website_main_layout_wraps_body_in_main_landmark`. Tests: 218 API + **34 CP** (32 + 2 new from H17 update) + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **270 / 270**. Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Closes A11y SEV-1.1 (post-R3), SEV-1.3 (bell focus trap / restore), SEV-1.4 (Website landmark), SEV-1.2 (toggle aria-pressed) from the post-R3 5-agent review in one commit. (E) closes the gap H6's `<html lang dir>` fix surfaced — every Arabic Website page renders RTL but had no landmark structure for AT navigation. (F) recovers the bilingual brand-name accessibility that H6 + the resx pattern make possible; the English fallback keeps existing test-only callers working. (N) chooses the disclosure pattern over a focus-trap implementation because (a) the dropdown's content is a flat list, not a modal dialog with form fields where containment matters; (b) AT users navigating with screen-reader rotor expect the popover to be a transient surface they Tab through and out of; (c) the focus-restore-on-close pattern is the load-bearing fix the reviewer named, and it works the same regardless of dialog-vs-disclosure. (O) is the cheapest possible fix — one attribute. All four fit together because they share the same code-base section (component library + Website layout) and they share the same test-update story (H17 absorbs all four contract changes). |
| D-080 | 2026-05-25 | **H19 — PasswordChangeRequired enforced at every token-mint path; body-buffer cap on the per-email rate-limit middleware; CheckAccountState now fires before the password-change gate.** Combined commit closes Security SEV-1.1 + SEV-1.2 + Code SEV-2.3 from the post-R3 5-agent review. Three changes: **(A)** New helper `RequirePasswordChangeNotRequired(SimfUser)` in `SignInService` throws `ApiException(AuthPasswordChangeRequired, 403)` whenever the flag is set. Called inside `VerifyTotpAsync`, `VerifyRecoveryCodeAsync`, `VerifyOtpAsync` (after `EnsureNotLockedOutAsync`), and `SessionService.RefreshAsync` (after the user is loaded by refresh-token-presented userId). Pre-H19 the H4 (D-059) gate only fired at the initial password step — a holder of a live MFA ticket or 30-day refresh token rotated forever after an admin set the flag. The full enforcement is now end-to-end across every mint surface. **(B)** `EmailRateLimitKeyMiddleware.TryReadEmailAsync` caps the body buffer at 8 KB: pre-buffer `Content-Length` guard refuses oversize bodies before any allocation; `EnableBuffering(bufferThreshold: 8192, bufferLimit: 8192)` caps the spill-to-disk too. Bare `catch` narrowed: `OperationCanceledException` propagates (was masked), `JsonException` returns null with a comment (legit parse failure → fall back to per-IP), `IOException` returns null (bufferLimit-exceeded mid-copy → same fallback). Closes Security SEV-1.2 + Security SEV-2.2 at the same time. **(C)** The `CheckAccountState` block now runs BEFORE the `PasswordChangeRequired` gate in `SignInService.SignInAsync` (was reversed pre-H19) — a Disabled / Registered user with the flag set previously got `AUTH_PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED` (an enumeration oracle that told the attacker the account exists, is provisioned, and is just awaiting a reset). Now the disabled/unverified state-block fires first; the password-change gate only applies to live, signable accounts. Regression test in `SignInTests.Refresh_is_blocked_when_PasswordChangeRequired_is_set_after_sign_in`: signs in as a 2FA-disabled visitor, mints a refresh token, flips the flag, asserts `/auth/refresh` returns 403 + AUTH_PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED. Tests: **218 API** (217 + 1 H19) + 32 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **268 / 268**. Build: 0 warnings, 0 errors at both Debug and Release. | Closes three findings from the post-R3 review in one commit. (A) is the highest-impact security defect surfaced by the review: H4 (D-059) shipped as a single-chokepoint check at the password step; the review correctly pointed out that any holder of a downstream artefact (MFA ticket, refresh token) walked around it. The fix is the helper-method pattern so each mint surface gets the same gate — the helper is `private static` so future Verify methods can't forget to call it (a follow-up Roslyn analyzer could enforce this, but the call-site explicit pattern is fine for now). (B) closes the body-buffer DoS amplifier the review flagged: `EnableBuffering()` with no `bufferLimit` lets an attacker post a 30 MB JSON body and force a 30 MB MemoryStream allocation per request on the pre-auth path. 8 KB is well above legitimate credential bodies (< 1 KB in normal use) and far below the abuse threshold. Same commit narrows the catch to avoid masking cancellation, which the review's Finding J flagged separately. (C) is the smaller enumeration-oracle leak: the H4 comment block defended the original order ("the right password is the wrong door"), but the right door for a Disabled account is the disabled-account block; routing the flagged-disabled user via PasswordChangeRequired tells the attacker the account exists, is alive, and has just been credential-rotated — exactly the signal account-enumeration controls are supposed to suppress. The findings the review tagged for the bonus shape (rotating the security stamp + revoking refresh tokens when the flag is set) are not added in this commit because `AdminAccountService.ResetTwoFactorAsync` and other admin flows already do both unconditionally; the seeder path doesn't need them (no live tokens exist at first boot). |

| D-076 | 2026-05-25 | **R3a — Introduce `IUserAccountRepository` abstraction; migrate `RegistrationService`.** New `IUserAccountRepository` interface in `src/Backend/SIMF.Application/IdentityAccess/Abstractions/` covers the full `SimfUser`-aggregate surface the six pre-R3 Application services need (lookups, lifecycle, credentials, lockout, roles, TOTP). Thirteen endpoint files migrate from `IAdminAccountService` to the specific interface they need — `ApproveStaffEndpoint` / `ApproveVisitorEndpoint` / `RejectStaffEndpoint` / `RejectVisitorEndpoint` → `IAdminUserApprovalService`; `CreateUserEndpoint` / `ListUsersEndpoint` / `ListPendingEndpoints` / `DuplicateUserEndpoint` → `IAdminUserProvisioningService`; `BulkDeleteUsersEndpoint` / `ExportUsersEndpoint` / `ImportUsersEndpoint` → `IAdminUserBulkService`; `ResetTwoFactorEndpoint` → `IAdminTwoFactorService`; `ListProfileTypesEndpoint` → `IAdminProfileTypeQueryService`. The factory-delegate DI shape is a deliberate `§17` move: keeping one shared instance backing all five registrations preserves the current per-request shared state of the implementation (audit, transactions, db context) without spreading them across five constructor signatures. A future commit that splits the impl into five classes can wire each one separately and the endpoint-side contract stays identical. The four bigger refactors (R3 through R6) are deferred to a dedicated refactor sprint per the plan doc — attempting them in this turn would risk multi-day regression work without enough validation runway. The plan doc names the size, the order, and the risk profile so the owner can scope the sprint. |

Two raw reads remain intentional: `Program.cs`'s Serilog setup at line 32 reads `Storage:LogDirectory` directly because the Serilog hook runs before the DI container is built (`IOptions` is unavailable there); and `LogFileService` mirrors the same `"logs"` fallback the prior code had. The other three Options classes in the project (`JwtOptions`, `EmailOptions`, `SuperAdminOptions`) already follow this pattern — R1 brings the storage seam in line. The `ValidateOnStart` upgrade is a small bonus: the previous boot gate lived in `Program.cs` and only checked AvatarBase, missing the other two required Storage keys; now AvatarBase is enforced at host build, and the constructor-time gates on EncryptedUserIdDocumentStorage and FilesystemAvatarStorage still fire on first service resolution (they throw before any request is served). | D-069 | 2026-05-25 | **H14 — `SimfFileUpload` exposes the most-recently-picked file in an `aria-live` region and composes the input's `aria-describedby` to include it.** Two new parameters: `PickedFilename` (the parent supplies after reading the file via JS interop, mirroring D-039's avatar-upload pattern) and `PickedLabel` (localised prefix like "Picked:" / "تم الاختيار:"). A `<p class="simf-fileupload__status" id="@_statusId" aria-live="polite">` is rendered immediately after the input; visible only when a filename is set, but kept in the DOM so live-region updates fire when the parent changes the parameter. `DescribedBy` now composes a space-separated list of relevant ids — error (if any) OR helper (otherwise), plus the status region when populated. Before this commit the `SimfFileUpload` had a `<label for>` (good — the input's accessible name was correct) and an `aria-describedby` that pointed at either the helper or the error, but no surface for "you picked X". A keyboard-only user (or a screen-reader user) had no way to know the picker accepted their file before pressing the external Upload button — they had to either trust silence or look at the native input's "1 file selected" toggle, which assistive tech does not consistently announce. The fix is the standard `aria-live="polite"` status pattern: visible state for sighted keyboard users, announced state for screen readers. Keeping the region in the DOM (vs `display: none` when empty) is the live-region contract — the browser must hold a stable node for AT to observe its text changing. The bigger reviewer item — placing the Upload button inside the field shell with `aria-controls` to pair the two — is intentionally deferred: that's an API-shape change for every caller, and the live-region status closes the actual barrier (the user not knowing whether their pick registered) without breaking the component contract. |
| D-068 | 2026-05-25 | **H13 — File-input pickers on the CP now carry programmatic accessible names.** Two `<input type="file">` elements that previously rendered with `id` + `accept` but no label were corrected. `Profile.razor` (CP `/account/profile`) avatar picker gains a visible `<label for="avatar-input">` localised as `Profile.Avatar.Picker` (EN: "Choose avatar image file" / AR: "اختر ملف صورة الحساب"), and the existing size-hint `<p>` gets `id="avatar-input-hint"` linked from the input via `aria-describedby`. `UsersList.razor` (CP `/admin/admins` Import button) — its picker is visually hidden (triggered by JS via a `<SimfButton>` label "Import" elsewhere on the page), so `aria-label` is used instead of `<label for>`; localised as `Admin.Users.Import.Picker` (EN: "Choose Excel file to import" / AR: "اختر ملف Excel للاستيراد"). Tests: **202 API + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = 238 / 238** (no test count change — markup-only). | Closes A11y SEV-1.2 from the 5-agent review. Before this commit a screen-reader user activating the avatar `<input type="file">` heard "Choose file" (the browser's default) with no context — the picker was indistinguishable from any other file picker on the page. The Import-XLSX picker was worse: visually hidden and label-less, an assistive-tech traversal that surfaced it would have heard nothing meaningful. The avatar picker gets the `<label for>` + `aria-describedby` pair (the standard WCAG 1.3.1 + 3.3.2 pattern); the visually-hidden import picker can't use `<label for>` (no visible text to bind to) so it uses `aria-label` instead. Both keys are bilingual via `Strings.resx` / `Strings.ar.resx`. The H14 follow-up will harden the `SimfFileUpload` shared component itself so future file pickers built on the primitive get the same affordance automatically. |
| D-067 | 2026-05-25 | **H12 — Password-reset and change-password success tests now prove the credential step actually accepted the new password.** `PasswordTests.Reset_password_with_a_valid_code_sets_the_new_password` and `PasswordTests.Change_password_with_the_correct_current_password_succeeds` previously asserted only `HttpStatusCode.OK` on the post-reset / post-change sign-in. Both tests use 2FA-enabled visitors (`RegisterVerifiedVisitorAsync` / `SignInVisitorAsync` flip `TwoFactorEnabled=true`), so a 200 is the OTP challenge — NOT a full token mint. A regression that broke the password step but still emitted an OTP challenge for a wrong password would have passed silently. Both tests now read the response body as `ApiResult<SignInResponse>` and assert `Success == true`, `MfaRequired == true`, and `OtpToken` is non-empty — proving the password step did pass. No test count change (existing tests strengthened in place). Tests: **202 API + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = 238 / 238**. | Closes Code SEV-1.4 from the 5-agent review. A status-code-only assertion on a 2FA endpoint is structurally insufficient — the endpoint returns 200 for both "password OK, here's the OTP challenge" and "password OK, here are tokens" — but it also returns 200 if a future refactor silently emits an MFA-required envelope without checking the password. The fix narrows the assertion to the actual contract: a successful password step against a 2FA-enabled visitor returns `MfaRequired=true` + non-empty `OtpToken`. The full lifecycle (OTP verify → tokens) is covered by other tests and not duplicated here; this test's job is to pin the *password*-step semantics. |
| D-066 | 2026-05-25 | **H11 — `PasswordChange.Failed` audit row now separates wrong-current from policy-rejected.** Inside `PasswordService.ChangePasswordAsync`, the post-`userManager.ChangePasswordAsync` failure branch inspects `result.Errors` for `"PasswordMismatch"`. If found, the audit row carries `ErrorCode = AuthInvalidCredentials` and `Detail = "WrongCurrent: {identityErrorCodes}"`; otherwise `ErrorCode = AuthPasswordPolicy` (new constant) and `Detail = "PolicyRejected: {identityErrorCodes}"`. The user-facing API error is unchanged — wrong-current still raises `400 / AUTH_INVALID_CREDENTIALS` with the same bilingual message, weak-new still raises the policy `DataValidationException`. Only the audit row gains forensic detail. Regression test in `PasswordTests`: a wrong-current attempt writes `PasswordChange.Failed / AuthInvalidCredentials / Detail starts with "WrongCurrent:"`. The PolicyRejected branch is intentionally not regression-tested at the HTTP level: per D-005 the FluentValidation `StrongPassword` rules and the Identity `PasswordOptions` config enforce the exact same baseline (length 8, one digit, one letter), so no normal HTTP request reaches the Identity-layer rejection path. The audit shape is in place as defence-in-depth — it fires when Identity's policy is tightened beyond the validator, or for non-HTTP callers (seeders, admin tools) that bypass FluentValidation. Tests: **202 API** (201 + 1 H11) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **238 / 238**. | Closes Code SEV-1.3 from the 5-agent review. The pre-H11 audit wrote `PasswordChange.Failed / AuthInvalidCredentials` for both failure modes. SOC reading the audit log could not separate brute-force-current-password attempts (where the attacker has the user's session and is iterating the current password) from a user typing weak new passwords five times in a row. The two have very different incident-response weights: the first is an active compromise signal, the second is a UX-friction signal. Adding the `AuthPasswordPolicy` error code + `WrongCurrent / PolicyRejected` Detail prefix makes the timeline reconstructable from the row alone. `AuthPasswordPolicy` lives in `ErrorCodes` because consistency demands it (every audit row's `ErrorCode` lookups to a constant), even though no API response surfaces it directly. |
| D-065 | 2026-05-25 | **H10 — `IEmailQueue.Enqueue` failures now write a distinct `Email.EnqueueFailed` audit row.** Three call sites wrapped: `PasswordService.ForgotPasswordAsync` (`PasswordReset` purpose), `RegistrationService.SignUpAsync` + `RegistrationService.ResendCodeAsync` (`EmailVerification` purpose), and `SignInService.SignInAsync` OTP-issue branch (`SignInOtp` purpose). Each `try { Enqueue(...) } catch (Exception ex) { logger.LogError; AuditAsync(EmailEnqueueFailed, Failure, detail: $"{purpose}: {ex.GetType().Name}") }`. The surrounding success audit row (e.g. `ForgotPassword.Requested = Success`) still fires — the contract that the response shape never reveals account existence is preserved — but SOC now sees the parallel `Email.EnqueueFailed` row and knows the user never actually received the message. New audit constant: `AuditEvents.EmailEnqueueFailed = "Email.EnqueueFailed"`. New test factory `ThrowingEmailQueueApiFactory` substitutes the `IEmailQueue` for an implementation that throws on every Enqueue. Two regression tests in `EmailEnqueueFailureTests`: forgot-password with throwing queue writes both Email.EnqueueFailed AND ForgotPassword.Requested(Success); sign-up with throwing queue writes Email.EnqueueFailed while the account is still created. Tests: **201 API** (199 + 2 H10) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **237 / 237**. | Closes Code SEV-1.1 from the post-H1/H2/H3 5-agent review. The three credential-flow paths persist a code/ticket row to the DB, then call into `IEmailQueue.Enqueue` — a separate scope outside the transaction. If the queue throws (channel closed during shutdown, OOM, downstream SMTP failure inside the worker that re-throws to the channel), the user holds a code they will never receive, and the success audit row says "ForgotPassword.Requested / Success" — masking the gap from SOC. The decision-log mention of an outbox-style guarantee under D-057 is the right long-term shape; this commit is the minimum-viable observability fix until then: catch, log, audit. Throws are NOT propagated to the caller — the always-200 contract for forgot-password (which prevents account-enumeration oracles per D-006) is preserved, and the sign-up / sign-in responses keep their existing shape. The user still gets a "request received" response; the operator sees the gap in the audit log. The same shape can later be lifted to a `WithEmailEnqueueAudit(...)` helper if a fourth call site appears. |
| D-064 | 2026-05-25 | **H9 — Skip-to-main-content link in the Control Panel shell.** `SimfAppShell.razor` (the shared layout used by every signed-in CP page) gains an `<a class="simf-skip-link" href="#simf-main">@SkipNavLabel</a>` as the first focusable element, and the `<main>` content region gets `id="simf-main" tabindex="-1"` so the link's anchor jumps focus there. New `SkipNavLabel` parameter (default English fallback). CP layout wires it via `Strings.resx` (`"Skip to main content"`) and `Strings.ar.resx` (`"تخطي إلى المحتوى الرئيسي"`). New CSS `.simf-skip-link` in `simf-components.css`: absolutely positioned, off-screen via `transform: translateY(-200%)`, slides into view on `:focus-visible` with a `120ms ease-out` transition and a visible accent-blue outline. `.simf-shell__content:focus` outline cleared so the programmatic focus on jump doesn't render a ring on the main region itself. Website skip-link deferred — the Website has no heavy navigation to bypass; per-page `<main>` landmarks added by H3 (D-058) on the state-banner pages suffice for that surface. Tests: **199 API + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = 235 / 235** (no test count change — markup-only, follows the D-061 precedent for App.razor). | Closes A11y SEV-1.4 (WCAG 2.4.1 Bypass Blocks) from the 5-agent review. The Control Panel renders ~30 navigation items + the top bar's brand, language switch, theme toggle, notification bell, profile link, and sign-out form before reaching the page heading on every page. A keyboard-only user had to Tab through all of them on every navigation. The skip-link sits as the first focusable element so the first Tab press lands on it, Enter jumps focus to the `<main>` region, and the navigation is bypassed. The link is invisible (via `transform`, not `display: none`, so the focus state can transition in smoothly) and only appears on `:focus-visible` — sighted users see no visual change. `tabindex="-1"` on `<main>` is required because non-form / non-link elements aren't normally focusable via JS (`#simf-main` won't accept focus from the link target without it). Direction-aware via CSS logical properties (`inset-block-start`, `inset-inline-start`) — works in both LTR (English) and RTL (Arabic). The Website's parallel fix is deferred because (a) the Website's pages are short and lack a comparable navigation block, (b) the H3 work already added `<main>` landmarks on the state-banner pages, and (c) the bigger Website gap is the page-level `<main>` landmark missing on the auth pages, which is a separate broader fix. |
| D-063 | 2026-05-25 | **H8 — `ChangePasswordRequest.CurrentPassword` is now length-capped at 128.** `ChangePasswordRequestValidator.cs` adds `.MaximumLength(128).Bilingual("The current password is too long.", "كلمة المرور الحالية طويلة جداً.")` on `CurrentPassword`, mirroring the same ceiling `StrongPassword()` already enforces on `NewPassword`. Regression test in `PasswordTests`: posting a 129-character `CurrentPassword` returns `400 / VALIDATION_FAILED` before any PBKDF2 work. Tests: **199 API** (198 + 1 H8) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **235 / 235**. | Closes Code SEV-1.2 from the 5-agent review. Without the cap a caller could post a megabyte-sized `CurrentPassword` string; the validator's `NotEmpty()` rule would let it through, and `userManager.ChangePasswordAsync` would then run PBKDF2 over the whole payload inside a transaction — a cheap, authenticated DoS vector. The 128-char ceiling matches `StrongPassword()`'s cap on `NewPassword`; the asymmetry between the two fields was the actual bug (one field had a runtime cost ceiling, the other had none). Aligns with CLAUDE.md §7 "validator length must match storage / runtime ceilings". |
| D-062 | 2026-05-25 | **H7 — Per-email rate-limit partition guards credential endpoints across IP rotation.** Adds a second rate-limit policy `"auth-email"` to `Program.cs`'s `AddRateLimiter` that keys its `FixedWindowLimiter` on the normalised email field from the request body. A new pipeline middleware `EmailRateLimitKeyMiddleware` peeks the body of `/auth/sign-in`, `/auth/forgot-password`, and `/auth/reset-password` (the three credential-touching paths), enables `Request.EnableBuffering`, copies the body into a `MemoryStream` to parse a `JsonDocument`, scans the root object case-insensitively for an `email` string property, and stashes the normalised value (`Trim().ToLowerInvariant()`) into `HttpContext.Items["SIMF.RateLimit.Email"]`. The original request body stream is rewound to position 0 so FastEndpoints' model binder still sees the full payload. The three credential endpoints chain both policies via `Options(b => b.RequireRateLimiting("auth").RequireRateLimiting("auth-email"))`. New options: `RateLimitOptions.EmailPermitLimit` (default 5) and `EmailWindowSeconds` (default 60). New test factory `EmailRateLimitedApiFactory` tightens `EmailPermitLimit=3` while the base `SimfApiFactory` sets `EmailPermitLimit=100000` (permissive — mirrors the existing per-IP test default — so legitimate tests like `SignIn_locks_the_account_after_five_wrong_passwords` are not capped). Two regression tests in `EmailRateLimitTests`: (1) four sign-ins against the same email trip the per-email cap on the 4th; (2) a second email retains its own partition and still gets through. Tests: **198 API** (196 + 2 H7) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **234 / 234**. The null-IP `?? "unknown"` fallback is left unchanged (deferred from the original H7 plan): tightening it to `Math.Min(5, PermitLimit)` broke ASP.NET TestServer (which sets no `Connection.RemoteIpAddress` on any request) by funnelling every test request into a 5-permit bucket; with the per-email partition now bounding credential stuffing independent of IP key, the value-add of further tightening "unknown" is small. Revisit if a production signal shows misrouted no-IP traffic abusing the fallback. | Closes Security SEV-1.4 from the post-H1/H2/H3 5-agent review. The previous "auth" policy keyed only on the source IP — an attacker rotating proxies against `victim@example.com` was bounded per-IP (20/min default) but unbounded per-target, so the per-account lockout (5 wrong passwords) would fire after 5 attempts across N IPs, denying the legitimate user. With the new per-email partition, the same attacker is bounded to `EmailPermitLimit` attempts against the victim across the entire IP space — credential stuffing is throttled before it can DoS the account. The middleware position is `BEFORE UseRateLimiter` (between `ErrorHandlingMiddleware` and `UseRateLimiter`) because the rate limiter needs the email key set when its policy delegate runs — a stash-in-HttpContext.Items pattern fits because `Items` is the canonical per-request bag for cross-middleware signalling. The body-peek uses `EnableBuffering` + a MemoryStream copy (rather than parsing the stream directly) so the original body's position is predictable for FastEndpoints' model binder downstream. The case-insensitive `email` scan handles both `PostAsJsonAsync` (PascalCase `"Email"`) and any hand-rolled client (lowercase `"email"`) without depending on a System.Text.Json naming policy change. The `NoLimiter` fallback (when the path is unknown to the middleware) means chaining `"auth-email"` on a route that doesn't carry an email is harmless. |
| D-061 | 2026-05-25 | **H6 — Website `App.razor` `<html lang dir>` now tracks the current UI culture.** `src/Website/SIMF.Web/Components/App.razor` adds `@using System.Globalization` and renders `<html lang="@CultureInfo.CurrentUICulture.TwoLetterISOLanguageName" dir="@(CultureInfo.CurrentUICulture.TextInfo.IsRightToLeft ? "rtl" : "ltr")">`. Exactly mirrors the Control Panel's pattern (D-028). The Website already calls `UseRequestLocalization` (Program.cs:120) so `CurrentUICulture` is set from the culture cookie / Accept-Language header per request — the App.razor change is the last mile that surfaces it to the document. No new test: matches the precedent set when the same pattern landed on the CP (D-028 shipped without a bUnit assertion for the markup-only behaviour). The bigger a11y review gap (no automated coverage for the CP and Website App.razor markup contract) is noted as a follow-up. Tests: **196 API + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = 232 / 232** (no test count change). | Closes A11y SEV-1.1 from the 5-agent review. Until this commit, every Arabic-culture Website page (`/account/profile`, `/account/notifications`, `/account/pending`, `/account/rejected`, plus the public marketing site's Blazor islands) shipped with `<html lang="en" dir="ltr">`. Screen readers (NVDA, JAWS, VoiceOver) announce Arabic strings with English phonemes when `lang="en"`, and the entire layout flows left-to-right while the localised text reads right-to-left — punctuation mirroring breaks, CSS logical properties (`inset-inline-start`, `text-align: start`) compute against the wrong direction, and the H3 state-banner pages' `<main>` landmark inherits the wrong direction. The CP shipped the correct pattern in D-028 (the CP localisation foundation increment); the Website regressed it. The fix is the one-line port that should have happened when the Website's localisation pipeline landed alongside the CP's. The change is markup-only — no API, contract, or behaviour change — and the build / test set is untouched. |
| D-060 | 2026-05-25 | **H5 — `OnTokenValidated` requires a non-empty `security_stamp` claim and compares it in constant time.** `JwtBearerSetup.OnTokenValidatedAsync` now does three checks instead of one: (a) the `security_stamp` claim must be present in the JWT and non-empty (otherwise audit `AccessTokenRejected` with detail `"security stamp claim missing or empty"` and 401); (b) the DB row's `SecurityStamp` must also be non-empty (defensive — no token validates against a null/empty DB stamp); (c) the comparison runs through a new `FixedTimeEquals` helper that converts both sides to UTF-8 bytes, checks length equality, and calls `CryptographicOperations.FixedTimeEquals`. Two regression tests in `JwtMiddlewareTests`: a JWT lacking the claim entirely → 401; a JWT carrying an empty-string claim → 401. Both share the existing helpers `MakeTokenWithStamp` / `MakeTokenWithoutStamp` so the existing `MakeToken` signature stays backwards-compatible. Tests: **196 API** (194 + 2 H5) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **232 / 232**. | Closes Security SEV-1.3 from the 5-agent review. The previous check was `user.SecurityStamp != tokenStamp` against a potentially null/empty `tokenStamp`. Two ways this fails open: (1) a forged token (or a buggy custom issuer) that omits the claim ends up comparing `null` to a user whose `SecurityStamp` is also null/empty — `null != null` is `false`, the check passes, the token is honoured; (2) a token whose claim is `""` against a user row whose stamp is `""` likewise passes. In both cases sign-out, password-change, 2FA-reset, and refresh-token reuse — every revocation surface in the system — silently does nothing for the bad token. The fix is defence-in-depth: require the claim, require the DB value, and compare in constant time so a stamp prefix match is not observable through response timing. The constant-time comparison is symbolic protection more than practical (a 32-char Identity stamp has 10^48 prefix combinations; timing attacks at the network layer are infeasible for it), but `CryptographicOperations.FixedTimeEquals` is the idiomatic primitive and costs nothing. Seeded super-admin: `userManager.CreateAsync(admin, settings.TempPassword)` calls ASP.NET Identity's stamp-update internally, so the seeded row's stamp is non-null/non-empty out of the box — no seeder change needed. |
| D-059 | 2026-05-25 | **H4 — `PasswordChangeRequired` is enforced at sign-in.** `SignInService.SignInAsync` gains a single check right after the credentials succeed and `AccessFailedCount` is reset, and before the account-state and audience gates: if `user.PasswordChangeRequired == true`, the call audits `SignIn.PasswordChangeRequired` (Failure / `AUTH_PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED`) and throws `ApiException(403)`. The user must use the existing `/auth/forgot-password` → `/auth/reset-password` flow (which clears the flag in `PasswordService.ClearChangeFlagAndEndSessionsAsync`) to mint any session. Bonus correctness fix: `SimfUser.PasswordChangeRequired` default flips from `true` to `false` — the previous default silently set the flag on every newly-created user (sign-up included), which the gate would have blocked from ever signing in; only the seeder + an operator-forced rotation should set the flag, and both already do so explicitly. New audit constant: `AuditEvents.SignInPasswordChangeRequired = "SignIn.PasswordChangeRequired"`. Two regression tests in `SignInTests`: (1) a flagged admin with the right password gets `403 / AUTH_PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED`; (2) the same admin once the flag is cleared completes the normal 2FA challenge. Tests: **194 API** (192 + 2 H4) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **230 / 230**. | Closes Security SEV-1.2 from the post-H1/H2/H3 5-agent review. The super-admin is seeded by `IdentitySeeder` with `PasswordChangeRequired = true` AND a committed temp password — the intended compensating control was "first sign-in is forced through a password change". That control was never built; the flag existed and was set but no code read it. Combined with the committed secrets (Security SEV-1.1, a separate operational issue), the seeded credential was a backdoor: the super-admin could sign in cleanly forever with the well-known password. The fix is a 12-line gate in the only place where the flag becomes relevant (the password step), wired to the audit event the SOC already understands. The default-value flip is the same bug at a different layer: a CLR default of `true` on a property whose semantic is "this is a temporary password" inverts the usual assumption (a NEW account has not yet been rotated and is not stuck with a temp password). With the default at `false`, only the seeder's explicit `= true` (and the operator-rotation path when it lands) holds it; every other create path stays naturally at `false`. The gate runs **before** the audience gate so a flagged user gets the same `AUTH_PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED` message regardless of which surface they tried — a wrong-surface user with a flagged credential should fix the credential first; routing them away by audience would mask the real blocker. |
| D-058 | 2026-05-25 | **H3 — Notification bell + state-banner pages accessibility hardening.** Bell trigger gains `aria-haspopup="dialog"`, `aria-controls`, and `aria-expanded` keyed to the open state; its accessible name folds the unread count in (`"Notifications, 3 unread"`) so a screen-reader user gets the same information the visual badge gives. New `UnreadSuffixLabel` parameter (defaults `"unread"`) — wired through CP `Strings.resx` (`unread`) and `Strings.ar.resx` (`غير مقروءة`). The dropdown becomes a focus-managed dialog: receives focus on open via `ElementReference.FocusAsync` in `OnAfterRenderAsync`, closes on Escape (handled on both trigger and dropdown), and closes on outside-click via a transparent fixed-position backdrop sibling at `z-index: 49` (the dropdown sits at 50). Inner clicks `stopPropagation` so the dropdown does not eat itself. Four state-banner pages (CP `/auth/pending` + `/auth/rejected`; Website `/account/pending` + `/account/rejected`) replace their top `<div>` wrapper with `<main aria-labelledby="...">` referencing the `<h1>` — gives assistive tech a landmark to jump to on these full-page state messages. Tests: **192 API + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = 228 / 228** (no test count change — H3 is markup + JS-runtime focus). | Closes SEV-1.3 from the 5-agent review. The earlier P12 bell shipped with `role="dialog"` on the dropdown and an `aria-label` on the trigger, but a keyboard-only user could not tell whether the popup was open, could not get focus into the popup, could not close it without clicking the bell again, and the unread badge was `aria-hidden` with no spoken substitute. The fix uses native primitives (HTML attributes, `KeyboardEventArgs`, `ElementReference.FocusAsync`) — no MudBlazor or third-party widget — matching D-021's "Simf* primitives are semantic HTML" stance. Click-outside is a transparent backdrop rather than a JS `document` listener because the backdrop participates in Blazor Server's event pipeline, leaves no listener to dispose of, and disappears with the dropdown automatically. The `<main>` landmark is a single-element change on each of the four banner pages — no layout impact — and gives the pages a screen-reader landmark they were missing (the banner is the page's primary content; without `<main>` the navigation-skip target was the body root). The `UnreadSuffixLabel` parameter is added with a default value so existing CP wiring stays working before the bilingual key is bound; the Website does not yet render the bell, so no Website wiring is needed here. |
| D-057 | 2026-05-25 | **H2 — `UserProfileService.UpsertMineAsync` first-save auto-transition is now transactional, and a state flip revokes every live refresh token.** Three writes — the profile rows, the `EmailVerified → PendingApproval` state change on `SimfUser`, and `IRefreshTokenRepository.RevokeAllForUserAsync` — now commit through one `ITransactionRunner.ExecuteAsync` scope. `INotificationDispatcher` calls (the visitor-thanks + every-admin-pending-visitor messages) move **after** the commit, so an email-send failure does not roll back the DB. Regression test in `UserProfileTests`: an `EmailVerified` visitor signs in (mints a live refresh token), upserts a valid profile, and the test asserts `AccountState == PendingApproval`, `StateChangedAt` is set, and zero refresh tokens remain unrevoked. Tests: **192 API** (191 + 1) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **228 / 228**. | Closes SEV-1.2 from the 5-agent review. P13 (D-054) wired the auto-transition outside the profile save — a crash between `dbContext.SaveChangesAsync` and `userManager.UpdateAsync(user)` would leave the user as `EmailVerified` (the UI has no path back to the profile form because the user thinks they already submitted), and the existing access + refresh tokens would keep encoding `account_state=EmailVerified` for their full lifetime, bypassing the Pending banner P11 added. Wrapping the three writes in the existing `ITransactionRunner` matches the established `RegistrationService` / `PasswordService` pattern (sign-up + first verification code committed together — D-002 era). Notifications stay outside the transaction deliberately: an email enqueue or in-app row write is a side-effect on a different scope (`IEmailQueue`, `INotificationService`) and a transient failure there should not roll back a settled state change — re-trying notifications is straightforward; un-settling an account state is not. The trade-off: a dispatch failure means the first-time visitor message is missed for that user, accepted because the state is correct and an admin can re-notify by re-rejecting/re-approving. An outbox-style guarantee for the first dispatch is a separate hardening item, recorded here so it is not lost. |
| D-056 | 2026-05-25 | **H1 — `RequireApprovedAccount` policy sweep across every admin endpoint.** Adds the existing `RequireApprovedAccount` policy alongside `AdministratorOnly` on all 15 endpoint classes under `src/Backend/SIMF.Api/Endpoints/Admin/`, including `Logs/LogsEndpoints.cs`. The policy gate keys off the `account_state=Approved` JWT claim added by P10 (D-051). Regression test in `AdminApprovalTests`: a Pending administrator signs in successfully (P10 allows authentication) but receives `403 Forbidden` when calling `POST /admin/admins/list`. Tests: **192 API** (191 + 1 H1 regression) + 18 CP + 13 client + 3 domain + 2 application = **228 / 228**. | Closes SEV-1.1 from the 5-agent sprint review. P10 — by design — lets `PendingApproval` and `Rejected` accounts mint a JWT so they can reach `/auth/pending` and `/auth/rejected`. Before H1 that same JWT was sufficient for every admin endpoint, because the only gate was the `Administrator` role; a Pending or Rejected admin held the role and could mutate user accounts, run bulk deletes, export the user list, and rotate other admins' 2FA. Layering `RequireApprovedAccount` keeps the role check (still needed — `Administrator` is the *who*) and adds the state check (the *whether* — only an Approved admin acts). The fix is a one-liner per endpoint (`Policies(nameof(AdministratorOnly), nameof(RequireApprovedAccount));`), not a middleware swap, so the diff is auditable file-by-file. The same policy is also added to other admin surfaces under `/account/*` separately if the review surfaces them; this commit scope is the API layer. |
| D-055 | 2026-05-24 | **CP page split per the three-UserType model (P7e).** Closes the P7 chain — P7a-d shipped the API + contracts; P7e finishes the rename on the CP UI. **Routes:** `/admin/staff` → `/admin/admins`; `/admin/staff/pending` → `/admin/admins/pending`; `/admin/staff/new` → `/admin/admins/new`. **New pages:** `OthersList.razor` at `/admin/others`, `PendingOthers.razor` at `/admin/others/pending`, `CreateOther.razor` at `/admin/others/new` (with a mandatory ProfileType picker sourced from `/admin/profile-types?userType=Other`).
