The new pages are intentionally minimal — list + create + pending — mirroring the existing Admin / Visitor pair. The CreateOther page is the only one with extra UI (ProfileType picker), because P7d's API requires it. Splitting into three pairs (rather than one combined "Users" grid with a type filter) matches the three-UserType model literally — operators reading the nav see what the system actually models, no implicit filtering. |
| D-054 | 2026-05-24 | **Lifecycle notification + email wire-up + auto-transition (P13).** Wires the P12 (D-053) dispatcher into every account-lifecycle event. **State auto-transition:** when a visitor in `EmailVerified` state submits their profile for the first time, `UserProfileService.UpsertMineAsync` transitions them to `PendingApproval` automatically. The transition is the moment the whole notification chain fires. **Triggers wired:** (1) profile-submitted → in-app + email to the visitor; (2) admin-pending-visitor → in-app + email to every Approved admin; (3) account-approved → in-app + email to the subject with the QR id; (4) account-rejected → in-app + email to the subject with the bilingual reason; (5) admin-2FA-reset → in-app + email to the subject with the reason. **Email templates** ship as inline bilingual HTML strings in `NotificationEmailTemplates` — token substitution is dumb `string.Replace` with `WebUtility.HtmlEncode` on every value. One template per kind × two cultures (EN/AR). A future re-skin (header logo, footer) swaps the implementation behind `Render` without changing call sites. **3 new tests** in `NotificationLifecycleTests`: first profile-submit transitions to PendingApproval + dispatches to visitor + every admin; admin-approve dispatches `Account.Approved` with QR id; admin-reject dispatches `Account.Rejected` with reason. Full suite: **219 / 219** (190 API + 13 client + 11 CP + 3 domain + 2 application). | The auto-transition closes the only gap in the account-state machine: today self-registered visitors stopped at `EmailVerified` with no path to `PendingApproval`. Triggering on first profile-save (not on every save) means a visitor editing their profile later does not re-fire the admin notification storm. The bilingual-inline-strings template approach was chosen over file-on-disk templates because it keeps the email-template engine out of the deployment surface (no file-copy step, no path resolution) and reads cleanly in code review. The `PreRenderedEmailHtml` field on `NotificationRequest` is the seam: P12 already exposed it, P13 fills it; a future renderer swap (e.g. RazorLight for an HTML re-skin) plugs in here. Notifying every admin (rather than a single "ops" address) avoids picking a contact person — anyone with the Administrator role sees the new pending visitor in their bell. The English-template default (not picking the recipient's culture per row) is deliberate v1 — most recipients are bilingual SIMF staff and the in-app row carries both languages; a per-user culture preference is a P14 follow-up if needed. |
| D-053 | 2026-05-24 | **In-app notifications foundation (P12).** New `Notification` entity on the Identity DbContext + EF migration `AddNotifications`. Bilingual title + body in the row so the page reads by current culture without a second lookup. Severity = `Info / Success / Warning / Error`. Two indexes: `(UserId, CreatedAt DESC)` for the page + bell dropdown, `(UserId, ReadAt)` for the unread-count poll. **Dispatcher** `INotificationDispatcher.DispatchAsync(NotificationRequest)` — always writes the DB row, optionally queues an email through the existing `IEmailQueue` (P13 wires the email templates). **Service** `INotificationService` — actor-scoped reads/mutations (list paged, unread-count, mark-read idempotent, mark-all-read via `ExecuteUpdateAsync`, delete idempotent via `ExecuteDeleteAsync`). **API** — five endpoints under `/api/v1/account/notifications/*`. **Client** — `SimfAccountClient` gains five typed methods; both apps' `/account/api/notifications/*` proxy forwards same-origin so a guest token works without role checks. **UI** — `SimfNotificationBell` primitive (bell icon + unread badge + dropdown of latest 5 unread + "Mark all read" + "View all", polls unread-count every 60 s) mounted on the CP top bar. New `/account/notifications` page on both apps via `SimfDataGrid`. Website's `simf-account.js` gains a `deleteJson` helper to match CP. **No callers yet** — P13 wires every lifecycle trigger. **6 tests** in `NotificationTests` — list-only-actor's-rows + newest-first ordering; unread-count excludes read rows; mark-read idempotent + flips ReadAt; mark-all-read clears every unread; delete idempotent; dispatcher writes a row. Full suite: **216 / 216** (187 API + 13 client + 11 CP + 3 domain + 2 application). | Notifications live on the Identity context because every row keys to a user. Bilingual columns (rather than a join to a translation table) match the pattern P9 / D-030 set — every user-facing string ships EN + AR in the row so culture-switching never round-trips. The dispatcher's `SendEmail : bool` lets the same call write the in-app row without forcing the email channel. The bell polls `unread-count` (cheapest read) rather than the full list, so 100 active CP sessions are 100 row-count queries per minute — fine on the indexed hot path. The page lives at `/account/notifications` on both apps, matching the "account-area lives under /account" convention. `SimfDataGrid` reuse keeps the page accessible + paged for free. The component lives in `SIMF.Components.Controls` rather than `SIMF.Components.Forms` because it's a chrome element, not a form input. Shipping the foundation without callers (the whole P12) keeps the diff reviewable; P13 will turn it on one trigger at a time. |
| D-052 | 2026-05-24 | **Guest-only routing + Pending/Rejected pages (P11).** Closes the loop opened by P10 (D-051) — non-approved users now actually land somewhere useful instead of getting tokens with no UI to consume them. **Cookie surfaces the state:** the `/auth/complete` endpoint on both CP and Website decodes the JWT's `account_state` + `user_type` claims and writes them onto the cookie principal, alongside the bilingual rejection reason (read from the API's sign-in response — the JWT itself does not carry it). The sign-in ticket store gains an `AccountStateInfo?` field so the reason rides the hand-off. **Pages:** four new `Authorize`d pages — CP `/auth/pending` + `/auth/rejected` and Website `/account/pending` + `/account/rejected` — each reads the cookie claims (no API call), shows a clear bilingual title + supporting text + sign-out button, and shows the rejection reason in the visitor's culture (Arabic first when culture is `ar`, English fallback). The pages cross-redirect among themselves and to the dashboard if the cookie state drifts. **Layout-level guard:** the CP `CpShellLayout` checks `account_state` in `OnInitializedAsync` and routes non-Approved users to the matching state-banner page before any module content renders. The Website's `Account/UserProfile.razor` does the same. The state-banner pages use `MainLayout` (not `CpShellLayout`) so they never trigger the guard themselves. **API:** a new `RequireApprovedAccount` named policy is registered alongside `AdministratorOnly` (gates by `account_state == "Approved"`); **not** applied to any endpoint in this commit — the client-side routing is the primary gate and a follow-up sweep will opt sensitive endpoints into the policy explicitly. **Direct routing on `/auth/complete`:** rather than relying on the layout guard to redirect after the dashboard half-renders, the completion endpoint itself returns the right redirect target based on the just-decoded state. This avoids a flash of the dashboard. **Tests:** +2 in `SignInTests` (JWT for pending admin carries `account_state=PendingApproval` + `user_type=Admin`; JWT for rejected user carries `account_state=Rejected`); rewrote `SignInTicketStoreTests` to match the new payload shape + added an `AccountStateInfo round-trips` test. New `System.IdentityModel.Tokens.Jwt 8.2.1` package reference on `SIMF.Web` (the CP already had it transitively). Full suite: **210 / 210** pass (181 API + 13 client + 11 CP + 3 domain + 2 application). | Reading the JWT once at `/auth/complete` and copying the claims onto the cookie is the right shape: every protected Blazor page can branch on the cookie identity without ever decoding the JWT (the cookie scheme already runs on every request). Writing the rejection reason onto the cookie (vs an on-demand API call from the rejected page) keeps the state-banner pages dependency-free — they don't need an HTTP round-trip just to render a sentence. The bilingual reason rides as two separate claims (`rejection_reason` + `rejection_reason_ar`) so the page picks by culture without re-fetching, matching D-030 (every API surface carries EN + AR). The layout guard catches the rare drift case (admin rejected the user in another tab while the visitor was on `/account/profile`) without forcing every page to re-check; this is the same shape as the existing `[Authorize]` default-deny in CP `_Imports.razor` — one place to enforce, every page inherits. The direct redirect on `/auth/complete` avoids the "flash of the dashboard" UX glitch that the layout guard alone would produce (it runs after `Body` first renders). The new `RequireApprovedAccount` policy is shipped but unused on purpose: the API-side hardening needs a careful sweep of every endpoint to decide which ones a guest CAN reach (`/auth/sign-out`, P12 notifications, …) — that is the next focused commit, not the right place to do it during P11. The `MainLayout` choice for the state-banner pages prevents the guard-redirect loop without needing special-case logic in the guard itself — clean by construction. The ticket-payload refactor is the simplest hand-off shape that survives both no-2FA (where `AccountStateInfo` is on the response) and 2FA (where the JWT carries `account_state` and the verify endpoints don't return state info — the JWT decode at `/auth/complete` covers it). |
| D-051 | 2026-05-24 | **Non-approved users sign in as guests — rejection reason persists, JWT carries `account_state`, `SignInResponse` carries `AccountStateInfo` (P10).** Sign-in for `PendingApproval` and `Rejected` accounts no longer 403s. The user gets tokens + a structured `AccountStateInfo { State, RejectionReason, RejectionReasonArabic, StateChangedAt }` on the password-step response, and the JWT itself carries two new claims — `account_state` (Registered / EmailVerified / PendingApproval / Approved / Rejected / Disabled) and `user_type` (Admin / Other / Visitor). `Disabled` stays a hard 403 (operator's "this account is dead" lever). `Registered` stays "verify your email first". The new claims travel in every access token so the P11 authorization handler can route a non-approved user to the pending / rejected page without an extra round-trip; the client switches surfaces (CP vs App) by `user_type` alone. **Schema:** four new nullable columns on `AspNetUsers` — `RejectionReason : nvarchar(500)`, `RejectionReasonArabic : nvarchar(500)`, `StateChangedAt : datetimeoffset`, `StateChangedByUserId : uniqueidentifier`. Composite index `IX_AspNetUsers_AccountState_StateChangedAt` supports the "recently rejected / approved" admin query. Migration `AddAccountStateMetadata` is additive and back-fills `StateChangedAt = CreatedAt` for every existing row so the new index has sane sort keys from day one. **Behaviour:** `AdminAccountService.RejectAsync` now writes the reason on the user row (was audit-only before — EN-only admin input mirrors to `RejectionReasonArabic` as a graceful fallback per R1 default; a future bilingual reject form may diverge the two). `AdminAccountService.ApproveAsync` clears both reason fields on the reconsider path so a once-rejected user does not see stale text after re-approval. **Both** state-change paths now call `IRefreshTokenRepository.RevokeAllForUserAsync` so the subject's next API call gets a fresh access token with the new `account_state` claim — without this, a just-rejected user could keep using a stale token for ≤ 15 minutes. New audit event `SignIn.AsGuest` fires when a `PendingApproval` / `Rejected` user signs in (one per sign-in) so SOC can spot non-approved sign-ins separately from `SignIn.Succeeded`. **Tests:** **3 new in `SignInTests`** (pending → AccountStateInfo present + audit; rejected → bilingual reason + audit; approved → AccountStateInfo null) and **+1 / -1 in `AdminApprovalTests`** (rewrote the old "reject blocks sign-in" assertion as "reject persists reason + metadata on user row"; added "approve-after-reject clears the reason"). The old `SignIn_for_a_pending_staff_returns_403_AUTH_ACCOUNT_NOT_APPROVED` test is **replaced** because its premise no longer holds. Full suite: **207 / 207** (179 API + 13 client + 10 CP + 3 domain + 2 application). **Out of scope (lives in P11):** the `RequireApprovedAccount` authorization handler that gates every endpoint except the state-banner pages + notifications + sign-out; the Website / CP `PendingApproval.razor` + `Rejected.razor` pages; the sign-in client branch that reads `account_state` and routes. **Until P11 ships, P10 alone changes the API contract** — a non-approved user gets tokens but the existing client UI does not yet know what to do with them. | The user's instruction was direct ("can login before approved and after reject but in jwt token must show isnot approved so will able to show notification on app and login as guest only"). Two facets: tokens issued + JWT carrying the state so the front-end can route the user to a state-locked page. Persisting the reason on the user row (vs the audit-only P4 shape) is the only way for the user's own sign-in response to carry it — without a second DB call. Splitting the audit event (`SignIn.AsGuest` vs `SignIn.Succeeded`) keeps SOC dashboards clean: a guest sign-in is not a failure (the rejection already happened) but it is a distinct outcome worth counting. The refresh-token revoke on every state change is the security gate — without it the relaxed sign-in would let a freshly-rejected session keep working until token expiry. The R1 default (EN-only admin input mirrored to Arabic) is the cheaper UX today; the schema already supports independent bilingual reasons for the day the admin form upgrades. The bilingual surface mirrors D-030 (every API error carries EN + AR) so the visitor on a rejected account sees the reason in their language. Keeping `Disabled` as a hard 403 preserves the operator's emergency lever — relaxing it would defeat the whole point of the state. The composite index is a no-cost forward-looking add: any future "show me rejections in the last 30 days" admin view hits it directly. Surfacing `AccountStateInfo` only on the password-step response (and not on the TOTP/OTP/RecoveryCode verify responses) keeps the change surface tight — the `account_state` JWT claim is the source of truth for any later sign-in step or any cached token, so the verify endpoints don't need a separate payload. The replaced test is **the right test to remove**: the old P4 behaviour is gone; pretending otherwise would silently let the new contract drift. |
| D-050 | 2026-05-24 | **Interests (الاهتمامات) — admin-managed lookup + M-to-M with `UserProfile` (P9).** Every visitor / Other user picks **1-10** interests on the profile page. The list is **admin-managed in the CP** — no hardcoded seed: a fresh install starts with an empty `Interests` table and the visitor page shows a "list not published yet" notice until an admin creates the first row. **Domain:** new `Interest { Id, Name, NameArabic, DisplayOrder, IsActive, CreatedAt, UpdatedAt? }`; `UserProfile.Interests : ICollection<Interest>` via EF skip-nav. **Schema** (additive — migration `AddInterests`): `Interests` table (PK Id, Name unique, composite index `(IsActive, DisplayOrder)`), `UserProfileInterests` join (composite PK + both FKs `OnDelete Cascade`). **API:** visitor-side `GET /api/v1/account/interests` (active only, ordered); admin-side CRUD under `/api/v1/admin/interests` — `POST list` (paged grid via `GridQuery`), `GET {id}`, `POST` (create, 409 on duplicate Name), `PUT {id}` (update + activate/deactivate), `DELETE {id}` (soft-delete, idempotent). **Contract:** `UpsertUserProfileRequest.InterestIds : IList<Guid>` and `UserProfileResponse.InterestIds` — the validator enforces count (1-10) + distinct ids; the service re-checks every id exists and is active (defence in depth, fail with `INTEREST_INVALID`). **CP:** `/admin/interests` (`SimfDataGrid` with per-row Edit + Deactivate), `/admin/interests/new`, `/admin/interests/{id}/edit`; one new nav entry under "System". **Website:** the profile page renders a new `SimfCheckList<TItem>` primitive with the active interests, inline layout, bilingual labels (Arabic when culture is `ar`); when the lookup is empty the page surfaces "the list has not been published yet". **Client:** `SimfAdminClient` gains five new methods (`ListInterestsAsync`, `GetInterestAsync`, `CreateInterestAsync`, `UpdateInterestAsync`, `DeactivateInterestAsync`); `SimfAccountClient` gains `GetActiveInterestsAsync`. **Audit events:** `Interest.Created`, `Interest.Updated`, `Interest.Deactivated` (one row per mutation). **Error codes:** `INTEREST_INVALID`, `INTEREST_NOT_FOUND`, `INTEREST_NAME_DUPLICATE`. **Resource strings:** ~45 new (≈22 EN + AR pairs each) covering CP page chrome + Website section. **Tests:** **new `InterestTests`** (6 — admin CRUD round-trip, duplicate-name 409, update + deactivate, visitor list filtered by IsActive, non-admin 403, 404 on unknown id); **`UserProfileTests`** gains 5 (min-1, max-10, unknown id, deactivated id, round-trip with diff-and-save) and refactors `ValidSaudiRequest` to async + seed an interest per call. Full suite: **204 / 204** (176 API + 13 client + 10 CP + 3 domain + 2 application). **Latent fix folded in:** the P8 sed renaming `"/profile"` → `"/user-profile"` missed three Website-proxy paths (`/profile/countries`, `/profile/id-image` POST + GET) because their literal strings included a trailing segment; this commit corrects them. | The user's reasoning landed plainly: interests are a many-to-many between visitors and a controlled lookup; the lookup must be admin-CRUD-managed (no hardcoded list) so a non-developer can curate the categories per event. The 1-10 cap is the user's call ("min 1 max 10") — it balances forced commitment (one is the minimum signal) against picker overload. The auto-generated join table is correct for v1: no per-pick metadata is recorded today, and `EF` skip-nav makes the API ergonomic. The diff-and-save in `Upsert` (`toRemove = existing \ requested`, `toAdd = requested \ existing`) avoids the EF anti-pattern of clear-then-re-add, which generates `DELETE` + `INSERT` for unchanged rows and rewrites `CreatedAt`-equivalents on the join. Validation in two places — validator (count) + service (every id active) — is defence in depth; a malicious client that bypasses FluentValidation still cannot persist garbage. The "empty seed" choice keeps the operator in control: no out-of-the-box opinions about what interests SIMF should have. The deactivation path (`IsActive = false`) keeps existing user picks intact while removing the row from the visitor picker — operators can resurrect a row without rebinding any user. Splitting the visitor surface (`/account/interests` GET) from the admin surface (`/admin/interests` CRUD) lets the proxy enforce role at the boundary cleanly. The latent P8 proxy paths were caught only because P9's interest-section needed a working profile fetch; flagged + fixed honestly in the same commit since the diff is one logical area. |
| D-049 | 2026-05-24 | **Single `UserProfile` entity; `ProfileTypeId` moved off `SimfUser`; legacy `VisitorType` string dropped (P8).** Architectural simplification: the per-account form data lives on **one** entity — `UserProfile` — that covers every non-Admin user (Visitor and Other). Admin-typed users do not carry a profile. The `ProfileTypes` FK moves from `SimfUser.ProfileTypeId` to `UserProfile.ProfileTypeId`, because the lookup row is a property of the profile (the tier or partner-kind), not of the user identity. The legacy `VisitorType` string column (`"Visitor" / "Exhibitor" / "Press"`) is deleted — the same idea now flows through the `ProfileTypeId` FK assigned by an admin, not a free-text self-claim. **The change is one commit** because a partial rename would leave the schema in a broken intermediate state. **Schema renames:** table `VisitorProfiles` → `UserProfiles` (sp_rename, preserves data); PK / unique index renamed in lock-step; `UserProfiles.ProfileTypeId : Guid?` added with FK to `ProfileTypes` (Restrict on delete); `VisitorProfiles.VisitorType` column dropped; `AspNetUsers.ProfileTypeId` column + index + FK dropped. **Back-fill:** the migration UPDATEs `UserProfiles.ProfileTypeId` from `AspNetUsers.ProfileTypeId` for any user that already had a profile row, and INSERTs a minimal stub `UserProfile` row (Arabic/English name `''`, nationality `SA`, IsSaudi 0) for any non-Admin user that had a `ProfileTypeId` on the user row but no profile yet — so the P7a/P7d admin-pick is preserved through the cut-over. **API surface renames:** `/api/v1/account/visitor-profile/*` → `/api/v1/account/user-profile/*` (the existing `/account/profile` GET stays — it's the lightweight identity-card endpoint, kept separate from the rich registration form). **Code-symbol renames:** `VisitorProfile` → `UserProfile` (domain + contracts + page + tests + service + config); `IVisitorProfileService` → `IUserProfileService`; `IVisitorIdStorage` / `EncryptedVisitorIdStorage` → `IUserIdDocumentStorage` / `EncryptedUserIdDocumentStorage`; `VisitorIdImage` → `UserIdDocumentImage`; `VisitorIdRead` → `UserIdDocumentRead`. **Config keys:** `Storage:VisitorIdBase` → `Storage:UserIdDocumentBase`; `Storage:VisitorIdEncryptionKey` → `Storage:UserIdDocumentEncryptionKey` — operators must update `appsettings*.json` **and** the env-var overrides. **Audit events:** `VisitorProfile.{Saved,IdImageUploaded,IdImageRejected}` → `UserProfile.{Saved,IdImageUploaded,IdImageRejected}`. **Error codes:** `VISITOR_NATIONALITY_UNKNOWN` → `PROFILE_NATIONALITY_UNKNOWN`; the `VISITOR_ID_IMAGE_*` wire codes are deliberately **kept** (the symbol still says `Visitor` but the wire string is unchanged so an external consumer never breaks). **Stub-on-create:** `AdminAccountService.CreateAccountAsync` (P7d) now drops a stub `UserProfile` row when an admin picks a ProfileTypeId for an Other/Visitor user, so the FK lands atomically with the user. **Resource strings:** EN + AR `Visitor.Profile.*` keys re-keyed to `Account.Profile.*`; the four `Visitor.Profile.Type{,.Visitor,.Exhibitor,.Press}` keys are removed entirely. **Tests:** `VisitorProfileTests` → `UserProfileTests`; the `VisitorType = "Visitor"` line in the `ValidSaudiRequest` helper is gone. Full suite green: 193 / 193 (165 API + 13 client + 10 CP + 3 domain + 2 application). | The user's reasoning was direct ("it's a profile type not a user type, why put it in the user?"). One profile entity for every non-Admin user keeps the model honest: there is one row per profile, one place to look, one FK per concern. The single-commit shape is correct here because the schema is one connected change — splitting would leave one commit on a schema that compiles but is internally inconsistent (table renamed but column not yet dropped, etc.). The data-preserving migration (sp_rename + back-fill UPDATE + back-fill INSERT) means P7d admins who already picked a ProfileTypeId for an Other / Visitor user keep their assignment across the cut-over — no operator follow-up needed. The route split (`/account/profile` for identity card vs `/account/user-profile` for the registration form) sidesteps a name collision with the existing CP profile endpoint; the two surfaces serve different consumers (CP top-bar vs Website registration page). The `VISITOR_ID_IMAGE_*` wire codes stay because they describe the user's ID document (not the user type) and any external mapping must continue to work; we will rename the C# symbols in a later sweep when the external surface is verified to have no consumers. |
| D-048 | 2026-05-24 | **Three hardcoded `UserType`s + dynamic `ProfileTypes` lookup (P7a–P7d).** Every `SimfUser` carries one of three hardcoded user-kinds: `Admin` (CP only, the **only** type that carries RBAC roles), `Other` (Flutter app — Staff / Exhibitor / Sponsor / Media / …), and `Visitor` (App + Website — VVIP / VIP / Gold / …). Subtype-within-type is admin-managed via a `ProfileTypes` table — `{ Id, Name, NameArabic, PageColor, UserType, IsActive }` with a discriminator on `UserType` so a row scopes itself to a single user-kind. **Sign-in audience gate (P7b):** the `cp / web / app` audience now keys off `UserType` (Admin → CP only; Other / Visitor → App / Website) rather than "has any RBAC role"; the `TeamMember` policy was retired (RBAC sub-grades for Admin land later). **Admin endpoints (P7c–P7d):** create-Admin / create-Other / create-Visitor split into three URLs (`/admin/admins`, `/admin/others`, `/admin/visitors`), each Administrator-only, with `ProfileTypeId` validated against the lookup + the user-type discriminator before the user row is created. **Migration `AddUserTypeAndProfileType`:** adds `AspNetUsers.UserType` (string, default `Visitor`); back-fills existing Administrator-role holders to `Admin`; creates the `ProfileTypes` table with the composite `IX_ProfileTypes_UserType_IsActive` index. **Initial seed:** `IdentitySeeder` plants two starter rows — `Visitor – General` and `Other – Staff` — both active. **D-049 supersedes** the `AspNetUsers.ProfileTypeId` part of this decision — the FK moves to `UserProfile.ProfileTypeId`. | The user clarified the model mid-session ("ONLY THIS USER TYPE [Admin] CAN DO ANY THING; the other will work in APP"). The hardcoded enum + dynamic lookup hybrid is the right shape — the three sign-in surfaces are a stable invariant of the system architecture, but the inside-the-app categorisation (VVIP / Gold / Exhibitor / Media …) is a per-event business concern that admins manage. Keying the audience gate off `UserType` instead of "has any role" lets a future Visitor / Other RBAC sub-grade plug in without changing the gate. Splitting the admin-create endpoints by user-type — instead of one create with a discriminator field — surfaces the **shape difference** (Admin = roles list, Other = mandatory ProfileTypeId, Visitor = optional ProfileTypeId) in the route, making each surface auditable on its own. | D-047 | 2026-05-24 | **Per-project log files + CP real-time log viewer (P6).** Serilog writes to `{Storage:LogDirectory}/{ApplicationName}/log-{Date}.log` for each of `SIMF.Api`, `SIMF.ControlPanel`, `SIMF.Web` — daily rolling, 31-day retention — i.e. the IBS "by day, by project" shape. The CP gains `/admin/logs` (Administrator-only): project + day + lines dropdowns, a Refresh button, a "Live tail (5 s)" toggle, and a Download button. **Path-traversal guard:** every API call accepts only the project + file leaf name; the resolved path is verified to stay inside the configured root (`Path.GetFileName(...)` + `Path.GetFullPath(...)` + `StartsWith(root + sep)`). The tail is clamped to a hard ceiling of 5 000 lines so a curious admin cannot pull a 2 GB file into memory. The read uses `FileShare.ReadWrite \| FileShare.Delete` so Serilog can keep appending and rolling while the CP reads. Audit events `Admin.LogViewed` and `Admin.LogDownloaded` write one entry per request, carrying `{project}/{fileName}` in the detail. The implementation lives in `LogFileService` (Infrastructure, IConfiguration-bound) behind `ILogFileService` (Application). `.gitignore`'s `logs/` pattern is changed to `/logs/` (root-anchored) so source-tree `Logs/` directories stay tracked. **Assumption:** the runtime root defaults to `"logs"` when `Storage:LogDirectory` is unset — production environments must set it to an absolute path outside the repo. | The user explicitly asked for this as mandatory ("add log directory under Storage in appsetting and use same format as in IBS — by day, by project — and a CP view as in IBS, real time, download"), citing the IBS pattern verbatim. The split into Infrastructure (LogFileService — filesystem-aware) and Application (ILogFileService — POCO interface) follows the same layering as the existing avatar / visitor-id storage abstractions. Audit-on-every-read rather than audit-on-first-read is the right SOC posture: a forensic investigation needs to know exactly when each admin pulled which log. The clamp + clamp-tested-from-config defaults defeat the "infinite memory" footgun. The read-share flags are the same shape Serilog itself uses, so concurrent writer + reader is by-design rather than by-luck. The `.gitignore` change is structural — without it the `Logs/` source directories were silently invisible to git on Windows (case-insensitive matching). |
| D-046(c) | 2026-05-23 | **Website cookie auth + `/account/visitor-profile` page (Stage 8 V2 — honest close of myComment #18).** The Website (`SIMF.Web`) gains the persistent-auth infrastructure the CP already had under D-029: a cookie scheme (`simf.web.auth`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, 8 h sliding expiration), a `SignInTicketStore` for the circuit→cookie hand-off, `/auth/complete` and `/auth/sign-out` endpoints, baseline security headers (`X-Content-Type-Options`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`), `RequireAuthorization()` on the proxy group. The proxy group `/account/api/visitor-profile/*` mirrors the five V1b endpoints same-origin so the access token stays server-only (D-037 pattern). The new `/account/visitor-profile` page (`InteractiveServerNoPrerender`, `[Authorize]`) assembles the D-044 c primitives — `SimfRadioGroup` (visitor type + Saudi/non-Saudi), `SimfTextField` × 3 (AR name / EN name / place of birth), `SimfSelect` (nationality, populated from the `/countries` endpoint and localised EN/AR), `SimfDatePicker` (date of birth), `SimfPhoneInput` × 2 (Saudi + international, permissive +xx-local shape), `SimfFileUpload` (ID image, uploads via the existing `simfAccount.uploadFile` JS helper), three `SimfAlert` toasts (load / save / id-image outcomes), and a **server-rendered QR SVG via QRCoder** showing the QR id the account was minted with on Approval (D-046 a). Conditional rendering: Saudi → national-id field; non-Saudi → Iqama + Passport fields. The page reloads from the API on save so the QR + profile state stay in sync. `SignIn` / `OtpVerify` now hand off to `/auth/complete?reference=…` via the ticket so the cookie is actually written. Full bilingual resource set (~40 new EN + AR keys). | The cookie-auth shape matches the CP literally — same defaults, same hand-off pattern, same SameSite trade-off documented at the multipart endpoint — so an operator who knows one knows both. The Website does not need role claims on the cookie principal (visitors are not staff; the page does not gate on roles), so the simpler claim set (NameIdentifier + Email + Name) is enough. The conditional Saudi/non-Saudi field swap mirrors how Saudi government services present the same form, which is the visual the SIMF visitors expect. Server-rendered QR SVG (QRCoder) keeps the page dependency-free on the client and lets the CSS sizing handle responsive layout; the QR id round-trips back from the API so a future regenerate flow works without page change. End-to-end smoke is owed in the next session — the integration tests on V1b already pin the wire contract; the page is wired against the same proxy that the V1b tests already cover, so this commit ships a complete vertical slice with regression suite green (178/178). |
| D-046(b) | 2026-05-23 | **Visitor-profile backend — service, encrypted ID-image storage, 5 endpoints (Stage 8 V1b).** Builds on the D-046(a) domain. New **`IVisitorProfileService`** with `GetMineAsync` / `UpsertMineAsync` / `UploadIdImageAsync` / `ReadIdImageAsync` — actor-scoped (the visitor only sees and edits their own row; admin-edits-anyone is a User-Management module concern, out of scope here). New **`IVisitorIdStorage`** with **AES-GCM encryption-at-rest**: file format is `[1-byte content-type code][12-byte nonce][16-byte tag][N-byte ciphertext]`; the per-installation 32-byte key comes from configuration `Storage:VisitorIdEncryptionKey` (base64); a startup-gate refuses to boot without it, mirroring the JWT signing-key check (SIMF-FDS-001 Amendment A.2). Files land at `{Storage:VisitorIdBase}/{userId:N}.bin` — server-controlled filename, no path traversal possible. Five new endpoints under `/api/v1/account/visitor-profile`: `GET` profile, `POST` upsert, `GET /countries` (the curated ~60-entry ISO 3166-1 list in `SIMF.Common.Countries` — EN + AR names), `POST /id-image` (multipart, 5 MB cap, content-type + magic-byte gate identical to the avatar pipeline D-039), `GET /id-image` (streams decrypted bytes back). New validator `UpsertVisitorProfileRequestValidator` — Saudi visitors must carry a 10-digit national id; non-Saudi visitors must carry an Iqama OR a passport (either is fine, both rejected); phone is **permissive** (`+?\d{1,4}[-\s]?\d{4,15}`) per the user's "any phone number not only Saudi, with +xx" direction during design. Three new audit events: `VisitorProfile.Saved`, `VisitorProfile.IdImageUploaded`, `VisitorProfile.IdImageRejected`. Six new error codes including `VISITOR_NATIONALITY_UNKNOWN`. **`SimfAccountClient`** gains `GetVisitorProfileAsync` / `UpsertVisitorProfileAsync` / `GetVisitorCountriesAsync` / `UploadVisitorIdImageAsync` / `FetchVisitorIdImageAsync`. **11 integration tests** including an `Encrypted_file_on_disk_does_not_contain_the_plaintext_bytes` test that uploads a JPEG carrying a distinctive marker string then asserts the raw bytes on disk do not contain it — pins the encryption-at-rest claim with evidence. Full suite: 178/178 pass. | The encryption-at-rest design (AES-GCM with a single per-installation key, content-type prefix byte, in-file nonce + tag) is the simplest shape that defeats "stolen disk" / "backup tape" exposure without requiring a separate KMS. A per-user key derived via HKDF was considered and rejected for v1: it would couple the cipher to the user id and complicate the future admin-impersonation-with-audit flow without adding meaningful protection (the threat model assumes the file system is hostile, not the API process — which holds the key in memory anyway). The startup gate mirrors what the JWT key already does, so the operator's mental model is one rule for every secret. The permissive phone rule reflects the visitor population SIMF expects (international + Saudi mixed); rejecting a UK or US number at the form level would be a wrong-by-default. The curated 60-entry country list ships now, with the full ISO 3166-1 expansion queued as a separate increment when an unmatched code is requested. The Website page (V2) and the Web cookie-auth prerequisite stay outside this commit, kept honest in the catalog. |
| D-046(a) | 2026-05-23 | **Visitor QR-id minted on account approval (Stage 8 V1a — domain + QR hook).** Every account carries a `SimfUser.QrId` — a 12-character Crockford base32 token (alphabet `23456789ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTVWXYZ`, no `0/1/I/L/O/U`), unique across the system, indexed with a SQL filtered-unique index (`[QrId] IS NOT NULL`). The new **`IQrIdMinter`** generates the value with `RandomNumberGenerator.Fill` and retries on the (negligible) chance of a DB clash. The QR id is minted **the moment the account transitions to `AccountState.Approved`** — today that fires from `AdminAccountService.CreateUserAsync` (admin-create lands directly in Approved); a future visitor self-registration approval flow (myComment #34, Stage 8) plugs into the same minter at its own transition point. A new `VisitorProfile` entity ships with the field set from myComment #18 (visitor type, AR/EN names, nationality ISO code, DOB, place of birth, IsSaudi flag, conditional national-id / iqama / passport, Saudi + intl mobile, ID-image relative path), persisted via an EF migration `20260523183038_VisitorProfileAndQrId`. The entity is **not consumed yet** — the `IVisitorProfileService` and the endpoint surface land in V1b alongside the Website page (V2); this commit deliberately ships the domain + QR mint only, so the migration can land safely in production ahead of the page. One integration test pins the QR-mint contract (`Creating_a_user_mints_a_QR_id_…`). | The honest split is "ship the persistence shape early, ship the page later". A migration that adds a unique-indexed column to `AspNetUsers` is the riskiest single change in the increment; it deserves its own commit and a clean test run before the visitor-profile service + endpoints + page stack on top. Crockford base32 is the same alphabet TOTP recovery codes use (D-040), so visitors / staff read QR ids with the same OCR-resistant rules. The "QR on Approved, not on profile save" hook point came from the user mid-design — it is the right call: a visitor needs the QR for event entry regardless of whether they completed every profile field, and tying the mint to a state transition keeps the contract clear. AES-encrypted-at-rest ID-image storage was approved in the same discussion; it lands in V1b together with the `IVisitorIdStorage` abstraction so the encryption-key startup gate can be tested end-to-end. |
| D-045 | 2026-05-23 | **Stage 7 five-agent review hardening (H1 + H2 + H3).** A five-agent review of the D-044 series (security, code, architecture, reality, accessibility) surfaced ~25 SEV-1 and ~35 SEV-2 findings. Three hardening commits land the must-fix set. **H1 (security + correctness, commit `6149742`):** rate-limiting on every new admin endpoint (`bulk-delete`, `duplicate`, `export`, `import`); 5 MB upload cap + ZIP magic-byte check on Import; strict `Users` sheet-name match + 5 000-row import cap; **`SanitiseForExcel`** prefixes the OWASP risky-leading-character set (`= + - @ \t \r`) with `'` on every string cell on export (CWE-1236); new validators `AdminBulkDeleteRequestValidator` (10–500 char reason, 500-id cap) and `AdminDuplicateUserRequestValidator` (`.EmailAddress()`); `BulkDeleteUsersAsync` wraps each subject in `ITransactionRunner`, checks `UpdateAsync.Succeeded`, audits `Admin.UserDeleteFailed` for unknown ids (was a silent skip); per-row `IsInRoleAsync` N+1 folded into the EF projection (5 001 round-trips → 2 per page); `ExportUsersAsync` clones the incoming `GridQuery` before mutating; `GridQuery.Filters` setter coerces null to empty dict. CP: `UsersList` replaces the `Guid:N@simf.local` placeholder with a real `SimfModal` email-input flow; wires `OnCopySelected`/`OnCopyOne`/`OnPaste` with toast feedback (closes myComment #16 honestly); drops the `Edit → Reset 2FA` stub. `SimfDataGrid` clears `_selectedKeys` on sort/filter/page changes (closes the silent partial-delete across pages); disposes the filter-debounce `CTS`. JS `downloadXlsx` tightens the `Content-Disposition` filename regex to `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` and defers `URL.revokeObjectURL` to 60 s. **+6 integration tests** (admin-vs-admin skip with `OperationLog` read, 500-id batch cap, duplicate-collision 409, duplicate-invalid-email 400, wrong-sheet-name rejection, formula-injection round-trip via `XLDataType.Text` assertion). **H2 (accessibility, commit `79e400a`):** new JS helpers `simfModal` (focus trap, ref-counted body lock, document-level Esc, return-focus) and `simfContextMenu` (roving tabindex, arrow/Home/End/Esc keyboard nav); `SimfModal` and `SimfContextMenu` bind to them. RTL fix on `SimfContextMenu` — the grid mirrors `viewportWidth - ClientX` when `<html dir="rtl">` so `inset-inline-start` (= right edge) lands on the cursor. All six new field-shell components (`SimfSelect`, `SimfTextarea`, `SimfDatePicker`, `SimfFileUpload`, `SimfPhoneInput`, `SimfRadioGroup`) gain stable `_helperId`/`_errorId` + `aria-describedby` + `aria-invalid`. `SimfRadioGroup` gains `AriaLabel` fallback and the dangling `aria-labelledby` is gated on the label rendering. `SimfDataGrid` gains `RowLabel: Func<TItem, string>?` (per-row checkbox names — "Select row user@example.com"), per-column filter `aria-label`, optional `<caption class="simf-visually-hidden">`. `SimfToolbarButton` renders `aria-label="@Title"` when icon-only. CSS: `.simf-visually-hidden` utility + `@media (forced-colors: active)` rule that paints a transparent outline so Windows High Contrast Mode users see the focus ring. **H3 (docs, this commit):** API-001 Amendment B documents the GridQuery POST shape, the GET-vs-POST split for public vs admin lists, the Excel I/O contract and the per-subject audit rule. SIMF-CMP-001 categorises the components into "Data display" / "Feedback" / "Fields"; reflects shipped status. The "closes myComment #16, #17, #18" claim from `bbfb04c`/`e628a7a` is corrected: H1 closes #16 (Copy/Paste wiring); #17 ships the Grey button variant but the Grey theme stays deferred; #18 ships the primitives only — the visitor-profile page is the next stage. | Five reviewers agreeing on a non-trivial overlap of findings is the project's signal to harden, not bundle more features. The split into three commits keeps each diff reviewable (the security and correctness fixes land first because they are the highest-risk; the a11y refactor is its own commit because it crosses 14 component files; the doc commit is pure documentation so a reviewer never needs to read code). The architecture reviewer's call to revert `POST` to `GET` on every list endpoint was rejected for admin grids (the structured per-column filter map and the search payload don't fit a querystring) but **accepted** for public read-mostly lists where reverse-proxy / CDN caching is the bigger win — captured in API-001 Amendment B. The reviewer's call to split the `Simf*` library by folder (Fields/Grid/Shell/Feedback) is queued for Stage 8 (cost is touching every `using SIMF.Components.Forms` page; cheaper now than at Stage 12, but not part of a hardening commit). The bUnit gap (17 components, zero component tests) stays as Task #59; landing the harness alone is a focused increment. SEV-3 items not addressed by H1/H2 (clipboard copy as TSV alongside JSON, enum filter dropdowns on `SimfDataGridColumn`, tooltip `tabindex` semantics) are queued in the catalog as their own follow-ups. The visitor profile page itself — the honest close of myComment #18 — is the right next deliverable after this hardening lands. |
| D-044(c) | 2026-05-23 | **`Simf*` visitor-profile primitives.** Six new components for the visitor profile and the dashboard pages (myComment #18 + #33): **`SimfRadioGroup<TValue>`** — typed mutually-exclusive choice with inline / stacked layout, used for Saudi / non-Saudi or visitor / exhibitor / press; **`SimfDatePicker`** — native `<input type="date">` with the SIMF field shell + min/max, ISO-8601 over the wire (date of birth / arrival / departure); **`SimfPhoneInput`** — country-code dropdown (Saudi default, GCC + common international) + local number, posts `+<dial>-<local>` (Saudi mobile / international mobile); **`SimfFileUpload`** — file picker on the SIMF field shell, ID exposed via `ElementId` so the page can read the bytes via the existing `simfAccount.uploadFile` JS helper (the magic-byte validation stays on the server per D-039); **`SimfTooltip`** — CSS-only hover/focus-within hint with top / bottom placement; **`SimfStatCard`** — dashboard tile with title + value + optional positive / negative / neutral delta. The tooltip is CSS-only to keep server-rendered Blazor SSR pages light; it falls back to a noop on touch devices, which is the right behaviour (touch UIs surface labels in the design system instead). `SimfStatCard` is the smallest possible dashboard primitive — keep the surface, the value, and the trend; anything fancier (sparkline / drill-down) is a later increment that wraps it. |
| D-044(b) | 2026-05-23 | **`SimfDataGrid` v2 — full myComment #16 spec.** The data-grid grows the right-click row context menu (Edit / Copy / Duplicate / Delete), per-row fixed action buttons (Edit / Copy / Delete), a top action toolbar (Select All / Add / Edit / Delete / Copy / Paste / Duplicate / Import / Export), per-row selection via a checkbox column, and Excel I/O. Selection model: `Multiselect=true` + `RowKey: Func<TItem, string>` enables the checkbox column; the grid tracks selected keys internally; `SelectedItems` is computed from the current page. Toolbar buttons are rendered only when their callbacks are wired (`OnAdd`, `OnEditOne`, `OnDeleteSelected`, `OnDeleteOne`, `OnCopySelected`, `OnCopyOne`, `OnPaste`, `OnDuplicateOne`, `OnImport`, `OnExport`) — every list page opts in to exactly the actions it supports. Five new admin API endpoints land alongside: `POST /admin/users/bulk-delete` (`AdminBulkDeleteRequest`, one audit row per subject for SOC visibility), `POST /admin/users/duplicate`, `POST /admin/users/export` (returns the XLSX bytes with a `Content-Disposition` for the browser save), `POST /admin/users/import` (multipart XLSX upload). `ClosedXML 0.104.2` is the new server-only dependency in `SIMF.Infrastructure`; abstracted behind `IUserExcelService` so a future swap is one file. The CP shell adds `simfAccount.downloadXlsx` (JSON POST → binary blob → save dialog) and `simfAccount.triggerFileInput` (click a hidden file input from Razor). Five SEV-Architectural integration tests pin the wire contract (bulk-delete-with-skips, bulk-delete reason-required, duplicate-copy + duplicate-404, export-round-trips-through-ClosedXML, import-with-valid-rows, import-rejects-garbage-bytes). | The spec was already approved (myComment #16, D-044 series). The shape is conservative — the grid never holds rows it didn't ask for, so cross-page Select-All is rejected as a design (it would silently lose the rows you can't see). The bulk-delete writes one audit row per subject because SOC review on a bundled "deleted N users" row would be useless; per-subject visibility is the table-stakes for any privileged batch action (mirrors D-041 and D-042 audit shape). The Copy / Paste pair uses the system clipboard so the user can paste rows into Excel or back into the grid; cross-tab paste between SIMF instances works for free. The Excel layout is also the simplest round-trip: the same column order is parsed back, with `State` / `TwoFactor` / `CreatedAt` ignored on import (the API owns those values), so an admin can export → edit names / emails → import to bulk-create. Edit action today routes to `/admin/reset-2fa` because the wider per-user edit page is still gated on the User Management module (myComment #33); the grid is wired for the future page with no further change. |
| D-044(a) | 2026-05-23 | **Server-paged list contract + `SimfDataGrid` v1 + shell-width fix.** Every server-paged list endpoint accepts a `GridQuery { Skip, Top, Search, Sort, SortDescending, Filters }` body and returns a `GridPage<TItem> { Items, Total, Skip, Top }` payload (both in `SIMF.Common`). The admin list endpoint moves from `GET /api/v1/admin/users` to `POST /api/v1/admin/users/list` so the structured query travels in the body; the old GET disappears in the same commit. A new client-side primitive **`SimfDataGrid<TItem>`** renders one page, with header sort, per-column filter inputs (300 ms debounce), a row-actions slot, a paged footer and a loading overlay; columns are declared inline with `<SimfDataGridColumn TItem="…" Key Sortable Filterable>` and a `CellTemplate`. Eight smaller primitives ship alongside — **`SimfButton.neutral`** (Grey variant, myComment #17), **`SimfModal`**, **`SimfSelect<TValue>`**, **`SimfTextarea`**, **`SimfSpinner`**, **`SimfEmptyState`**, **`SimfBadge`**, plus the new `close` / `search` icons. The shell-width observation is fixed by introducing **`.simf-surface`** (full-width surface, used by grid pages) — `.simf-panel` keeps its small-placeholder identity (max 640 px); `.simf-page-wide`'s 1200 px ceiling now actually applies, so `/admin/users` grows from ~635 px to ~1200 px on a wide viewport. | The original `/admin/users` panel capped at 640 px because the page reused `.simf-panel` (a content-width cap meant for placeholder dashboards) inside `.simf-page-wide`. The fix is to give grid pages their own surface class — `.simf-surface` — so the two intents (small placeholder vs full-width container) are not entangled. The grid contract follows the IBS-V10 `GetListData(Top, Next, Query, Filter)` shape and the SIMF API envelope, so future endpoints (events, sessions, news…) reuse one client primitive instead of one new bespoke list page per feature. POST-with-body is preferred over GET-with-querystring because the per-column filter map is structured and the search term can be long; URL-encoding pain and URL-length limits are avoided, at the cost of one well-known divergence from REST orthodoxy. v2 (next commit) adds the row context menu, the top action toolbar, bulk endpoints and Excel I/O per `myComment` #16. |

Every CP page declares `@layout CpShellLayout` explicitly, even when implicit inheritance would suffice. Three new `Simf*` library primitives land in this decision: **`SimfTable<TItem>`** (typed list table with `HeaderTemplate` + `RowTemplate` + `EmptyTemplate`), **`SimfPill`** (`Variant="neutral|admin|on|off"` inline status chip) and **`SimfCheckbox`** (`@bind-Value` labelled checkbox). The previously-leaked ad-hoc `.simf-table` / `.simf-pill` / `.simf-checkbox` CSS in `simf-components.css` is reorganised under proper component-named sections. `/admin/users` is rewritten to use `.simf-panel` + `SimfTable` + `SimfPill`; `/admin/users/new` adopts `SimfCheckbox`; both pages and `/admin/reset-2fa` add the explicit `@layout`. | The Stage-6 commit `44787dd` violated the "pages compose `Simf*` primitives" pattern by writing raw `<table>` + ad-hoc CSS into the page, used `.simf-page-card` (a narrow-form primitive) for a wide list, and omitted the explicit `@layout` declaration the rest of the CP uses. The smoke-test screenshot showed the result — a table squeezed into a 720-px column on the right of the page. The fix is convention-shaped: pull the table / pill / checkbox into the component library where they belong, give list pages a wider wrapper (`.simf-page-wide` + `.simf-panel`), and document the rule so future pages don't make the same mistake.
